Elementary school.

MAURICE and CAROL AMAN

Maurice Aman was born on April 26, 1942, to Elmer and Ida Aman.  Maurice grew
up on his parents' farm, graduated from Hosmer High School.  On April 11, 1964,
he was united in marriage to Carol Dockter.  Carol was born September 9, 1947,
to Alvin and Wohla Dockter of Eureka.  They have two sons: Ryan Lee, born
November 16, 1971 and Dean Roy, born October 1, 1977.  They are both students
at Hosmer School.  They are members of St. John's Lutheran Church.

Maurice and Carol are now farming on the home farm northwest of Hosmer.  Their
farming consists of raising Hereford cattle, pigs, sheep and chickens.  The
cropland consists of small grain, hay and alfalfa.

MILTON and ERMINA AMAN

Milton Aman was born on December 15, 1927, to Jacob and Louisa Aman near
Hosmer, South Dakota.  He farmed with his parents on the family farm six miles
northeast of Hosmer, and attended rural school in that area.  Milton entered
the Military Service in 1951 and served overseas in the Korean War.  He
received his discharge in 1954.

Milton married Ermina Albrecht on June 27, 1954.  They continued to live in
Hosmer.  Ermina is the daughter of the late William Albrecht and Christina
(Hoffman) Albrecht, a resident of the Hosmer Senior Citizens Home.

Milton and Ermina were blessed with eight children. Peggy was born on June 3,
1955, and is living in Aberdeen, South Dakota, with her son Christopher, age
11.  Peggy attended the Aberdeen School of Commerce and is employed by the
Northeast Council of Governments.

Clark and Claud, twin sons, were born on August 28, 1956.  Claud passed away on
August 30, 1956.  Clark and his wife, Mary Jane (Kottenbrock) are living in
Bismarck, North Dakota.  They have one son, Tyler, three years old.  Clark is
employed by Rolfson Construction, and Mary Jane is employed by St. Alexius
Hospital, Bismarck.

Brenda Marie was born on October 30, 1957.  She suffered from spina bifida and
passed away in October of 1959.

Wayne was born on October 2, 1960.  He attended grade and high school in
Hosmer, and Northern State College in Aberdeen.  Wayne died as a result of a
snowmobile accident in Hosmer on March 7, 1984.  He was employed with Rolfson
Construction at the time of his death.

Dale was born on August 16, 1962.  He graduated from the North Dakota State
School of Science at Wahpeton in May of 1985.  Dale has his own upholstery
business in Hosmer.

Allen was born on May 30, 1966, and is attending the Lake Area Vocational
Technical Institute in Watertown studying Electronics.

Dwight was born on October 14, 1968, and is attending Hosmer High School.

Milton, Ermina, and boys are members of the United Church of Christ in Hosmer.
Milton is the Highway Superintendent for Edmunds County, and is a member of the
American Legion, the VFW, and the Moose Lodge.  Milton also sits on the Board
of Directors for the Valley Telco Cooperative Association, Inc., and has held
that seat since 1961.  Ermina is a homemaker, and works part-time at the Hosmer
Senior Citizens Home.

Milton, Allen, and Dwight have an old-time dance band and entertain for
weddings, anniversaries, etc.

OTTO and ANNA AMAN

Otto Aman was born in McPherson County near Hillsview, South Dakota in 1898.
It was here where he attended school and worked with the family until he
married Anna Gross.

Anna Gross was born on a farm in McPherson County near Hosmer in June 1900.
She attended grade and high school in Leola, South Dakota.  She taught country
school in McPherson County for a few years.

In 1921, Otto and Anna were married and lived on a farm near Hillsview.  After
a few years of farming, Otto being afflicted with a skin condition that was
aggravated by the dust of the fields, decided to quit farming.  In 1933, they
moved to Hillsview where Otto worked at various jobs.  When the teacher
shortage became acute, Anna went back to teaching and kept on teaching for 29
years.  She acquired her B.S. degree by attending summer sessions at Northern.
She taught in and around Hillsview until 1956 when she taught in Hosmer.

In 1962, Otto and Anna moved to Hosmer where Anna continued to teach until
1970, and Otto drove the school bus until 1972.  After that he helped out here
and there until death claimed him in 1978.  Anna is living in her home which
they built in 1960.

RUDOLPH and KATHY AMAN

Rudolph (Rudy) Otto Aman was born December 29, 1950, to Otto 0. and Pauline
(Hieb) Aman.  Otto was born May 17, 1919, and died August 23, 1965.  Pauline
was born January 19, 1921, and has been living in Eureka since 1983.

Rudy grew up on the farm three miles southeast of Hillsview, where he is
presently residing.  He attended Hosmer Public School except for his freshman
year which he attended in Eureka.  Following his graduation in 1969 from Hosmer
High School, he attended Mitchell Vo-Tech, taking courses in Agricultural
Management.  He became a member of the South Dakota National Guard from
1971-1977.  During this time he was also helping his mother manage the family
farm and working in the winter months as a tractor mechanic at K&A in Eureka.

October 6, 1978, he married Kathryn (Kathy) Schlenker at Forbes, North Dakota.
Kathy was born August 28, 1957 to Ivan and Lorentina Schlenker of Forbes.  She
attended and graduated from Forbes Public School and attended Aberdeen School
of Commerce taking a course in accounting.  She then taught Title IV at the
Spring Creek Colony at Forbes during the 1977-78 school term.

Rudy and Kathy have two sons, Blaine Andrew born October 21, 1981, and Garrett
Christian born January 5, 1984.

Rudy is a deacon of the Hosmer Congregational Church and Kathy is church clerk.
 Kathy also is a member of the women's softball team and president of the Busy
Belles Club.

BARBARA (HENNE) and WILHELM BARTH

Barbara Henne Barth was born July 25, 1863, in Neudorf, South Russia, to John
and Louisa Henne, she being the oldest of eight children in her family.  She
married Wilhelm Barth in 1886.. They lived on a farm near Odessa, Russia.  They
had ten children, four died in infancy while still in Russia.  They left Russia
in early spring 1899 by train to Bremen, Germany, and then boarded a ship with
children Barbara, John, Louise and Gust.  Daughters Katie and Helen were born
later in America.  Barbara had two uncles already living in the Eureka area.
This family settled on a farm a couple of miles north of Eureka in a sod house.
 Wilhelm worked at the Wittmayer farm and later in the wheat mill in Eureka. In
1908, Barbara and Wilhelm moved to Eureka where they did the cooking, baking,
washing, and all the janitorial work at the Eureka Lutheran College.  Some of
the children received their education there.  They lived in Eureka for about
six years and then returned to the farm living there until Wilhelm passed away
at the age of 65 in 1925.  After this Barbara made her home with her children,
passing away in 1952 at Hosmer at the age of 89.

Their children were: Barbara, born March 5, 1890, married Jacob Kallenberger in
1908, and resided all their lives in the Hosmer area.  They had two children:
Maggie (Mrs.  Gust Job), Aberdeen; and a son, Bill, Lead, South Dakota.
Barbara passed away in 1966 and Jacob in 1957.

John was born August 15, 1893, attended school at Eureka, was in the army from
1918-1919.  He married Theresa Becker in 1919, making their home on a farm in
the Hosmer area until 1932, at which time they moved to Bowdle.  Here they
farmed until retirement in 1958.  Theresa passed away in 1956 at which time he
moved to Aberdeen, living with his daughter.  Born to this marriage were four
children: Walter, Huron, South Dakota; Elmer, Lodi, California; Olivia (Mrs.
Eugene Aldinger), Aberdeen, South Dakota; and Ella (Mrs. LeRoy Schick), Lodi,
California.

Louisa was born November 7, 1896, and married Jacob H. Aman in 1916, at Hosmer.
They had six children: Erwin, Julius, Edmund, Milton and Jake.  Their only
daughter, Lorraine, passed away at the age of four.

Gust was born August 31, 1898, attended school at Eureka and in 1926 married
Marie Krischenowski at Bison, South Dakota.  They made their home on a farm
northeast of Hosmer.  In 1933, they moved to a farm five miles north of Bowdle,
living there until failing eyesight forced him to retire, moving into Bowdle.
In 1969, Gustave became a resident at the Hosmer Senior Citizens home living
there for one year. He passed away in 1970 at the Mobridge Care Center. Gustave
loved farming and the raising of livestock; however, he and his family faced
many disappointments during the dirty thirties.  Those of us who knew him
revere the memory of him as a brave and courageous man, even though he was
blind for many years.  His family includes his wife, Marie, living in Bowdle,
two sons, Ernest of Pierre, South Dakota and Neil, Pierre; and two daughters,
Esther (Mrs.  Gerald Ekholm), Mobridge and Lenora, (Mrs.  Norman Gross), Bowdle.

Katie was born September 25, 1900.  She married Andrew Lehr at Hosmer in 1922.
They had two children, Raymond and Marvin.  Andrew Lehr passed away in 1963 and
Katie is a resident of the Senior Citizens Home in Hosmer.

Helen was born October 20, 1903 and married Jacob Schuler in 1928.  They had
eight children, four died in infancy. The Schuler children are: William, Jacob,
Norman, Rosetta.  Jacob Schuler passed away in 1960, and Helen still lives in
her own home in Eagle Butte, South Dakota.

CHRIST and BEATA BAUER

Christ Bauer was born June 6, 1903, to George and Katherine Bauer.  Christ
lived with his parents until his marriage to Beata Preszler December 2, 1926.
Beata was born January 15, 1906, to Christoph and Magdalene (Schnabel) Preszler.

Christ and Beata farmed southeast of Hosmer until they semi-retired and moved
into Hosmer.

This couple was blessed with three children.  Vera was born December 2, 1932,
and died May 30, 1933.

Robert was born June 27, 1934, and married Fern Schill of Leola, June 10, 1956.
They have two daughters, Penny and Cindy, and reside in Elgin, Illinois.

James was born November 20, 1941, and married Maggie Heier October 1, 1964.
They reside in Faulkton and have three sons Randall, Brian, and Eric.

Christ Bauer died November 2, 1974.  Beata lived in Faulkton with her son and
family from 1975 until her death, April 14, 1984.

GERHARDT and HILDEGARDE BAUMAN

Gerhardt Bauman was born July 7, 1909, in Walworth County four miles east of
Java, South Dakota to Freiderich and Christina Fiechtner Bauman.

Hildegarde was born December 28, 1912, in Hosmer, South Dakota to Christ and
Margaret Vilhauer Jakober.

Gerhardt spent part of his time on the farm until the age of fifteen years,
when his father moved to Bowdle, South Dakota and Gerhardt went to school and
did some musical entertaining.  He was also involved in business until 1929,
when he sold his service station and went into the music orchestra business on
a larger scale.

He married Hildegarde Jakober of Hosmer on March 8, 1936.  This same year the
new couple started the Gamble Store in Hosmer which, at this printing, is 50
years old.

Gerhardt was very interested and involved in community affairs.  He served for
a time on the Lutheran Church Board, was on the Organ Committee when the new
Lutheran Church was built, he served on the Town Board for 17 years, was a
member of the Civic Club for 36 years, served on the Volunteer Fire Department,
was a member of the Sportsman's Club, and was a member of the Edmunds County
Zoning Committee, plus, during all this time, he kept up his musical career.

The Baumans were blessed with two children: son, Paul Gerhardt, is a doctor of
divinity serving as Pastor at St. Andrew Lutheran Church in Sheboygan,
Wisconsin.  He is married to Rae Blevins and they are blessed with three
children: Michael Paul, Kathryn Rae, and Andrew Paul.

Daughter, Margaret Ann, is married to David Trefz. They live at Webster, South
Dakota, where she has been teaching vocal music in the school system for 18
years.  She also has a major in English.  They are blessed with three children:
Rachel Margaret, Matthew David, and Maryann Margaret.

CHRISTIAN BIEBER FAMILY

Christian Bieber was born on December 13, 1896, to Philip and Fredericka
(Huber) Bieber in Glueckstal, South Russia.  When he was eleven years old, he
and his family came to this country and settled on a farm two miles east and 1
1/2 miles south of Hosmer.  During World War I he was drafted into the armed
forces but the war ended before his training was completed.

On June 18, 1922, he married Maria Buechler.  Maria was the daughter of Jacob
and Katherina (Rott) Buechler.  She was born on a farm north of Bowdle on
February 4, 1900. They made their home on the family farm and they lived there
until the time of Christ's death at which time Maria sold the farm and moved to
Hosmer.  Three children were born to this union.

Renata was born March 4, 1923.  She is currently residing in Minneapolis,
Minnesota and is employed by the Bemis Bag Co.

Raymond was born June 8, 1924.  He resides with his wife in Eden Prairie,
Minnesota, and is a civil service employee.

Hertha was born March 27, 1929.  She resides in Minneapolis, Minnesota, with
husband, Charles Lutz.  She is on the board of directors and trustee of Holden
Village, Chelan, Washington, a national retreat center for all Lutheran
churches.  They have three children: Timothy, Gretchen and Nathan.

Christ was a member of the American Legion and served as a deacon of St. John's
Lutheran Church for many years. He died suddenly in October, 1953.  Maria was
an active Ladies' Aid member and Legion Auxiliary member.  She died on July 4,
1963.  Both are buried in the Bethany Cemetery in Hosmer.

MR. and MRS.  JOHN BIEBER

John Bieber was born to Philip and Fredericka (Huber) Bieber on August 4, 1890,
in Glueckstal, South Russia.  He came to this country with his parents in the
year 1907.  Most of the land around the Hosmer area had been homesteaded by
that time so the Bieber family were not able to homestead a farm but they were
able to purchase a farm two miles east and one and one-half miles south of
Hosmer, at which site they lived a good number of years.  During World War I,
John was drafted into the military service but the war ended before he was to
be transferred to Europe.  On January 12, 1922, he married Barbara Buechler.
Barbara was born to Jacob and Kathrina (Rott) Buechler on March 5, 1898, on a
farm approximately nine miles north of Bowdle, South Dakota.  The Buechlers
came to this country from Neu Glueckstal, South Russia, in 1890.

After their marriage John and Barbara made their home on a farm about one mile
north of the family farm and lived there until their retirement in 1946.  One
daughter, Amanda, was born to this marriage on November 8, 1924.  She died in
1933.

John was a member of the American Legion and both were members of St. John's
Lutheran Church of Hosmer. John died on October 22, 1960.  Barbara was an active
member of the St. John's Ladies Aid and the Legion Auxiliary. On November 16,
1984, she became a resident of the Hosmer Senior Citizen's Home.  She passed
away in July, 1986.

PHILLIP S. BIEBER FAMILY

Phillip S. Bieber was born November 26, 1882, in Glueckstal Colony in South
Russia to Solomon J. Bieber and his wife Fredericka (Kummet) Bieber.  On
November 24, 1909, he married Elizabeth Goetz, daughter of Gottlieb and
Fredricka (Schneid) Goetz.  In April, 1911, they came to America and lived on a
farm 10 miles northeast of Hosmer for eight years and then moved to a farm 11
miles northeast of Hosmer.

Four children were born to them: Jacob, born August 29, 191 1; Emma, born
January 8, 1913; Solomon J., born August 7, 1915; and Martha, born April 13,
1917.

Mr. Bieber farmed with his family until July 11, 1927, when he passed away.
Mrs. Bieber continued farming with her children until 1959 when she moved to
Hosmer.  Mrs. Bieber passed away November 18, 1968.

Jacob married Magdalina Guthmiller in October, 1936. They have two children:
Janice and Donley.  Mr. Bieber passed away May 24, 1978.  Emma passed away May
15, 1982, Solomon passed away November 2, 1975.  Martha is married to Reinhold
Gienger.  They have five children: Delbert, Leila, Patty, Wesley, and Donna.

DARVIN and PATTY BLUMHARDT FAMILY

Darvin Blumhardt was born June 4, 1947, to Theodore and Martha Blumhardt in
rural Roscoe, South Dakota.  He attended school in Roscoe, graduating in 1967.
He served in the National Guard for seven years.

In 1969, Darvin married Patty Gienger, daughter of Reinhold and Martha Gienger
of rural Leola.  Patty was born January 22, 1949.  She attended grades 1-5 in
Blaine Township I and 6-12 in Hosmer.  After graduation she attended Stewart's
School of Hairstyling.

Darvin and Patty have two children: Stacey, born September 6, 1972, and Darek,
born August 9, 1978.

The Blumhardts are presently farming the Reinhold Gienger family farm 16 miles
northeast of Hosmer.

LANCE and MELODY BONEN

Lance Bonen was born in Deadwood, South Dakota, in 1957.  He moved to Hosmer in
1963 with his parents, Lawrence and Velma (Sauer) Bonen, and two younger
brothers, David and Douglas.  His parents operated the Bar & Grill in Hosmer,
which they purchased from Jacob Goehring; the first of many endeavors.

Lance attended all 12 years of school at Hosmer.  During this time he worked
part time for Jim Bauer at the Super Value; Gideon Kaul at Kaul's Repair; he
also worked closely with his dad in the salvage business, " Larry's Wrecking."
He graduated in 1975.

Since 1977 Lance has been Maintenance Engineer at the Sr. Citizens Home.  He
began driving school bus for the Hosmer School District in 1978.  In 1979, he
married Melody Peterson of Roscoe.  At this time they purchased the family
salvage business, and purchased his parents' home (formerly owned by Kermit
Ulmer).  His parents then moved to Bowdle. Lance is also a dealer in raw hides
and furs.  He began raising silver fox in 1980.

Melody was born to Harold and Adelida (Schumacher) Peterson of Roscoe.  She
attended 12 years of school at Roscoe.  She has two brothers, Harlow Peterson
of Aberdeen and Timothy Peterson of Vermillion.  Melody began working at the
Sr.  Citizens Home of Hosmer in 1973.  Since 1983 she has been the church
typist for St. John's of Hosmer, and is presently a Medication Aide at the
nursing home.

OTTO and EMMA BORKIRCHERT

Otto Borkirchert was born September 7, 1902, at Bowdle, South Dakota, to Jacob
and Magdalena (Burrer) Borkirchert. He attended rural school and worked on his
parents' farm.  In December, 1926, he married Emma Wittmeier, daughter of Henry
and Christina (Hieb) Wittmeier.

Emma was born May 12, 1909; on a farm near Zeeland, North Dakota.  She attended
rural school and worked on her parents' farm.  In 1923 she moved with her
family to a farm south of Hosmer, South Dakota.

After Emma and Otto were married, they lived with Otto's parents on the farm
nine miles southeast of Hosmer in Glen Township for seven years.  After the
death of Otto's father in 1934, his mother moved to Hosmer, and Otto and Emma
took over the farm.

Otto passed away July 5, 1969.  Emma remained on the farm until the spring of
1971, when she moved to her home in Hosmer.

The Borkircherts are members of the Reformed Church.

Otto and Emma have two children, Arvina Goehring, who lives in Hosmer, and
Melvin, who is married to Ruth Morrill, and lives on the family farm.

ARTHUR BURRER FAMILY

Arthur A. and Catherine M. (Gisi) Burrer were married on August 26, 1952, in
Hosmer.  Katy is the daughter of Andrew and Carolina (Schoch) Gisi of Hosmer.
Art is the son of Christian and Rosina (Hagel) Burrer of Eureka.

Art was born on November 24, 1922, on the family farm eight miles west of Long
Lake.  He attended a rural school until his family moved to Eureka where he
completed his high school education, graduating with the class of 1942.  After
graduation, he attended Northern State Teacher's College for two years.  In
1944, he entered the army and spent two years in the Pacific Theater.  Art
returned to the States in 1946, upon receipt of his discharge, once again
returned to NSTC and completed his undergraduate studies earning a Bachelor of
Science in 1950.  His first high school teaching job was in Hosmer 1951-56.  He
earned his Masters Degree in 1956.  In 1956-59 he served as Superintendent of
the Hosmer School System.  In 1959-78 he served as a principal at the Selby
School System, Superintendent at Bowdle, Iroquois, Estelline and McLaughlin,
and principal and classroom teacher in Harrold. In 1978, they returned to
Hosmer where he served as principal for two years and classroom teacher until
1982.

Katy was born in Hosmer on January 11, 1932.  She received all of her schooling
in the Hosmer School system and graduated with the class of 1950.  After
graduation, Katy attended NSTC during the summer months and taught rural school
for nine years.  For the past nine years she has been working for the State
Department in Pierre.

Art and Katy were blessed with three sons during their marriage.  All three
boys were born in nearby Ipswich, South Dakota.  Douglas Arthur was born on
November 2, 1956.  He is a graduate of the U.S. Military Academy at West Point.
He married Elizabeth Ruth Potter, also a West Point graduate, on May 30, 1981.
He is presently a captain in the U.S. Army serving at the Headquarters of
Training and Doctrine at Ft. Monroe, Virginia.

Patrick John was born on March 14, 1959.  He is a sergeant in the U.S. Army and
is presently serving his second tour of duty in the Federal Republic of Germany.

Gregg James was born on July 7, 1963, and is a 1981 graduate of Hosmer High
School.  He married Laurie Diane Smith on September 29, 1984.  Gregg is a
sergeant in the U.S. Air Force and is serving with the Headquarters of the
Strategic Air Command at Offut Air Force Base, Omaha, Nebraska.

LYLE and RUTH CLOPINE

Lyle Clopine was born June 22, 1928 to Mervin and Ellen Clopine of Oshkosh,
Nebraska.  He married Ruth E. Wheeler January 28, 1948.  Ruth was born July 18,
1928, to Robert and Manta Wheeler of Reydon, Oklahoma.

The Clopines farmed in Julesburg, Colorado, from 1948 through 1953, when they
moved to the Cresbard, South Dakota area.  They farmed in this area until 1958
when they moved to a farm north of Huron.  In 1959, Lyle and Harold Peterson
purchased the Jacob Goehring Elevator.  In February, 1960, Mr. Peterson
suffered a stroke and Lyle operated the business under the name Farmers
Elevator Company Incorporated.  In February, 1967 the elevator was destroyed by
fire.

In 1969 through 1975, Lyle worked with construction. He left the construction
business and began trucking until 1982.

In 1982 through 1985, he started the farrowing and finishing of hogs at a site
one-half mile west of Hosmer.

Ruth Clopine was employed by the Hosmer Senior Citizens Home in the spring of
1967, a job she is enjoying at the present time.

The Clopines have four children.  Karen was born December 6, 1948, lives in
Groton, South Dakota and is married to Dale Wolter.  Dale is engaged in farming
and Karen is a Registered Nurse employed at St. Luke's Hospital in Aberdeen.
They have four children: Collette Sue, Keith, Kervin, and Cora Lea.

Korene was born May 29, 1950, lives in Rapid City, South Dakota and is a
self-employed tax and accounting consultant. She is also engaged in raising and
training thoroughbred race horses.

Katherine LuEllen was born July 9, 1956, lives in Minneapolis, Minnesota, and
is married to Timothy Bort.  Tim is employed by Servicemasters and Katherine
manages a Day Care Center.  They have two children: Bethany Ellen and Caryn Sue.

Karmyn Lanea was born August 26, 1964, lives in Aberdeen, is a Registered Nurse
and is employed at the Aberdeen Nursing Center and is attending Northern State
as a psychology major and sociology minor student.

DALE and KATHY DOCKTER

Dale Dockter graduated from Hosmer High School in 1970.  He furthered his
education attending Mitchell Vocation Trade School in a two year mechanics
program.  Returning to the farm, he then was employed with the Chevy garage for
a year before starting his own repair shop on the farm.

On October 19, 1985, Dale was united in marriage to Kathy Anderson at St.
John's Lutheran Church in Hosmer. They are now making their home on the family
farm north of Hosmer where they are also operating Dockter's Repair.

Dale is the son of Elmer and Adelia Dockter and was born in 1952.  Kathy is the
daughter of Gordon and Beverly Kilber of Ipswich and was born in 1961,
graduating from the Ipswich High School.

ELMER and ADELIA DOCKTER

Elmer Dockter, son of Fred and Dorthea (Pietz) Dockter, was born June 24, 1923.
He grew up on the family farm taking over after his marriage to Adelia
Borkirchert on October 9, 1949.  Adelia was born October 2, 1923, and is the
daughter of Jacob and Magdelena (Burrer) Borkirchert.

To this marriage a son, Dale, was born in 1952.  Elmer and Adelia made their
home on the farm until November 1985, when they moved into Hosmer.  Their son,
Dale and his wife Kathy (Anderson), are now operating the farm.

FOUR GENERATION DOCKTER FARM

In 1900 Mr. and Mrs. John J. Dockter came to America from Neudorf, South
Russia, and homesteaded six miles north of Hosmer.  In 1922 son Fred and wife
Dorthea moved on the farm until 1949 when they retired.  Fred's son Elmer and
wife Adelia then took over the farm for the next thirty-six years at which time
they retired and moved to Hosmer.  Their son Dale and wife Kathy are now living
on the family farm.

FRED and DORTHEA DOCKTER

Fred Dockter was born in 1900, in McPherson County and grew up on his father's
farm.  In 1922, he married Dorthea Pietz.  This marriage was blessed with three
children: Lorma (Mrs.  Albert Beck), Eureka; Elmer, Hosmer; and Arline (Mrs.
Helmuth Fisher), Eureka.

Fred continued farming until 1949 when he sold his farm and moved into Hosmer.
Fred kept himself very busy with all type of repair work wherever needed.
Dorthea, due to health reasons, is now a resident at the Eureka Lutheran Home
since September of 1984.  Fred continues living in their home at Hosmer.

VERNON and ALEANA EBERHART

Vernon Eberhart was born March 10, 1930, in McPherson County, to George and
Emma (Schnaible) Eberhart.  Vernon attended grades 1-8 at a farm school in
Campbell County. He lived on the farm with his parents until he joined the
Marines, December, 1951.  After his discharge in December, 1953, he married
Aleana Ehresman January 28, 1954.

Aleana was born in McPherson County April 27, 1928, to Jacob and Martha
(Wacker) Ehresman.  Aleana attended grades 1-8 at a farm school near Hosmer.
She lived with her parents north of Hosmer until her marriage to Vernon.  They
live on the farm where Aleana grew up.

Vernon and Aleana were blessed with three girls.  Delene was born July 11,
1957, married Dale Schutz of Eureka August 6, 1977, and they have one son,
Dustin Dale.  Delene is employed at the Farmers State Bank and Dale is employed
by Eureka Building Supplies.  They reside on a farm north of Hosmer.

Shelly was born February 20, 1961, married Brian Aman May 30, 198 1, and they
have one daughter, Tiffany Margaret. Shelly graduated from Hosmer High School
and the National College of Business in Rapid City.  She is employed at Super 8
Headquarters in Aberdeen as an office manager and Brian is employed by
Burlington Northern of Aberdeen as a centralized traffic control operator.
They live in Warner, South Dakota.

Peggy was born September 28, 1965.  She graduated from Hosmer High School and
Stewarts School of Hairstyling in
Aberdeen.  Peggy married Richard Mohr on May 17, 1986.

HISTORY OF JOHN ECKMANN FAMILY

John Eckmann was born July 31, 1894, in McPherson County near Hosmer, South
Dakota, to Fred and Magdelina Gramm Eckmann.  He grew to manhood in this
vicinity.  John was united in marriage to Martha Hieb on June 13, 1922. Martha
was the daughter of Wilhelm and Christine Hieb from Hague, North Dakota.

John and Martha made their home on the Eckmann family farm six miles east of
Hosmer.  They sold this farm and moved to a farm west of Roscoe.  John and his
family moved to Igloo, South Dakota, for a few years, then returned to the
farm.  In 1954, they sold the farm and moved to Aberdeen where they lived until
the time of their death.

To this union three children were born.  Irene was born September 15, 1925.
She lived in this area until she married Ben Schlichenmeyer of Roscoe.  They
had two children, John and Milton.  Raymond was born February 25, 1926.  He
married Virginia Schlichenmeyer of Leola, South Dakota.  To this union six
children were born: Larry, Lana, Loren, LaVonne, Leon, and Lonnie.  They reside
in Britton, South Dakota, where Ray is the owner and operator of an implement
shop. Milton is employed at the Flame Lounge in Aberdeen.

CHRIST and MARGARETHA EHRESMAN

Christ Ehresman was born August 11, 1890, to Mr. and Mrs. John Ehresman on a
farm northeast of Hosmer.  After he had grown to manhood he married Margaretha
Neuharth on November 15, 1915.  Margaretha was born in the Eureka area on June
3, 1889, to Mr. and Mrs. George Neuharth. They settled on a farm six miles east
and one-fourth mile south of Hosmer.

Christ and Margaretha were blessed with four sons.  Edwin, born December 2,
1916; Herbert, born March 10, 1918; and twins Harold and Eugene were born May
18, 1921.  Herbert died of cancer May 6, 1981.  Eugene lives in Aberdeen, Edwin
lives in New Ulm, Minnesota, and Harold lives in Hosmer.

Christ and Margaretha moved to Aberdeen in 1942. Christ passed away on May 28,
1952 at the age of 62. Margaretha lived in Aberdeen until the 1970s at which
time she moved to Hosmer to live with Harold and Winnifred Ehresman.  Later she
moved into the Hosmer nursing home. She passed away on July 17, 1982, at the
age of 93.  She had 11 grandchildren and 18 great-grandchildren.

HAROLD and WINNIFRED EHRESMAN

Harold Christ Ehresman, the son of Christ and Margaretha (Neuharth) Ehresman,
was born May 18, 1921, on his parents' farm six miles east and one-fourth mile
south of Hosmer.  He went to rural school in the Northeast Sangamon School.
Harold farmed until 1961 when they moved into Hosmer.  He sold Farmer's Union
Insurance for 27 years, from which he had to retire due to ill health.

On November 3, 1940, he married Winnifred Haar.  Winnie is the daughter of
Benjamin and Emelia Haar from rural Roscoe.  She was born at Freeman, South
Dakota, on May 17, 1921.  She moved to the Roscoe area and attended rural school
there.

Harold and Winnifred have four children.  Phyllis was born June 2, 1941.  She
attended the rural township school and graduated from Hosmer High School in
1959.  She graduated from Augustana College in Sioux Falls, in 1963.  She
married Thomas Jacobson June 26, 1965, in Hosmer at St. John's Lutheran Church.
They reside in Spicer, Minnesota, and have two children.  Dawn was born
December 28, 1969, and Troy was born May 24, 1973.

Sharon (see Roland Sandmeier history in this book).

Coradean was born in Aberdeen on May 23, 1946.  She attended rural and Hosmer
Public School and graduated from Hosmer High School in 1964.  She worked in
different towns before marrying Orvie Reuer at Selby in the Lutheran Church on
August 14, 1970.  They reside in Watertown, South Dakota.  They have three
children: Kimberly Ann, born on April 18, 1976; Eric Robert, born December 1,
1978; and Jason Andrew was born September 6, 1979.

Curtiss was born in Ipswich, South Dakota on May 19, 1947.  He attended rural
and Hosmer Public School and graduated from Hosmer High School in 1965.  He
graduated from Huron College in 1969 and is a schoolteacher.  He also is a
referee for many basketball games.  He married Coleen Schaffer from rural Java
on June 6, 1970.  They live in Watertown, South Dakota, and have two children,
Kari, born May 7, 1973; and Karl, born June 26, 1981.

Winnie is a member of ALCW and Harold was active in church and civic affairs.
They now live in the Hosmer apartments in retirement.

JACOB and MARTHA EHRESMAN

Jacob Ehresman was born January 26, 1900, in McPherson County to John and
Margaret Ehresman.  He lived on a farm northeast of Hosmer until his marriage
to Martha Wacker in November, 1926.  Martha was born July 17, 1905, in
McPherson County to Jacob and Margaretta Wacker.

Jacob and Martha settled on a farm five miles north of Hosmer.  In 1962 they
retired and moved to Hosmer.  Jacob passed away May 20, 1981.

The Ehresmans were blessed with two daughters.  Aleana Ehresman was born April
27, 1928, married Vernon Eberhart January 28, 1954, and they have three
daughters, Delene, Shelly, and Peggy.  They live on the Ehresman family farm.

Esther Ehresman was born November 27, 1931, married Edwin Holzworth September
22, 1952, and they have three children, Roger, Glenda, and Becky.  They live on
a farm near Java.

FAMILY OF JOSEPH EISENBEIS

Joseph Eisenbeis emigrated from Russia to South Dakota and eventually settled
in the Hosmer area.  He was married to Katherina Kirschenmann.

To this union was born Julius, Magdalina, Gottlieb and Albert.  Katherina died
in 1930 and Joseph died in 1957. Magdalina is still a resident of Hosmer.
Albert died in April, 1986.  Gottlieb died in February 1983 and Julius died in
April 1983.  Gottlieb, Magdalina, and Albert never married.  Julius married
Lydia Mayer and they have two children, Milton and Verna.

JULIUS L. and LYDIA (MAYER) EISENBEIS

Julius was born September 16, 1901, to Joseph and Katherina (Kirschenmann)
Eisenbeis near Bowdle, South Dakota.  He moved with his parents to a farm four
miles southwest of Hosmer where he grew up .

Lydia was born August 16, 1907, to Jacob and Caroline (Hieb) Mayer about 18
miles northeast of Hosmer.  Julius and Lydia were married October 13, 1925, and
built a farmsite 17 miles northeast of Hosmer where they farmed and raised
livestock for 37 years.

In 1963, they moved to Aberdeen and developed the Eisenbeis Mobile Home Court
which is still in the family. They have two children: Milton (November 15,
1927) of Wheaton, Minnesota; and Mrs. Allen (Verna) Kulm (June 17, 1930) who is
making her home in Aberdeen.  Julius died in April, 1983.  Lydia is still a
resident of Aberdeen.

DAVID M. and JULIANA EISENBEISZ

David M. Eisenbeisz was born in South Russia in 1887. He came to America in
1893 with his parents who settled on a farm north of Bowdle, South Dakota.  He
farmed there until he married Juliana Rohrbach, daughter of Jacob and Juliana
Rohrbach, on March 11, 191 1. Juliana was born in Neudorf, Russia, on June 30,
1893.  She came to the United States with her parents and settled on a farm
near Hosmer, South Dakota.  David and Juliana farmed in the Bowdle and Hosmer
area where their six children were born and raised.

In 1943 they retired and moved to Hosmer, South Dakota.  David passed away on
March 14, 1963.  Juliana remained in her home in Hosmer except for the last few
years when she moved to Aberdeen, South Dakota.  She passed away on October 19,
1972.

They had two sons, David Jr., who lived in Aberdeen, and died on March 29,
1977, and Melvin, who lives at Brentford, South Dakota.  They also had four
daughters, Katie, Mrs. Gottlieb Kaul, who lives in Aberdeen; Mary, Mrs. Edwin
Kallenberger, also lives in Aberdeen; Christina, Mrs. Erwin Aman, who lives on
a farm near Hosmer; and Martha, Mrs. Dave Hardy, who lives in Hosmer.

GARDNER and GERTRUDE EISENBEISZ

Gardner Eisenbeisz was born September 9, 1919, at rural Hosmer to Matthew and
Rosina (Oster) Eisenbeisz.  Gertrude Eisenbeisz was born January 13, 1926, at
Eureka, South Dakota to Fred and Christina (Rohrbach) Kammerer.

Gardner grew up on a farm and attended country school near Hosmer.  He served
in the U.S. Army during World War II from April, 1942, to November 26, 1945.
He served in North Africa and Italy with the station hospitals.  After his
discharge, he did some farming and drayline work.  In 1950, he started his own
trucking business which he still operates at the present time.

Gertrude graduated from Hosmer High School and began her teaching career in
1945.  She received her degree in elementary education in 1971 from Northern
State College. She taught in Edmunds and McPherson County country schools and
at the present time is teaching third and fourth grade in the Hosmer school
system.

Gardner and Gertrude were married November 27, 1947, in St. John's Lutheran
Church where they are members. Gardner is a member of the American Legion, VFW
and Civic Club.  He was a volunteer fireman for 35 years and at present is
serving on the city council.  Gertrude is an American Legion Auxiliary member
and was a Hosmer Homemakers Extension Club member for more than 25 years.

MILBERT G. and MARIAM EISENBEISZ

Milbert G. Eisenbeisz was born on September 16, 1922, in the Hosmer, South
Dakota area, to Jacob and Katherina Jundt Eisenbeisz.

On November 24, 1946, he was united in marriage to Mariam D. Jung, daughter of
David and Katherine Gutjahr Jung.  She was born December 5, 1923, on a farm
five and one-half miles southeast of Hosmer, South Dakota.

They lived on a farm northeast of Bowdle, South Dakota, until February 13,
1958, when they moved to the farm where Mariam was born southeast of Hosmer.
Milbert, or Gordon as he was known, passed away on November 24, 1981.  Mariam
is still residing on the farm and is working as a Dietary Aide in the Senior
Citizen's Home at Hosmer, South Dakota.

Four sons were born to Gordon and Mariam.

David Jacob was born January 1, 1948, now living in Arvada, Colorado, and
married to Judith Barbarick.  David was wounded while serving his country
during the Vietnam War. He is now employed with Sears, Roebuck & Co. in Arvada.

Thomas Gordon was born April 6, 1949, now living in Aberdeen, South Dakota, and
married to Rosemary Froehle. They have two children, Bradley and Kevin.  Thomas
graduated from Northern State College in Aberdeen, South Dakota, with a B.S. in
Business Administration.  He is now employed at Brown County Implement at
Aberdeen.

Peter Edmund was born August 7, 1956, now living in Hosmer, South Dakota, and
married to Carolyn Heilman. They have one son, Brett, and one daughter, Sommer
Marie.

John Henry was born April 10, 1958, lives on his parents' farm with his mother
Mariam.  John, a fourth generation, operates the family farm with his brother
Peter.

PETER and CAROLYN EISENBEISZ

Peter Eisenbeisz and Carolyn Heilman were united in marriage in the Holy
Trinity Catholic Church on April 17, 1982.  Their son, Brett, was born on
August 13, 1983, their daughter, Sommer Marie, March 7, 1986.

Peter is the son of Mariam Eisenbeisz and the late Milbert G. Eisenbeisz.
Carolyn is the daughter of Christ and Agatha Heilman.  Peter was born August 7,
1956, and Carolyn was born November 5, 1958.

After graduating from Hosmer High School in 1974, Peter attended Northern State
College for one year.  In June, 1975, he was employed by the former Jake Mohr
and Sons Construction Company and worked for them until the company dissolved
in March, 1983.  He was then self-employed in the construction business until
February, 1984, when he was employed by the Hosmer Elevator Company.  He is
presently employed at the elevator and is engaged in farming with his brother,
John Eisenbeisz.

Carolyn graduated from Hosmer High School in 1977. She attended Lake Area
Vocational Technical Institute in Watertown, South Dakota, September, 1977 to
July, 1978 to become a medical assistant.  She was employed at the Shinghal
Professional Assn. in Aberdeen, South Dakota from July, 1978 until August,
1981.  Since September, 1981, she has been employed at the Eureka Medical
Clinic.

After their marriage, Peter and Carolyn lived on a farm 11 miles west of
Hosmer.  On July 3, 1985, they moved into the former Art Herrboldt home in
Hosmer.

HARRY and LORRAINE FISCHER

Harry, the son of David and Elizabeth (Beck) Fischer, was born May 19, 193 1,
into a family of six children.  Harry's twin brother was stillborn.  Harry
attended country school for eight years about a mile from the home place
located 14 miles southwest of Eureka, South Dakota.  He helped his parents with
the farm work until he was inducted into the armed services February 26, 1952,
serving 18 months in Germany, and was discharged February 17, 1954.

In March of 1954, he met his wife to be, Lorraine Brockel, the daughter of
Philip and Rosie (Beck) Brockel, who was born February 9, 1935, into a family
of 12 children. Lorraine attended country school and graduated from Herreid
High School.  Prior to their marriage, she worked as a dental assistant and
taught school.

They were married September 26, 1954, at the Herreid Baptist Church.  Harry
really surprised Lorraine when on the evening before their wedding he arrived
with a new blue 1954 Ford to go to rehearsal.  They began their life together by
moving onto the farm with Harry's parents.  One year later his parents retired
and they took over full responsibility of the farm.

In October, 1961, they decided to move to Aberdeen. Harry worked at Standard
Auto Supply and K. O. Lee, but the rural environment was still with them.  In
October 1962, they bought a farm from Eugene Kusler which was located near
Hosmer and made the move.  They started all over, purchasing equipment,
animals, and things necessary to make farm life a reality.  In 1973, they
bought the Reinhold Weishaar farm adding it to the present one to make the farm
as it is today.

Their first child, Gailand Davis, was born premature weighing 2 lbs. and 131/4
oz. on July 5, 1956, in the Eureka Hospital and did not go home until September
9, 1956.  One of the most heartbreaking situations to accept was when Dr.
Rudolph told them Gailand would never be able to see. Gailand attended the
School for the Visually Handicapped in Aberdeen, South Dakota, for 13 years
graduating in 1975. That same year he went to work at the Lions Rehab Center
and is employed there today.

Their second son, Kim Harry, was born May 17, 1958, growing up to be quite a
helper to his older brother.  Kim attended Hosmer grade and high school
graduating in 1977.  He attended the Brown Institute, Minneapolis, studying
radio technology.  In 1979, Kim and Jolene Moerke were married. They made their
first home in Hosmer moving to Aberdeen where they now reside.  Kim works at
Dakota Electronics. They have three daughters, Jennifer, Tanya, and Danielle.

Their third child, Nyla June, was born June 5, 1967.  She attended grade and
high school at Hosmer graduating in 1985.  Nyla is presently attending Northern
State College at Aberdeen, South Dakota, majoring in general business.

BRUCE FLITTIE FAMILY

Bruce Flittie was born December 25, 1946, in Sioux Falls, South Dakota.  He
attended school in the area and received his B.S. degree in Wild Life
Management from the South Dakota State University in Brookings, South Dakota.

Bruce married Sandra Pietz, daughter of Alexander and Irene (Geist) Pietz of
rural Hosmer.  Sandra graduated from Hosmer High School and attended South
Dakota State University in Brookings, where she received a B.S. degree in
Nursing.

The young couple moved to the former Edwin F. Mohr farm in January 1977.  The
farm is located six miles east and six miles north of Hosmer.  Bruce is engaged
in farming and Sandra is employed as a Registered Nurse in the Senior Citizens
Home in Hosmer.

Bruce and Sandra have two children: Amanda Beth, born September 10, 1978, and
Brett Alexander, born July 23, 1981.

The family attends the United Methodist Church in Eureka, South Dakota.

BILL and LORINA FORKEL

Bill, the son of Wilhelm and Magdalene (Gutjahr) Forkel Sr., was born September
23, 1917, near Hosmer.  Bill was united in marriage to Lorina Kranzler February
26, 1938.  She is the daughter of Jacob and Bertha (Muhlbeier) Kranzler and was
born May 15, 1916, near Leola.

They are residing at the homestead which is located 12 miles east of Hosmer.
Bill has lived there for 67 years and is still farming at the present time.  He
has served on the township board, Hosmer school board of education, fire
department, and a Gideon member.

They are members of the United Church of Christ in Hosmer where both
participate in the church choir, and have taught Sunday school.  Bill was a
Sunday school superintendent and church secretary, and Lorina taught Bible
School for many years and is a member of the Women's Fellowship. This union was
blessed with four children: LeRoy Forkel, Rapid City; Virginia Aman, Eureka;
Sylvia Preszler, Hosmer; and Marcella Opp, Underwood, North Dakota. They have
11 grandchildren and 4 great-grandchildren.

EDWIN and EMMA FORKEL

Edwin Forkel was born October 21, 1910, to Wilhelm and Magdalena (Gutjahr)
Forkel, on a farm three miles east of Hosmer.  Wilhelm was born April 2, 1889,
in Odessa, South Russia and passed away May 3, 1976, in Aberdeen. Magdalena was
born March 20, 1892, at Odessa, South Russia, to Jacob and Eva (Stotz) Gutjahr
and passed away July 13, 1985, at Aberdeen.  Edwin has two brothers, Bill and
Adolph; and three sisters, Lydia Ehresman, Mrs. Adam (Maggie) Kainer, and Mrs.
Herbert (Esther) Lauer.  One sister died in infancy.

Edwin lived with his parents on a farm 12 miles east of Hosmer until his
marriage to Emma Gienger at a country church west of Leola July 29, 1934.
Edwin and Emma farmed in the Hosmer area until 1970 when they retired and moved
into Hosmer.  Edwin and Emma celebrated their 50th wedding anniversary July 29,
1984.

Emma was born August 8, 1912, on a farm five miles east of Long Lake to Conrad
and Katherina (Stoecker) Gienger.  Emma passed away October 31, 1984.  Conrad
Gienger was born December 24, 1885, six miles east of Long Lake, and passed
away January 17, 1937.  Katherina was born April 6, 1885, at Odessa, South
Russia and passed away April 27, 1972.  Emma had three brothers, Reinhold,
Herbert and Eugene Gienger and two sisters, Mrs. John (Hilda) Keller and Mrs.
Ted (Alvina) Bauer.

Edwin and Emma were blessed with 6 children, 23 grandchildren, and 4
great-grandchildren.

Lorina was born June 9, 1935, married Leo Jung and resides on a farm northeast
of Roscoe.  They have one son, Larry, four daughters, Mrs. Ray (Linda)
Studnicka, Mrs. Kim (Lora) Jundt, Linnet and Luanne and three grandchildren.

Verna Ardella was born August 8, 1937.  She married Leo Schumacher and they
reside on a farm near Long Lake.  They have a son, Milo, and two daughters,
Victoria and Heidi.

Esther was born April 1, 1939, and graduated from Hosmer High School in 1958,
and is presently a nurse.  She is married to Wayne Steinmetz and they reside in
Garden City, Kansas.  The family consists of three sons, Mark and Kirk Gross,
Wayne Steinmetz; and four daughters, Lana, Gina and twin daughters, Kelly and
Kari.  One son died in infancy.

Wilbert was born October 13, 1943 and graduated from Hosmer High School in
1961.  He is residing on the family farm and is married to Vickie Teske.  They
have one son, Patrick, and one daughter, Kristi.

Herbert was born May 3, 1946, and graduated from Hosmer High School in 1964,
and is employed at Safeguard in Aberdeen where they reside.  He married Kathy
Weishaar and they have two sons, Ricky and Todd, and one granddaughter.

Marjorie was born April 25, 1947, is a 1965 graduate of Hosmer High School, and
is employed as head bookkeeper of the Hoven Bank.  She is married to Sydney
Baus of Hoven, where they reside.  They have one daughter, Denise, and two sons
Wade and Nick.

As active members of the United Church of Christ in Hosmer, Edwin and Emma
served as custodians and sang in the church choir.  Edwin is serving as trustee
and custodian at the present time.  Emma was an active member of the Women's
Fellowship and a member of the Senior Citizens Home Auxiliary.  Edwin and Emma
enjoyed sharing their musical talent in the community.

WILBERT and VICTORIA FORKEL FAMILY

Wilbert was born on his parents' farm ten miles east and one mile south of
Hosmer, October 13, 1943, to Edwin and Emma (Gienger) Forkel.  Wilbert attended
elementary school near the farm and graduated from Hosmer High School in 1961.
After graduation Wilbert began farming, and is presently owner and operator of
Forkel's Registered Dairy.

Wilbert married Victoria Teske on August 30, 1964, at the Zion Lutheran Church
in Eureka.  Victoria was born on March 4, 1946, to Edward and Adeline (Hieb)

she worked in the Luncheonette at Eureka.

Willie and Vickie have two children.  Patrick Wilbert was born on March 17,
1965, and graduated from Hosmer High School in 1983.  Patrick attended Lake
Area Vo-Tech at Watertown and took Ag Production courses.  He is presently

Kristi Kay was born on February 21, 1967, and graduated from Hosmer High School

The Forkels are members of the United Church of Christ where Wilbert has served

Vickie is a member of the Highlighters Extension Club and is a 4-H leader.
Vickie and Kristi served on the Hosmer Centennial Cookbook Committee.

THE ALLAN A. GAB FAMILY

Allan Albert was born October 30, 1923, in McPherson County to Jacob and Katie
(Stotz) Gab.  Jacob was born in the U.S. in 1895, and died in 1971.  Katie was
born in South Russia in 1904.  She came to America when she was eight years old.

Allan graduated from Eureka High School.  He lived with his parents on a farm
in southwest McPherson County until his marriage to Winnie E. Goehring on June
20, 1948. Allan and Winnie moved to a farm in the Hosmer area where they
reside.  Winnie was born north of Hosmer on October 9, 1929 to John and Martha
(Maisch) Goehring.  Winnie graduated from Hosmer High School, and is employed
in the Hosmer Public School as the head cook.

Allan and Winnie were blessed with six children.  Rex Neal was born on December
4, 1950.  He graduated from Hosmer High School, two years in electronics at
Vo-Tech in Mitchell, and served four years in the Air Force.  After his
discharge from the Air Force, he moved to Bowdle where he was employed for ten
years.  He returned to college at Mitchell Vo-Tech in computer system
technology.  He married Marjean Nolz on August 2, 1971.  Marjean graduated from
Parkston High School and Mitchell Vo-Tech.  She is employed as a bookkeeper in
a mini-mall in Mitchell.  Rex and Marjean have two daughters.

Craig Allan was born November 3, 1954.  He graduated from Hosmer High School
and served five years in the Air Force.  After his discharge, he entered USD/S
in Springfield, South Dakota.  He graduated with a B.S. in electronic
technology. Craig is employed as a supervisor at the ARCO Pipe Line Company in
Independence, Kansas.  He married Polly Bolin on November 26, 1976.  Polly
graduated from Tucson High School.  They have two sons.

Brian Lee was born March 16, 1957.  He graduated from Hosmer High School and
LATVI of Watertown, South Dakota.  He is employed in computer repair and
salesperson in San Francisco, California.

Beverly Ann was born April 20, 1959.  She graduated from Hosmer High School and
LATVI in Watertown.  Beverly is employed as a respiratory supervisor in a
hospital in Lafayette, Indiana.

Blair David was born July 15, 1962.  He graduated from Hosmer High School.
Blair is a heavy equipment operator in Denver, Colorado.

Kurt Eric was born December 21, 1964.  He graduated from Hosmer High School and

technician. He married Donna Reed on March 24, 1984.  Donna graduated from
Northwestern High School and LATVI.  She is employed as a dental assistant in
Aberdeen.  They have one son.

DENNIS and MARGO GAB

Margo Mohr and Dennis Gab were married on August 10, 1985, at St. John's
Lutheran Church in Hosmer.

Dennis was born in Eureka, South Dakota on October 13, 1951, to Aaron and
Margaret Gab.  He grew up on a farm 11 miles west of Hosmer graduating from
Eureka High School in 1969.  Following graduation he attended ND State School
of Science, SD State University, and Northern State College.  He is presently

THE CONRAD GIENGER FAMILY

Conrad Gienger was born six miles east of Long Lake, on December 24, 1885.  He
married Katherina Stoecker of Hosmer on November 24, 1908.  Together they
homesteaded five miles east of Long Lake until 1926 when they moved to a farm
12 miles northeast of Hosmer.

Six children were born to this family: Hilda, Emma, Reinhold, Alvina, Herbert,
and Eugene.

On January 17, 1937, Conrad passed away and Katherina moved to Hosmer where she
lived the rest of her life.  The last six years she was a resident of the
Hosmer Senior Citizens Home.  On April 27, 1972 she passed away.

Hilda married John Keller of Harvey, North Dakota. They farmed in the Harvey
area until 1982, when they retired. They have five children: Jerry, Helen,
James, Russel, and Jane.

Emma married Edwin Forkel of Roscoe, South Dakota. They farmed until 1970, when
they retired and moved to Hosmer.  They have six children: Lorina, Verna
Ardella, Esther, Wilbert, Herbert, and Margie.  Emma passed away October 31,
1984.  Edwin is living in Hosmer.

Reinhold married Martha Bieber of Hosmer.  They farmed until 1978, when they
moved to Hosmer.  They have five children: Delbert, Leila, Patty, Wesley, and
Donna.

Alvina married Theodore G. Bauer of Zeeland, North Dakota.  They live in Eureka
where Alvina worked as a nurse in the Eureka Hospital until 1977.  They had two
children: Jerry and Ruth, both passed away in infancy.

Herbert married Esther Adam of Leola.  They live in Aberdeen where Herbert
works as a machinist.  They have three children: Kenneth, Robert, and Dixie.
Robert died in an accident in Fargo, North Dakota, on October 23, 1971.
Kenneth was married to Mavis Kirshenman.  He died in a car accident near
Redfield, South Dakota, on November 27, 1972.

Dixie is married and lives in Aberdeen.

Eugene married Marianne Fischer of Witzenhausen, Germany.  Eugene is a retired
U.S. serviceman.  They live in Albuquerque, New Mexico.  They have three
children: Michael, Barbara, and Kathy.  One son died in infancy.

DELBERT and DARLENE GIENGER

Delbert Gienger was born June 2, 1939, on a farm near Hosmer, to Reinhold and
Martha (Bieber) Gienger.  He attended country school and Hosmer High School,
graduating in 1957.  He served two years in the army after which he did
construction work for several years.

On November 24, 1963, he married Darlene Junkert of Eureka, South Dakota.  She
graduated from Eureka High School in 1962.  They now live on a farm ten miles
northeast of Hosmer.

They have two children.  Karen Kay was born October 14, 1965.  She graduated
from Hosmer High School in 1984 and attended Stewart School of Hairstyling,
Aberdeen, South Dakota, graduating in 1985.

David Delbert was born September 26, 1968.  He will graduate from Hosmer High
School in 1987.

Delbert has served as secretary-treasurer for the Hosmer Rural Fire Protection
and has served in different positions on St. John's Lutheran Church Council.
Delbert and Darlene are serving on the entertainment committee for Hosmer's 1987
Centennial.

REINHOLD GIENGER FAMILY

Reinhold Gienger was born six miles east of Long Lake in Wacker Township on
September 18, 1914, to Conrad and Katherine (Stoecker) Gienger.  In 1926, he
moved with his parents to a farm 10 miles northeast of Hosmer where he worked
with his parents and also hired out as a farmhand.

On October 24, 1937, he married Martha Bieber, daughter of Phillip and
Elizabeth Bieber.  They farmed for five years with his brother, Herbert.  In
1942, they bought the old Adam's place 16 miles northeast of Hosmer where they
farmed until 1978 when they sold the farm to a son-in-law. They moved into
Hosmer where they built a new home.  Reinhold still does some farming and helps
as a hired hand when needed.

Five children were born to them.

Delbert was born June 2, 1939, and married Darlene Junkert November 24, 1963.
They live on a farm northeast of Hosmer.  They have two children: Karen and
David.

Leila was born March 17, 1941, and married Charles Maule August 23, 1964.  They
farm in the Long Lake area. They have two children: Mark and Barbara.

Patty was born January 22, 1949, and married Darvin Blumhardt May 30, 1970.
They live on the home place.  They have two children: Stacey and Darek.

Wesley was born December 28, 1949, and married Sherry Plepp on July 24, 1976.
They live in Aberdeen where Wesley works for Brown County Implement.  They have
one child: Matthew.

Donna was born January 2, 1952 and married Dr. Joel Huber on June 25, 1983.
They live in Redfield, South Dakota.

ANDREW and CAROLINA GISI

Andrew Gisi is the son of Joseph and Katherine (Brown) Gisi.  He was born at
Leola, South Dakota on June 23, 1899. On November, 24, 1920, he was united in
marriage to Barbara Imbery, daughter of Sigfried and Barbara (Streifel) Imbery
at Holy Trinity Catholic Church in Hosmer, South Dakota.  Barbara died in
childbirth on February 20, 1922. They had one son, Ludwig.

On June 2, 1923, in the Holy Trinity Catholic Church in Hosmer, Andrew married
Carolina Schoch, the daughter of Joseph and Katherine (Benfit) Schoch.  She was
born on her parents' farm west of Hosmer on July 28, 1903.

After their marriage, they moved to Andrew's father's farm, which was five
miles west of Hosmer.  They lived on this farm for six years.  They bought a
pool hall in Hosmer (currently known as Good Friend's Tavern).  They moved into
the apartment above the business on October 28, 1928, and lived there for nine
years, until they lost the business.  They then moved to Carolina's brother,
John Schoch's home.  Andrew Gisi died on October 30, 1943, leaving Carolina a
widow with 14 young children.

Carolina inherited 80 acres of land from her parents. Times were bad and she
was losing the land to taxes, so she went to John Heckenlaible to ask for help.
He helped her out by mortgaging a crop until the sum was paid.  Carolina traded
this land to her brother, John Schoch, for the house in Hosmer.  She lived
there for 25 years.  In 1962, when all of her children were out of school, she
sold her home to her son, Frank, and moved to Aberdeen where she bought a house.
She was employed as a dishwasher at the Flame cafe for two and one-half years.
The Flame burned, so Carolina worked at Mae Brownstine's cafe for two and
one-half years.  When she turned 65, she sold her house and moved back to
Hosmer, where she still resides.

Carolina and Andrew had 15 children.  Ludwig was born on September 21, 1921.
He married Marcella Wolf on September 3, 1945.  They have five children and
reside in Pitcarin, Pennsylvania.

Albert was born on April 15, 1924.  He married Delores Scott.  They have three
children and reside in Midwest City, Oklahoma.

Otillia was born on May 5, 1925.  She married Ed Divine on August 15, 1956.
She now resides in Aberdeen.

Joseph was born on October 10, 1926.  He died on February 10, 1927, and is
buried in the Holy Trinity Cemetery.

was born on January 1, 1928.  She married William Karn on June 16, 1951.  They
have two children.  She died on her father's birthday, June 23, 1985, in
Jackson, Mississippi.

Leona was born on February 1, 1929.  She married Alvin Angerhofer on June 2,
1949.  They have two children and reside in Manson, Washington.

Florence was born on May 27, 1930.  She married Albert Lindemann on June 2,
1947.  They have two children and reside in Aberdeen, South Dakota.

Catherine was born on January 11, 1932.  She married Art Burrer on August 26,
1952.  They have three children and reside in Hosmer.

Agnes was born on January 21, 1933.  She married Donald Roberts on April 16,
1956.  They have three children and reside in South Milwaukee, Wisconsin.

Paul was born on October 24, 1935.  He married Margaret Oe on May 24, 1956.
They have eight children and reside in Aberdeen, South Dakota.

Frances was born on November 25, 1936.  She married Norbert Grabowska on
November 6, 1954.  They have five children and reside in Zimmerman, Minnesota.

Frank was born on January 21, 1938.  He married Marlerie Hinds on August 6,
1962.  They have four children and reside in Hosmer.

Carolyn was born on October 13, 1939.  She married Gary Rath on January 4,
1958.  They have five children and reside in Lead, South Dakota.

Andrew was born on October 16, 1941.  He married Lois Spering on June 19, 1965.
They have four children and reside in Aberdeen, South Dakota.

Rosemary was born on August 31, 1943.  She married Russell Hanaburgh on July 6,
1968.  They have three children and reside in Patterson, New York.

At this printing Carolina has 49 grandchildren and 29 great-grandchildren.

ANTON and FLORENCE GISI

Anton (Tony) Gisi was born March 16, 1917, to Baldaser and Theresa (Heier)
Gisi, Sr.  Tony served in the Army 1943-1946.  Upon returning home he was a
truck driver.  In 1951 he married Florence Schmidt at the Holy Catholic Church,
Hosmer.  They established a home on a farm moving into Hosmer in 1964.  Since
that time Tony has worked at the John Gutjahr Meat Market, Cenex, for the city
of Hosmer, and presently for Floyd Ulmer.

Florence was born January 28, 1931, to Ludwig and Clara Schmidt.  She has
worked at the Senior Citizen's Home since 1970 as head housekeeper and then
transferred to the nursing department as a nurse's aide.

Tony and Florence are the parents of five children.

Leo was born March 6, 1952.  He graduated from Hosmer High School after which
he entered the army serving in Vietnam.  Leo married Connie Heinz in 1979 and
they now live in Pomona, California, where he is employed as an iron worker.
They have two children: Jonathan and Jenny.

Robert was born December 4, 1953.  He graduated from Hosmer High School and
served in the army two years.  He presently lives in Newburg, Oregon, working
for the Computer Data Co. He has three children: Tamie, Heather, and Tony.

Joanne was born May 26, 1955.  She served in the U.S. Air Force 1975-1976.
Joanne lives in Austin, Texas, and works as a bookkeeper-secretary.  She has
one child, Michael, who lives in Hosmer.

Patrick was born September 14, 1958, and graduated from Hosmer High School in
1977.  He is employed part time by Lustermar Cultured Marble Mfg. in Hosmer,
and is self-employed as a painter.  Pat is a member of the Hosmer Volunteer
Fire Department.

Shirley was born September 21, 1962.  She graduated from Hosmer High School in
1981.  Following graduation Shirley went to Eureka, California, where she was
employed by Ramada Inn.  She returned to Hosmer in 1982, and since then has
been employed at the Senior Citizen's Home as an aide.

Cindy was born August 27, 1967.  She graduated from Hosmer High School in 1985.
She resides in Pomona, California, attending college, majoring in computers.

BALDASER B. JR. and CLARA GISI

Baldaser B. Gisi, Jr. was born November 1, 1913, at Hosmer, South Dakota, to
Baldaser and Theresa (Heier) Gisi, Sr. He married Clara Weron, daughter of the
Emanuel Werons of Bowdle, South Dakota.

Baldaser was janitor at the Hosmer School for 13 years. He drove school bus for
three years.  He was also janitor at Northern State College and at the Hosmer
Senior Citizen's Home for II years until his retirement.  Clara was a cook's
helper for many years at the Hosmer School Lunch Program. If you needed someone
to do painting or wallpapering you just called Clara for help.  Clara passed
away on March 22, 1982.

Baldaser and Clara had five children: Berneice Welch, living in Tucson,
Arizona.  She has two boys, Gary, born April 4, 1957, and Steven, born May 14,
1962, her husband, Duane, passed away on September 18, 1975; Gladys, Mrs. Leven

married to Rose Marie Heilman from Bowdle, South Dakota.  They have two
children: Timothy Lee, born May 29, 1972, and Ann Marie, born June 18, 1975.

FRANK and MARLENE GISI

Frank Gisi was born on January 21, 1938, to Andrew and Carolina (Schoch) Gisi,
both pioneer residents of the Hosmer area.  Frank graduated from Hosmer High
School in 1956.  He spent two years in the U.S. Army, including 16 months in
Korea, and worked at various jobs before becoming employed with Valley
Telephone Cooperative as the local area manager in February, 1961.

He married Marlene Hinds, daughter of George and Margaret (Griffith) Hinds, on
August 6, 1962 at the Holy Trinity Church in Hosmer.  Marlene was born in the
Powell area south of Ipswich, South Dakota on October 15, 1940 and graduated
from Lead High School in 1958.  She then spent two years at NSTC and came to
Hosmer in the fall of 1960 as the sixth grade teacher, a position she held for
five years. After that she did occasional substitute teaching and presently is
employed as a teacher's aide in the Hosmer School.

They have four children, Janet, born October 16, 1965, graduated from Hosmer
High School in 1983, and is currently attending NSC in Aberdeen; Amy, born
October 23, 1968; Mark, born March 5, 1971; and Brian, born July 6, 1973. They
are all students in the Hosmer school system.

Frank and Marlene still live in the same house, although remodeled and added
to, in which Frank was born in 1938.

FRANK GLATT

Frank Glatt was born in 1912 to Peter and Elizabeth (Geier) Glatt at Zeeland,
North Dakota.  When he was nine years old he moved with his parents to the
Hosmer-Bowdle area.  In the fall of 1923, the family moved to the Trail City
area and lived there until 1937, when they moved to Bowdle.

During 1933, Frank spent time working in the Civilian Conservation Corps.

Frank was inducted into the army in 1941 and served his country until the war
ended.

In 1945, he attended barber school in Minneapolis, Minnesota. In 1947, Frank
opened his barber shop in Hosmer which he operated until failing health forced
him to retire in 1979.

In December, 1983, Frank entered the Hosmer Nursing Home and in February, 1984,
passed away.

PETER GLATT FAMILY

Peter Glatt was born in 1889, in Zeeland, North Dakota. He farmed in the
Zeeland, Hosmer, Bowdle, and Trail City areas.  Peter worked with oxen and when
money was available he purchased a team of horses.

In 1910, he married Elizabeth Geier.  Elizabeth was born in Russia and at the
age of 19 years came to America with her parents.  The Glatts had 11 children.

In 1947, they moved to Hosmer to live with their son, Frank, who had purchased
the house now occupied by the Christ Heilman family.

Mr. Glatt passed away in 1963 and Mrs. Glatt in 1967.

THE EDWARD GOEHRING FAMILY

Edward Goehring was born December 30, 1897, in McPherson County, to Simon and
Christina (Schumacher) Goehring.  Simon and Christina came to America from South
Russia in the late 1800's.  Simon died in 1932 and Christina died in 1951.

Edward lived with his parents on a farm northwest of Hosmer until his marriage
to Katherine Nies on March 15, 1921.  Katherine was born June 16, 1901, to John
and Fredericka (Kurle) Nies.  Edward and Katherine farmed their homestead about
a mile north of his father's farm until they moved to a farm directly east of
Hosmer in 1945.  They lived there until Edward's death in 1967, after which
Katherine purchased a home in Eureka, where she has lived since 1968.

Edward and Katherine were blessed with four children: Mervin, Verna, Bernice
and Ardella.

Mervin was born May 27, 1926, attended elementary school in a rural setting in
McPherson County, helped his parents with farming and was inducted into the
service in 1950.  After completing his service duty in 1952, he returned home
to take over part of the farming.  He now resides at the Goehring farm east of
Hosmer and has a diversified farming operation.

Verna was born August 24, 1928.  She attended a rural elementary school in
McPherson County, graduated from Hosmer High School in 1946, and attended a
six-week session at NSC, Aberdeen, South Dakota.  For one year Verna taught in
a rural school one mile east of Hosmer.  She then enrolled in the Presentation
School of Nursing in Aberdeen, South Dakota, and graduated in 1950.  Her
employment took her to the Lutheran Hospital at Hot Springs, South Dakota.  In
1952, she transferred to the Veterans Hospital at Hot Springs, South Dakota,
where she is presently supervisor of the Surgical Operating Room.  She married
Robert Schur at Hot Springs, August 26, 1951.  To this union were born three
children: Randy, Barbara Ann, and Mark.

Bernice was born March 9, 1931.  She attended a rural elementary school and
Hosmer High School, graduating in 1949.  She attended NSC and taught rural
schools for several years.  In 1958 through 1962, she taught third grade in the
Eureka school system.  From 1962 to 1964, she taught at Ridgefield, Washington.
At present she is employed at the Douglas school system, Ellsworth AFB, Rapid
City, South Dakota.  Bernice has traveled extensively throughout Europe,
Australia, New Zealand, United States, Canada, and some South Pacific Islands.

Ardella (Sis) was born December 13, 1939.  She attended the Hosmer schools,
graduating from high school in 1957. Ardella attended a business college and
Northern State College. She was employed by the Bureau of Indian Affairs at
Aberdeen, South Dakota.  She died on April 9, 1961, after a lengthy illness.

JACOB and PAULINE GOEHRING

Jacob Goehring was born November 28, 1900, northwest of Hosmer to Simon and
Christina Goehring.  In December, 1925, he was married to Pauline Job, daughter
of John and Carolina Job northeast of Hosmer.  The Goehrings farmed northwest
of Hosmer for 18 years.  In 1943, they moved to Aberdeen where Mr. Goehring was
a mail carrier for the three depots.  In the early 1940s, he bought the
Kranzler Elevator which he operated until 1959, when he retired.  He sold the
elevator to Lyle Clopine.

Mr. Goehring passed away October 28, 1970.  Mrs. Goehring lives in Hosmer.  She
is a member of the Congregational Church and the Ladies Fellowship of the
church.

The Goehrings have three children: Leo in Sioux Falls, South Dakota; Waldo,
employed by Cenex in Hosmer; and Ray in Redfield; one grandson and two
great-grandchildren.

THE JOHN GOEHRING FAMILY

John Goehring was born on December 10, 1894, on a farm northwest of Hosmer to
Simon and Christina (Schumacher) Goehring.  Simon was born in South Russia in
1863 and died in 1932.  Christina was born in South Russia in 1870 and died in
1951.

John lived with his parents on a farm northwest of Hosmer until his marriage to
Martha Maisch.  Martha was born in the Hillsview area on April 20, 1899, to
Ludwig and Katherine (Hoffman) Maisch.  Ludwig was born in South Russia in 1876
and died in 1969.  Katherine was born in South Russia in 1879 and died in 1971.
John and Martha farmed near Roscoe for a few years, then moved on a farm in the
Hosmer area.  They resided there until John's death on October 3, 1953.  Martha
moved to Aberdeen and lived there until her marriage to Jake Ladner in 1961,
when she moved to Mobridge.  After Jake's death Martha moved back to Aberdeen.

John and Martha were blessed with 11 children.  Howard was born July 11, 1919.
He worked for the Homestake Gold Mine in Lead, South Dakota until his
retirement.  He married Ann Voegele.  They have five children.

Milbert was born February 17, 1921.  He is employed as a night watchman in Los
Angeles, California.

Junis was born June 1, 1922.  He manages his own insurance agency in Aberdeen,
South Dakota.  He married Adeline Hoff and they have five children.

Heron was born in 1925 and died in 1926.

Chyclinda was born June 26, 1927.  She graduated from Hosmer High School and
married Reinhold Schutz.  They lived on a farm near Eureka until Reinhold's
death in 1975. Chyclinda moved to Eureka where she is employed as a waitress.
They have two children.

Winnie was born October 9, 1929.  She graduated from Hosmer High School,

at the Hosmer Public School.  They have six children.

Wilma was born June 17, 1932 and died in July 1968.  She married Erwin Voegele.

EUGENE and METHA GRAFF

Eugene, son of Jacob and Katherina (Jenner) Graff, was born September 24, 1924,
on a farm northeast of Hosmer, South Dakota.  In 1948, he married Metha
Rohrbach, daughter of George and Christina (Wolff) Rohrbach.

They purchased the August Gross farm and lived there from 1948 to 1972 at which
time they bought the Breeze Inn Motel in Aberdeen, South Dakota.  In 1974 they

Eugene has been associated with Mehlhoff Realty Inc. as a farm and land sales
representative.

HERBERT and SHEILA GRAFF

MR. and MRS.  REINIE GRAFF

Reinie Graff was born near Ashley, North Dakota.  As a young man he came to
Hosmer with his parents, Mr. and Mrs. John Graff.

Darlene (Rohrbach) Graff was born near Long Lake, South Dakota.  Her parents
were Mr. and Mrs. George Rohrbach, of Hosmer.  As a young girl she came to
Hosmer with her parents.  She was confirmed at St. John's Lutheran Church and
went to the rural school near Hosmer.

On May 7, 1950 Reinie Graff married Darlene Rohrbach at Salem's Lutheran Church
near Leola, South Dakota.

Reinie and Darlene had seven children: Roy, Rapid City, South Dakota; Roger,

Reinie and Darlene farmed northeast of Hosmer.  In 1953 they moved into Hosmer
