In 1957 the Graffs moved to Aberdeen, South Dakota. Reinie went to work for
Aberdeen Glass Company.

Reinie and Darlene Graff are now residing in Rapid City, South Dakota.  They
are managing the Greenway Apartments.

ADAM and PAULINE GRAMM

Adam Gramm was born in 1892 to Fred and Christina Gramm who lived in Russia.
At the age of six months he came to America with his parents settling on a farm
in the Hillsview area.  Adam married Pauline Lehr in 1917, who was the daughter
of Andrew and Frederricka Lehr, and was born in 1892.  After their marriage
they farmed four miles north of Hosmer.  This union was blessed with four
children: Emma (Martel), Hosmer; Anna (Haselhorst), died in 1965; Ervin,
Bowdle; and Walter, who lives on the family farm.

In 1949 they retired and moved into Hosmer.  In 1980 they became residents of
the Senior Citizens Home in Hosmer.  Pauline passed away in January 1985, while
Adam is still a resident of the home.

BEN and ELLA GRAMM

Ben Gramm was born July 27, 1908, near Hosmer in McPherson County, South
Dakota, to Frederrich and Christina Warmer Gramm.  Frederrich Gramm was born in
South Russia in 1865 and immigrated to the United States in 1893.

Ben lived on a farm with his parents until they retired and moved to the city
of Hosmer.  He attended rural school. Ben married Ella Neifer on May 23, 1936,
at Ashley, North Dakota.  Ella was born April 24, 1918, to John and Rosina Hieb
Neifer in McPherson County near Hosmer.  She also attended rural school.  After
her marriage to Ben, they made their first home on a farm three miles northwest
of Hosmer. They moved to Hosmer in 1940.  Several years later, Ella became
employed at the Boldt Store in Hosmer.  She worked there a number of years and
then Ben and Ella purchased the store in February of 1970.  They operated this
store under the name of Gramm's Store.  Ben also did carpenter work and was
employed for a number of years with Holzworth Construction of Mobridge, South
Dakota.  Ben and Ella were custodians at St. John's Lutheran Church in Hosmer
for several years.

Ella died June 12, 1970 of an apparent heart attack at her home.  Ben was a
resident of Hosmer's Senior Citizen's Home until the time of his death on
December 18, 1977.

Ben and Ella were blessed with three sons.  Calvin was born November 25, 1936.
He attended grade school and graduated from Hosmer High School and Northern
State College in Aberdeen, South Dakota with a degree in accounting. He married
Dorothy Steffan of West Point, Nebraska. They have five children: Steven,
Carol, Ellen, Sharon, and Jeffrey.  They are presently residing in Sioux Falls,
South Dakota where Calvin works with St. Paul Fire and Marine Insurance Co.

James was born August 6, 1939 and also graduated from Hosmer High School.  He
received a Bachelor's Degree from the University of South Dakota at Vermillion.
James taught school for a number of years and, for several years, has been
involved in computer business.  James married Betty Wolenitz and they have two
daughters: Susan and Cheryl.  Jim and Betty are residing in Dallas, Texas.

Gary was born March 23, 1944.  He graduated from Hosmer High School, received a
Bachelor's Degree in secondary education and a Master's Degree from Northern
State College in Aberdeen, South Dakota.  He taught school for a number of
years and is presently employed with Horace Mann Insurance Co. He married Marie
Scherf of Roscoe, South Dakota and they have two children: Mark and Susan. Gary
and Marie are currently residing in Brookings, South Dakota.

ERWIN and FLORENCE GRAMM

Erwin Gramm was born on May 7, 1923, at Hosmer, South Dakota to Adam Gramm and
Pauline Lehr Gramm.

He married Florence Schuh Bollinger on October 21, 1978, of Bowdle, South
Dakota, and became the stepfather of five children.  Florence was born August
15, 1925.

Erwin attended rural school north of Hosmer for eight years.  He has been
engaged in farming, three miles north of Hosmer all his life.  He sold all of
his cows in February of 1981 and since then is doing only grain farming.

In 1980 Erwin and Florence built a new home in Bowdle, and in February of 1981
moved to their new home, commuting to the farm almost daily.

Erwin and Florence are members of St. John's Lutheran Church at Hosmer, South
Dakota.

ROGER and KATHY GRAMM

Roger, son of Walter and Gladys (Klein) Gramm, was born December 28, 1949.  He
attended Hosmer Public School and graduated from Hosmer High School in 1968.
He attended North Dakota State School of Science at Wahpeton for two years and
served in the U.S. Army from 1971-1973.  He then was employed at Kaul's Repair
until February 1975 when he became an employee of A. Herrboldt and Company.  He
married Kathy Malsam September 28, 1974, who is the daughter of Frank and
Adeline (Imberi) Malsam.  She was born December 5, 1952 and attended Hosmer
Public School. She graduated from Hosmer High School in 1971 and was employed
at Control Data in Aberdeen until her marriage.

They have two children: Rebecca, born August 2, 1976; and Brian, born December
24, 1980.

WALTER and GLADYS GRAMM

Walter Gramm was born June 14, 1925 in Cleveland Township in McPherson County
on the homestead where he grew up to manhood.  He farmed with his parents Adam
F. and Pauline (Lehr) Gramm.  In April of 1945 he was called to service
returning in November of 1946.

In 1949 he married Gladys Klein after which his parents moved into Hosmer.
Gladys was born on November 21, 1928 to Emil and Lydia Klein on a farm 13 miles
northeast of Hosmer.  Emil Klein passed away in January of 1977.  Lydia Klein
now resides in Eureka.

Walter and Gladys continued farming and were blessed with two children: Roger,
born December 28, 1949 who lives in Hosmer with his wife and family; and
Miranda (Scherr) who was born on December 19, 1951 and is living in Fargo,
North Dakota with her husband and family.  A soia, Scott, died in infancy on
May 11, 1959.  They now have four grandchildren and are still farming on the
home farm.

JOHN and KATHERINE GRISCHKOWSKY

John Grischkowsky was born in 1880 in Crimea, Russia. In 1909 he and his wife
Katherine (nee Schuler) along with three children, Alita, George and Gunda,
came to the U.S. settling in Roscoe, South Dakota.  From 1911 to 1912 they both
worked on a farm near Loyalton.  Returning to Roscoe John worked on the
railroad until 1914 at which time they moved to Hosmer.  He continued working
for the railroad being section foreman until 1921.  In 1926 they sold their
house and bought a farm 10 miles south of Hosmer.  Due to crop failures and
then hail destroying the crops they lost the farm. In 1941 they rented a farm
south of Leola where they lived for only one year.  Returning to Hosmer they
again bought a home and made their livelihood here.  John owned and operated a
small gas station (located to the east of A. Herrboldt & Co.) for several years.

To this marriage 13 children were born: Alita (Heyne), Hosmer; George, St.
Louis, Missouri; Gunda (Kruse), North Bend, Oregon; Rosalie (died in infancy);
Henry, Stewart, Minnesota; Martha (Jung), Leola, South Dakota; Edna (Duvall),
Gold Beach, Oregon; Frieda (Philippi), Coos Bay, Oregon; Oscar, Carvallis,
Oregon; Dorothy (Gabriel), Hanover, Illinois; Laura (Franson), North Bend,
Oregon; Harold, Coos Bay, Oregon; and John, Aurora, Colorado.

Katherine passed away in 1943 and John in 1965.

BERNARD and JUDY GRISMER

Bernard was born April 4, 1945, to Baldaser and Grace Grismer.  He graduated
from Hosmer High School in 1962 and from the Aberdeen School of Commerce in
1964.  He worked for the Internal Revenue Service in Aberdeen until entering
the service in 1965.  Upon returning home from the service in 1967, he was
appointed acting postmaster and later postmaster of the Hosmer post office.

Bernard married Judy Roth of Roscoe, South Dakota, on September 11, 1968.  Judy
was a 1965 graduate of Roscoe High School and a 1967 graduate of the Aberdeen
School of Commerce.  Judy has been housekeeping supervisor at the Senior
Citizen's Home in Hosmer since August 1982.

Bernard and Judy have three children: Richard James was born January 12, 1970;
David Michael, May 18, 1972; and Charles Bernard, June 10, 1977.

FRANK and MAGDALENA (FIX) GRISMER

Frank B. Grismer was born July 28, 1906 in Hosmer.  He was the son of Benjamin
and Barbara (Reinhold) Grismer. Benjamin was born in Russia on February 9, 1881
coming to South Dakota in 1890 when he was nine years old.  He died in Aberdeen
in April of 1959 at the age of 78.  Barbara Reinbold Grismer was born on
February 2, 1886 in rural Hosmer and she died November 1, 1967 at the age of 81
in Aberdeen.

Frank B. Grismer was married to Magdalena Fix at the St. John the Baptist
Catholic Church in Onaka on November 22, 1927 by the Rev.  Father Celestin
Bache.  She was born on June 11, 1906, her parents are Andrew and Magdalena Fix,
Andrew Fix was born June 4, 1882 at Odessa, Russia and he died October, 1942
and he came to the United States at the age of 16 years and Magdalena
(Hopfinger) Fix was born in 1884 on May 22 at Odessa, Russia and she died March
19, 1957.  She came to the United States at the age of four years.

Frank and Magdalena, after their marriage, lived at Hillsview, they then moved
to Hosmer and later moved on their farm nine miles southwest of Hosmer where
they lived until November of 1966 when they retired and moved to Bowdle. He was
a past school board member for the Hosmer School System.  Frank died July 11,
1975.  Magdalena died May 17, 1979.

Frank and Magdalena were blessed with ten children.

Leo was born on August 2, 1928 and he lives in Bowdle, he never married.

Melvina was born on May 27, 1930, she lives in Tucson, Arizona and has three
children: Mike, Andy and Madonna.

Roger Dale, all of Tucson, Arizona.

they live at Tolstoy.

Francis (Frankie) was born on March 21, 1936.  He married Marlyn Schmidt of
Tolstoy on June 26, 1956.  They have five children: Mary Ann, Carol Ann, Allen,
Amy and Gary and they live in Bowdle.

Joe was born October 28, 1937.  He married Bertha Cook of Big Sandy, Montana on

Adam was born April 13, 1944.  He married Arlys Graff of Hosmer on February 6,

JACOB and EVA GRISMER

Jacob and Eva (Heilman) Grismer were married in 1935 during the dirty thirties,
when the turkeys lived on grasshoppers and they lived on turkey.

Jacob was born on November 29, 1912, on the family farm seven miles southwest
of Hosmer.  He moved to Hosmer with his parents when he was six years old due
to his father's poor health and attended school in Hosmer.  His father bought
the present Mervin Goehring farm and built a new house and farm buildings and
lived there for sometime before Jacob married Eva who was born and raised in
the Bowdle area.

In 1939, they bought the family farm which is located six miles west of Hosmer.
They were active members of the Holy Trinity Catholic Church and Jacob was on
the Modina Township School Board.  In 1966, they moved off the farm and lived
in Mobridge before making their present home in Aberdeen.

Jacob and Eva have five children: Tony, Hosmer; LaVern (Mrs.  Math Fuhrmann),
Roscoe; Jeannette (Mrs.  Arnold Lunders), Hosmer; Arnie, Stillwater, Minnesota;
and Tom, San Diego, California.

TONY and MADONNA GRISMER

Tony Grismer was born August 23, 1935 to Jacob and Eva Heilman Grismer who were
both born in the Hosmer-Bowdle area.  He graduated from eighth grade at a
country school.

Madonna Fuhrmann was born January 6, 1937 to Pius and Caroline Leboldus
Fuhrmann.  Tony and Madonna were married May 21, 1956 in the Holy Trinity
Church in Hosmer. They lived on a farm five miles west of Hosmer until February
1957 then moved to Lead, South Dakota where Tony worked for the Homestake Gold
Mine until June 1957. They then moved back on the farm five miles west of
Hosmer.  In 1958 they bought the Bill Hieb farm twelve miles east and three
miles north of Hosmer.  In July 1959, a tornado and hailstorm struck the farm
destroying the garage, granary, pole barn and breaking windows in the house.
They never started up the combine that year.  In 1963 another terrible
windstorm hit and destroyed the new garage, pole barn and chicken barn.  In
1965 they sold that farm and bought the Jacob Grismer farm six miles west of
Hosmer, where they still live.  Madonna has been assistant cook at Hosmer
school for the past five years.

They were blessed with seven children.  Robin Ann was born April 29, 1957,
graduated from Hosmer High School and Dickinson College.  She works with
mentally retarded adults.  She is married to Allen Ollila.

Ramona was born May 26, 1958, graduated from Hosmer High School and Watertown
Vo-Tech as a dental assistant.  She married Harlan Malsom and has four children.
They live in Pella, Iowa.

Rhonda was born May 12, 1959, graduated from Hosmer High School and Stewart
School of Hairstyling in Aberdeen.  She married Mark Karels.  They have two
children and live on a farm near Watertown.

Roy was born December 9, 1961, graduated from Hosmer High School, attended two
years of college at Morris, Minnesota and 2 1/2 years at St. Paul, Minnesota.
He is farming with his father.

Russel was born November 6, 1964, graduated from Hosmer High School and is
attending School of Mines in Rapid City, South Dakota.

Rodney was born May 26, 1966, graduated from Hosmer High School and is
attending NSC in Aberdeen.

Roger was born March 10, 1969 and died September 2, 1973 from an accidental
gunshot.

HENRY GROSS FAMILY

Henry Gross (1896-1959) was born to Jacob and Elizabeth (Wruck) Gross on a farm
in Blaine Township in McPherson County northeast of Hosmer.  He grew to manhood
working on the farm with his parents and brothers and sisters.  He married
Hulda Pressler (1907-1979), the daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Adam Pressler Sr.
They made their home on the family homestead with his brother Reinhold and his
mother.  Five children were born to this union.

Marvin received an elementary education at the Blaine Township School and
graduated from Hosmer High School. He continued his education at Northern State
College in Aberdeen and also at SDSU in Brookings.  He married Verdella
Schumacher and they are living in Volga, South Dakota where they own and
operate the Gross Construction Co. They have four sons and one daughter.

Lloyd attended the Blaine Township School and graduated from Hosmer High
School.  He continued his education in the ecumenical field and is currently
serving a Reformed Parish.  They live in Sutten, Nebraska.  His family includes
four children.

Elaine attended the Blaine Township School and graduated from Hosmer High
School.  She is married to Marvin Monsma and they are living in Grand Rapids,
Michigan where she is employed as a librarian at Calvin College.  Their family
consists of three children.

Betty also attended the Blaine Township School and graduated from Hosmer High
School.  She is married to Vern Paulman, pastor of a Reformed Church.  They
live in Lincoln, Nebraska.  Their family consists of four children.

Daughter Hulda died as an infant.

Henry died suddenly on September 24, 1959.  Following her husband's death,
Hulda sold the personal property at public auction and moved to Sheboygan,
Wisconsin to help her sister Lea in caring for her mother.  After her mother's
death she made her home with her children until the time of her death.

JACOB GROSS SR.  FAMILY

Jacob Gross was born in Klein Neudorph, South Russia, on January 4, 1860.  At
the age of 14 he immigrated with his widowed mother to America.  They settled
in Tyndall, South Dakota where he worked at various jobs to support himself and
his mother.

In 1884, he married Elizabeth Wruck, who was born in Worms, Russia and came to
America at the age of six.  They resided in the Tyndall area for a short time,
but the opportunity to acquire free land brought them north to homestead in
Blaine Township, McPherson County.  Here, after weathering the unending toil
and hardships of the pioneers, they acquired a large farm, which was the family
home for the rest of their lives.  However, for a number of years while Father
Gross served as auditor and register of deeds, the parents with the younger
children established a home near Leola, while the older children operated the
large farm.  Before his years in the courthouse, he served as county
commissioner for 20 years.

Besides a large farm, a large family was acquired.  Sixteen children were born,
three dying in infancy.  Seven sons and six daughters reached adulthood.  At
one time all the sons lived on farms within a six mile radius of the parental
home.

children were: Emma, born 1886, married Jolin Hieb, had four sons, was widowed,
married William Meier, Emma died in 1953.

Margret, born in 1888, married Jake Wruck, had three children, died in 1918.

Christine, born in 1889, married John Meier, had two daughters, died in 1972.

Christ, born in 1892, married Katie Hieb, had five children, was widowed,
married Maggie Reiner, Christ died in 1959, Mrs. Gross lives in the Bowdle
Nursing Home.

Katie, born in 1894, married William Hieb, had two children, was widowed, died
in 1985.

Henry, born in 1896, married Hulda Preszler, had four children, Henry died in
1959.

Albert, born in 1898, married Rose Janisch, had four children, died in 1978.

Jacob (twin), born in 1899, married Minnie Lee, had two children, died in 1976.

August (twin), born in 1899, married Emma Dockter, had ten children, died in
1977.

Anna, born in 1900, married Otto Aman, was widowed and is now living in Hosmer.

Herman, born in 1902, married Emelia Satler, had two children, was divorced,
married again and had four children, Herman died in 1967.

Martha, born in 1904, married Arthur Quast, had one son, was widowed, married
John Maier, Martha died in 1981.

Reinhold, born in 1906, presently living in the Hosmer Senior Citizens Home.

Father Gross died in 1929 at the age of 69, and Mother Gross died on her 84th
birthday in 1948.  They both died at their beloved farm home.

ALVIN GUTHMILLER FAMILY

Alvin Guthmiller was born January 27, 1915 to Phillip and Rosina Martel
Guthmiller northeast of Hosmer, South Dakota.  He had two brothers Ernest and
Otto Guthmiller and one sister, Mrs. William (Anna) Mohr.  He attended rural
school near Hosmer and farmed northeast of Hosmer from 1936 until 1966 when he
moved to Roscoe, South Dakota where he bought and operated Al's Place for about
a year and a half.  He then worked for Haar's Custom Combining and then for
Hieb Repair until his retirement in 1976.  After his retirement he continued
working for various farmers in the area until his death in 1984.

On September 27, 1936 he married Maria Magdalena Mohr who was born on a farm
northeast of Hosmer on March 5, 1915.  She was the daughter of Gottfried and
Fredricka Stoecker Mohr.  She had eight brothers: Fred, Christ, Gottleib,
Gottfried, Jacob, William, Theobald, and Julius; and three sisters: Mrs. Adam
(Rosina) Wunner, Mrs. Fred W. (Katherina) Mohr and Mrs. August (Margaretha)
Martel.  Magdalena passed away April 7, 1960 at the farm and is buried at
Bethany Cemetery in Hosmer, South Dakota.

On July 4, 1976 he married Hulda Miller Baer at St. Paul's Lutheran Church at
Roscoe.  She died June 22, 1979 in Roscoe and is buried at the Morningside
Cemetery in Roscoe, South Dakota.

Alvin passed away July 10, 1984 at Roscoe and was buried July 13, 1984 at the
Bethany Cemetery in Hosmer, South Dakota.

Alvin and Magdalena had one son, Roger Guthmiller who was born September 19,
1945 at Aberdeen, South Dakota and presently resides in Roscoe.

AUGUST and MELITHA GUTHMILLER

August Guthmiller, son of Theobald G. Guthmiller and Katherine Mayer was born
in 1906.  He grew up on the farm six miles east of Hosmer, South Dakota and
attended rural school.

He married Melitha Walth, daughter of Mr. and Mrs. Jacob F. Walth, Hosmer,
South Dakota on December 2, 1928 at Jehovah Church, rural Hosmer.

After 32 years of farming, the Guthmillers moved to Aberdeen, South Dakota.
They retired after 22 years, being custodians at the Masonic Temple.

They have two daughters, both of whom are Hosmer High School graduates.

Alice is married to Lenhardt Voegele.  Lenhardt is a Vactor operator for the
city of Aberdeen.  Alice is employed as secretary-bookkeeper with the Retired
Senior Volunteer Program.

Gladys is married to Elmer Voegele.  Elmer is a machinist at K. O. Lee Co.
Gladys is a librarian at Alexander Mitchell Library in Aberdeen.

August Guthmiller passed away June 18, 1985.

FREDERICK and CHRISTINA GUTHMILLER

Frederick Guthmiller immigrated to America in 1882 with his parents, Mr. and
Mrs. Michael Guthmiller.  Frederick was born in South Russia November 27, 1876
and was united in marriage to Christina Hoff in 1898.  Christina was born March
26, 1881.

Frederick and Christina farmed six miles northeast of Hosmer which is at the
present time occupied by son, Herbert and wife, Edna.

To this union 12 children were born.  Emma, who married Gottlieb Schutz and
passed away June 8, 1930.  Margret married George Walth and died December 25,
1954.  Adeline married Henry Jung and died July 16, 1940.  Emil died in
infancy. Martha married Jacob Naasz and lives in Mobridge. Lydia resides in
Eureka.  Ella married Edwin Mohr and lives in Lodi, California.  Magdalena
married Jacob Bieber and lives in Hosmer.  Alma married Alvin Martell and lives
in Hosmer.  Katherine died in infancy.  Althea married David Zimmer and resides
in Bowdle.  Herbert married Edna Schaible and they farm the family homestead.

Frederick passed away June 13, 1939 and Christina died November 30, 1953.

GEORGE GUTHMILLER SR.  FAMILY

George Guthmiller Sr. was born in Neudorf, South Russia on November 11, 1850.
When he had grown to manhood he married Katharina Wagenman who was born on
October 27, 1852.  In 1888 they decided to leave Russia and immigrate to
America to join relatives and friends that had left Russia a few years before.
They came as far as Marion, South Dakota which at that time was sort of a
stopover place.  They spent some time here to get together some supplies along
with a wagon and a team of oxen with which to make the journey northward to
McPherson County.  Spending almost a month enroute they finally arrived at
their destination and filed on a claim on the SW 1/4 of Section 22, in
Cleveland Township.

Eleven children were born to this family of which the oldest daughter died and
is buried in Russia.  Two daughters and five sons were born in Russia and came
to this country with their parents: Mrs. Christ (Katharina) Jung (1875), George
Jr. (1877), Theobald (1879), Mrs. John (Carolina) Lechner (1882), Phillip
(1885), Frederich (1887), Ludwig (1888).  Two more daughters and one son were
born in this country, namely Margaret (1890), Christine (1892), and John
(1894).  Margaret and Christine died during a scarlet fever epidemic in 1898.
Mrs. Guthmiller passed away on June 10, 1910 and Mr. Guthmiller passed away on
October 25, 1928.

        Pictured are: Back Row-Ludwig, Phillip, Frederich and John. Front
        Row-Theobald, Katharina and George.

HERBERT and EDNA GUTHMILLER

Herbert Guthmiller was born April 26, 1925, to Fred and Christina (Hoff)
Guthmiller on a farm five miles northeast of Hosmer where they live and farm
today.

On November 17, 1946, he married Edna Schaible, daughter of Fred and Eva
(Gutjahr) Schaible.  Edna was born December 23, 1923.  Prior to their marriage
Edna taught school in the country for four years.

Herbert has been a life-long member of the Reformed Church of Hosmer and is
serving as elder.  Herbert and Edna are members of the church choir and the
senior citizens singing group.

On September 4, 1947, their first and only son, Gerald, was born.  He served
two years in the United States Army with one year being in Vietnam.  He married
Janice Afrank on September 27, 1969, at Morristown, South Dakota.  She is a
registered nurse at the Jamestown Hospital.  Gerald was an x-ray technician in
the Jamestown Hospital and State Hospital.  Presently he is an insurance
salesman for Aid Association for Lutherans at Jamestown.  He plays and sings
with the Midnight Ramblers Band.

Gerald and Janice are the parents of two children, Bryan and Nicole.

THE JOHN GUTHMILLER FAMILY

John Guthmiller, son of George Guthmiller Sr., and Kathrina Wagenmen, was born
in 1894 on the family farm six miles northeast of Hosmer.  He was the youngest
of 10 children.  He attended McPherson County schools, and eventually took over
operation of the family farm.  John is a World War I veteran.  He married Anna
Dusel in 1927.  They have two children: Roy, born in 1930; and Milna, born in
1934.

Roy and his wife Louise reside in Denver, Colorado.  Roy is employed with a
trucking firm in that city.  They have no children.

Milna married Elmer Fauth in 1952.  In 1954 they purchased the family farm near
Hosmer.  The farm was sold in 1976.  Elmer and Milna presently reside in the
city of Hosmer, where Elmer is employed by the city, and Milna works for the
Department of Social Services.  John lived with them until he passed away on
April 29, 1986.

Milna and Elmer have three children: Roy, born in 1952; Jacalyn, born in 1954;
and Anne, born in 1962.  All three, like their mother, are graduates of Hosmer
High School.  Roy resides in Mobridge with his wife Jacqueline and daughter
Stefanie, where he is employed as an accountant.  Jacalyn and her husband,
James Uhrich, live on a farm near Roscoe. Jacalyn is the business manager for
Hosmer School.  Anne and her husband, Warren Retzlaff, are residents of Webster,
South Dakota.  Anne is a secondary coach and math instructor.

PHILIP and ROSENA GUTHMILLER

Philip Guthmiller, son of George and Kathrina (Wagenmen) Guthmiller was born in
1885 in Russia.  At the age of four he came to America with his parents.
Philip was married to Rosena Martell, a neighbor girl who was also born in
Russia the same year.  Rosena's parents were Mr. and Mrs. George Martell.  They
farmed in McPherson County until 1949 when they moved to Hosmer.

Roger Guthmiller was born September 19, 1945 to Alvin and Magdalena Mohr
Guthmiller at Aberdeen, South Dakota. He lived on a farm twelve miles north and
one mile west of Roscoe with his parents.  He attended rural school and
belonged to St. John's Lutheran Church at Hosmer.  In 1965 he began working for

THEOBALD and CHRISTINE GUTHMILLER

Theobald Guthmiller, the youngest son of George and Karolina Hieb Guthmiller,
was born December 17, 1862, in Neudorf, Russia.  His brothers and sisters were
Frederich, Johann, Eva, Karolina, and Christina-these family members stayed in
Russia.  Those who came to America were: Ludwig, who settled near Tripp, South
Dakota; and George and Margaretha (Mrs.  Johann Junkert), both settled near
Hosmer, South Dakota.

Christine Ehmann Guthmiller, the daughter of Frederich and Dorada Krein Ehmann,
was born May 9, 1866 in Moldeau, Glueckstal, Russia.  It is known that she had a
brother, Christian, and a sister, Katherine, living in Russia. Those who
immigrated to America were: Jacob, who made his home in Canada; Frederich and
Philip in North Dakota; and Magdalena (Mrs.  Johann Weishaar), lived near
Hosmer, South Dakota.  Christine's parents suffered many hardships and were
very poor, so she became a foster child in Neudorf.

Theobald and Christine were married in Neudorf where they farmed to make their
living.  Theobald also had to serve in the Russian Army.  Two children were
born to them in Neudorf: Fredrich in 1888, and, Christine in 1890-she died in
infancy.  Theobald's brothers, Ludwig and George, were already living in South
Dakota, so they, too, decided to come to this "New Land." Theobald and
Christine, with their four-year-old son, Fredrich, boarded ship and came to New
York and then to South Dakota in May of 1893.  They filed for a homestead in
McPherson County, six miles northeast of Hosmer, on 160 acres of land.  At
first they lived with his brother, George, and his family until their two-room
sod and mud house was built.  On this homestead, five children were born:
George in 1893; Anna Marie in 1895; Karolina in 1898, who died in infancy;
Karolina in 1900; and Jakob in 1902.

In March of 1905, the family moved on a farm three miles east of Hillsview,
South Dakota.  On this farm, Johann was born in 1905 and Arthur was born in
1907.  On October 20, 1920, Theobald, Christine and their son, Arthur, moved to
Hosmer, South Dakota.

Christine passed away on December 18, 1947 at her home, due to a stroke at the
age of 81 years.  Theobald lived in Hosmer until fall of 1948, then he lived
with his children, and later at the Lutheran Home in Eureka, South Dakota.
Theobald passed away on August 12, 1954, at the Lutheran Home at the age of 91
years.  Both are buried in the Bethany Cemetery at Hosmer, South Dakota.

Theobald and Christine Guthmiller were faithful members of St. John's Lutheran
Church in Hosmer and in their quiet Christian ways were an example to many.

Children and grandchildren of Theobald and Christine Guthmiller: Fredrich
Guthmiller, born December 14, 1888, died September 19, 1973.  Married
Friederika Stugelmeier, born April 11, 1892, died November 9, 1963.  Their
children are: Edwin, Otto, George, Eugene, Edward, Adelina (Mrs. Oscar Krebs),
and Allen.

Christine Guthmiller, born in 1890, died in infancy in Neudorf, Russia.

George Guthmiller, born July 19, 1893, died January 30, 1981.  Married
Christine Landenberger, born February 15, 1900.  Their children are: Irene
(Mrs.  Erwin Feiock), Milbert, John, Walter, Lillian (Mrs.  Vernon Farnsworth),
Delores (Mrs.  Robert Froid), Floyd, and Robert.

Anna Marie Guthmiller, born December 18, 1895.  Married Edward Zelmer, born
June 16, 1892, died June 15, 1962. Their children are: Otto, Alma (Mrs.  Wm.
Weishaar) now deceased, Adeline, Milbert, Edmund, and Anna Marie (Mrs. Wayne V.
Smith).

Karolina Guthmiller, born March 12, 1898, died of pneumonia in 1899.

Karolina Guthmiller, born April 8, 1900.  Married George Ketterling, born July
20, 1898, died June 14, 1954. Their children are: Esther (Mrs.  Fred Schock),
Clara (Mrs. Gilbert Melaas), George, Emma (Mrs.  Emil Kleinknecht), Ernest,
Anna (Mrs.  Eugene Smith), Margaret-now deceased, and Martin.

Jakob Guthmiller, born October 24, 1902, died by drowning in a well in 1904.

Johann Guthmiller, born in 1905, died of pneumonia in 1908.

Arthur Guthmiller, born April 28, 1907, died August 18, 1962.  Married Lea
Heyd, born June 14, 1909.  Their children are: Luella (Mrs.  Eugene Hoff), Ruby
(Mrs.  Jack Mondor), Delbert, James, Richard, and Thomas.

MR. and MRS.  THEOBALD G. GUTHMILLER

Theobald G. Guthmiller, the son of George Sr. and Katharina (Wagemen)
Guthmiller, was born on October 21, 1879 in Neudorf, South Russia.  In 1887 he
came to America with his parents who then homesteaded in Cleveland Township,
McPherson County northeast of Hosmer.  Here he grew to manhood and on December
6, 1904 he married Katharina Mayer.  Katharina, the daughter of Jacob and
Katherina (Hieb) Mayer, was born on November 20, 1884.  When she was
approximately six months old her parents moved from South Russia to the United
States and settled on a homestead fourteen miles northeast of Hosmer.  The
newly married couple homesteaded on the NE 1/4 of Section 2 of Sangamon
Township in Edmunds County at which location they farmed for 39 years.  In
March 1943 they sold their personal belongings at public auction and the farm
to son August and moved to Hosmer to retire.  He did carpenter work for many
years and also maintained a large garden to pass the time while living in
retirement.  They were members of the Jehovah Lutheran Church northeast of
Hosmer until they moved to Hosmer.  They then became members of St. John's
Lutheran at Hosmer for the duration of their lives.  Theobald died February 13,
1951 and Katharina died March 3, 1961.  Both are buried in the Bethany Cemetery
in Hosmer.

Eleven children were born to this union.  Pictured below are the surviving
children and their original spouses, whose anniversaries range from 41-57 years
as of November 1984, at which time this portrait was taken.  Standing left to
right are: Julius Mohr, Hosmer; Mrs. Jacob (Martha) Weishaar, Aberdeen; August
Guthmiller, Aberdeen; Edwin Walth, Hosmer; Emil Walth, Deadwood; Mrs. Fred
(Clara) Schumacher, Ipswich; Ervin Kranzler, Aberdeen; Mrs. Milbert (Melitha)
Walth, and Milbert Walth, Aberdeen.  Sitting are: Mrs. Julius (Magdalena) Mohr,
Hosmer; Jacob Weishaar, and Mrs. August Guthmiller, Aberdeen; Mrs. Edwin (Alma)
Walth, Hosmer; Mrs. Emil (Albina) Walth, Deadwood; Fred Schumacher, Ipswich;
and Mrs. Ervin (Rose) Kranzler, Aberdeen.

On June 18, 1985 the family circle was broken by the death of brother August.

CHRISTIAN and EMMA GUTJAHR

Christian Gutjahr was born September 13, 1896 to Jacob and Eva (Stotz) Gutjahr,
who came from Russia in 1893, and homesteaded 6 1/2 miles east of Hosmer.  In
1901 they built a farm four miles south and three miles east of Hosmer, where
Christ farmed with his parents.  Christ was a World War I veteran and married
Emma Preszler December 10, 1918 at the Hosmer Reformed Church.  She was born
January 29, 1899 to Christ and Magdalena (Schnabel) Preszler, who farmed 4 1/2
miles southeast of Hosmer.

Christ and Emma lived on the family homestead and were blessed with three
daughters and four sons.  Delila is married to Brunno Eisenmann of Eureka.
Lorina Jung resides in Hosmer.  Alvera is married to Edwin Beck of Eureka.
Arthur is married and lives in Poulsbo, Washington. Gerhardt is married and
lives on the family farm.  Reinhold is married and lives in Aberdeen.  Allan is
married and resides in Belen, New Mexico.

Christ passed away April 4, 1965 and Emma is retired and is residing in Hosmer.

GERHARDT and BETTY GUTJAHR

Gerhardt Gutjahr was born to Christian and Emma (Preszler) Gutjahr April 29,
1930.  He is a veteran of the Korean War, serving from November 1951-October
1953, spending 13 months in Korea.  He farmed with his parents until his
marriage to Betty Job June 27, 1957.  She was born July 19, 1937 at Leola to
John and Rosina (Geist) Job.

Gerhardt and Betty farm the family homestead, four miles south and three miles
east of Hosmer.  They were blessed with five children.  Myron Lee was born June
1, 1958 and graduated from Hosmer High School in 1976.  He married Carol Muller
and is presently residing at the Minot AFB, where Myron is stationed.  ShirLee
Betty was born November 22, 1960 and graduated from Hosmer High School in 1979.
She married Don Suedmeier and they are living in Aberdeen. Gail Rose was born
April 19, 1962 and graduated from Hosmer High School in 1980.  She married
Colin Jeschke and they are residing on a farm near Roscoe.  Carla Ann was born
December 21, 1968 and is presently a junior at Hosmer High School.  Mark Gary
was born November 18, 1973 and is in the 6th grade at Hosmer Elementary School.

They are members of the United Church of Christ at Hosmer, Christian
Cattlefeeds Assoc., NFO members and Gerhardt is Sangamon Township supervisor.

JOHN and BERTHA GUTJAHR

John Christ Gutjahr was born on December 2, 1899 to Christ and Katherine
(Keszler) Gutjahr.  They had only been in America a few months when John was
born.

As a young man, John farmed with his father and also did carpentry work in the
Hosmer area.  Christ Gutjahr, John's father, was one of the men who organized
the Congregational church in Hosmer, so when they decided to build a church,
John was one of the carpenters.  He also helped build the present Senior Center
on Hosmer's Main Street and also the present home of the Christ Heilmans.

He married Bertha Steiger on November 10, 1925, in Leola, South Dakota.  Bertha
was the daughter of Gottlieb and Sophie (Berreth) Steiger.  The couple moved to
a farm five miles northwest of Hosmer.  In 1927, they bought John's father's,
Christ Gutjahr, homestead southeast of Hosmer where they lived until moving to
Hosmer in 1958.

In 1958, they purchased the Hosmer Meat Market which they operated in
partnership with their son, Milbert, for six years.

John and Bertha were the parents of two children. Milbert was born August 21,
1926 and is presently living with his mother in Hosmer.

Fernella was born November 23, 1932.  She married Gilbert Grosz and they have
four children.  They make their home in Ipswich.

John Gutjahr died on January 8, 1984.

PHILIP and MAGDALINA GUTJAHR

Philip J. Gutjahr was born October 30, 1897 in McPherson County near Hosmer to
Jacob and Eva (Stotz) Gutjahr. Philip lived with his parents on a farm
southeast of Hosmer until his marriage to Magdalina Wiederrich on December 6,
1921.  They farmed in the Hosmer area until 1948 when they retired and moved
into Aberdeen.

Magdalina was born February 26, 1901 to Jacob and Phillipina (Will) Wiederrich.

They were blessed with two children.  Elenore was born September 6, 1922.  She
married Alfred Borchard of Mansfield, South Dakota.  They live on a farm near
Mansfield. They have six children.

Ramona was born July 22, 1927.  She married Alton Jung of Roscoe.  They live on
a farm northeast of Roscoe.  They have three children.

Philip passed away on January 12, 1966.  Magdalina lives in Aberdeen.

ALBERT and MARTHA HAERTER

Albert J. Haerter was born July 5, 1903, to John and Christina Heckenlaible
Haerter at Hosmer, South Dakota.

He attended public school at Hosmer, high school at Eureka Academy, and also
attended Aberdeen Business College. He worked with his father in the Ford
garage, which his father owned, until 1920 when the family opened the Farmers
State Bank, in which he served as cashier until his retirement in 1975.

He married Martha Jakober on July 6, 1930.  Martha was born at Hosmer, South
Dakota to Christian and Margaretha Vilhauer Jakober on May 22, 1907.  Here she
attended public and high school and then worked in her father's general
merchandise store until her marriage to Albert.  One son was born to this
union, Charles, who is a general surgeon at Lake Havasu City, Arizona since
1973.  He has two sons, David and Charlton.

Albert was an active member of the Hosmer School Board for 28 years; was County
Heart Fund chairman for almost 20 years.  He was an active promoter in the
building of Hosmer's Senior Citizen's Home, tennis court and golf course.

Albert died February 15, 1977.  Martha lives at Hosmer where she takes care of
her home and yard.

ARTHUR and ROSE HAERTER

Arthur John Haerter was born February 25, 1897, at Menno, South Dakota, to John
and Christina (Heckenlaible) Haerter.  He married Rose Stoecker June 24, 1926,
at Hosmer.  Rose was born May 3, 1901, to Gottlieb and Katherina (Trefz)
Stoecker.

At the age of one Arthur moved with his family to Hosmer.  He attended Eureka
College and the Aberdeen Business College.  He entered the U.S. Army in 1918
and served until the end of World War I. He returned to Hosmer and in 1920
helped start the Farmers State Bank of which he served as cashier from 1920
until 1926 and then as president until his retirement in 1975.  Arthur passed
away January 31, 1981.

Arthur served as president of the Haerter-Heckenlaible Corporation.  He was
mayor of Hosmer from 1931 to 1935 and again from 1942 to 1946.  He was a
charter member of the Hosmer American Legion.

Arthur and Rose were blessed with two sons.  John was born August 21, 1927,
married Doris Gruenewald, and they have three children, Debra, Bruce and
Denise.  They reside in Hosmer.

Plin was born April 16, 1932, married Ingegerd and they have two children,
Katrina and Jens.  They reside in San Diego, California.

JOHN and DORIS HAERTER

John Arthur Haerter was born to Arthur and Rose (Stoecker) Haerter August 21,
1927, at Hosmer, South Dakota.  He married Doris Gruenewald, June 3, 1951, at
Armour, South Dakota.  Doris was born at Mitchell, South Dakota to Fred and
Bertha Gruenewald.

John graduated from Hosmer High School in 1945. Following graduation he
enlisted in the U.S. Coast Guard. After basic training in Brooklyn, New York,
he was assigned to U.S. Coast Guard Headquarters in Washington, DC, as a U.S.
Navy Code Specialist.  He received a B.S. degree in economics and government
from the University of South Dakota in 1950 after which he joined Farmers State
Bank as a bookkeeper.  In January, 1973, he was elected president of the bank.
John served as president of the South Dakota Bankers Association in 1984-85, is
presently serving on the Board of Directors with BancInsure, and on the
American Bankers Association Government Relations Board.

Doris attended Corsica High School and received a B.S. degree in business
education from the University of South Dakota in 1951.  She is presently
employed by Westworld as a receptionist in the Hosmer Medical Clinic.

John and Doris are the parents of three children.  Debra (Debbie) was born
February 14, 1953.  She attended Hosmer High School and received a B.F.A.
degree in music education from the University of South Dakota in 1975.  She
presently is teaching nursery school and gives piano lessons.  In June, 1977,
she married Robert Schaub of Faulkton, South Dakota. Robert received a B.S.
degree in accounting in 1973 and a J. D. from the School of Law at the
University of South Dakota in 1976.  He is practicing in Chamberlain, South
Dakota, where they live.  They are the parents of Nicholas, born in 1979; and
Alicia, born in 1981.

Bruce, born July 18, 1956, attended Hosmer High School and received a B.S.
degree in business administration from Northern State in 1978.  Since that time
he has been employed by First Bank of Aberdeen and is now serving as a
commercial loan officer.  He is living in Aberdeen, South Dakota.

Denise was born April 18, 1969, and will graduate in 1987 from Hosmer High
School.

DAVID and MARTHA HARDY

David Hardy was born February 18, 191 1, near Hosmer, South Dakota, to Wendlyn
and Regina (Stolz) Hardy.  He attended rural school and grew to manhood on this
farm.  He entered the army in 1942 and saw action in Southern France and
Germany.  He was wounded and received the Purple Heart when he was stationed in
Germany.

Following his discharge he returned to Hosmer and married Martha Eisenbeisz on
November 22, 1946.  Martha was the daughter of David M. and Juliana (Rohrbach)
Eisenbeisz. Martha was born April 30, 1923, at Bowdle, South Dakota. She
attended rural school and worked on her parents' farm until her marriage.

Dave worked on construction, and retired in 1971.  Martha worked at the Senior
Citizen's Home as a nurse's aide. Dave passed away on December 9, 1977.

Dave and Martha have three sons and one daughter: Raymond, who lives in Yuma,
Colorado, and is married to Joan Kunde of Marvin, South Dakota, they have three
daughters; Margie, who lives in Aberdeen, South Dakota and is married to Leo
Imberi, of Hillsview, South Dakota, they have one son; Harvey, who lives in
Aberdeen, South Dakota, and is married to Debbie Maisch of Hosmer, South Dakota,
they have one daughter; and Larry, who lives in Aberdeen, South Dakota.  Martha
has one son from a previous marriage, Howard, who lives in Guthrie Center,
Iowa.  He is married to Nancy Rich, and they have four boys and one girl.

JOSEPH and ELIZABETH HARTY

Joseph W. Harty was born January 10, 1918, to Wendelin and Regina (Stolz)
Harty.  He farmed with his father southwest of Hosmer until 1937 when he joined
the CCC Camp for two years.  Upon returning to Hosmer he married Elizabeth
Hartman.  Joseph worked for area farmers until 1943 at which time he was
employed by Milwaukee Railroad. After serving in the Army from 1945 to 1946, he
returned to Hosmer and continued with the railroad.  In 1950 he was transferred
to Hauge, North Dakota, for one year.  Once again, the family returned to
Hosmer and Joseph continued working for the railroad until his retirement in
1966.

Elizabeth (Hartman) Harty was born August 21, 1922. Her parents, Joe and
Katherine (Heier) Hartman, passed away when she was seven years old.  Elizabeth
grew up in the home of her uncle, Joseph J. Heier and family.  In 1968 she
began working at the Hosmer Citizen's Home as a nurse's aide and is employed
there today.

Joseph and Elizabeth have six children: Donald, Charles, Virginia, Lester,
Daniel, and Bonnie.

Donald was born August 7, 1940, married Maria Herman, and they have two sons,
Michael and Donnie.  They are currently living in Killeen, Texas.

Charles was born April 21, 1942, married Anna Imberi and they have two
children, Warren and Wanda.  Charles is machinist at Safeguard Industries and
Anna is employed by Holiday Inn of Aberdeen.  They live in Aberdeen.

Virginia was born May 11, 1943, married Lyle Sillma and they have one daughter,
Denise.  They live in Milbank where Lyle is an independent trucker and Virginia
is employed by Milbank Insurance.

Lester was born February 7, 1953, married Gayle Johnson, and they have three

Daniel was born December 15, 1955, and is employed by Rolfson's Construction in

PETER and KATHERINA HAUCK

Peter Hauck, born in South Russia in 1888, came to the, country in 1889 at the
age of one year.  His parents settled in McPherson County.  In 1909 he married
Katherina Preszler who was born near Eureka South Dakota.

This union was blessed with six children: William, living in Pharr, Texas; Lea
Mettler, living in Wessington Springs, South Dakota; Emanuel, living in
Carefree, Arizona; Tillie Job, living in Post Falls, Idaho; Lorine Neuharth,
living in Leola, South Dakota.  One daughter died in infancy.

The family farmed in McPherson County until retirement in the fall of 1943,
when they moved to Hosmer, South Dakota.  Mr. Hauck passed away in September
1958, and Mrs. Hauck passed away in February 1965.

JOHN and CORA HECKENLAIBLE ON TAKING ROOTS

John Heckenlaible came to Hosmer from his home southern South Dakota, as a
young man, to realize his dream of owning a general merchandise store--a dream
instigated when, at age 13, he worked in the Seydel Store at Menno. Upon his
arrival he homesteaded on land a few miles from town, at the same time, setting
the wheels in motion toward owning the store which, in due time, elicited a
thriving trade.

In 1903 John and Cara Knodel (also a transport from the south") were united in
marriage.  They built their first home in 1905, in such a sparsely settled area
that gypsy encampments frequented the neighborhood.

Despite excursions to "greener grasses," the couple took firm root here, John
branching out into other business ventures--banking, lumber, and real estate
among them.

Of their six children, there are two survivors--Ramona and Arline.

Ramona, widow of Pastor Otto Schaible, has two daughters and four grandsons,
and resides in Fargo, North Dakota.

Arline, having taken root again, after having lived in kinder climes and
greener pastures, resides in the same house that was once neighbored by
gypsies--the home in which John and Cora Heckenlaible lived out the days of
their lives to ages 97 and 101, respectively.

WILMER and AMY HECKENLAIBLE

Wilmer Heckenlaible was born at Menno, South Dakota, on November 17, 1916, to
Karl J. and Regina (Seydel) Heckenlaible.  He graduated from Menno High School,
and attended Yankton College, Yankton, South Dakota, where he received a
teaching certificate.  He attended Northern State College at Aberdeen, South
Dakota, and received a B.S. degree in elementary education and a minor in music.

In 1941, Wilmer married Amy Jakober, daughter of Christ and Charlotte (Huber)
Jakober of Hosmer, South Dakota.  Amy was born August 4, 1923, at Hosmer, South
Dakota.  She graduated from Hosmer High School and worked as a sales clerk at
the Red & White Store, and G. J. Walth Super Valu.  She also was clerk and
business manager of the Hosmer School District for 33 years.

Wilmer taught school at Hosmer, Roscoe, and Okaton, South Dakota, for a total
of 37 years.  He served in the army during World War II, from March 1943, to
March 1945. After being discharged he returned to Hosmer and operated a grocery
store, the Hosmer Red & White Store, for five and a half years.  Wilmer has
held various offices in the American Legion, Civic Club, St. John's Lutheran
Church, Hosmer Education Association, and the Hosmer City Council.  He has also
been a director and member of St. John's Lutheran Church choir since 1945.

Amy has held offices in the American Legion Auxiliary and St. John's ALCW,
taught confirmation class and Sunday School, and has been a member of the
church choir for many years.  She is also an active member of the Hosmer Civic
Club.

Wilmer and Amy have two children.  Elizabeth, who graduated from Hosmer High
School and Aberdeen School of Commerce in Aberdeen, South Dakota.  She is
married to Wilmer Wiederrich, son of Edwin and Pauline (Stoecker) Wiederrich of
Hosmer, South Dakota.  They live in Aberdeen where Wilmer is employed at
Safeguard Industries, and Elizabeth does baby-sitting in her home.  They have
two sons, Steven, who works for Texas Instruments in Dallas, Texas, and Mark,
who is attending the School of Mines at Rapid City, South Dakota, and will be
graduating in 1987.

Douglas, who graduated from Hosmer High School, has spent many years working
and living in Germany.  He enlisted in the navy after graduation from high
school, was discharged a year later, and re-enlisted in the navy again in 1981,
and is presently stationed out of Norfolk, Virginia.

Wilmer and Amy have been retired since 1981, and live in the home in which Amy
was born and grew up.  They keep busy with gardening and yard work in the
summer months, and Amy works part time in the winter months taking applications
for Low Income Energy Assistance Program.

WILLIE and LYDIA HEER

Willie Heer was born December 25, 1896 on a farm nine miles northeast of Eureka
in McPherson County.  He attended country school near their home and farmed
with his parents until 1919.  He married Martha Stoebner November 2, 1919.  One
daughter, Viola Irene, was born to them, July 10, 1921.  She attended grade
school in Stickel Township and married Edwin Metzger.  They reside on a farm
southeast of Eureka and have five children: Judy, Kenneth, Jacelyn, Lynette,
and Arliss, and eight grandchildren.

Mrs. Heer died July 1924 following an accident.  Later, he married Lydia Sayler
of Venturia, North Dakota.  She was born July 25, 1904 and attended grade
school in Venturia and was an assistant schoolteacher for two years.

Willie and Lydia farmed 8 1/2 miles southeast of Eureka in Stickel Township
until 1945 when they moved to Hosmer, where Willie was chief of police and
water superintendent.  He also enjoyed auctioneering for approximately 40 years.
August 23, 1972 they moved to Eureka.  Mr. Heer passed away September 28, 1983
and Mrs. Heer is presently residing in Eureka.

They had two sons.  Quentin was born November 19, 1926, at Eureka and married
Lavina Schaible.  He attended grade school in Stickel Township and high school
in Eureka. Lavina attended school in Hosmer and college in Aberdeen. They have
two children, Karen and Rick.  Quentin was a guard for many years in the Men's
Correctional Institution in San Luis Obispo, California and Lavina was a
secretary in the Institution.  They are retired and reside in San Luis Obispo,
California.

Larry was born September 18, 1940 in Eureka and graduated from Hosmer High
School and Northern State College with a Masters degree.  He married Evelea
Olson of Rosholt, South Dakota.  They have a daughter, Andrea. Larry is
supervisor of the Correctional Education Program at the California State Prison
at San Quentin, California. Evelea teaches in the Dept. of Corrections at the
State Prison and they reside in San Quentin Village, California.

ADAM and MARGARET HEIER

Adam Heier was born December 24, 1921, to Philip Heier and Barbara (Lowenstein)
Heier on a farm northwest of Hosmer.  He grew up there and went to country
school.  In 1938 he went to CC Camp in the Black Hills and was there until
1941, being drafted into the army in 1942.  Adam served in France, England and
Germany, being discharged in 1946. Returning home he farmed with his father
until his marriage in 1947.

Margaret (Hopfinger) Heier was born February 18, 1927, to Adam and Mary
(Reinboldt) Hopfinger at Bowdle, South Dakota.  She was raised on a farm south
of Hosmer moving to Aberdeen in October 1943, with her parents.  She graduated
from Central High School in 1945 after which she worked at K. O. Lee until 1947.

Adam and Margaret were married November 10, 1947, at St. Mary's Catholic Church
in Aberdeen.  They lived on various farms near Hosmer before moving to the
Heier family farm in 1953.  In 1975 they sold their farm and moved into Hosmer.

Adam worked for the A.S.C.S. office for 13 years, country school board member
for 6 years and 8 years on the Hosmer school board.  He has served several
positions in the American Legion, a board member for Farmers Union for many
years and a council member for Holy Trinity for three years.  After moving to
Hosmer, Adam has done various jobs. Since retirement he still does odd jobs
when available.

Margaret has been an active member and held different offices for Holy Trinity
Altar Society, is an active American Legion Auxiliary member being treasurer
for 11 years.  She has been employed at the Senior Citizen's Home since 1975,
as a medication aide.

Adam and Margaret have been blessed with 13 children. Leonard married Jean
Pietz, lives in Rapid City, is a salesman for Dakota Typewrite and has one
daughter.  Pius married Geraldine Senger, lives in Aberdeen, South Dakota and
is a design engineer for K. O. Lee and they have three children. Bernard
(Barney) is married to Pamela Schatz, is owner of Heier's Building Supplies at
Roscoe and they have two children.  Raphael lives in Fargo, North Dakota, is
sergeant in the Air Force and works as a material controller for civil
engineering branch of North Dakota Air National Guard.  He has two daughters,
is married to Nancy Hoppe, who has a son.  Kathleen is married to Stanley Mohr,
is a housewife and bookkeeper for Stan's Auto.  They have three children.
Robert lives in Kulm, North Dakota and is a diesel mechanic. Wendelyn lives in
Arnold, California and is an assistant manager in a meat market.  Bernadine,
married to Jack Davis, lives on a farm near Forestburg, South Dakota, is a
housewife and does substitute teaching.  They have one son.  Caroline married
Tom Scholten, lives in Baltic, South Dakota, is a registered nurse and they
have one daughter.  John lives in Aberdeen and is an inspector for the WEB
rural water line. Michael is attending South Dakota School of Mines and
Technology in Rapid City.  Edward is a student at Northern State College in
Aberdeen.  Timothy will graduate from Hosmer High School in 1987.

DAVID J. and TILLIE HEIER

David J. Heier was born November 28, 1949 in Edmunds County to Joe P. and
Josephine Hopfinger Heier.  David lived on his parents' farm with his parents
and eight brothers and sisters until his parents purchased the Hosmer Meat
Market (currently Heier's Meat Market) in 1966 and moved into the city of
Hosmer.

David graduated from Hosmer High School in 1968. After high school graduation,
he went into the Army in 1969. He served in West Germany until he was honorably
discharged in 1971.  He then worked with his father in Heier's Meat Market
until Joe passed away in October of 1976, then David took over co-management of
the meat market with his youngest brother, Gerard.

Tillie Malsom Heier was born to Paul and Florence Heilman Malsom on November
21, 1955.  She lived on the farm with her parents until she married David on
May 11, 1974.  This union was blessed with three children: Liza, who was born
December 1, 1975, and Sean and Shannon (twin boys), who were born October 4,
1985.

David and Tillie are both active members of the Holy Trinity Catholic Church in
Hosmer.

ELOY and JANIS HEIER

Eloy R. Heier was born June 12, 1956, to Raphael and Helena Heier.  Eloy
attended school in Hosmer and graduated from high school in 1975.  Since that
time he has farmed southwest of Hosmer with his father.  On May 28, 1983, he
married Janis Dutenhoeffer in Roscoe, South Dakota.

Janis is the daughter of Stanis and Leone Dutenhoeffer of Roscoe, South Dakota.

also enjoys teaching fitness classes.

In the fall of 1984 Eloy and Janis bought their home in Hosmer from Fern Ulmer.

JOE P. and JOSEPHINE HEIER

Joseph P. Heier was born on April 26, 1912 to Philip and Barbara Lowenstein
Heier on a farm northwest of Hosmer. He was the eldest of 16 children--3 of
whom died in infancy. (See Philip and Barbara Heier History.) Joe lived on the
family farm until he was inducted into the Army in April of 1942. He served as
Private First Class in the Field Artillery as a truck driver in Europe during
World War II.  Joe was honorably discharged on October 15, 1945, when he
returned to the family farm in Hosmer, South Dakota.

Josephine Hopfinger was born on April 30, 1923 to Adam and Mary Reinboldt
Hopfinger on a farm west of Hosmer.  She received an education in the rural
schools of this area, completing the eighth grade.  Josephine has five sisters:
Mrs. Frances (Val) Heilman, Bowdle; Mrs. Lena (George) Harty, Aberdeen, South
Dakota; Mrs. Phillipine (Roy) Harty, Aberdeen; Mrs. Margaret (Adam) Heier; and
Mrs Leona (Dave) Malsam, both of Hosmer.  In 1943, the family moved from their
farm to Aberdeen, South Dakota where Josephine was employed until the time of
her marriage.

Joe and Josephine were united in marriage at St. Mary's Catholic Church in
Aberdeen on July 1, 1946.  They farmed east of Hosmer until April of 1966 when
they purchased the Hosmer Meat Market and turned it into a successful and very
well-known business establishment in a very few years. (See Heier's Meat Market
History.) This marriage union was blessed with nine children.

Mrs. Therese Rux, Aberdeen, South Dakota, was born March 22, 1947 and graduated
from Hosmer High School in 1965.  She married Travis Rux in 1969 and they have
four children as of this printing.

Mrs. Marlene Bechard, Onida, South Dakota, was born August 24, 1948.  She
graduated from Hosmer High School in 1966 and from Aberdeen School of Commerce
in 1967.  She married Floyd Bechard in 1970 and the couple has five children.

David J. Heier, Hosmer, South Dakota, was born November 28, 1949. (See David J.
and Tillie Heier History.)

Mrs. Barbara Erickson, Rapid City, South Dakota, was born January 27, 1951.
She graduated from Hosmer High School in 1969 and from Aberdeen School of
Commerce in 1970.  Barbara married Gary Erickson in 1972 and they have four
children.

Anthony P. Heier, Hosmer, South Dakota, was born May 6, 1952 and graduated from

There are a total of 25 grandchildren in the Heier family as of this printing.

Joe P. Heier was very active in the city of Hosmer, being a city council member
for eight years.  He was also a Commercial Club member, a volunteer fireman,
and a member of the American Legion.  Joe and Josephine were also members of
the Holy Trinity Catholic Church in Hosmer where Josephine is still a member.
She is also a member of the church's Christian Mothers Society, having served
for a time as its president. Josephine is also a member of the American Legion
Auxiliary.

Joe P. Heier passed away on October 15, 1976 of an apparent heart attack while
driving the meat market van on a delivery route.  He was 64 years old.

Josephine Heier is still living in Hosmer and is sole owner of Heier's Meat
Market.

Joe died February 9, 1982 and Frances passed away January 20, 1975.

PAUL and MARY HEIER

Paul Heier was born to Joe and Frances (Hoerner) Heier October 9, 1940 in
Edmunds County.  He grew up on a farm three miles west of Hosmer and received
his elementary education at a country school near their farm home and graduated
from Hosmer High School in 1959.  He entered the U.S. Marine Corp in October
1959 and received his basic training at San Diego.  During his tour of duty
oversees he was stationed at Japan, Guam, Okinawa and Philippines and spent two
years in Barstow, California.  Upon returning home in October 1963 he began
working for the Hosmer Co-op and later attended Aberdeen School of Commerce.
He married Mary Walth, daughter of Milbert and Melitha (Guthmiller) Walth. She
was born August 21, 1946 and attended rural school through 5th grade and Hosmer
Public School graduating in 1964.

Following their marriage in 1966 Paul and Mary lived in Aberdeen where Paul was
employed at Hub City Iron Co. and Mary worked at St. Luke's Hospital.  In 1969
they moved to Hosmer and farmed with his parents and brother until 1975 when
they purchased the Adam Heier farm, three miles northwest of Hosmer, where they
are currently residing. Mary was employed at the Hosmer Public School during the
1978-79 school term and worked at Doug's Fairway in Hosmer 1979-82.

They have four children.  Troy was born January 6, 1967 and graduated from
Hosmer High School in 1985 and is presently attending Lake Area Vo-Tech at
Watertown as a diesel mechanic.

Ronn was born January 27, 1970, Chad was born July 4, 1972, and Samantha was
born April 30, 1982.

PETER and SANDIE HEIER

Peter and Sandie Heier were both born and raised in the Hosmer area.  Peter was
born June 18, 1946 to Joe and Frances Hoerner Heier.  He grew up on his
parents' farm three miles west and one-half mile north of Hosmer, attended
rural school and Hosmer Public School graduating in 1964. He farmed with his
parents until 1970 when they retired and moved into Hosmer.  He continued to
farm with his brother, Paul, until 1975 when he purchased a nearby farm.  Peter
married Sandie Weishaar, October 24, 1970 at Hosmer.  She was born May 20, 1951
to Reinhold and Viola (Ulmer) Weishaar.  She grew up on a farm northeast of
Hosmer and attended country school in Cleveland Township through the 3rd grade
and completed grade school at the Hosmer Public School and graduated in 1969.
Prior to her marriage she was employed at the Aberdeen American News.

Peter and Sandie and family are the third generation to own and operate the
Heier homestead.  They have three children: Tracie was born August 7, 1971 at
Aberdeen; Kevin was born April 27, 1973 at Aberdeen; and Dawn was born May 25,
1979 at Aberdeen.

Peter is currently serving on the Hosmer school board of education and is
chairman of the Hosmer Township.  Sandie was the local news correspondent for
the Northwest Blade and the Bowdle Pioneer from 1974-1985.

PHILIP and BARBARA HEIER

(Excerpts from a diary written by Mrs.  Barbara Lowenstein Heier.  Portions of
this history is from "word-of-mouth "to her family.)

My parents, John and Margaret Zimmerman Lowenstein, came to America on December
27, 1900. I was seven years old.  I remember when we children were told we were
coming to America--I was so happy!  But my oldest brother, Wendelin, who was 17
years old at the time, had a girl friend in Russia and he didn't want to leave
her.  One time, shortly before we set sail, I walked behind a barn on our place
in Russia and Wendelin was standing there, crying.  I'd never seen my brother
cry before and I couldn't understand why anybody would be sad to be going to
America.

On the way to America, our ship had to stop at the Port of Liverpool, England,
because of some very thick fog.  There was a Jewish man on board the ship who
could speak many languages and he translated for everybody else.  The Jewish
passengers were kept on a separate part of the ship and some people didn't like
to talk to them.  I could never understand that and I had a good friendship
with a little Jewish child.

One time the ship encountered a bad storm at sea and I remember laughing so
hard because the chamber pots kept sliding back and forth across the floor of
the ship!  My mother was angry at me for laughing and told me to stop.  Now I
realize she was afraid of what might happen to the ship in the storm.

After we got to America, the first place we settled in was a place by
Hillsview, South Dakota.  We were there for a month.  Then we moved to Hosmer
on a farm west of town.  It was a sod house with a dirt floor.  Mother had to
plaster the walls and floor with clay.  There were two rooms in this sod
house--a kitchen and a big room which we used for a bedroom and living room.
We had an old cook stove and an old table made from wooden boards.  We had two
benches but no chairs until my dad could make some.  There was no well on that
place so we got our water from the snow in the winter and from a nearby lake
the rest of the year.  We had a calf who was like a pet to us--he followed us
around wherever we went.  One day he followed us to the lake, and while we
filled water, he did his duty in the water!  Mother said we had to go to a
different lake to get some more water and next time we had to leave the calf
home!  We went to our closest neighbors to get drinking water and we had to
burn cow chips to get warm and to cook our meals.

My dad worked for a Mr. John Kinn, who lived about two miles west of our place.
Mother often walked over there to bring him his washed clothes and my youngest
brother, Leonard, and I went along, too.  It was fun!  I still remember the
place--it was on the west side of the Bowdle-Eureka road and is about eight
miles west of Hosmer.  My dad worked there for a whole year, earning $30 a
month in the summer and $20 a month in winter.  He did blacksmith work and
repaired harnesses for the horses and did just about everything else, too.  He
liked the work because he was his own boss.  Mr. Kinn was always gone because
he was a county commissioner and was also on the school board.  My mother often
cooked for the Kinns because the Kinn girls didn't like to cook.  They
especially liked my mother's soup.

We went to school there too.  I was nine years old and my brother, Leonard, was
seven years old.  We had a very good teacher, who was a lady.  There were 16
people in school--10 boys and 6 girls.  I loved to go to school!  We had to
walk two miles to go to this school--there was no bus in those days.  It was in
1901 and 1902.

We moved to Ipswich, South Dakota in April of 1902 on a farm four miles east of
Ipswich.  We rented about six cows on shares--we got the milk, cream, and every
third calf.

Soon after we moved to Ipswich they began putting up a telephone line from
Ipswich to Aberdeen.  The workers had their trailer house in our yard--they
called it the "cook shanty" and it was pulled with horses.  The workers would
get their milk, water, potatoes and other vegetables from us.  We lived on this
farm for one year and then moved to a farm three miles east of Ipswich.

For awhile, on our new farm, we had to walk three miles to school.  After a
time we started to drive to school with a horse and buggy.  There was no barn
by the school so we drove with the old brown mare and buggy to the schoolhouse
and turned her around.  Then my brother stood on the buggy and drove for a few
yards and jumped off and let her go. She'd go all the way home by herself!  One
day my older brother said he'd try to send the horse to pick us up after school
and the old brown mare did it!  We had to watch for her, then when she saw us
walking from a distance, she'd stop right there and we had to walk up to her!
But we were glad to get a ride home from school.

In 1905 we moved out farther west--about 14 miles southwest of Roscoe, South
Dakota in Hillside Township. That is where I lived until I got married to
Philip Heier on August 28, 1911. After we were married we lived on a farm two
miles west of Hosmer for two years and then moved one mile southeast of Hosmer,
where we lived for four years. Then we moved    to our own farm three miles
northwest of Hosmer in 1919 and we lived there until we moved into Hosmer in
1952.  We bought the Jacob Diedrich house on the southeast corner of Hosmer.
We had 100 chickens and also two cows, so we had all the exercise we needed!

My father, John Lowenstein, died in 1915 and my mother, Margaret Zimmerman
Lowenstein, died in March of 1938.

Barbara Lowenstein Heier was born to John and Margaret Zimmerman Lowenstein on
May 29, 1893 in Mariantal, South Russia.  Philip Heier was born to Joseph Sr.
and Theresa Heintzman Heier on November 26, 1887 in Kleinliebental, South
Russia.  Philip and Barbara were married on August 28, 1911 and were blessed
with 16 children--three of whom died in infancy.  Their children are: Joseph
P., deceased October 15, 1976 (see Joseph P. and Josephine Heier History);
George P., Hosmer, South Dakota; Tony, Bozeman, Montana; John, Lodi,
California; Martin, Pleasant Hill, California; Barney, Oakland, California;
Philip Jr., Bremerton, Washington; Adam, Hosmer, South Dakota (see Adam and
Margaret Heier History); Margaret Barstad, San Leandro, California; Jacob,
Huntington Beach, California; Michael, La Mirada, California; Theresa Jarrett,
San Leandro, California; and Raymond, Seattle, Washington.

Philip had six brothers and sisters: Martin, Mrs. Helen Lowenstein, Mrs.
Barbara Hartman, Mrs. Katie Hartman Benfit, Joseph, and John.  Barbara had 15
brothers and sisters--11 of whom passed away.  Many of these died at a very
young age--between two and four years of age--of diphtheria.  Those who lived
to adulthood were: Wendelin, Leopold, John, and Leonard.

Philip Heier passed away on November 28, 1964 and Barbara Heier is currently
residing at the Hosmer Senior Citizen's Home.  She has 47 grandchildren and 55
great-grandchildren.  She has one grandson and one great-grandson who were
born on her birthday.  They are Barney Heier, Roscoe, South Dakota, son of Adam
and Margaret Heier, Hosmer; and Justin Mohr, Hosmer, son of George and Mary Jo
Mohr, Hosmer and grandson of Mrs. Josephine Heier, Hosmer.
