ARNOLD and ARLENE HERRBOLDT

Arnold (Arnie) Herrboldt was born March 23, 1924 to Edwin J. and Olga (Huber)
Herrboldt in Hosmer.  He graduated from Hosmer High School in 1942, attended the
University of Minnesota School of Business, served in the infantry during World
War II in central Europe, and graduated from the University of South Dakota
with a B.A. degree.  He married Arlene Mehlhoff, daughter of John and Lydia
(Bender) Mehlhoff on September 3, 1950.  They lived in Billings, Montana for
one year where Arnold was employed with Grinnell Corp. of the Pacific, and
Arlene was a bookkeeper at Midland National Bank.  The failing health of
Arnold's father prompted them to move to Hosmer where Arnold became a partner
in A. Herrboldt & Co.

Some of Arnie's past pursuances include: president and secretary of the Hosmer
Civic Club; school board of education member; St. John's Lutheran Church Sunday
school superintendent; congregational president and choir member; American
Legion adjutant and commander; Republican state delegate and committeeman; and
a graduate of the Dale Carnegie course.  He is a member of the VFW, the Great'
Plains Development Association, and the Hosmer Development Corporation.

Arlene was born December 13, 1927, graduated from!, Hosmer High School in 1946,
attended NSC for two years and was employed at Farmers State Bank in Hosmer
before marriage.  She earned a diploma from the Chicago School of Interior
Decoration, was editor of the Hosmer Bulletin for six years, and assistant
activity director at the Senior Citizen's Home for two years.  Arlene is
presently an area representative for Educational Foundation for Foreign Study.
She has directed choirs and taught classes at St. John's Lutheran Church, held
offices in the local, conference and district ALCW; also in Extension Club,
Civic Club and Senior. Citizen's Home Auxiliary.

Born to this marriage were four girls: Paulette, Sydney and Tamara; Michelle
died in infancy in 1963.

Paulette was born January 21, 1953 and received a B.A. in nursing from
Augustana College.  She was a surgical nurse at Bismarck Hospital before her
marriage to Dr. Nyle Wollenhaupt, an assistant professor of agronomy at the
University of Missouri in Columbia.  They have one daughter, Sarah, and one
son, Grant.

Sydney was born September 11, 1954, received a B.S. degree from Northern State
College and an M.S. degree from the University of Wisconsin.  She is employed
with the Division of Developmental Disabilities in Tucson, Arizona, functioning
as a speech-language pathologist and consultant.

Tamara was born October 8, 1959 and graduated from Northern State College in
1981 with a B.S. degree in management and marketing.  She is an administrative
assistant at South Dakota Student Loan Corp. in Aberdeen.

Arnie and Arlene have traveled extensively but Hosmer is home.

ARTHUR J. and EMMA HERRBOLDT

Arthur Herrboldt, born July 22, 1906 in Wittenberg, South Dakota (Hutchinson
County; Olivet, county seat) came to Hosmer with his family, the Adam
Herrboldts, when he was only eight months old.  The family resided in two
different homes before building their own residence on the lot where a son,
Milton, now lives, and a sister of Arthur's could recall that he stood by the
living room window and said, "Ich kann sehn mein haus," viewing their previous
place of residence.  He was either five or six years old at that time.

Arthur attended the school in Hosmer through the 7th grade after which he went
to Eureka for the 8th grade plus one year beyond that but not in consecutive
years. (The school in Eureka was called E.L.C.--Eureka Lutheran College--but
in actuality it offered only two years of college training and was more an
academy or advanced high school. When it extended the curriculum to two more
years it did become an official college.)

After schooling at E.L.C., Arthur attended Sweeney Automotive School in Kansas
City, Missouri for a year and in Chicago for six weeks, returning to Hosmer
after graduation, working as a carpenter and doing other odd jobs.  His father
had a lumberyard so Arthur helped him as well.  In 1919, A. Herrboldt & Co.
Implement business came into existence and Arthur joined the partnership of
father, Adam, and brother Ed, in 1926 after being married to Emma Streeb.  The
area was hard hit during the depression years and Art, as he was most often
addressed, did welding on his own with help from a man who came through selling
welding equipment.  That was a boon to local farmers since they could not
afford new machinery and had to get by with repairing the old.

Anyone who knew Art learned that he had a sense of humor and enjoyed playing
practical jokes.  A favorite was to lure a man standing around the shop over
the welding machine when he turned it off, knowing that there would be a final
"pop" when the last drop of gas was created, which would scare the bystander
and their reaction was duly reported at the family table.

Art and Emma had six children: Robert, Brush, Colorado; Doris (Carlson),
Deerwood, Minnesota; Milton, Hosmer; Betty (Scheutte), Powell, Ohio; Janis
(Feichtner), Cheyenne, Wyoming; and Sharon (Mayer), Golden, Colorado. Arthur
passed away December 23, 1983.  Emma is residing at Tabor Apartments, in Brush,
Colorado.

EDWIN and OLGA HERRBOLDT

Edwin Jacob Herrboldt, son of Adam and Friedricka Herrboldt, (nee Ulmer) was
born December 22, 1893, in Hutchinson County near Menno, South Dakota.  In 1907
he moved with his parents to Hosmer.  He attended Eureka Junior College at
Eureka and Wartburg Academy at Waverly, Iowa.  He was a veteran of World War I.
On December 2, 1917, he married Olga Huber.  Mr. Herrboldt was a partner in the
implement business known as A. Herrboldt & Co. until the time of his death on
November 19, 1964.

Olga Wilhelmina Huber was born June 23, 1895, to Jacob and Salbina (Walz) Huber
in Freeman, South Dakota. Olga was a charter member of the Lutheran Ladies Aid
and the local unit of the American Legion Auxiliary.  She was a resident of the
Senior Citizen's Home at the time of her death, February 24, 1977.

They were blessed with five children: Irma, Verna, Arnold, Gordon, and Lora.

Irma was born November 26, 1918, graduated from Hosmer High School and attended
Northern State College. She is currently employed as an administrative
assistant to the vice president of Planning & Development, New Vector
Communications, Inc. in Bellevue, Washington.

Verna was born December 31, 1920, graduated from Hosmer High School, and was
married to Melvin Sonnenfeld, who passed away September 5, 1981.  Verna resides
in Aberdeen. She has three children: Tom, Sandra, and Karen, and four
grandchildren.

Arnold was born March 23, 1924, graduated from the University of South Dakota
and is married to Arlene (nee Mehlhoff).  They own the implement business in
Hosmer. They have three daughters: Paulette Wollenhaupt in Columbia, Missouri;
Sydney in Tucson, Arizona; and Tamara in Aberdeen.  They have one
granddaughter, and one grandson.

Gordon was born March 27, 1929, received a B.S. degree from Northern State
College and a M.A. degree from the College of St. Thomas, St. Paul, Minnesota.
He married Neoma Bertsch.  Gordon is employed as a teacher in the St. Paul
school system.  They have two sons, Murray and Brooks. They have one
granddaughter.

Lora was born April 13, 1933, and attended Northern State College.  She married
Raymond Holzworth and they reside in Edina, Minnesota.  They have three
children, Liisa Langmade, Kenton, and Joanna, all living in the Twin Cities.
They have one granddaughter.

GORDON and NEOMA HERRBOLDT

Gordon Herrboldt, son of Edwin J. and Olga Herrboldt, was born on March 27,
1929, in Hosmer, South Dakota.  His youth and formative years were spent in the
Hosmer area.  He worked part-time for the G. J. Walth and Co., A. Herrboldt and
Co., and/or various farmers during the fall harvest.  Gordon graduated from
Hosmer High School in the spring of 1947.

The years from 1947 to 1955 were a potpourri of events and locations.  From
1947 to 1950 Gordon attended Northern State College, Aberdeen.  In the fall of
1950 he was hired by the Hosmer school system and taught in the upper elementary
grades.  In the summer of 1951 he was drafted by the United States Government
and was a member of the U.S. Army for two years, serving fifteen months in
Korea.  He was discharged in 1953 with an honorable discharge.  In the fall of
1953 Gordon was again hired by the Hosmer school system. After teaching for one
year he returned to Northern State College to complete his degree requirements.
He received his Bachelor of Science degree from Northern in the spring of 1955.

Since 1955, Gordon has made education his chosen profession and has been
employed by various school systems throughout the upper Midwest.  These school
systems have been: Bryant, South Dakota; Sioux Falls, South Dakota; and
Bloomington, Minnesota.  In 1960 he was hired by the St. Paul, Minnesota school
system as a secondary teacher.  He is currently employed by this school system.
In 1973 Gordon received his Master of Arts degree from the College of St.
Thomas, St. Paul, Minnesota.  Since 1960 he has been living in Richfield,
Minnesota, a suburb of Minneapolis.

In May of 1956, Gordon was joined in marriage to Neoma Bertsch, Ashley, North
Dakota.  This marriage was blessed with two children: Murray, employed by the
Minnesota State Highway Patrol; and Brooks, living in Richfield, Minnesota.

MILTON and JOYCE HERRBOLDT

Milton and Joyce were both born in Hosmer.  Milton was born in 1934 to Emma
(Streeb) and Arthur J. Herrboldt and has one brother and four sisters.  Joyce
was born in 1940 to Alita and August Heyne, has four sisters.  Joyce had a twin
sister, Janice, who died in infancy.

Milton grew up in Hosmer attending both grade and high school there.  He served
in the armed forces for two years, after which he attended Dunwoody Industrial
Institute in Minneapolis, Minnesota.  Returning to Hosmer he was employed at A.
Herrboldt & Co., later becoming a partner.  In 1984 he sold his share and
purchased shares in Gab Motors Inc. at Eureka, South Dakota.

Joyce was raised on the farm, attended country school and then graduated from
Hosmer High School.  She attended Northern State College for one year finding
employment at Lutheran Brotherhood Life Ins.  Co. in Minneapolis, Minnesota.
Marriage to Milton in 1961 brought her back to Hosmer where they are still
residing.

Four sons were born to Milton and Joyce.  Nathan, born in 1963, is attending
the University of Minnesota; Kevin, May 5, 1964-May 6, 1964; David, born in
1967, is a graduate of Area Vo-Tech of Watertown; and Jonathan was born in 1977
is now a student at Hosmer elementary.

In 1965 they purchased the lot and house that Milton's grandparents had owned
completely dismantling this home and built a new home using the lumber they had
salvaged from the old house.  Joyce's father, August Heyne, was the main
carpenter.  They moved into their new home in 1966.

Milton and Joyce are both active in the community. Milton has served as fire
chief since 1971 and has been on the Rural Fire Protection District Board since
it was organized.

Joyce is a receptionist for the dentist that comes to Hosmer one day a week, is
the news correspondent for the local paper, has a weekly taped radio spot over
KOLY, and does cake decorating.  The family is active members of St. John's
Lutheran Church.

JOHN and LYDIA HETTICH

At the turn of the century, John came to South Dakota by way of steamer from
Russia to Ellis Island, New York, and by rail to Roscoe at the age of 10.
Enroute to South Dakota they stopped in Illinois where the family spent several
days visiting friends, and nearly settled, before moving on to Roscoe and
relatives.

There John met Lydia Kiesz, courted her, and soon afterwards they married.
They were married in the Lutheran Church located near Lydia's family farm,
Gottlieb Kiesz's east of Hosmer.  Their first home was on a farm south of
Roscoe.  It was there, on a January day in 1928, Oliver was born.  There is
where they lived several years before moving to Hosmer where Geraldine was born
in March 1930.

John and his brother Fred had the drayline and in addition, John was a rural
route mailman.  Apparently, still trying to find his niche, John and Lydia
moved to Bowdle in 1932 where he and Lydia's brother, Ed Kiesz, managed the
butcher shop.  In 1934 the family moved to a farm southwest of Roscoe to take
on another farm, drought and dust.  This farm was too small, so on to a bigger
farm closer to Roscoe in 1937.  The family moved to the prospering city of
Hosmer in 1944.

Grandfather Kiesz was retiring from the house moving business and offered it to
John and brother-in-law, Ed Kiesz. They were very busy and during summer
vacation Oliver was allowed to work with them.  After Oliver graduated from
Hosmer High School in 1946, he enlisted in the Navy.  On a sunny day in
November 1946, Dennis was born.  Early in 1948 Ed Kiesz and family moved to
Aberdeen.  John continued the house moving, but as the need for this work
declined, he decided in 1952 to accept the city of Hosmer marshall job.

In 1954 John and Lydia opened the Hosmer butcher shop with Dennis being the
helper.  They were very busy making sausage, keeping the smokehouse stoked,
grinding meat, etc.  The butcher shop was sold to Heier & Sons and Lydia and
John opened the restaurant and tavern located next to Stoecker's Jewelry store.
Hosmer was to celebrate their 75th anniversary which kept Lydia and John busy
managing this new business and preparing for the celebration.  During this busy
and happy time other exciting events were taking place. Geraldine married
Walter Knittel.  Oliver married Regina Krumm, Dennis married Linda McCabe.

John passed away in 1967 and Lydia in 1970.

Oliver attended Northern State College after returning from the Navy in 1946.
He married Regina Krumm in 1954. Oliver has been employed with the U.S.
Geological Survey for 33 years retiring in December 1985.  Oliver and Regina
are the parents of three children: Paula, John, and Michael.  They reside in
Tacoma, Washington.

Geraldine married Walter Knittel in 1948 and they presently live in Pitts,
California.  They are the parents of Russell, Lyle, Ron, and Bryan.

Dennis married Linda McCabe of Edgemont, South Dakota, in July 1966.  In
September 1975, Dennis developed kidney failure, was on dialysis until 1980
when he received a kidney transplant.  They live in Rapid City, South Dakota,
where Dennis is employed by Peterson Sign Art and Linda is employed by Norwest
Bank.  They have one son, Dennis Gary Jr.

JULIUS and IDA HEUPEL

Julius E. Heupel, son of Jacob and Christina (Loebs) Heupel, was born October
27, 1915.  His wife Ida Rohrbach, daughter of George and Christina (Wolff)
Rohrbach, was born November 16, 1920.  They were married on January 3 1, 1939.

They farmed five miles south of Long Lake for a few years, moving into Long
Lake in 1950 where Mr. Heupel operated a blacksmith shop.  In 1953 they moved
to Hosmer where Mr. Heupel again operated a blacksmith shop for Schlaht and
Job.  In 1957 they moved to Aberdeen, South Dakota, where they retired.  Julius
passed away February 13, 1986.

Julius and Ida are the parents of four children: Harlan, born in 1939, lives in
Groton, South Dakota; Betty Krueger, born in 1943, lives in Grattinger, Iowa;
Kathleen Lappegard, born in 1946, lives in Estherville, Iowa; and Ted, born in
1951, lives in Aberdeen, South Dakota.

HARVEY and BETTY (GRISMER) HEYD

Harvey H. Heyd was born April 7, 1938 at Long Lake, was married to Elizabeth
Ann (Betty) Grismer at St. Augustine's Catholic Church by Rev.  Father G. A.
Althoff on September 3, 1966, at Bowdle.  She was born November 12, 1946.
Harvey was the son of Jacob and Emma (Leicht) Heyd of Long Lake.  Jacob Heyd
died on April 7, 1984 and Emma Leicht Heyd died on December 30, 1956.  Elizabeth
Ann (Betty) was the daughter of Frank B. and Magdelena (Fix) Grismer.  Frank
died July 11, 1975 and Magdelena died May 17, 1979.  The live on a farm
northeast of Long Lake.

They were blessed with four children.

Roger Dean was born August 1, 1965 at Aberdeen, he married Karla Justice of
Chamberlain on June 1, 1985.  They live at Philip.

David Doran was born on May 26, 1967 at Aberdeen.

Malissa Ann was born on May 1, 1972 at Bowdle.

Leon Harvey was born on December 8, 1976 at Ipswich.

AUGUST and ALITA HEYNE

August was the youngest son in a family of II born to pioneer homesteaders,
George and Christina (nee Dockter) Heyne, who immigrated from Neudorf, Russia.
The Heyne family homesteaded two miles east and one-half north of Hosmer in the
spring of 1894 where August was born on March 4, 1899.  This homestead became
the family farm. George Heyne was a charter member of the Evangelical Lutheran
Church, now St. John's Lutheran, which was organized in 1895.  He also served
as church treasurer for many years.  George and Christina retired from farming
in 1921 moving into Hosmer, with August taking over the farm. In the fall of
1922, on a hot, dry, windy day, a prairie fire coming from the southwest,
almost destroyed the farm and all the hay, but hardworking fire fighting men
managed to control it.  But the wind was too strong, it escaped and burned a
barn and hay on the Christ Jung farm about two miles north of the Heyne farm.

August Heyne married Alita Grischowsky on April 8, 1923.  Alita was born
December 22, 1904, in the Crimea of South Russia.  She came to America with her
parents in 1909, who settled in the Roscoe area, coming to Hosmer in 1914.

August and Alita continued farming the Heyne homestead until 1962 when they
moved to Hosmer.  August kept himself busy with carpentry and some plumbing
work.  Alita was busy being a homemaker and making quilts as a hobby. Six
daughters were born to this union: Selma (Mrs.  E. F. Mohr) of Hosmer; Leonora
(Mrs.  Roy Preszler) of Aberdeen, South Dakota; Verna (Mrs.  Ed Freemole) of
Muscatine, Iowa; Betty (Mrs.  Ben Loebs), Amherst, South Dakota; Joyce (Mrs.
Milton Herrboldt), Hosmer; and Janice (died in infancy).  Joyce and Janice were
twins.

One educational significancy is that the five daughters all attended the same
country school in Sangamon Township as their father did.

DENNIS and JOYCE HEYNE

Dennis Heyne was born May 31, 1952, to Edward and Ida (Preszler) Heyne.  He
attended Hosmer school and graduated in 1970.  He served in the U.S. Army from
1972 to 1974 being stationed in Anchorage, Alaska.  On June 21, 1975, Dennis
and Joyce (Kirschenmann) were married at the U.C.C. Church in Hosmer.  Joyce
was born November 2, 1956, to Alvin and Adina (Schaible) Kirschenmann.  She
graduated from Hosmer High School in 1975.

Dennis worked for the Edmunds County Highway Department and the N. C. Rolfsen
Construction Co. for a short time.  In 1975 he began farming with his
father-in-law, Alvin Kirschenmann.  Dennis is presently Sangamon Township clerk
and has served on the Lutheran Church board.

Joyce worked for Doug's Fairway in Hosmer from 1976-1979.  She is a member of
the ALCW and has held offices in the various circles.

They are parents of two children: Ryan Scott was born July 15, 1981; and
Michelle Marie was born November 19, 1985.

Dennis and Joyce live on the Kirschenmann family farm 2 1/2 miles southeast of
Hosmer.

JAMES and ROSELLA HEYNE

James Heyne was born to Edward and Ida Preszler Heyne on March 20, 1941.  He
attended rural school during his grade school years and graduated from Hosmer
High School in 1959.  Jim's paternal great-grandparents, George (1857-1937) and
Christina Dockter Heyne (1857-1937), settled two miles east and one-half mile
north of Hosmer in 1894. They had 11 children, one of whom was Gottlieb.  Jim's
paternal grandparents, Gottlieb (1886-1945) and Juliana Wiederrich Heyne
(1889-1964), raised five children in the Hosmer area.  In 1913 Edward was born
and he married Ida Preszler in 1937.  Ida was born in 1914 to John (1877-1957)
and Louisa Handel Preszler (1880-1974).  In 1941 James was born and is the
eldest of three children.  James' father, Edward, passed away on January 23,
1985.

Rosella Malsam was born to Anton and Catherina Zacher Malsam on October 11,
1948.  She graduated from Hosmer High School in 1966 and attended Aberdeen
School of Commerce for one year and received a secretarial degree. Rosella's
paternal grandparents, George (1887-1960) and Katherina Glatt Malsam
(1890-1959), settled near the Hosmer area in the late 1800s.  One of their
children was Anton, born in 1916.  He was one of eight children.  In 1939 Anton
married Catherina Zacher, born in 1918 to Steven (1894-1971) and Ludwiga Geffre
Zacher (1891-1931).  Anton and Catherina raised 10 children on a farm near
Hosmer, South Dakota-Rosella being the sixth oldest.

Jim and Rose were married on September 30, 1972 and are are blessed with three
sons: Scott James, born June 7, 1981; Greg Alan, born December 20, 1982; and
Brad Edward born August 16, 1985.  They are currently farming near Hosmer.

ARNOLD and NAOMI HIEB

Arnold Reinhold Hieb was born in McPherson County June 21, 1916, to Rudolph and
Maria Walth Hieb.  Arnold's dad was born in Neudorf, South Russia, in 1884 and
died in 1972.  Maria was born in McPherson County in 1891 to Jacob and
Katherine Eisenbeisz Walth and died in 1976.  Rudolph came to America in 1895
with his parents, Jacob W. and Carolina Helm Hieb.  Rudolph's dad homesteaded
the NE 1/4 , Section 3, Township 125, Range 71.  When Rudolph was of age, he
went west and homesteaded the SW 1/4 , Section 2, Township 15, Range 12 in
North Perkins County, South Dakota, but later came back to McPherson County to
farm. He built his farm on the NW 1/4, Section 2, Township 125, Range 71.  It
was on this farm that Arnold grew up, attended rural school and helped his
parents farm until he married Naomi Ulmer.

Naomi Leona Ulmer was born January 2, 1920, to Gustave and Elenora Walter
Ulmer.  Gustave was born at Menno, South Dakota, in 1890 and died in 1962.
Elenora was born in Bloechem, Austria, in 1896 and lived at the Senior
Citizen's Home in Hosmer until her death in January, 1986.

Naomi grew up on the farm with her parents, attended school and helped her
parents farm until her marriage to Arnold on November 5, 1939.  They then moved
to a farm in Hillsview Township, Section 13-125-72 and reside there today.

Arnold and Naomi enjoyed arming.  There were never two years alike.  They had
good crops and some that were not so good, but there never was a crop failure.
Many changes have taken place in the 45 years they farmed.  Some of those
changes include the price of an acre of land selling for $8.00 an acre to
$400.00 an acre; 80 cents per bushel wheat to $9.00 per bushel; cows 8 cents
per lb. to 55 cents per lb.; 400 lb. calf 15 cents per lb. to $1.00 per lb.;
from horsepower to diesel power; header and box harvesting to air-conditioned
combines; baking bread with cowchips to heat the oven to microwave cooking.
All in all the good Lord blessed them and they are very thankful for good
health and five wonderful children.

Their children are: Stanley Hieb, who was born June 19, 1940.  He graduated
from Hosmer High and married Joyce Saylor.  They have two children, Stacy and
Gregg and make their home in Eureka, South Dakota.

Phyllis was born October 29, 1941, graduated from Aberdeen School of Commerce
and married Larry Sanderson. She has two children, Kevin and Susanne and they
live in Kansas City, Kansas.

Patricia was born August 31, 1943, graduated from Northern State College and
married Dennis Hollan.  They have one son, Robert John, and live in Hosmer.

Kenneth and Karen, the twins in the family, joined the group January 20, 1947.
Kenny graduated from Hosmer High and married Frances Krumm.  They have three
children: Gina, Kelly and Darin.  They live on the farm with Arnold and Naomi.
Karen graduated from Aberdeen School of Commerce and married Keith Ballweg.
They have one daughter, Lisa, and live in Belvidere, Illinois.

CHRIST and PAULINE HIEB

Christ Hieb was born November 10, 1905 in McPherson County to Michael and
Rosina (Borkirchert) Hieb Jr. As a youth, Christ worked for the railroad
company in Montana, during the time it was being built to the West Coast.  On
November 23, 1928, he married Pauline Martel.  Pauline was born April 18, 1911
in McPherson County to Frederick and Elizabeth (Henne) Martel.  Christ and
Pauline made their home on a farm in McPherson County until 1948, when they
moved to Aberdeen.  While living in Aberdeen, Christ was employed at the Nash
Finch Company and Pauline was employed at the Dixie Cream Donut Shop.  In 1954,
they moved to a farm two and one-half miles east of Hosmer and are now
semi-retired there.

Christ and Pauline have three children.  Lucille was born December 26, 1929.
She married Peter Fischer from Timber Lake.  They are now living in Neenah,
Wisconsin.  They have six children: Elizabeth, Julie Ann, Anthony, Patrick,
Christine, and Florence.  Delores Weber was born May 3, 1934.  She is living in
Sioux Falls, South Dakota.  She has six children: Vinson, Michael, Mirian,
Duane, Richard, and David.  Willard (in another history in this book).

ELMER and ELLEN HIEB

Elmer Hieb was born February 14, 1913 to John and Emma Gross Hieb.  He grew up
on the family farm east of Hosmer with his three brothers and his mother, who
was widowed in 1916.  In 1920, Emma remarried and the family moved into the
city of Hosmer.  His stepfather, William Meyer, managed the Heckenlaible-Meier
Lumber Co. in Hosmer.

Elmer attended grade and high school in Hosmer, graduating in 1931.  He farmed
during the depression for three years before attending Northern State Teachers
College for one year and earned a degree to teach in the rural schools. He paid
for his room and board by delivering laundry on foot for his Aunt Tina Maier in
Aberdeen.  Elmer then taught in the McPherson County schools northeast of
Hosmer, staying at the Albert Gross farm, which is now the Arnold Lunders'
farmsite.  His starting salary was $45.00 per month.  In 1937 Elmer became
manager of Heckenlaible-Meier Lumber Co. per his stepfather's request shortly
before his death.

Elmer married Ellen Reisdorph of Houghton, South Dakota on July 23, 1940.
Ellen was raised in Houghton, the oldest daughter of Charles and Effie Marlow
Reisdorph.  Mr. Reisdorph was the International Harvester dealer in Houghton
and was involved in other businesses as well as farming. Ellen graduated from
Hecla High School in 1932.  The following two years she was employed as a
housemaid for various families in Houghton and Aberdeen areas, Ellen attended
college at Northern State College in Aberdeen, South Dakota where she met Elmer.

To this couple were born five sons: Verl in 1941, Lynn in 1943, Lee in 1944,
Neil in 1949, and Jere in 1955.  All the boys graduated from Hosmer High
School, an education being a priority in the Hieb household.  All five boys
furthered their education at various colleges.

Verl graduated from Northern State College with a teacher's certificate and
married Carolyn Holsing of Ipswich, South Dakota.  He has been a teacher at
Ipswich and, most recently, at Brookings, South Dakota.  They have five
children. Verl has received awards for his excellence in teaching in the social
studies field and has traveled to seminars and also to India for firsthand
insight into social and historical information.  Verl was selected as teacher
of year in the Brookings school district in 1982.

Lynn attended Northern State College to obtain a teacher's certificate also,
and has taught at various schools in North and South Dakota.  Lynn also was
employed for a time in Alaska during construction of the Trans-Alaska Oil
Pipeline. Since completion of the pipeline he returned to teaching, the most
recently at Perryville, Alaska.  Lynn is also a Vietnam war veteran.

Lee attended Le Tourneau College at Longview, Texas after high school,
obtaining an engineering degree.  Lee was employed at Texas Instruments Corp.
and Tracor, Inc. before entering business management, specifically in the
automotive sales field.  Lee is currently a used car sales manager at Eagle-
Lincoln-Mercury in Dallas, Texas.  While attending college, Lee met and married
Karen Henderson from Longview, Texas.  They have three children and one
grandchild.

Niel attended Northern State College, Aberdeen, and Control Data Institute in
Minneapolis, Minnesota after graduation from high school.  Niel also served in
the Marine Corps, stationed at Okinawa, Japan.  After his service duty, Niel
was employed in Austin, Texas for a time before moving back to Hosmer and
starting an auto-body repair shop.  Niel was often busy during high school and
college doing this type of work on a part-time basis.  In 1973 Niel transferred
the business to Stanley Mohr and moved back to Texas, starting a van
customizing and metal fabricating business.  While at school in Minneapolis,
Niel met and later married Brenda Bastyr of Hopkins, Minnesota.  They have
three children and currently reside in Cedar Hills, Texas.  They also operate a
restaurant business in nearby Duncanville, Texas.

Jere, the youngest of the five boys, currently resides in Hosmer with his wife,
Dee, and their two daughters. (See Jere and Dee Hieb History.)

Somehow, Ellen Hieb managed to find the time to be a homemaker, organist at
Hosmer's Reformed Church, and still teach school.  Except for a few years, when
she stayed home to raise the boys, when they were young, she continued to
teach--mainly Jr. high school English and literature.  She served several years
at Roscoe Public School and most recently before retiring at the Aberdeen,
South Dakota school system.  In 1976 Elmer and Ellen built a new house in Hosmer
and began a so-called "retirement pace." Elmer and Ellen, when they relax,
enjoy traveling, visiting relatives and family, reading, and gardening.  Ellen
especially keeps her retirement years busy at craft and hobby works, sewing,
playing piano, cooking, and serving on the Senior Citizen's Home Auxiliary.
Some of Elmer's activities and offices served have been: past president of the
Hosmer Commercial Club, member of the Hosmer school board, the Edmunds County
school board, Hosmer city mayor, and an elder in the Hosmer Reformed Church for
39 years.  Elmer still spends some time at the lumberyard in between gardening.
He will celebrate 50 years as owner of Hosmer Lumber Co. in 1987.

Elmer and Ellen have enjoyed their years in Hosmer and wish to thank their
family and many friends for all the good memories of years gone by.

FERDINAND and CHRISTINA HIEB

Ferdinand Hieb was born March 29, 1918 to Frederick and Christina Job Hieb on a
farm northeast of Hosmer, South Dakota.  He attended country school and farmed
with his parents.

On August 31, 1938, Ferdinand was married to Christina Huber and lived on the
home place.

Christina Huber was born December 17, 1919 to Gottlieb and Christina Dockter
Huber at Bentley, North Dakota.  A few years later the family moved to a farm
in the Hosmer area.

In 1940, Ferdinand and Christina moved to Eureka, South Dakota, where Ferdinand
helped build the Wolff Dam. In 1946, they moved to Java, South Dakota, where
Ferdinand worked in the Pfitzer Garage as a mechanic.

In 1948, Ferdinand tried farming again, but due to health reasons had an
auction sale and moved to Hosmer, South Dakota, into the former Esther Maisch
house in 1956.  In the spring of 1957, Ferdinand and Christine purchased the
former Red & White Store, started remodeling for an insurance office and living
quarters, and moved in by May of 1958.

The Hiebs operated a Radio, T.V. Service, including electrical supplies, paint
shop, plus insurance agency.  Ferdinand also did electrical work for 35 years.

In 1972, they sold the building to their son Merle and purchased Katie Hieb's
trailer home where they are presently residing.

Ferdy sells insurance, but most of all looks forward to the spring and summer
so he can go out and help Merle with farm work.  He is a music lover, enjoys
playing a variety of instruments, especially the accordion, guitar, and drums.
Tina enjoys her scrapbooks in the winter, and garden and flowers in the summer.

Four children were born to Ferdinand and Tina: Merle, born June 10, 1940, is
now living in Eureka, South Dakota.

Ronald, born June 23, 1943, is now living on a farm northeast of Hosmer, South
Dakota.

Orville, born July 26, 1948, is now living in Coon Rapids, Minnesota, and is
married to Sherida Pietz.  They have three children: Nathan, Rebecca, and
Andrew.  Orville received a B.S. degree in medical technology from Northern
State College in Aberdeen, South Dakota, and a Master of Divinity from Bethel
Theological Seminary in St. Paul, Minnesota.  He is presently working as a
medical technologist at Mercy Medical Center in Coon Rapids, Minnesota.

Rhonda, born June 1, 1955, is now living on a farm southeast of Bowdle, South
Dakota, and married to Ronald Strouckel.  They have two children: Jesse Anton
and Lindsey Renee.  Rhonda received a B.A. in music from South Dakota State
University in Brookings, South Dakota.  She taught music in the Cresbard, South
Dakota school system for one year, and in the Bowdle, South Dakota school
system for two years.  She has ended her teaching career and is staying at home
to care for her family.

FRED J. and CHRISTINA HIEB

Fred J. Hieb was born to Jacob and Elizabeth (Renschler) Hieb on August 18,
1893.  On March 27, 1919, he married Christina Werre at Aberdeen, South Dakota.
She is the daughter of John and Christina (Hottman) Werre.  This union was
blessed with four children.

Fred and Christina farmed for one year northwest of Leola after which they
moved to the August Spitzer farm eighteen miles northeast of Hosmer where they
lived for two years.  Herbert and Erwin were born on this farm.  They then
moved to Grandfather Jacob Hieb's farm where Alfred and LaVerna were born.
Alfred died in infancy.

Herbert was born October 1, 1920.  In 1939 he attended the Hanson Auto, Diesel
and Welding School in Fargo, North Dakota.  On May 11, 1940, he married Irene
Mohr.  They lived on the farm until June 2, 1957, when they moved to Hosmer. On
June 2, 1959, they moved to Roscoe where Herbert went into business with his
brother, Erwin.  This business was called Hieb's Repair.  Herbert and Irene had
two daughters, Caroline and Phyllis.  Herbert passed away March 16, 1975.

Erwin was born October 17, 1922.  He graduated from Roscoe High School in 1940
and attended Northern State College, Aberdeen, South Dakota.  He taught rural
schools until 1945 when he was drafted into the army spending some time in
Frankfurt, Germany.  On June 22, 1947, he married Alice Krebs.  They farmed,
owned the Hosmer Pool Hall for a short time, and moved to Roscoe in 1957 where
they were in the implement business for 26 1/2 years retiring October 1, 1985.
They are the parents of three children: Marjorie; Linda, who died in infancy;
and John.

LaVerne was born December 2, 1929.  She attended school in the Hosmer area and
the New York Hairdressing Academy in Fargo, North Dakota.  She married Lawrence
Hettich on October 2, 1949.  Lawrence started a Barber and Beauty Shop in
Roscoe which they operated until 1953 when they moved to a farm 11 miles north
of Bowdle and continue to live there today.  They have three children: LaDean,
LaNette, and LaVon.

HERBERT and PHYLLIS HIEB

Herbert Hieb was born to Rudolph and Maria Hieb on March 15, 1922.  He grew up
on the farm 11 miles northeast of Hosmer.  Herbert attended country school, and
after graduating from the 8th grade stayed at home and helped with the farming.
When the work was caught up at home, he would work for others.  In the spring
of 1947, he moved to the farm four miles south of Hosmer where he and Phyllis
are now living. He married Phyllis Schmidt in November of 1947.

Phyllis is the daughter of Ludwig and Clara Schmidt. Her dad died in 1966 and
her mother lives in the Senior Citizen's Home in Hosmer.  Phyllis grew up in
Hosmer and graduated from Hosmer High School.  She taught school for two years,
but after all the snow in 1947 she decided to stay home and be a homemaker.

In 1959 Herbert and Phyllis built a new house northeast of their original
house.  After that the farm took on a new look because all new buildings were
built closer to the road.

They were blessed with four daughters.  Joy, Mrs. Darrel Huft, lives in
Billings, Montana.  They have two children, Matthew and Cara.  Joy teaches
part-time in a preschool and Darrel is manager of a Safelite Auto Glass Store.
Londa, Mrs. Curt Dahlke, lives in Littleton, Colorado.  They have two children,
Paul and Sheena.  Londa is a medical technologist and works part-time in the
University Hospital in Denver and Curt is an employee specialist for U.S.
Wildlife.  Connie is living in Aberdeen and is an R.N. at Dakota Midland
Hospital. Vickie, Mrs. Mark Koch, lives in Sioux Falls.  Vickie is a

manager.

MR. and MRS. JACOB HIEB

Jacob Hieb was born in Russia in 1858 and died in 1928. Carolina (Helm) Hieb
was born in Russia in 1859 and died in 1944.

They came from Neudorf, South Russia in 1895 and settled on a farm northeast of
Hosmer.  Jacob Hieb lived there until his death.  Carolina moved into Hosmer,
built a new home and resided there until her death.  There were seven children
born to this union: Fred, Mrs. Jacob (Magdalina) Vilhauer, Mrs. Fred (Carolina)
Meidinger, Mrs. Fred (Katherina) Buechler, Rudolph, Mrs. John (Lydia) Walth,
and Mrs. Henry (Maria) Mehlhoff.  They have all passed away.

JERE and DEE HIEB

Jere Hieb, the youngest of five sons of Elmer and Ellen Hieb, was born August
1, 1955 at the Ipswich Hospital.  Jere grew up and attended grade and high
school in Hosmer, graduating from Hosmer High School in May 1973.  He also

Jere returned to Hosmer and worked for Jake Mohr & Sons Construction Co. for a

In March of 1982, Jere met Dee Ann Clarambeau of Letcher, South Dakota and they

Jere and Dee are both active in community affairs.  Both are members of Hosmer

Jere and Dee enjoy outdoor activities such as boating, hunting, fishing, bike

JOHN and EMMA HIEB

John Hieb was born in 1883 to Michael and Rosina Borkirchert Hieb in Bon Homme
County, Dakota Territory. In 1884, he and his parents moved to a new homestead
15 miles northeast of Hosmer where he grew to manhood and eventually
homesteaded his own farm east of Hosmer.

John married Emma Gross on October 28, 1907.  Emma was the daughter of Jacob
and Elizabeth Wruck Gross and was born in 1886 in Scotland.  John and Emma were
blessed with five children: Elliard, born in 1908; Ella, born in 1912, but who
died as an infant; Elmer, born in 1913; Raymond, born in 1914; and Helmuth,
born in 1915.  John died from tuberculosis in 1916 at the young age of 34
years, leaving his wife and four sons.  Emma remained on the farm with the boys
until marrying William (Bill) Meyer in August 1920.  Bill had two sons, Howard
and Bill Jr., both deceased; and a daughter, Evelyn, still living in New
Mexico.  Upon manhood, Elliard and Helmuth went to Oregon and started a lumber
business.  Elliard passed away in 1971.  Hal continued to live in Portland,
Oregon.  Raymond became a lawyer and also served in the state legislature.  He
resided with his wife, Alice, and family in Ipswich, South Dakota.  Ray passed
away in 1973. Elmer remained in Hosmer and has run the Hosmer Lumber Company
since 1937. (See Elmer and Ellen Hieb History.) William Meyer passed away in
1937 but before his death asked Elmer Hieb, his stepson, to take over
managership.  Emma passed away in 1953.

residing in Aberdeen, South Dakota.

MICHAEL and ROSINA HIEB

One of the first families to homestead in the trade area of Hosmer was that of
Michael Hieb.  He immigrated to America from South Russia in 1874.  Michael's
parents took up a homestead in Bon Homme County, Dakota Territory about that
time.  In 1881 Michael married Rosina Borkirchert, a woman whom he'd met as a
girl on the voyage to America.

In 1884, the Michael Hieb family, including a young son, John, and other
families somewhat related, all disembarked from a train at Ipswich, South
Dakota and proceeded to the general area-about 15 miles northeast of
Hosmer-where homesteads were filed by all.  The reason so many people were
settling in this part of the Dakota Territory was because the land in the south
and east had pretty well been claimed by 1885 and new ground was needed for the
expanding population.

Michael and Rosina Hieb were blessed with 11 children. They were: Magdalena
(Lehr), Gottlieb, Rosina (Neifer), Katherine (Gross), Josephine (Walth), David,
Christina (Adam), Theobald, John, Ida (Meidinger), and Christ.  Those still
surviving include Christ, who farms two miles east of Hosmer, and Ida, who
lives in Aberdeen, South Dakota.

As in common with their neighbors, the Michael Hieb family endured the rigors
of extreme cold, heat, drought, crop failures, and the deaths of family
members.  Mrs. Hieb was known as a kind and generous neighbor, a noted
horticulturist and a midwife who attended at the births of hundreds of area
residents.  This couple was blessed by God with an instinct for survival and a
kindness which they handed on to their children.  They were a fine example of
America's settling pioneers.

OTTO and VIRGINIA HIEB

Otto Hieb, was the tenth child born to Rudolph and Maria (Walth) Hieb July 26,
1926 in McPherson County.  He attended rural grade school in Cleveland
Township.  On January 17, 1945 he was called to serve his country during World
War II.  He received his basic training at Ft.  Sill, Oklahoma, where he was
assigned to the 152nd Field Artillery Battery as Battery Recorder.  On July 26,
1945 he was sent overseas serving in the Philippines and Japan.

On November 27, 1952 he was united in marriage to Virginia Schick, daughter of
John and Martha (Oster) Schick. Otto and Virginia began farming the family
homestead and are still actively engaged in farming.

They have two children.  Terry is married to Debbie Stanley of Clark, South
Dakota.  They are farming a partnership with his parents on the family farm.

Dixie is married to John Haas, son of Clarence and Mary Lou Haas, and they
reside in Eureka.

RONALD and CAROL HIEB

Ronald Hieb was born on June 23, 1943, at Eureka, South Dakota, to Ferdinand
and Christine (Huber) Hieb.  He lived with his parents on the farm northeast of
Hosmer, South Dakota, until 1956 when he moved with his parents into Hosmer.
He attended grade and high school at Hosmer, graduating in 1961.  After
graduation he enlisted in the U.S. Air Force for four years.  After his service
in the Air Force he attended the Watertown Vocational School for two years,
specializing in radio and TV electronics.

In 1966 he was united in marriage to Carol Krull, daughter of Enno and Verna
Krull of Hollandale, Minnesota. Carol was born April 2, 1946, at Corsica, South

brothers.

In 1967 Ron worked for Boeing Aircraft at Grand Forks, North Dakota.  In late
1967, Ron and Carol moved to Hosmer, South Dakota, where Ron was hired as the
manager of the Farmers Union Co-op, and Carol became the bookkeeper. Ron worked
for the Co-op for nine years.  He then was employed with the State Highway
Department for two years and also worked for his brother Merle in the farming
business.

They have two children: Troy A., who was born February 21, 1967, and is

The family is presently residing on the old Helmuth Vilhauer farm northeast of
Hosmer, about a half-mile from the homestead of Ron's Grandfather Hieb.

RUDOLPH HIEB HISTORY

Rudolph Hieb was born December 29, 1884, at Neudorph, South Russia, to Jacob W.
and Caroline (Helm) Hieb.  At the age of nine he came to America with his
parents. His father homesteaded on the NE 1/4 , Sec. 3, Twp. 125, Range 71.
Rudolph farmed with his dad until he was of age to homestead for himself.
Since there was no more land left to homestead east of the Missouri River, he
had to go west of the river.  He left on horseback and headed west to homestead
the SW 1/4, Sec. 2, Twp. 15, Range 12 in Perkins County, South Dakota.  After
his claim was approved he came back to McPherson County and on January 21,
1913, married Maria Walth, daughter of Jacob and Katherine (Eisenbeisz) Walth.
They built their farm on the NW 1/4 , Sec. 2, Twp. 125, Range 71 and there they
farmed until their retirement in 1952 when they moved to Hosmer, South Dakota.
In June of 1965 Rudolph had a stroke which left his left side paralyzed.  From
that time on he spent considerable time in the hospital.  The last six years he
was a resident of the Senior Citizen's Home at Hosmer.  On February 2, 1972, he
died.  Maria spent the last four years of her life in the Senior Citizen's Home
at Hosmer and died June 28, 1976.

Rudolph and Maria were blessed with 13 children who are still living and well.
They are: Mrs. William Althea (Hieb) Buechler Weishaar, Aberdeen; Mrs. Gerald
Alma (Hieb) Bratton, Ojai, California; Arnold Hieb, Hosmer; Mrs. Leontena
(Hieb) Morlock, Ellendale, North Dakota; Harold Hieb, Aberdeen; Herbert Hieb,
Hosmer; Mrs. Randy Erna (Hieb), Huber, Roscoe; Otto Hieb, Hosmer; Robert Hieb,
Roscoe; Mrs. Jules Helen (Hieb) Satmary, Brea, California; and Harry Hieb,
Caputa, South Dakota.

DENNIS and PATRICIA HOLLAN

Dennis Dwight Hollan was born on November 25, 1942, at Kulm, North Dakota, to
Lee Robert and Alice Fregein Hollan.  Dennis spent his childhood on a farm
seven miles south of Kulm and attended country school where his mother was his
teacher until fifth grade.  He then attended Kulm Public School and graduated
in 1960.  Dennis attended Northern State Teachers College majoring in business
and music. While at Northern he was active in Phi Mu Sinfonia, a member of the
Al Hess Dance Band, Northern State Marching Band, chorus and orchestra.  In
1963 he became engaged to Patricia Hieb and was married June 7, 1964.

Patricia Naomi Hieb was born at Bowdle, South Dakota, on August 31, 1943, to
Arnold Reinhold and Naomi Ulmer Hieb.  She spent her first 17 years on her
parents' farm 6 miles north of Hosmer.  She attended grade school at Hillsview
#2 rural school.  In 1955 when Hillsview #2 closed she attended Hosmer
Elementary and in 1961 graduated from Hosmer High School.  She attended
Northern State Teachers College and graduated in 1963 with a two-year teaching
certificate. Her first teaching position was teaching third grade at Hosmer.
She had 36 students in her first class and earned a salary of $3300.  On June
7, 1964, she married Dennis Hollan and they moved to Aberdeen where Patty
taught at Howard-Hedger Elementary and Dennis attended Northern part-time and
taught band at Pierpont.  Dennis graduated from Northern in 1965 and was music
instructor at Columbia the following year.  In 1966 Dennis was offered the
music position at Hosmer High and Patty was offered the 6th grade teaching
position.  They moved to Hosmer where they taught in the school system until
1971 at which time Dennis was offered a position with Farmer's State Bank.

In 1968 Patty graduated from Northern State College with a Bachelor of Science
degree in elementary education.

On December 20, 1969 their family was complete.  Robert John Hollan was born at
Dakota Midland Hospital in Aberdeen.

In 1974 Patty earned her Master of Science degree in elementary administration
and in 1978 she completed the Real Estate Curriculum at Northern, passed her
state real estate test and became a real estate salesperson.

In 1975 they purchased the Helmuth Mohr farm and became city farmers.  In 1978
they purchased 11 acres from the Haerter-Heckenlaible Corp. on the southeast
edge of Hosmer and in 1979 built a home there.  At one time in Hosmer's history
the 11 acres on which they built their home supported a silver fox ranch owned
by a group of Hosmer businessmen.

At present Dennis is vice president and cashier of Farmer's State Bank, Patty
is elementary principal at Hosmer Public School and Robb attends Hosmer High
School.

CHRISTIAN and LYDIA HUBER

Christian J. Huber was born September 21, 1881 in Glueckstahl, South Russia.
As a young boy he came to America with his parents.  He married Lydia Hoffman of
Menno, South Dakota, and they made their home in Hosmer.

In the early 1900s Christ opened a cream station which he operated for many
years.  In the 1920s he purchased the building (now the Senior Center) from
Jacob Schumacher, and operated Hosmer's first ice cream parlor which served
many flavors of ice cream and fountain drinks, much to the delight of the young
children in the community.  He served on the city council and St. John's
Lutheran church council.

The Hubers were the parents of 13 children.  Six sons served in the Armed
Forces in World War II.  Ray lives in Nevada City, California; Alma
Pawlawwhich, in Miller, South Dakota; Albert in Los Angeles, California; Edwin,
deceased; Rupert in Everett, Washington; Hilder in Oakland, California; Helmuth
in Miller, South Dakota; Milbert in El Monte, California; Winfred, deceased;
Emil in San Jose, California; Donald in San Mateo, California; Mervin and
Melvin, deceased.

Mr. Huber died in 1952 and Mrs. Huber in 1982.  Both are buried in the Bethany
Cemetery in Hosmer.

OSCAR and JUDY HUBER

Oscar E. and Judith E. Sheppard Huber are living on the farm homesteaded by
Jacob and Sabina Walz Huber in 1896, 3 1/2 miles west and 6 miles south of
Hosmer.  Oscar was born at Hosmer to Richard and Rosina Schlaht Huber.  Oscar
and Judy were married in 1974 and have four children: Paul Jacob, 1975; Kirk
Alan, 1977; Ryan Richard, 1981; and Rosann Inez, 1983.

Judy, the daughter of Wilbur and Inez Cooper Sheppard, was Extension Home
Economist for Brown County at the time she married Oscar.  She received her
degree from North Dakota State University.

Oscar made his home on the farm all of his life.  At the same time he taught
rural school one year and eleven years at Roscoe High School and one year in
Hosmer High School. He served 12 years in the South Dakota House of
Representatives. He graduated from Bowdle High School, received a bachelor
degree from Northern State College at Aberdeen and graduate credits from the
University of Minnesota and Northern.  Being vice president of the Edmunds
County W.E.B. domestic water project, he helped with the membership drive and
spent time promoting the project.

RICHARD and ROSINA SCHLAHT HUBER

Richard F. Huber (1890-1969) and Rosina Schlaht Huber (1890-1981) spent most of
their married life on the farm 3 1/2 miles west and six miles south of Hosmer.
They were married in 1916 and spent one year on land they homesteaded near
Trail City west of the Missouri River.  They had one son, Oscar.

In 1917 when World War I started they moved back to the farm near Hosmer.

Rosina Schlaht was the daughter of John and Barbara Eisenbeisz Schlaht.  She
was living in Hosmer at the time of their marriage.  She moved to a farm near
Hosmer with her parents in 1896.

Richard F. Huber was the son of Jacob and Sabina Walz Huber and moved from
Freeman, South Dakota to the homestead with his parents in 1896.  He was active
in township government and served on the ASCS board.

Their early farming was mostly seeding wheat and oats and milking a few cows.
In the 1920s a Case tractor and horses were used for field work.  Later a 1530
Farmall with steel lugs was used.

In 1927 a new McCormick Deering combine with a 12 foot platform was purchased
for $1500.  This made harvesting much easier.  It was the first in Edmunds
County and many people came to see it work.  The windrowing was done with
horses pushing the header.

A new model A John Deere with rubber tires was purchased in 1941 for $1165.  In
1947 a new 30 Cockshut tractor with a price tag of $1742 became a part of the
farm operation. In 1948 a 1 1/2 ton Reo truck was purchased for $3903 which
included a 16 foot box.  In 1952 a new Massey Harris four plow diesel tractor
was added for $2925.  From there on equipment prices went up faster than prices
for farm produce.

There was no plumbing in the house until 1926 when a gravity flow water line
was installed.  The pipes also went to the hog barn and to the cattle barn from
a 3000 gallon reservoir. In 1934 a sewer system was added and a water pressure
pump was installed in the house.  In 1950 R.E.A. electricity came to the farm
and the 32 volt battery system became obsolete.

With the adequate electrical power came deep freezers, refrigerators, electric
motors, electric stoves and a modern home.

FRANK M. IMBERI FAMILY

Frank and Cecelia Imberi moved to Hosmer in 1963. Frank was employed at the
Ford Motor Company, the City Liquor Store and at the school before his
retirement.

Cecelia was employed at the meat market then at the Senior Citizen's Home,
where she is currently employed.

Frank and Cecelia manage the American Legion Club.

The couple has six children: Cecelia Rivas, Austin, Texas; Robert Imberi,
Aberdeen, South Dakota; Margaret Wiederrich, Hosmer, South Dakota; Katherine
Entzel, Aberdeen, South Dakota; Thomas Imberi, Aberdeen, South Dakota; and
Teresa Schmidt, Aberdeen, South Dakota.

PETE and FRANCES IMBERY

Excerpts from an account written by Frances Imbery in 1975 Frances (Schoch)
Imbery, the youngest child of Joseph and Katherine (Benefit) Schoch, was born
on December 20, 1907.  Pete, the son of Sigfried and Barbara (Streifel) Imbery
was born December 17, 1904.

Frances and Pete were married November 8, 1926.  For the first four years of
their married life, they lived in a sod house on a farm 3 1/2 miles west of
Hosmer.  There were no tractors or modern machinery of any kind at that time,
everything was done with horses or their own power.

Frances got five cows and her furniture from home when they got married and
Pete got three horses, harnesses for two horses, one wagon with a box, a plow
and drag.  They also borrowed $200 at five percent interest from Frances' mother
and with this money bought two horses, harnesses for two horses, a drill and
other machinery.  They rented land to put in a crop.  The hay land they rented
was 3 1/2 miles from where they lived, so they had to start the day early.
First they had to go out to the pasture and get the horses and cows in.  The
horses had to be fed and harnessed, the cows milked by hand. Then the cream was
separated from the milk, the calves and chickens fed.  After this they had
breakfast and fixed a lunch to take to the field, and then were off to the
field for the whole day.  By the time they got home in the evening, it was
almost dark.  They then went through the same chores as in the morning, had a
late supper and were ready for a good night's sleep.

The first winter they were married their only income was about 40 cents a week
for one gallon of cream, but they were never cold or hungry.  During the winter
all the work was done, so all they had to do was to take care of the animals.
The worst job in the winter was hauling hay when the weather was cold or stormy.

Pete had a little one-seater Ford roaster to drive, out during the winter when
the roads were blocked, they put two horses on the sled to get groceries and to
go to church.  At that time when the roads were blocked, they were closed for
the winter as there were no snowplows to open roads.  Sometimes when there was
just big drifts, the men got together with their shovels and shoveled the roads
open.

Bad roads never stopped the people from taking in all the "namesday" parties in
the neighborhood.  They would take all the furniture out of one room of the
house, get someone to play an accordion and have "a heck of a good time."
Sometimes there were parties three nights a week, everyone had a namesday, if
they didn't they were given one!

After the first winter the family became very self sufficient. They didn't need
much money-only for a little gas and groceries.  They had their own meat and
vegetables.  In the fall, wheat was taken to a mill in Bowdle to be made into
flour.  Of course they always baked their own bread and had many meals made
from dough-knaphala, noodles, dumplings, struddles, etc.

Pete and Frances' first child, a son, Leo, was born prematurely on April 28,
1928.  He lived only four hours.  This was the only time a doctor was there for
a delivery, the other three sons were born with just a midwife present.

In 1930, Pete and Frances moved in with her mother on the Schoch farm just west
of Hosmer.  It was there that their second son, Harlan Joseph, was born on
October 27, 1931. Leslie Sigfried was also born there on June 29, 1933.

In 1933, they moved to town where Louis Peter was born on October 5, 1937.
During this time, Pete and most other men in the area worked for WPA.  After
this Pete worked for 10 cents an hour, 9 hours a day wrecking old buildings and
such.  In 1937, he did carpenter work for 15 cents an hour. During this time,
they moved many times as they would buy an old house, fix it up themselves and
then sell it.

In 1942, the family moved to Lodi, California, but only stayed a year because
Pete and the boys didn't like it out there.  They arrived back in Hosmer in
October 1943.  Pete got a job with the railroad and later worked in a grain
elevator.  In 1947, Pete became the manager of the City Liquor Store where he
remained until his retirement on January 15, 1970. After that he did some
part-time maintenance work for the city.

Frances worked in the Boldt Store, Meat Market and also as a cook in the Hosmer
School and the Senior Citizen's Home.

Frances died on September 12, 1982 and Pete now makes his home with his son,
Harlan, in Sacramento, California. Harlan and his wife, Dorothy have three
children.  Leslie lives in Chicago, Illinois and he and his wife, Lorna have
four children.  Louis and his wife, Lori also live in Chicago, Illinois and
have two children.

THE REV.  JOHN H. ISZLER

The Reverend John H. Iszler attended a rural elementary school near Streeter,
North Dakota.  After graduation from high school he attended Northern State
College graduating in 1930.  He entered the Evangelical Theological Seminary in
Naperville, Illinois and received his Bachelor of Divinity and Master of
Divinity in 1933.

On August 30, 1932 he married Ida Wentz of McClusky, North Dakota.  In 1933,
the young couple came to South Dakota where Reverend Iszler served two
churches.  In 1934 he was ordained.

Reverend Iszler taught in a country school and summer church camps.  He held
many offices in the church conference of South Dakota.

The Iszlers are the parents of two children and two grandchildren.  John
Everett and his family live in Midwest, Wyoming.  Maryalice is married to James
Hanson and lives in Clarks Grove, Minnesota.

Reverend Iszler, Mrs. Iszler and Maryalice arrived in Bowdle, South Dakota June
14, 1962 from Sioux Falls, South Dakota to serve the Evangelical United
Brethren Church and the United Church of Christ.  He held four services every
Sunday until the three denominations became one congregation.

Reverend Iszler was called to serve the United Church of Christ in Hosmer on
June 1, 1969.  The family moved to Hosmer on November 22, 1971.  After serving
over 40 years he retired December 31, 1973.

After suffering with amyotrophic schlerosis he passed away October 2, 1974.
His final resting place is in the Milbank, South Dakota Cemetery.

Mrs. Iszler lives in their home in Hosmer and remains active with her church
work.

JOHN and MARY MARGARET JACOBS

John and Mary Margaret moved to Hosmer in March of 1984 when they purchased
Doug's Fairway and changed the name to Jacobs' Fairway.

John Lewis Jacobs was born in Webster, South Dakota, on December 29, 1940, to
Russell and Iowa Jacobs.  He was raised in Webster and graduated from Webster
High School. John attended Northern State College for one year and graduated
from General Beadle (Dakota State) in Madison with a B.S. degree in secondary
education.  He moved to Wyoming where he taught school and coached.  A move to
Tulsa, Oklahoma, brought a job with Gulf Oil Corporation and more schooling to
obtain an engineering degree.  He worked with Gulf Oil for 10 years.  John then
worked for Tank Service Inc. which moved him to Houston, Texas, in 1980 where
he met Mary Margaret.

Mary Margaret Casler was born in San Diego, California on October 20, 1952, to
Wilfred "Cas" and Elizabeth "Betty" Casler.  Since her father was in the Navy,
Mary Margaret and her family moved every three to four years.  She graduated
from St. Francis High School in Honolulu, Hawaii, attended the University of
Hawaii, Pensacola Junior College, and graduated from the University of Northwest
Florida in Pensacola, Florida, with a marketing degree.  Mary Margaret went to
work for K-Mart and worked seven years as an office supervisor and auditor.
She traveled the Houston area where on June 14, 1980, she met John.

They were married September 26, 1981, and lived in Houston until their move to
Hosmer.  They have three children, Dawn married to Fred Trebit and living in
Tulsa, Oklahoma, Erin Elizabeth and John Michael living in Dayton, Wyoming.
They have one grandson, Frederick John Trebit.

CHRIST JAKOBER FAMILY

Christ Jakober was born September 12, 1879, in Glueckstal, South Russia, to
Jacob and Fredericka (Nehlich) Jakober.  He came to this country with his
parents when he was eight years old.  They settled on a farm near Eureka, South
Dakota.

On April 2, 1902, he married Margaretha Vilhauer of Hosmer, South Dakota,
daughter of Fred and Sophia (Hieb) Vilhauer.  Margaretha was born on September
28, 1882, at Tripp, South Dakota.

After their marriage they farmed in McPherson County, eight miles northeast of
Hosmer, South Dakota.  In 1910, they quit farming and moved to Hosmer.  Christ
bought an interest in a general merchandise store.  His business associates
through the years were William Kundert, George Guthmiller, Frank Boldt, Martin
Preszler, and John J. Gutjahr.  Later he became sole owner of the store,
employing his son-in-law, John Benfit, and his daughters.  Along with the
general merchandise store Christ also operated a butcher shop, doing all his
own butchering and sausage making.  On bologna making day many people could be
found having lunch in the back room of the store, tasting and eating the hot
sausage as it came from the kettle.

On February 20, 1922, Margaretha passed away leaving Christ with seven
daughters and one son: Mathilda Thielen, Pierre, South Dakota; Anna Benfit
Fuchs, Zap, North Dakota; Lydia Schumacher (deceased on June 28, 1946); Martha
Haerter, Hosmer; Sophie Wentz, Diamond Bluff, Wisconsin; Hildegarde Bauman,
Hosmer; Amanda Reitan, Scottsdale, Arizona; and Roland C. Jakober (deceased on
November 25, 1959).

In September 1922, Christ married Charlotte (Huber) Leidholdt, daughter of
Jacob and Sabina (Walz) Huber of rural Bowdle, South Dakota.  Charlotte was
born May 8, 1888, at Freeman, South Dakota.  To this union two more daughters
were added: Ida Junker, Riverside, California; and Agnes Huber, Los Angeles,
California.  One daughter, Amy Heckenlaible, Hosmer, was born to Christ and
Charlotte. Charlotte enjoyed gardening, and sewing clothes for her children, at
which she was very talented.  She also enjoyed baking bread, even selling it
regularly to some of her friends.

Christ was active in community and church activities.  He was clerk and board
member of the Hosmer School District for many years.  He also served as deacon
and Sunday School teacher at St. John's Lutheran Church for many years.  He was
also a charter member of the Bethany Cemetery Association.

Christ died of a heart attack in Wichita, Kansas, on March 29, 1935, where he
had gone to seek medical attention. Charlotte died of a heart attack on March
7, 1962, in Aberdeen, South Dakota.

The business was sold to Eisenbeisz and Stoecker after Christ's death, and they
in turn sold it to Gottlieb Walth.  The Lustermar Cultured Mfg.  Co. is now
located in the building.

FREDRICH and CAROLINA JOB

Fredrich and Carolina (Pietz) Job lived 12 miles northeast of Hosmer.  They had
a family of seven children: Emil died in 1976; August died at the age of seven
years; Helen (Mohr) died in 1979; Emma (Mohr) lives in Rapid City; Gustave
lives in Aberdeen; Lydia (Klein) lives in Eureka; and Pauline (Maisch) lives in
Mobridge.

Fredrich and Carolina lived on their farm all their lives, where they did all
their work with horses, raising small grain and cattle.

GUSTAVE and MAGDALENA JOB

Gustave was born on June 3, 1907 to Fredrich and Carolina (Pietz) Job northeast
of Hosmer.  He married Magdalena Kallenberger on November 1, 1931.  Magdalena
was born August 30, 1911 to Jacob and Barbara Kallenberger north of Hosmer.
Gust and Maggie made their home on the Fred Job farm until the fall of 1946
when they moved into Hosmer.  Gustave went into business with Gust Pietz, owning
the Phillip's 66 gas station.  They were partners for five years after which
Gust Job sold his share to his partner.  Gustave then was employed with A.
Herrboldt & Co. for 20 years as a mechanic.  After retirement he repaired lawn
mowers at his home.  Maggie helped out at various business places also being a
cook at the Hosmer school for five years.

Gust and Maggie lived in the Hosmer area all their lives, except for two years
they lived with their son and family in Washington, Iowa.  They now reside in
Aberdeen.

Gust and Maggie have one son, Raymond, born April 14, 1937, who lives in Sioux
City, Iowa, and has five children: Rodney, Brenda, Tim, Michael and Matthew.

JOHN J. and ROSINA JOB

John J. Job, son of Jacob and Katherina (Perman) Job was born in South Russia
near Odessa May 7, 1890.  He moved with his family to the United States at the
age of nine months and settled fifteen miles northeast of Hosmer.  He attended
rural school in McPherson County.  On January 6, 1914 he married Rosina Geist,
daughter of Christian and Johanna (Dockter) Geist.  She was born in
Glueckstahl, South Russia August 25, 1895.  She attended school in Russia until
the age of nine years when she came to this country with her parents.

John and Rosina farmed in McPherson County near Hosmer until 1953 when they
built a new home and moved into Hosmer.  John died July 19, 1971 and Rosina
passed away May 15, 1983.  They were blessed with 10 children.  Samuel married
Martha Schlaht and died November 16, 1980.  Eugene married Tillie Hauck and
lives in Post Falls, Idaho.  Elmer married Lavera Herman and died August 19,
1985.  Jackie married Arlene Pfeiffle and lives in Clinton, Iowa.  Emma married
Art Schick and resides in Ellensburg, Washington. Esther Casey lives in Redwood
Falls, Minnesota.  Clara married Norman Maisch and resides in Redwood Falls,
Minnesota. Alice married Harold Grischowsky and lives in Coos Bay, Oregon.
Betty married Gerhardt Gutjahr and lives on a farm near Hosmer.  Kathy married
Carl Roten and resides in Albany, Oregon.

SAM and MARTHA JOB

Sam Job, son of John and Rosina (Geist) Job was born on September 6, 1916.
Martha Schlaht was born October 9, 1919, to John and Lena (Oberlander) Schlaht.
Sam and Martha were united in marriage on October 26, 1939.  Sam worked with
his father-in-law in the blacksmith shop.  Sam and Albert Schlaht operated
Schlaht and Job Blacksmith Shop having purchased it from John Schlaht.  Martha
and Sam also did some farming living on a farm south of Hosmer for three years.
In 1956 Sam and his brother-in-law, Albert Schlaht, purchased Midwest Motors
and moved with their Case tractor franchise to the Ford garage location.  In
1968 the partnership was dissolved.  Sam owned and operated Job Motors with his
wife, Martha, being the bookkeeper.  In 1971 the business was sold to Gideon
Kaul.  Sam was then employed at Farmers Union Co-op operating their gas truck.
In 1973 he owned and operated Mobil Bulk Truck business until the time of his
death in 1980.  Martha continues to operate Martha's Store, the business she
owns.

Children of Sam and Martha are: Tom, born in 1948, is living in Sioux Falls,
South Dakota, he married Julie Volk and they have two daughters; Terry Cox was
born in 1949, is married to Judy Roesch, they also have two daughters and make
their home in Aberdeen, South Dakota; and Carol was born in 1955 and is married
to Lew Paulson and lives in Hosmer.

WILLIAM and REBECCA JOHNSON

William Charles (Charley) Johnson was born March 15, 1936, to Alfine and
Albertina (Chesky) Johnson at Selby, South Dakota.  He grew up in the Selby
area on his parents' farm.  He married Rebecca (Becky) L. Jasper on August 22,
1962, at Pipestone, Minnesota.  Becky is the daughter of Herbert and Ada
(McCurdy) Jasper and was born April 12, 1943, in Mitchell, South Dakota.

Charley and Becky lived in Timber Lake, South Dakota, where he was a truck
driver.  Being in the trucking business they lived in various places moving to
Hosmer in 1972. Charley is now a truck driver for P & A Trucking in Hosmer.

To this marriage five children were born.  Kimberly Kay was born in Platte,
South Dakota on May 28, 1963.  She graduated from Hosmer High School in 1981
and went to Pierre school of nursing.  She is now employed at the Walden Books
as assistant manager in Pierre.

Lesia Dawn was born in Mitchell, South Dakota on January 13, 1965, and
graduated from Hosmer High in 1983 after which she attended Pierre school of
nursing and is now employed at the Mobridge Care Center, Mobridge, South
Dakota, as an L.P.N.

William (Billy) Arnold was born on August 1, 1969, in Mobridge, South Dakota
and is presently a student at Hosmer High School.

Melissa Joy was born on September 30, 1976, at Aberdeen, South Dakota, and is a
student at Hosmer Elementary.

Nicole Lee was born in Ipswich, South Dakota on April 4, 1980 and is also a
student at Hosmer Elementary.

ALTON and RAMONA JUNG

Alton Jung was born July 5, 1927 to Emil and Emma (Gramm) Jung.  Alton lived
with his parents on the farm east of Hosmer until his marriage to Ramona
Gutjahr on March 13, 1949.  They farm northeast of Roscoe.  Ramona was born
July 22, 1927 to Philip and Magdalina (Wiederrich) Gutjahr. They were blessed
with three children.

Doris was born March 11, 1950.  She graduated from Roscoe High School and
Augustana College.  She is presently in the real estate business.  She married
Dwight Jenkins of Dallas, Texas.  They reside in Boise, Idaho.

Jean was born August 3, 1953.  She graduated from Roscoe High School and the
University of South Dakota.  She is at the present time assistant principal and
librarian at Holy Cross School at Ipswich.  She married Ken Volk of Ipswich.
They live on a farm.  They have three children, Lori, Julie, and Lisa.

Dale was born September 22, 1963.  He graduated from Roscoe High School and
Lake Area Vocational Technical Institute.  He married Sherry Fuhrman of Roscoe.
He is farming with his parents.

DAVID and KATHERINE JUNG

David Jung was born January 24, 1897, five and one-half miles southeast of
Hosmer, South Dakota, to Jacob and Dora Hottman Jung.  He grew up on this farm,
and during World War I served his country in the U.S. Army.

On March 23, 1923, David was united in marriage to Katherine Gutjahr, daughter
of Christian and Katherine Keszler Gutjahr.  Katherine was born October 3,
1897, in Glueckstal, South Russia.  In 1898, she came with her parents to
America and settled on a farm southeast of Hosmer, South Dakota.

After their marriage they lived on the family farm, which David's aunt, who was
deaf and dumb, had homesteaded. The aunt lived in her own home until the last
year of her life, when she lived with David and Katherine.  She died at the age
of 85 years.  They lived there until February of 1958, when they moved into
Hosmer.  David did some carpenter work, and for his hobby he would chisel grave
markers out of native rock.

The couple has two children: Mariam, who was born December 5, 1923, married
Milbert G. Eisenbeisz, and is living on the Jung family farm with her son John;
and Edmund, who was born September 13, 1925, is living in Ipswich, South
Dakota.  He works for the State Highway Department.

David and Katherine are still residing in their own home in Hosmer.

EMIL and EMMA JUNG

Emil Jung was born April 16, 1900 in McPherson County near Hosmer, to Fred and
Carolina (Will) Jung.  Fred was born in Russia March 16, 1876, came to America
in 1889 and passed away on June 10, 1959.  Carolina Will was born in South
Russia April 16, 1878.  Fred and Carolina were married in 1898 in Hosmer.  Emil
lived with his parents northeast of Hosmer until his marriage to Emma Gramm on
January 1, 1924.  Emma was born to Fred and Christina (Warmer) Gramm near
Hosmer on October 6, 1901.  Emil and Emma farmed east of Hosmer until he passed
away on July 11, 1971, and Emma passing away on July 21, 1982.

Emil and Emma were blessed with five sons.  Melvin was born January 25, 1926,
lives in Aberdeen and has five children: Phyllis Berte, Denver, Colorado, has
two children; Leslie, married Kathleen Hieb, lives in Aberdeen and has two
children; Darlene married LaDell Lehr and lives in Aberdeen, also has two
children; Stanley lives in Morris, Minnesota; and Steven married Brenda
Markovetz and lives in Aberdeen. Alton was born July 5, 1927, lives on a farm
near Roscoe and married Ramona Gutjahr.  They have three children: Doris
Jenkins, Jean Volk and Dale.  Leo was born May 21, 1929, married Lorraine
Forkel and lives on a farm near Roscoe. They have five children: Larry, Linda
Studnicka, Lora Jundt, Linnette and Luanne.  LeRoy was born December 15, 1934
and lives on the family farm.  He married Peggy Schumacher and they have three
sons: Glenn, Joey and Louie.  Benjamin was born April 3, 1937, married Elnora
Mohr and lives in Aberdeen.  They have four children: Connie married Jay
Kirschenmann and lives in Bradenton, Florida; Scott, Mary Ann and Michael live
in Aberdeen.

GLENN and THERESA JUNG

Glenn Jung was born to LeRoy and Peggy Schumacher Jung on November 25, 1960.
He grew up on a farm east of Hosmer, South Dakota.  He attended school in

HERBERT and FLORA JUNG

Flora Ulmer was born to Edward J. and Anna Ulmer on March 14, 1913 in Menno,
South Dakota, moving to the Hosmer area in 1917.  She lived on the farm with

remained in Oregon until the children were grown returning to Hosmer in 1974.

at Fort Leonard Wood, Missouri.  He was sent overseas where he was stationed in
Korea.  He was discharged in June 1953.

Lorraine Forkel was born June 9, 1935, to Edwin and Emma (Gienger) Forkel on a

grew up.  She received her education at a country school, located near the farm.

On October 16, 1953, Leo and Lorraine were united in marriage.  Shortly
afterwards they moved on a farm 15 miles southeast of Hosmer, where they are
still residing.  Leo does some farming, and has been employed by Safeguard
Industries at Aberdeen, South Dakota, since August of 1966.

The Jungs are members of St. John's Lutheran Church at Hosmer.

Leo and Lorraine have one son and four daughters.

Larry was born March 28, 1958, and graduated from Hosmer High School and Lake

LEROY and PEGGY JUNG

LeRoy Jung was born December 15, 1934 in Edmunds County to Emil and Emma

nurse's aide at the Bowdle Nursing Home.

School and attended Lake Area Vo-Tech in Watertown and graduated as a diesel

MELVIN and LORINA JUNG

Melvin Jung was born January 25, 1926 at Roscoe to Emil and Emma (Gramm) Jung.
Emil and Emma are both deceased and were members of St. John's Lutheran Church
at Hosmer.  He lived with his parents on a farm 13 miles northeast of Roscoe,
except when he served in the U.S. Army in Massachusetts and Virginia.  He
married Lorina Gutjahr March 21, 1948 at Hosmer United Church of Christ.  They
lived on a farm 9 miles cast of Hosmer for 38 years.

Lorina Gutjahr was born on a farm seven miles southeast of Hosmer to Christian
E. and Emma (Preszler) Gutjahr. Christ passed away in 1963 and Emma is residing
in Hosmer. They were members of the United Church of Christ.  Lorina moved into
Hosmer November 1985, and is employed at the Senior Citizen's Home in Hosmer as
part-time housekeeper. She teaches Sunday school and is stewardship secretary
for St. John's ALCW.

The couple were the parents of five children.  Phyllis was born June 22, 1949.
She graduated from Hosmer High School and attended mechanics school for one
year in Laramie, Wyoming.  She married Charles Bertie from Rock Springs,
Wyoming, and they have two children, Lori Lynn and Charles Anton Jr. She and
her children are presently living in Denver, Colorado where she is employed
with Public Service Electric Co.

Leslie was born June 3, 1955 and graduated from Hosmer High School.  He married

Steven was born October 1, 1964 and graduated from Hosmer High School.  He

JACOB and BARBARA KALLENBERGER

Jacob Kallenberger was born in Hofensingsburg, South Russia on September 24,
1886, while yet a small boy came to America with his parents.  He married
Barbara Barth on December 11, 1908.  Barbara was born March 5, 1890, in South
Russia and came to America with her parents at the age of eight years, settling
in the Hosmer area.

This marriage was blessed with two children: William, who resides in Lead,
South Dakota; and Magdalena (Gust) Job, who lives in Aberdeen.

FRED and CHRISTINA KAMMERER

Fred Kammerer was born in Neudorf, Russia on November 16, 1900, to Fred and
Lydia (Perman) Kammerer. He came to the United States in 1910 and settled in
the Eureka area.  Fred did some farming and was a labor man most of his life.

Christina Rohrbach was born in Neudorf, Russia on July 8, 1902, to Jacob and
Christina (Lammle) Rohrbach.  She came to the United States in 1905 and settled
with her parents in the Hosmer area.

Fred and Christina were married February 19, 1925, in St. John's Lutheran
Church in Hosmer where they are still members.

During their years of marriage they lived in the Eureka, Hillsview and Hosmer
areas retiring and now living in Hosmer.  The marriage was blessed with four
children: Gertrude Eisenbeisz and Viola Knittle, both living in Hosmer; Eugene
Kammerer, living in Groton, South Dakota; and Lavina Keene, living in Boulder,
Colorado.

began farming with his parents.  They are presently farming the homestead in
Hillsview Township, northeast of Hosmer.

Melissa was born November 2, 1977; Nicole, July 21, 1983; and Domonic, May 30,
1985.

WILFRED and CATHERINE KAPPENMAN

Wilfred Kappenman, son of George and Emila (Splonskowskie) Kappenman, was born
on August 4, 1918, near Hillsview, South Dakota, in McPherson County.  On
