THE KAUL HISTORY

Gideon Kaul's history is one of German-Russian heritage, as is most members of
this community.  We know that in 1907, Gideon's grandfather, Joseph Kaul, and
three children came to North Dakota from Russia.  The Kauls came from the
Glueckstahl area in Russia.  This is where Christian Kaul, Gideon Kaul's father
was born in November 1874.  In 1909, Christian Kaul and his wife, Christina,
and two sons, John and Jacob, decided to come to the United States.  They
arrived in New York City 21 days after they left Russia by ship.  Here they
boarded a train to come to North Dakota, where they settled near Streeter.
Here, in North Dakota, five more children were born to the Kauls: Gottlieb,
Christian, Joseph, Lydia and Edwin.  In 1920 Christina Kaut died, leaving her
husband to raise and care for the children.  In 1922, Christian Kaul met and
married another woman named Christina, Christina Hieb Wittmeier, who also was
raising five children alone after the death of her husband, Henry Wittmeier, in
1918.

The children in this history now continue on to be a history of his, hers, and
ours.  As after this marriage the Kauls had their own children.  The Wittmeier
children were Martha, Emma, Esther, Lydia, Edwin and Pauline.  To the Kauls were
born twin boys, Wilhelm and Gideon, in 1923; Christina, born in 1925; Mary,
born 1928; and Frieda, born 1931.  After their marriage, the Kauls moved to a
farm near Hosmer and at one time lived on the farm now owned by Francis
Schwinglers.  They also lived on a farm south of Hosmer, now owned by Joe
Scherrs.  It was while living here that Christian Kaul passed away in February,
1936.  After his death, Christina moved her family into Hosmer and established a
home in the south part of town where the Gideon Kauls now live.  Here she
finished raising her children and spent the rest of her life.

It would be appropriate to recall some history concerning those mentioned
herein, starting with Christian Kaul's first family, as all have at one time or
another or are still living in this community.  John Kaul, the oldest, married
Caroline Stotz of Eureka in 1923.  They farmed awhile here, then moved to
Montana, where some other Kaul relatives lived.  John died in 1983.  Caroline
lives in Montana near her daughters. Jacob Kaul married Bertha Borkirchert, of
the Roscoe area. They farmed and owned land southwest of Hosmer for many years,
eventually moving to Rapid City, where Jacob was employed.  Jacob died in 1978.
 Bertha Kaul is in a nursing home in Rapid City.  Gottlieb Kaul married Katie
Eisenbeisz. They farmed south of Hosmer for a few years and later moved to
Aberdeen, where they now live.  Christian Kaul served his country during World
War II in the European area.  After coming home he decided he liked Montana,
too.  There he married Hazel Voss and they established their home in Montana.
Christ, as he was usually called, died in 1959.  His wife Hazel lives in
Montana near their children.  Joseph, better known as Joe, married Sybil Esche
of Bath, South Dakota. After a few years of farming in the Bath and Mellette
areas, they moved to California, where they now life at Victorville. Lydia
married Israel Scherbenski.  They live in Sacramento, California.  Edwin Kaul
married Virginia Benfit.  They live in Aberdeen.

Christina Wittmeier Kaul's first family also mostly grew up near Hosmer.
Martha married August Dewald, living near and in Hosmer.  'He died quite young
and a few years later, Martha married Albert Kittock.  Both are now deceased.
Martha died in 1982, in Fargo, North Dakota, where she had spent the last few
years of her life.  Emma married Otto Borkirchert and they farmed in the Roscoe
area.  Otto died in 1969.  In 1971 Emma moved into Hosmer, where she now lives.
Esther married Philip Aman.  They reside in Roscoe. Lydia, better known to her
Hosmer friends and relatives as Ida, married Emil Eisenbeisz.  They farmed
southwest of Hosmer many years, eventually moving to Aberdeen.  In 1980 Emil
passed away.  Ida resides at their home in Aberdeen. Edwin Wittmeier married
Nettie Wolff from the Java area.  They farmed in the Ashton area for several
years, later moving to Aberdeen, where they now live.  Pauline married Edwin
Kirschenmann.  They farmed the Kirschenmann home farm until Edwin's health
brought them to Hosmer to live.  Edwin passed away in the spring of 1985.
Pauline still lives in their home in Hosmer.

Christian and Christina Kaul's children all grew up near or in Hosmer.  Wilhelm
or Bill, as he is better known, and Gideon spent much of their time together
and experienced a closeness that twins often have.  They both served their
country in World War II, Gideon in the Army in the South Pacific and Bill in
the Marines.  Bill married Elsie Bauman in 1948. They reside in Aberdeen.
Gideon married Janice Shippee Holdorf in 1972.  They live at the same residence
established by his mother.  Christina Kaul married Anton Salwei in 1947. They
farmed in North Dakota for awhile.  They now reside in Aberdeen.  Mary Kaul
married Francis Cully in 1949.  They lived in Bird Island, Minnesota.  In
October 1973, Mary passed away.  Frieda married George Roiger in 1949.  They
live in Springfield, Minnesota.

Gideon spent most of his life in Hosmer, except for the time spent as a young
man, working for Carl Meyer, on his farm near Mansfield, South Dakota, and the
years spent in the service.  After his discharge from the Army, he came back to
Hosmer where he started work repairing automobiles, trucks, and machinery for
various businessmen.  Eventually in 1971, he owned his own business and called
it Kaul's Repair. In 1983, after 37 years in this work, he sold the business to
Stanley Mohr, who now operates it as Stan's Auto.  Gideon is partially retired,
doing seasonal work with the State Game, Fish, and Parks.  Gideon and his
mother lived together many years until her death January 27, 1970.

REINHOLD KIESZ FAMILY

Reinhold Kiesz was born November 27, 1916 on a farm which his parents
homesteaded in the year 1900.  The farm is located five miles east, two miles
south of Hosmer in Edmunds County.  He attended the Hosmer schools.  He farmed
with his parents, Gottlieb and Marie Kiesz.  In 1939 his parents moved to the
city of Hosmer.

On October 8, 1939 Reinhold married Matilda Voegele at the Hope Lutheran Church
near Hosmer.

To this union were born four children.

Jeanette, born November 3, 1943, lives in Aberdeen, and is married to Kap
Scherr of Roscoe.  Jeanette is teaching. They have four children: Len, Kim,
Vickie and Joel.

Robert, born March 16, 1946, lives in Aberdeen, is teaching music in
Northwestern (Mellette) and is a realtor at Chell Real Estate in Aberdeen.  He
has one son, Barry.

Patricia, born November 11, 1950, lives in Aberdeen, and is married to Peter
Kirkpatrick.  Patty works at St. Luke's as public relations director.  They
have one daughter, Karrie.

Kathleen, born July 9, 1954, lives in Overland Park, Kansas, and is married to
Gary Hover of Clear Lake.  Kathy is sales representative for National Steel
Company in Kansas City, Missouri.  They have one son, Joshua.

Reinhold and Matilda lived on the farm near Hosmer for 41 years and were
members of St. John's Lutheran Church. In November 1980 they retired and moved
to Aberdeen, South Dakota.

ALBERT KIRSCHENMANN FAMILY

and Louisa Ehresman Kappes from Long Lake, South Dakota.  She was born on

were born to this couple: Jacob was born on August 23, 1953 and graduated from

School in Watertown, South Dakota and studied diesel mechanics. Jacob married
Janice Scherbenske of Aberdeen, South Dakota and they have three children:
Tricia, Jason, and Kari. Jacob is farming one-half mile west of the family farm.

Carol was born July 7, 1954 and graduated from Hosmer High School in 1973.  She
also attended Watertown Business College and Huron Bible College.  Carol is
employed at the Aberdeen Day Care Center in Aberdeen, South Dakota.

Jerome was born November 10, 1956 and graduated from Hosmer High School in
1975.  He also attended Lake Area Vocational Technical School in Watertown,
South Dakota studying carpentry.  Jerome is currently working on the family
farm.

Linda was born on March 20, 1958 and graduated from Hosmer High School in 1976.
She also attended Presentation College in Aberdeen, South Dakota, studying to
be a medical secretary.  Linda is married to Wayne Fischer and they have one
son, Joshua.  They are currently residing in Aberdeen, South Dakota.

Albert's wife, Edna, passed away on June 2, 1979 at University Hospital,
Minneapolis, Minnesota.  On October 26, 1985, Albert married Lorraine Geiszler
of Fredonia, North Dakota.  They are living on the family farm.

ALVIN and ADINA KIRSCHENMANN

Alvin Kirschenmann was born February 22, 1920, to John and Christina (Kurie)
Kirschenmann.  He grew up on the family farm 2 1/2 miles southeast of Hosmer.
He attended country school and graduated from Hosmer High School in 1938.  He
continued to farm with his parents until his marriage to Adina Schaible on
April 11, 1943.  They were married in the Hope Lutheran country church, Hosmer,
South Dakota.  After their marriage, they made their home on the Kirschenmann
family farm.  Adina was born August 23, 1920, to Fred and Eva (Gutjahr)
Schaible.  She attended the country school that was near her farm home.

Alvin and Adina were blessed with two daughters, Eloise and Joyce.  Eloise was
born May 25, 1951.  She graduated from Hosmer High School in 1969 and attended
Aberdeen School of Commerce.  She married Edwin Appletoft Jr. in 1972.  Eloise
is currently a full time homemaker and her husband, Ed, is an independent
trucker.  They have six children: Estelle, Eric, Elisha, Evanie, Eline, and
Eliott.  They reside in Aberdeen, South Dakota.

Joyce was born November 2, 1956.  She graduated from Hosmer High School in
1975.  She married Dennis Heyne. They have two children, Ryan and Michelle.
They reside on the Kirschenmann family farm southeast of Hosmer.

Alvin and Adina are members of the U.C.C. Church in Hosmer.  Adina is a member
of the Women's Fellowship and has held numerous offices in this organization.
Alvin was on the board of the Hosmer Farmers Telephone Co. for several years.
He has held positions on the U.C.C. church board, the Sangamon Township board,
and was a Hosmer Farmers Union director for a number of years.

Alvin and Adina have lived on the family farm for 43 years and continue to be
actively engaged in the farming operation.

AUGUST and HENRIETTA KIRSCHENMANN

August Kirschenmann was born November 11, 1893 at Menno, South Dakota to Jacob
and Christina (Heyne) Kirschenmann.  Jacob was born in Neudorf, South Russia
March 19, 1859 and died in 1941.  Christina was born August 22, 1864 in
Neudorf, South Russia and died in 1928.

August lived on a farm northeast of Hosmer until his marriage to Henrietta Hieb
February 4, 1919.  Henrietta was born October 15, 1896 to Wilhelm and Salmina
Hieb. Wilhelm was born August 1, 1869 at Neudorf, South Russia and Salmina was
born September 20, 1863 in Kolenie Neudorf, South Russia.  Wilhelm died
February 13, 1933 and Salmina died January 21, 1925.

August and Henrietta farmed one year in Wakpala, South Dakota and then farmed 2
1/2 miles northeast of Hosmer until September 1, 1951 when they moved into
Hosmer.

They were blessed with four children.  Arthur was born April 28, 1920 and died
February 27, 1984.  He married Lydia Bitz December 1941 and they had four
daughters: Leona, Mavis, Linda, and Betty.

Emil was born July 19, 1923 and married Gladys Guthmiller.  They reside in
Arlington, Texas and their family consists of three children: Ronald, Dianna,
and Timothy.

Reinhold was born March 1, 1925.  He married lone M. Pautsch, who died
September 14, 1985.  They had three sons: Roger, Dale, and Terry, and reside in
East Moline, Illinois.

Vilena was born January 7, 1928 and married Raymond Vilhauer.  Their family
consists of two children: Darlene and Duane and they live in Roscoe.

August Kirschenmann died November 5, 1984 and Henrietta died August 10, 1981.

GOTTLIEB and CHRISTINA KIRSCHENMANN

Gottlieb Kirschenmann was born on January 8, 1888 to Christian and Philipina
Walz Kirschenmann.  Gottlieb married Christina Mayer from Hosmer, South Dakota,
where Gottlieb owned the flour mill from 1924 to 1930.  In 1930, the flour mill
burned down and Gottlieb and his family moved to Roscoe, South Dakota where
they farmed.  When Christina passed away in 1946, Gottlieb sold the farm and
moved to Aberdeen, South Dakota.  In 1961, Gottlieb passed away.

Gottlieb and Christina had ten children.  Of these, a baby girl died when she
was four years old and they also lost a baby boy in infancy.

The rest of their children are: Clara Sutter, who lives in Rapid City, South
Dakota.  Her husband passed away on May 25, 1982.  Albina Glaser Layburne
passed away on July 15, 1981.  Elmer lives on a farm near Roscoe, South Dakota
and married Mildred Oban from Loyalton, South Dakota. Adelina Preszler lives in
Rapid City, South Dakota and married Albert Preszler, who passed away on April
9, 1985.  Ella Bosch married Leo Bosch from Roscoe, South Dakota and now they
reside in Aberdeen, South Dakota.  Irma Nelhick married Edward Nelhick and
lived in California.  Irma passed away on September 22, 1981.  Helmuth married
Neva Beyers from Roscoe, South Dakota and they currently reside in Ipswich,
South Dakota.  Ervin lives in Rapid City, South Dakota.  He married Eileen
Keintz from Eden, South Dakota, who passed away on December 20, 1984.  Ervin
later married Esther Kirschmayer from Bismarck, North Dakota.

HARRY and PHYLLIS KIRSCHENMANN

Harry Edwin Kirschenmann, the son of Edwin and Pauline (Wittmeier) Kirschenmann
was born June 26, 1941, in Edmunds County.  He received his elementary
education at a country school two miles southwest of the farm and then attended
Roscoe Public School.

On February 7, 1906 the farm was homesteaded by Harry's grandparents, Christian
F. and Louise Kirschenmann. Years later, Edwin and Pauline purchased the
homestead and resided on the farm until October 1983 when they retired and
moved to, Hosmer.  Edwin was born October 26, 1911 and passed away March 2,
1985, and Pauline continues to live in Hosmer.

On November 26, 1962 Harry married Phyllis Klein, daughter of Emil and Lydia
(Job) Klein.  She was born in McPherson County and attended rural school and
graduated from Hosmer High School in 1961.  Emil and Lydia farmed northeast of
Hosmer until October 1969 when they retired and moved to Eureka.  Emil passed
away January 17, 1977 and Lydia continues to make her home in Eureka.

Harry and Phyllis farmed with his parents until 1965 when they moved to Hosmer
and in October 1983 they returned to the farmstead, where they are presently
residing.  In addition to farming they are also in the trucking business.

They have three children.  Dean, born July 7, 1963, attended Hosmer Public
School and graduated in 1981.  He attended Lake Area Vo-Tech at Watertown
graduating in 1985. Cheryl was born December 24, 1964.  She attended Hosmer
Public School and graduated in 1983.  Rodney was born January 20, 1968 and is
presently a senior at Hosmer High School.

They are members of the United Church of Christ in Hosmer.

JACOB and EVA KIRSCHENMANN

Jacob Kirschenmann was the eldest son of Christian Sr. and Philipina Walz
Kirschenmann.  He was born on March 7, 1874 in Kassel, South Russia.  In 1883,
as a young boy, he came to America.  He worked for many years for only room and
board.  Jacob married Eva Hoffman and began married life farming three miles
northeast of Hosmer.  Six children were born to this couple: Emma, Anna, John,
Samuel, Alma, and Sarah.  Later they moved to a farm southeast of Eureka, South
Dakota.  Eva died on December 20, 1914 in childbirth and is buried near Mathew
Church, Hosmer, South Dakota.

Jacob married Dorthea Schick (nee Weller) in 1916. Dorthea was born May 2,
1886.  She had three children from a former marriage: Ida, Bill, and Art
Schick.  Four children blessed the home of Jacob and Dorthea: Mathilda, Emil,
Albert, and Ruth.  In all, there were 13 children.

Emma married Christian Herman.  After Christian passed away, she married Jacob
Trefz and he has since passed on,  too.

Anna married Fred Walz, who is deceased.  Anna now lives in Quincy, Washington.

John married Ida Mettler.  After Ida passed away, John married Sophie Salwei
and they live in Aberdeen, South Dakota.

Samuel never married and lived in Palo Alto, California until his death in
1979.  He is buried in Eureka, South Dakota.

Sarah married Fred Phaff and they resided in Whitefish, Montana.  Fred has
since passed away and Sarah is now in the retirement home at Lewiston, Montana.

Ida married Otto Beck and they farm southwest of Artas, South Dakota.

Bill married Ella Beck and farmed southwest of Artas, South Dakota.  Bill is
deceased.

Mathilda married Chet Poland and they are living in Yakima, Washington.

Emil is living in Eureka, South Dakota.

Albert is living on a farm near Hosmer, South Dakota. (See Albert Kirschenmann
History.)

Ruth married Oscar Kappes and they reside in Minnesota.

In 1948, Jacob and Dorthea moved to Eureka, South Dakota.  Jacob passed away on
February 8, 1952 and Dorthea passed away on October 14, 1963.  They are buried
at Eureka, South Dakota.

JACOB and JANICE KIRSCHENMANN

Jacob Kirschenmann was born on August 23, 1953 to Albert and Edna (Kappes)
Kirschenmann.  He grew up on the family farm north of Hosmer.  He received

JOHN KIRSCHENMANN FAMILY

John Kirschenmann was born December 23, 1879, in Kassel, South Russia, to
Christian and Philipina (Walz) Kirschenmann.  Christian was born August 15, 185
1, and died June 27, 1928.  His wife, Philipina was born May 3, 1852, and died
November 23, 1934.

John came to this country with his parents when he was four years old.  In 1885
his parents settled on a farm near Menno, South Dakota, and in 1887 they moved
3 1/2 miles north of Hosmer.  John lived with his parents on the farm until
1902 when he married Christina Kurle, who was born April 6, 1883, and moved to
a farm northwest of Roscoe.  In 1906 John bought a farm 2 1/2 miles southeast
of Hosmer.  This was known as the Frank Boldt farm.

John and Christina were blessed with eight children: Bertha, Ida, Ruben, Elmer,
Esther, Marie, Sarah, and Alvin.

Bertha was born February 1, 1905.  She married John J. Pietz.  They had two
sons, Melvin and Levin.  She passed away on September 24, 1985.  John resides
in Aberdeen, South Dakota.

Ida was born September 3, 1906.  She married Jacob Rueb.  He passed away on
July 3, 1969.  She married Karl Schock in 1973.  He passed away on March 29,
1982.  Ida resides in Hosmer.

Ruben was born January 4, 1909.  He married Mathilda. Schick.  They had two
sons, Loren and Harley.,Ruben passed away on March 8, 1978.  Their son, Loren
passed away on June 28, 1985.  Mathilda resides in Roscoe, South Dakota.

Elmer was born on March 31, 1910, and died on February 19, 1911.

Esther was born on May 25, 1913.  She married Adolph Knittel.  They had two
children: Mabel and Marlin.  They reside in Lakewood, Colorado.

Marie was born on November 2, 1914.  She married Edwin Weishaar.  They had
three children: Mavis, Joyce and Brian.  Joyce was born August 1, 1946 and died
on July 4, 1947.  They reside in Lakewood, Colorado.

Sarah was born on December 17, 1917.  She married Eugene Mehlhaff.  They had
two children: Charlotte and Charles.  They reside in Aberdeen, South Dakota.

Alvin was born on February 22, 1920.  He married Adina Schaible.  They had two
daughters: Eloise and Joyce.  They reside on the family farm southeast of
Hosmer.

John and Christina moved to Hosmer in 1943 and resided there until their
deaths.  John passed away on January 3, 1950, and Christina passed away on July
11, 1955.

GERHARDT and VIOLA KNITTEL

Gerhardt Knittel was born at Tripp, South Dakota, on August 16, 1922 to William
and Elizabeth (Walz) Knittel.  He had three sisters: Esther Werre, Helen Werre
and Irene Fordham (deceased).  He lived in the Roscoe vicinity until he was
drafted into the Army in July 1944.  Gerhardt was sent overseas to the
Asiatic-Pacific in January of 1945 where he participated in the liberation of
the Philippines and served in the Army of Occupation in Japan.  He was
discharged in August 1946.

Viola Knittel was born on March 9, 1927 at Eureka, South Dakota, to Fred and
Christina (Rohrbach) Kammerer and has one brother, Eugene, and two sisters,
Gertrude Eisenbeisz and Lavina Keene.

Gerhardt and Viola were married in December 1953 in St. John's Lutheran Church
of Hosmer and are still members there.  They have two daughters: Wanda, a
registered nurse and Kristie, a beautician.  Both are living in Rapid City,
South Dakota.

They farmed for a few years after which they operated a cream station in
Hosmer.  Gerhardt was then employed at Farmers Union Coop for several years and
then was employed at Heier's Meat Market as a butcher for a number of years.
Viola at the present is employed at the Senior Citizen's Home at Hosmer as a
medication aide.

Gerhardt passed away in November 1984.

HENRY and CAROLINA KREBS

Henry Krebs was born on January 1, 1892, on a farm near Java, South Dakota,
where his parents homesteaded.  In September of 1916 he married Carolina Maier,
who was born January 5, 1900, on a farm near Mound City, South Dakota, where
her parents farmed.  They lived for brief periods near Bowdle and Java, South
Dakota, before moving to Hosmer, South Dakota.  He worked for the Chicago,
Milwaukee, St. Paul and Pacific Railroad as a section foreman for 34 years
after which he retired to gardening, fishing and relaxing.

Henry was active in civic affairs and served on the county draft board and city
council for many years.  Carolina was active in church work and quiltmaking,
crocheting and embroidery.

The couple had ten children, as follows: Emma (died at six months); Ruth
Kirschenmann (died in 1966 in Aberdeen, South Dakota); Rev.  Arthur Krebs,
retired, and living in Burlington, Iowa; Oscar Krebs, retired and living in
Roscoe, South Dakota; Norman Krebs (died in 1961 in Seattle, Washington); Alice
Hieb, retired and living in Roscoe, South Dakota; Lenora Spitzer, living in
Kent, Washington; Marvin Krebs, Gaithersburg, Maryland; Donald Krebs, Seattle,
Washington; and Carson Krebs, Fountain Valley, California.

The picture shown was taken in 1961.  Henry and Carolina had 32 grandchildren
and 28 great-grandchildren.

They were members of the Congregational Church, now known as United Church of
Christ.  Mrs. Krebs died in 1973. Mr. Krebs married Magdalena Ehresman in 1975,
who resides in Bowdle, South Dakota.  Mr. Krebs died in 1979.

VALENTINE and CLARA KRUMM

Valentine Joseph Krumm was born near Bowdle January 7, 1908 to Joseph and
Margaretta Krumm.  He attended school and grew up there.  He married Clara
Maria Dewald July 1930 at Bowdle.

Clara was born June 8, 1909 to Anton and Dorthea Dewald of Bowdle.  She
attended school and grew up there until her marriage.

Valentine and Clara made their home in Bowdle until 1942 when they moved to a
farm near Hosmer.  In 1960 they moved into Hosmer where Valentine was employed
with the Edmunds County Highway Department until his retirement in 1974.

Valentine and Clara had six children: Margaret Krumm of Dell Rapids; a son,
died in infancy; Mrs. Francis (Madonna) Kunkel of Akron, Ohio; Joe Krumm,
Aberdeen; Mrs. Darwin (Kay) Ulmer, Roscoe; and Mrs. Kenny (Frances) Hieb,
Hosmer.

Valentine died November 28, 1980 and Clara continues to live in Hosmer.

ARTHUR KUNDE FAMILY

Arthur Kunde was born February 25, 1894, the oldest of four surviving children
of Fred and Bertha (Boldt) Kunde, early pioneers of Hosmer.  As a young man he
worked with his father in the Hosmer Flour Mill and later with his Uncle
Rinehart Kunde in the meat market.

In 1918 Art married Ernestine Stabbe, daughter of Ferdinand and Regina (Reede)
Stabbe.  Before her marriage Ernestine worked at the Hosmer Hotel.  Art served
in the army in 1918 and after returning to private life, he and his brother
managed the Hosmer Meat Market.  Later in life, Art was employed by the Hosmer
Lumber Company until his retirement. Art had been a member of the baseball
teams, a tuba player in the band and a member of the American Legion.

The family were members of St. John's Lutheran Church of which Art's father was
a charter member.

Art and Ernestine had four children, all of whom were graduates of Hosmer.
Louis and Marvin served in the armed forces.

Irene and her husband Cliff Isachsen live in Willmar, Minnesota.  Cliff is a
senior biology teacher.  Their children are: Paul, Terry and Carla.

Gladys and her husband George Newberry live in Freeport, Illinois.  George
works for Freeport Housing.  Their children are: Ginny, Vickie, Bonnie, Nory
and Rick.

Louis and his wife Laverna (Eisenbeisz) live in Rapid City.  Louis is an
engraver for Landstrom's Black Hills Gold. Laverna works at the Pennington
County Courthouse.  Their children are: Kerma and Steve.

Marvin worked for Walth Motor Company before his death in February 1978.

Art lived his entire life in Hosmer.  He passed away November 9, 1984.
Ernestine lives in the Hosmer Senior Citizen's Home.

PAUL and OLIVIA KUNDE

Paul Kunde, son of Frederick and Bertha (Boldt) Kunde was born March 18, 1900
in Hosmer, and died October 7, 1970.  Olivia (Ulmer) Kunde was born September
27, 1900 in Hutchinson County near Menno, South Dakota to Christian and
Katherine Ulmer, and died June 29, 1984.

They were married on June 2, 1929 in Hosmer.  Paul was associated with his
brother in the Meat Market.  Olivia was the first cook of the Hosmer School Hot
Lunch Program and served in that capacity for 13 years.

They were the parents of three daughters: Elodie, Mrs. Eldon Schmierer of
Bowdle, South Dakota; Inez, Mrs. Cornelius Albrecht, of St. Paul, Minnesota;
and Joy, Mrs. Don Wander, of Minneapolis, Minnesota, and two grandchildren:
Lisa and Mark Albrecht of St. Paul, Minnesota.

JACOB and KAROLINA LAMMLE FAMILY

Jacob Lammle, son of John and Katherine (Heil) Lammle was born October 4, 1884.
He came to America with his parents in 1892 and settled near Tripp, South
Dakota.  The family came to Hosmer in the early 1900s and bought a farm from
John Stoecker which was part of Section 20 in Blaine Township, in McPherson
County, where Jacob grew to manhood.

In November 1902 he married Karolina Walth, and they made their home on the N
1/2 of Section 18, Township 125, Range 70.  In 1919 his health began to fail
and they moved into Hosmer.

He bought a half interest in an elevator and implement business with John
Haerter.  A partnership was formed with Jacob G. Walth and Jacob Lammle.  The
partnership was dissolved in 1929 when Walth took over the elevator and Lammle
the implement business.  Later Lammle sold this business to Sonnenfeld and
Gutjahr, and entered into partnership with J. C. Schaible in the Ford Garage.
This he sold when he retired in 1945.

Mrs. Lammle passed away in 1947 and Mr. Lammle in 1962.  The couple had five
children.

Julius married Martha Herrboldt, he is a retired bread salesman, now living in
Yuma, Arizona.  They have one son, James, living in San Jose, California.

Reinhold married Margaret Vilhauer.  Reinhold was a businessman in Hosmer for
many years.  He was custodian for the Hosmer school until moving to Aberdeen
where he and Margaret were custodians at the Aberdeen American News until their
retirement.  They have two daughters: Judeen of Tioga, North Dakota; and Ranea
of Minneapolis, Minnesota; and one granddaughter.  Reinhold passed away April
9, 1986.

Matilda married John Lehr.  Matilda did clerking in various stores while living
in Hosmer.  After John's retirement as a mail carrier, they moved to Aberdeen
in 1970.  John passed away in 1975, since then Matilda does volunteer work at
St. Luke's Hospital to keep busy and make other people happy.

Erwin married Ruth Kreiger.  Erwin is a retired salesman now living in Acampo,
California.  They have one son and three grandchildren.

Milbert married Elizabeth Meier.  Milbert worked for the Milwaukee Railroad and
is now retired and does taxidermy work in his home in Roscoe.  He is a member
of the Roscoe Senior Citizens and the Hosmer Sportsmen's Club.  They have three
children: Carolyn, Marilyn and Thomas.

DOUG and ILEEN LINDNER

Ileen Lindner is the daughter of Rupert and Orma Ulmer.  Upon completion of
high school she attended business school in Aberdeen, South Dakota.  She
married Doug Lindner of Watertown, South Dakota and resided in Aberdeen for 15
years.  Doug and Ileen have two children: Susan and Chuck.

In 1977 Ileen and her family returned to Hosmer.  They purchased the inventory
and fixtures of the Super Valu store from the Farmers State Bank and rented the
building which was the former Walth Store.  They opened the store as Doug's
Fairway on December 13, 1974.  Doug and Ileen then purchased the Boldt Store
and moved into that store until the completion of their new store.  They moved
into the new store in October 1976.  They operated the store until April 1984,
at which time they sold it to John and Mary Margaret Jacobs of Houston, Texas.

The Lindner family moved to Dickinson, North Dakota, where Doug is managing a
store and supervising another store in Belfield, North Dakota, for Fairway
Foods.  Ileen is employed at the Midwest Federal Bank.

ARNOLD and JEANNETTE LUNDERS

Arnold and Jeannette (Grismer) Lunders were married September 9, 1957, in
Hoven, South Dakota.  They lived with Arnold's parents, Mark and Hilda Lunders,
on a farm near Hoven for a short time then moved to the former Beck farm
located northwest of Hosmer.

In the spring of 1960, they bought the former Albert Gross farm located 11
miles east and 1 mile north of Hosmer and have made it their permanent home.

Susan Marie was born March 3, 1959, and attended Hosmer School.  After
graduating from Aberdeen School of Commerce, she married Marlin Lehr on June 9,
1978.  They have one daughter, Melissa, and live in Aberdeen, South Dakota.

Edward Arnold was born December 22, 1960, and attended Hosmer School.  During
that time, he was selected Hosmer Homecoming King and Boy's Stater.  He was
also active in 4-H and chosen Edmunds County 4-H King in 1979. On September 2,
1979, he died in a car accident shortly after beginning school at South Dakota
State University in Brookings.

Jacob Mark was born November 1, 1962, and graduated from Hosmer School in 1982.
In August of 1982, he was chosen Edmunds County 4-H King.  After graduation,
he attended school in Watertown then worked in Brookings, South Dakota, for two
years.  He worked in San Diego, California, for a year and is currently at home
on the farm.

Keith Allen was born February 27, 1966, and graduated from Hosmer School in
1984.  The day he was brought home, a winter storm hit and there was no
electricity for a week. Arnold fixed a gas motor for the furnace and the
bathroom was the only place to keep warm so the bassinet was kept in the
bathtub.  In order to get the cattle out of the pole barn, a hole was cut in
the roof.  It was a long hard week.  Keith is currently attending school in
Watertown, South Dakota.

Blake Lee was born June 25, 1971, and is currently attending Hosmer High School.

Arnold and Jeannette are members of the Holy Trinity Catholic Church in Hosmer.
They have a dairy operation, farm crop land, and raise livestock.

EMANUEL and KATHERINE MAISCH

Emanuel, son of Jacob and Rosena (Levi) Maisch, was born September 17, 1904.
Katherine was born November 16, 1910 to Jacob and Katherine (Perman) Job.

Emanuel and Katherine were united in marriage in 1932. They farmed on the
homestead northeast of Hosmer until 1970 when they retired and moved to Hosmer.
This marriage was blessed with seven children: Lyilia (Rueb), lives in
Glendale, Arizona; Mary Rose (Voegele), lives in Diamond Bar, California;
Walter (deceased); Harold, lives in Montgomery, Minnesota; Gloria (Gerber),
lives in Montevideo, Minnesota; and Debbie (Hardy), lives in Aberdeen, South
Dakota.  The Maisches have eleven grandchildren and one great-grandchild.

GUST and EMILY MAISCH

Gust Maisch was born on September 17, 1898 in the Hosmer, South Dakota area, to
John and Rosina Maisch.

In 1922 he married Emily Aman, daughter of Jacob H. and Rosina Goehring Aman.
Emily was born September 21, 1902 in the Hosmer area.

Gust and Emily lived in Hosmer where he was a cream buyer from 1923 to 1947.
After quitting the cream buying business, they moved to Aberdeen, where Gust
purchased and remodeled homes for resale.

They were blessed with five children: Deloris Bauer, living in Sun City,
Arizona; Bernice Dahmer, living in Pleasant Hills, Tennessee; Lorena Arden, in
Dayton, Ohio; Gordon, Grand Island, Nebraska; and Raymond, living in Aberdeen,
South Dakota.

Gust passed away in June 1979.  Emily is still living in her home in Aberdeen,
South Dakota.

ANTON and CATHERINA MALSAM

Anton Malsam was born near Hillsview, South Dakota on September 12, 1916 to
George Malsam (August 18, 1887-May 24, 1960) and Katherine Glatt Malsam
(1890-November 29, 1959).  Catherine Zacher was born near Hague, North Dakota
on September 15, 1918 to Steven Zacher (February 19, 1894-June 22, 1971) and
Ludwiga Geffre Zacher (August 2, 1891-August 28, 1939).

Anton and Catherina were married on August 21, 1939 and settled on a farm near
Hosmer, South Dakota.  This union was blessed with 11 children: Steve, born
July 23, 1940, is residing on the family farm; Janice, born January 13, 1942,
is residing in Hosmer; Ardell, born November 10, 1943, married Duane Mertz on

ANTON G. and MAGDELENA MALSAM

Anton G. Malsam was born on November 10, 1888 to George Sr. and Kathrina Heier
Malsam.  He immigrated to America from South Russia with his parents when he
was five years old.  He grew up on the farm homesteaded by his parents.
Magdelena Fischer was born on February 28, 1893. Anton and Magdelena were
married on June 17 1913.

This union was blessed with nine children: Michael, Hosmer, South Dakota;
Alfanus, Mrs. Katie (Paul) Vetch, Mrs. Pauline (Leonard) Grajczyk, all of
Aberdeen, South Dakota; Ludwig, Pierre, South Dakota; Philip, Grandview,
Washington; Adam, killed in France during World War II; George, died in 1959;
and Wilbert, Hosmer, South Dakota (see Wilbert and Vivian Malsam History).

In 1917, Anton and Magdelena bought a farm located five miles west and
one-fourth mile north of Hosmer.  Anton was a good manager and so was a
successful farmer.  The couple retired in 1954 and made their home in Hosmer.

Magdelena passed away on February 16, 1969 at the age of 75 years.  Anton
passed away on January 1, 1970 at the age of 82 years.

FRANK and ADELINE MALSAM

Frank, the son of Peter and Hellen (Heintzman) Malsam was born August 17, 1913.
 He farmed with his parents near Hillsview until his marriage to Adeline Imberi
March 4, 1946. She was born to Peter and Katherine (Hilzendeger) Imberi
February 20, 1919.

They farmed near Hillsview seven years and then moved to a farm three miles
west and one-half mile north of Hosmer. Frank and Adeline moved into Hosmer in
1980 and are semi-retired.

They were blessed with three children.  Margie, born August 18, 1946, graduated
from Hosmer High School in 1964 and married Joel Sayler April 4, 1965.  They
have two daughters, Joyce and Jackie, and reside on a farm northeast of Eureka.

Joe was born August 2, 1949.  He attended Hosmer Public School and is presently
employed at Aberdeen.

Kathy was born December 5, 1952 and graduated from Hosmer High School in 1971.
She married Roger Gramm and they have two children, Rebecca and Brian.

GEORGE MALSAM SR.  FAMILY

George Malsam Sr. was born June 24, 1861 in South Russia.  He married Katherine
Heier in November 1883.  After ten years of marriage they decided to move to
America with their three children: Theresa, George and Anton.

They homesteaded on the farm presently owned by David Malsam.  The young couple
broke sod with oxen and later bought horses to till the soil of their new home.

The couple was blessed with nine children.

In 1920 they retired from farming and moved into Hillsview. Mrs. Malsam passed
away in 1946 and Mr. Malsam in 1949.

GEORGE MALSAM JR.  FAMILY

George Malsam Jr. was born in 1887 in South Russia to George Sr. and Katherine
(Heier) Malsam.  At the age of six he came to America with his parents.

In 1910 George married Katherine Glatt of Hague, North Dakota in Hillsview,
South Dakota.  The young couple bought a farm northwest of Hosmer and lived
there until their retirement.  Nine children were born to the Malsams: Nick,
Anton and Andrew of Hosmer; Martin of Hillsview; Anna, Lizzie and Agnes of
Aberdeen; Katie of Portland, Oregon; and one child who died in infancy.

In 1946 the Malsams retired and moved to Hillsview; later moving to Aberdeen.

Mrs. Malsam passed away in 1959 and Mr. Malsam in 1960.

HENRY and ADELINE MALSAM

Henry Malsam was born April 15, 1929 on a farm near Hillsview to Kasper G. and
Margaret (Fischer) Malsam.  He grew up in the Hillsview area and attended rural
school. Henry is on the board of directors of the Hosmer Cenex, officer of the

member of the Hosmer Public School board of education.

Adeline Heier was born April 11, 1934 on a farm three miles west of Hosmer to

school.  Adeline is a housewife and is a member of the Christian Mothers of
Holy Trinity Parish.

Henry and Adeline were married October 29, 1952 in the Holy Trinity Church in
Hosmer.  They farmed the family homestead for six years and then moved to a
farm 3 1/2 miles southwest of Hosmer.  In 1968 they built a new farm 2 1/2 miles
southwest of Hosmer where they are presently living.

Henry and Adeline have five children.

Roger, born September 4, 1953 in Aberdeen, graduated from Hosmer High School in

ROGER and SANDRA MALSAM

Roger H. Malsam was born September 4, 1953 in Brown County at St. Luke's
Hospital in Aberdeen, South Dakota to Henry and Adeline (Heier) Malsam.  He
lived with his parents on a farm southwest of Hosmer and attended Hosmer Public
