University of South Dakota at Vermillion graduating in 1975 with a B.S. degree
in science and math.  He then returned to Hosmer where he started farming with
his father.

On June 30, 1979 Roger married Sandra E. Burgad.  Sandra was born July 4, 1952
in Burleigh County at St. Alexius Hospital in Bismarck, North Dakota.  She
lived in Napoleon, North Dakota with her parents, George and Elsie Burgad. She
attended St. Philips Catholic Grade School, graduated from Napoleon High School
in 1970, attended BJC at Bismarck and VCSC at Valley City where she received
her degree in teaching.  She moved to Hosmer in 1975 to teach kindergarten and
remedial reading in the Hosmer public school s stem.

Roger and Sandra have a daughter, Tass Marie, born July 3, 1982 in Edmunds
County at the Bowdle Hospital.

Roger, Sandra and Tass live in Hosmer in the City Apartment which is located
one block south and one-half block east of the post office.  Roger is engaged
in farming with his parents and Sandra is presently teaching kindergarten in
the Hosmer public school system.

THEOPHIEL (DAVE) and LEONA MALSAM

Theophiel (Dave) Malsam and Leona Hopfinger were married on October 20, 1947 in
Aberdeen, South Dakota.

Dave was born to Frank and Johanna Goetz Malsam on October 15, 1923 in
Hillsview, South Dakota.  He has two living brothers: Joe lives in Worthington,
Minnesota; and Ben lives in Sioux Falls, South Dakota; and four sisters: Frances
Gab, Aberdeen, South Dakota; Aggie Heilman, Hosmer; Marcella Scherr, Aberdeen;
and Theresa Jones, Huntsville, Ohio.  Steve, Dave's oldest brother, passed away
in October, 1984.

Leona was born to Adam and Mary Reinboldt Hopfinger on June 17, 1929 in Bowdle,
South Dakota.  She has five sisters: Mrs. Frances Heilman, Bowdle, South Dakota;
Lena Harty and Phillipine Harty, both of Aberdeen, South Dakota; Josephine
Heier and Margaret Heier, both of Hosmer, South Dakota.  Leona is a 1947
graduate of Aberdeen Central High School.

Their story: Our first home was a farm with a sod house one mile east of
Hillsview, South Dakota.  The house was very warm in the winter, and cool in
the summer.  The walls of the sod house were papered, there were ceiling boards
on the ceiling, and wood floors with linoleum.  Actually, it was quite nice.

In 1950 we moved to the Malsam homestead, which was homesteaded by Dave's
grandfather in the 1800s.  In 1940 the house burned down and another one was
moved there.  In 1980, we moved to Hosmer and Jerome, our son, and his family
took over the family farm.  This is the fourth generation of Malsams living
there.

We have eight sons and five daughters.

Robert was born September 1, 1948.  He is married to Janet Struss of Huron,
South Dakota.  They have two children: Becky and Dustin.  Robert is co-owner of
Schumacher Sales and Service of Aberdeen, South Dakota.

Yvonne was born November 18, 1949.  She graduated from Hosmer High School in
1968 and later married Teddy Opp of Eureka, South Dakota.  They have two sons:
Keith and Wade.  Yvonne is a district manager of Northwest Fabric in Cheyenne,
Wyoming and she also oversees several other stores in various cities in Wyoming
and Colorado.

Jerome was born March 9, 1951 and graduated from Hosmer High in 1969.  He is
married to Gerri Urich of Roscoe, South Dakota and they have three sons: Jared,
Micha, and Isaac.  Jerome is a farmer and Gerri is head nurse at Hosmer Senior
Citizen's Home.  Their entire history is also in this book.

John was born February 8, 1952 and graduated from Hosmer High in 1970.  He is
married to Gwen Kappenman of Eureka, South Dakota.  They have three sons:
Jeremy, Eric and Christopher.  John is a grain merchandiser for Italgrani in
St. Louis, Missouri.

Carol was born March 14, 1953 and graduated from Hosmer High in 1971.  She is
married to Richard Barth of Mobridge, South Dakota.  They have two sons and one
daughter: Richard Jr., Justin, and Heather.  They live in Lodi, California.

Thomas was born July 21, 1955 and graduated from Hosmer High in 1973.  He is
married to Joan Ichier of Aberdeen, South Dakota.  They have two sons: Shawn
and Shane. Thomas is a boiler engineer at Hilty of Tulsa, Oklahoma.

Joanie was born on August 19, 1956 and graduated from Hosmer High in 1974.  She
is married to Ronald Janish of Waubay, South Dakota.  They have one son, Kevin,
and one daughter, Stacie.  They are living in Aberdeen, South Dakota.

Richard was born August 14, 1958 and graduated from Hosmer High in 1977.  He is
married to Mary Welch of Tulsa, Oklahoma.  They have one son, Brian.  Richard
is on maintenance at Hilty of Tulsa, Oklahoma.

Lucy was born November 9, 1960 and graduated from Hosmer High in 1979.  She is
married to Mike Vadakin of Bloomington, Minnesota.  They had one daughter, Tina
Lucille, who passed away at the age of six months.  Lucy is working as a
nurses' aide at a senior citizen's home in Bloomington, Minnesota.

Margaret was born September 18, 1962 and graduated from Hosmer High in 1980.
She is currently going to college at the University of South Dakota in
Vermillion.  She is working on her master's degree in public administration.
She has one daughter, Deanna Lee.

David was born April 10, 1964 and graduated from Hosmer High in 1982.  He is
employed at Aberdeen T.V. but intends to move back to Tulsa where he worked at
Hilty of Tulsa, Oklahoma, for two years.

James was born June 15, 1969.  He is in the 11th grade at Hosmer High School
and is employed at Jacob's Fairway Store in Hosmer.

Troy was born August 14, 197 1. He is in the eighth grade at Hosmer Elementary
School.

WILBERT and VIVIAN MALSAM

Wilbert Malsam was born October 2, 1930 to Antony and Magdalena Fischer Malsam.
He grew up on the family farm five miles west and one-fourth mile north of
Hosmer.

Vivian Thomas was born July 5, 1937 to Adam and Jenny Malsam Thomas.  She
married Wilbert Malsam in Bowdle, South Dakota on June 16, 1958.  This union
was blessed with seven children.

Margaret, born March 27, 1962, graduated from Hosmer High School in 1980.  She
attended Presentation College, Aberdeen, South Dakota, and Mary College,
Bismarck, North Dakota.  She worked for a time at Beaumont, Texas, returning to
South Dakota to work as a registered nurse at McKennan Hospital in Sioux Falls,
South Dakota, where she is still employed.

Melvin, born May 12, 1963, graduated from Hosmer High School in 1981.  He
studied diesel mechanics at Watertown Vocational Technical School and is
currently working for Barbarossa Construction from Osseo, Minnesota.

Marvin, born July 1, 1964, graduated from Hosmer High School in 1983.  He is
currently working with his father in Malsam Construction.

Wayne, born November 21, 1965, graduated from Hosmer High School in 1984.  He
attended Watertown Vo-Tech, studying to be a machinist and graduated May 29,
1986.

Julie, born June 9, 1967, graduated from Hosmer High School in 1985.  She is
attending Stewart School of Hairstyling in Aberdeen, South Dakota as of this
printing.

Carolee, born March 13, 1973, will be in the eighth grade at Hosmer public
school in the spring of 1987.

David, born December 18, 1979, will be in the second grade at Hosmer public
school in the spring of 1987.

Wilbert and Vivian lived on several farms in the Hosmer area until October of
1970, when they moved into the city of Hosmer.  Wilbert began Malsam
Construction, a gravel business, in 1971, which he still owns and operates.
Vivian is currently employed as a cook at the Hosmer Senior Citizen's Home.
The family belongs to the Holy Trinity Catholic Church in Hosmer.

JEROME and GERALYN MALSOM

Jerome Malsom was born March 9, 1951 to Theophiel and Leona (Hopfinger) Malsom,
 who farmed seven miles northwest of Hosmer.  On May 30, 1975 he married Geralyn
Uhrich of Roscoe.  Geralyn was born May 27, 1956 to Marvin and Lorraine
(Scherr) Uhrich of Roscoe, who farmed seven miles south of Roscoe.

Jerome was a 1969 Hosmer High School graduate. Following graduation he was
employed at the Hosmer Lumber Co., truck driver for the Eureka Cheese factory
and for Wilbert Malsam and Lyle Clopine.  He worked as a beekeeper in Texas for
Jim Darnell of Roscoe.  In 1973 he was employed as a mechanic at A. Herrboldt &
Co. Following their marriage in 1975 Jerome and Geralyn moved to Kulm, North
Dakota where Jerome was employed as a diesel mechanic for Carlson and Systrum.
In 1976 they moved to Roscoe and owned and operated their repair shop, until
June 1978 when they sold their business and started farming the Malsom
homestead seven miles northwest of Hosmer, where they are presently residing.

Jerome's hobbies include hunting, fishing, and working with his Belgium work
horses.  In 1980 and 1981, he went fishing and black bear hunting in Canada.

Geralyn, a 1974 graduate of Roscoe High School, attended USD at Vermillion for
a year, attended NSC and graduated from Presentation School of Nursing in
Aberdeen as an R.N. She has been employed at the Bowdle Community Hospital,
Bowdle Nursing Home, and was also a home healthcare nurse at St. Luke's
Hospital in Aberdeen.  As a free lance artist, Geralyn's oil paintings received
honorable mention at the 1982 Mobridge 7th Annual Area Art Show, in 1983 she
won first place in the portrait division.  In 1985 a selection of her artwork
was displayed at the Mini Gallery of Presentation Heights.

They are members of the Holy Trinity Church in Hosmer.  Geralyn is a member of
the Christian Mother's Society and the Busy Belles Club.  Jerome is a member of
the Hosmer Sportsmen's Club, Hosmer Elevator Board director, and co-manager of
the 102 Ballroom.

Jerome and Geralyn have three sons: Jared, born November 23, 1975; Micah, born
April 26, 1977; and Isaac, born April 3, 1978.

MATT and MARION MALSOM FAMILY

I, Marion Malsom, was born on my mother's birthday, May 14 in Hosmer, South
Dakota, year 1923, to John and Katherine Schoch.  John Schoch was born March
29, 1889, Katherine Kreitel Schoch was born May 14, 1892.  Two years after I
was born, we moved to the farm.  At age six, we moved to a farm three miles
west of Hosmer, I went to a country school four miles west of Hosmer from
grades one through grade eight.  At that time all eight grades were taught in
one room; that's as far as my education went.  Growing up on the farm the girls
worked out in the field, too.  I remember my sister Frances and I hauling
bundles with a team of horses when we threshed, just like the men did.  In 1945
I tried my luck in city life, and went to Aberdeen and worked two years in
Tiffany Laundry.

On October 27, 1947, I married Mathew Malsom of Hillsview and went right back
to the farm again.  Mathew Malsom was born October 24, 1923 to Kasper and
Margaret Fischer Malsom, he grew up in the Hillsview area.  Our first home
after marriage was on a farm northwest of Hosmer.  We lived there five years
then moved close to Roscoe.  In 1955 we bought the John Sitter farm seven miles
east and three miles south of Roscoe.  We farmed until 1977 then decided to
call it quits.  We moved to Aberdeen and let our two oldest sons farm.

We became parents to nine children: Diane, born September 11, 1948, married
Robert Allbee, are farmers in the Loyalton area and have five children;
Angeline, born February 3, 1950, married Randy Crawford, lives in Aberdeen and
has two daughters; Marilyn, born February 11, 1951, married Raymond Wik, lives
in Fort Sill, Oklahoma and has two sons; Frances, born August 13, 1952, married
Phillip Monks, lives in Louisville, Kentucky and has three children; Mathew,
born March 2, 1954, married Londa Preszler, lives on the home place in the
Beebe area and has two sons; Pius, born September 25, 1955, married Susan
Schumacher, lives on a farm in the Beebe area and has three children; Harlan,
born January 13, 1957, married Ramona Grismer, lives in Pella, Iowa and has
four children; Mariann, born October 29, 1963, lives in Aberdeen at this
writing; and John, born July 9, 1969, died the same day and is buried at
Morningside Cemetery in Roscoe.

MR. and MRS.  FREDERICK MARTEL

Frederick Martel was born in Kassel, South Russia on February 1, 1871.  He came
to this country in 1892 and settled in Menno, South Dakota, where he worked as
a common laborer.  In April of the same year, Frederick left Menno and settled
10 miles northeast of Hosmer in Cleveland Township, McPherson County.  He
brought with him a wagon, a drag and a walking plow.

Frederick was married to Elizabeth Henne in 1893.  The couple had ten children:
Gust (deceased); Carl, in the Hosmer Nursing Home; Emil (deceased); Otto
(deceased); Richard, in Oregon; Mrs. Carl (Christina) Schnaible, in the Hosmer
Nursing Home; Mrs. David (Ida) Hieb, in Aberdeen; Mrs. Ed (Alvina) Fischer, in
Aberdeen; Mrs. Christ (Paulina) Hieb, near Hosmer; and Anna (deceased).

The Martels lived on the farm until 1925, when they retired and moved to
Hosmer.  Elizabeth passed away on February 8, 1945, and Frederick on August 25,
1945.

HENRY and PAULINE MARTEL

Henry and Pauline (Krein) Martel were both born and raised in McPherson County
near Eureka.  Henry was born in 1891 to the Philip Martels.  He grew up under
pioneer standards. He married Pauline Krein, daughter of the Friedrick Kreins,
who was born in McPherson County in 1892.  Her parents came to McPherson County
in 1885.

Henry and Pauline started their own farm five miles northeast of Hosmer in
1913.  They continued farming until the fall of 1947 when they retired and
moved to Hosmer. They had three children: Walter, Hosmer; Hilda (Mrs.  John
Powell), Buffalo Gap, Texas; and Frieda (Mrs.  Wm.  Hauck), Faar, Texas.  Henry
passed away in 1964 and Pauline in 1970.

WALTER and EMMA MARTEL

Walter was born in 1920 to Henry and Pauline (Krein) Martel in McPherson
County.  He grew up on his father's farm five miles northeast of Hosmer.  In
1946 he was united in marriage to Emma Gramm, born in 1919 to Adam and Pauline
(Lehr) Gramm.  Walter and his wife, Emma, took over the family farm.

They were blessed with four children: Charles W., born in 1946, is married to
Alayne Daniels and they reside in Philadelphia, Pennsylvania.  Ruby, born in
1949, is married to Ronald Fuhrman.  They have two sons and live in Omaha,
Nebraska.  Donald, born in 195 1, is married to Christina Grismer and lives in
Aberdeen, South Dakota.  Elaine, born in 1956, is married to Gary Scherbenski
and they have one daughter and live in Eureka.

Walter and Emma retired in 1982, moving into Hosmer.

CARL and LYDIA MARTELL

Carl Martell was born on June 28, 1895, in McPherson County, Hosmer, South
Dakota, to George Martell, born August 16, 1855 and Gertrude Oberlander
Martell, born March 16, 1860.  The family came from Russia in 1895.  He was one
of fourteen children.

Carl was born in a barn on the homestead.  The family had not been here from
Russia too long and had no house to live in.  Carl farmed with his dad until he
entered the U.S. Army in 1918, and served until 1919.  He was married to Lydia
Heyd on March 10, 1921.  They continued to farm northeast of Hosmer until
October 1933, when they moved into Hosmer.  Carl worked with Art Guthmiller on
the dray line, WPA, and was city police in Hosmer for many years.  He also was
custodian at St. John's Lutheran Church and manager of the Legion Hall several
years.  At the time of his death on November 16, 1975, he was working part time
at Schlaht & Job Motors.

The parents of Lydia were John and Eva Gienger Heyd. After moving into Hosmer,
Lydia and Carl lived and worked in the Hosmer High School Dormitory.  They
housed approximately 16 boys and served 3 meals a day to these boys and 14
girls.  All the water for dormitory use had to be carried from the city well
which was two blocks away.  She was also employed at the Koerner's Nyal Drug
Store & Cafe as a cook. She prepared many German dishes to be served to the
customers.

Two children were born to Carl and Lydia: Melvin, who was born October 1, 1939,
and passed away at birth; and Milbert, born December 23, 1921, living in
Marshall, Minnesota with his wife Loraine.  They have three children, Karen and
Kevin, living in San Jose, California; and Kent, living in Minneapolis,
Minnesota.

Carl retired in May of 1973, and he and Lydia moved to the Senior Citizen's
Home, where Lydia is still a resident.

FAMILY OF JACOB MAYER

Jacob Mayer came to the Hosmer area from Russia.  He was married to Katherine
Hieb.

To this union were born five daughters: Mrs. Philip (Eva) Heil, Mrs. Theobolt
(Katherina) Guthmiller, Mrs. Christ (Caroline) Nelich, Mrs. Gottleib
(Magdalina) Hieb, and Mrs. Gottlieb (Christina) Kirschenmann.  Katherine died
and Jacob then married Caroline, sister of Katherine. Caroline had one son,
John Dockter.  To Jacob and Caroline were born five sons and two daughters:
Jacob, Bernard, William, Mrs. Fred (Rosa) Jundt, Otto, Mrs. Julius (Lydia)
Eisenbeis, and Leonard.

Jacob died in February 1937 and Caroline died in August 1941.  Jacob and
Caroline farmed northeast of Hosmer, later moved about two miles southwest of
Hosmer, then moved to Hosmer where they lived their retirement years.

OTTO and EMMA MAYER

Otto Mayer was born April 24, 1905, in Hosmer, South Dakota, to Jacob and
Carolina (Hieb) Mayer.  He attended school in Blaine Township in rural Hosmer.
After his schooling he farmed prior to his marriage.

On December 2, 1928, he married Emma Gugel at Hosmer.  Emma was born November
27, 1906, to John and Maria (Fiel) Gugel in McPherson County.  Otto and Emma
farmed northeast of Hosmer until 1969 when they retired to Mobridge, South
Dakota.

In the early years of their marriage they endured the drought and depression
along with their friends, relatives and neighbors.  As the years passed the
farm was increased and modernized.  They were active in church and community
affairs.  Otto was also in the trucking business and a partner in a threshing
rig.  He served on the church board, rural school board and was on the board of
directors for the co-op in Leola.  After retiring to Mobridge he served as
treasurer of the Moose Lodge, was a member of the board of directors of
Products Miniature of S. Dak., Inc., a church treasurer and central
committeeman of his political party in McPherson County.

Otto and Emma were blessed with five children.  James, born March 8, 1931
(deceased), Huntington Beach, California, he married Ruby Maisch and had four
children: Stacey (Mrs.  Terry Poyzer), Sheila (Mrs.  John Burnett), Eric (his
wife Ginger), and Sherrie; Ruby, born April 14, 1930, Thornton, Colorado, she
married Arthur Schilling and they have one child, Dale; Lester, born August 8,
1938, Golden, Colorado, he married Sharon Herrboldt and they have three
children: Carla (Mrs.  Chris Beller), Tod and Candice; Gloria, born September
8, 1941, Westminster, Colorado, she married John Schlaht and they have two
children: Don and Ann; and Clark, born November 28, 1945, Hagerstown, Maryland,
he married Joan Gooden and they have a daughter, Tonya.

Emma is still active in church and community affairs in the Mobridge area.
Otto passed away March 1981 and James, their son, passed away in April 1983.

EDWARD and CHRISTINA MEHLHAFF

Edward Mehlhaff was born on October 21, 1891 to Jacob Jr. and Christine Koerner
Mehlhaff.  Christina Thurn was born in 1898.  Edward and Christina were married
on May 16, 1916.  They moved to the Hosmer, South Dakota area where they built
a new farmstead 4 1/2 miles northwest of Hosmer.  The marriage union was
blessed with three children: Eugene, born June 8, 1919, lives in Aberdeen,
South Dakota; Norman (see Norman and Emilia Mehlhaff History); and Arthur, born
February 5, 1927 and died February 27, 1928.

Edward and Christina farmed for 32 years.  They then retired into the city of
Hosmer.  They were lifelong members of the Reformed Church in Hosmer.

Edward died on March 3, 1982 and Christina died on February 17, 1985 at the age
of 86 years.

HENRY and MARIA MEHLHAFF

Henry Mehlhaff was born on a farm three miles southeast of Hillsview, South
Dakota on September 10, 1895. Henry was the son of John Mehlhaff Sr. and
Margareta (Goehring) Mehlhaff.  Henry's wife, Maria, was born in a sod house
northeast of Hosmer to Jacob Hieb and Carolina (Helm) Hieb on April 7, 1897.
They both grew to adulthood at these places.  Henry was drafted to serve in
World War I and was a private of 163 Depot Brigade.  He was in many battles,
one major battle at Argonne, France.  Upon Henry's return from the Army, they
were married a few months later. Henry and Maria spent their entire lifetime
working hard. Their first home was the John Mehlhaff Sr. home place at
Hillsview for nine years, then they moved to a farm 15 miles northeast of
Hosmer.

The Henry Mehlhaffs had seven children.  Ervin E. was killed during World War
II at Leyte Island, Pacific on October 31, 1944.  The remaining six children
are still living. By name they are: Florence Schock, Leola, South Dakota;
Walter, Wishek, North Dakota; Harold, Aberdeen, South Dakota; Edward, Aberdeen,
South Dakota; Esther Ottenbacher, Aberdeen, South Dakota; and Arnold, Aberdeen,
South Dakota.

Henry passed away on August 23, 1957 at age 61.  Maria stayed on the farm for a
few years and then sold the farm and made her home in Aberdeen.  She always was
capable of taking care of herself and lived alone until she passed away on
April 1, 1982 at age 84.

Henry and Maria were highly respected in the rural farm community.  They were
known as honest, hard-working people and both with outgoing personalities.
They were members of the Lutheran Church of Hosmer.

JACOB SR. and CHRISTINE MEHLHAFF

Jacob Mehlhaff was born in South Russia in approximately 1858. He came to the
Menno, South Dakota area in 1874.  Jacob married Christine Koerner in 1885, at
which time they moved to McPherson County, Eureka, South Dakota. They farmed
three miles south of Eureka for many years and raised a large family of twelve
children, Edward being the third oldest.  Jacob died in April of 1934 and
Christine passed away on December 1, 1955.

MR. and MRS.  JOHN MEHLHAFF SR.

John Mehlhaff Sr. was born in Kassel, South Russia on June 26, 1866.  He
married Margartha Goehring on January 1, 1889 at Menno, South Dakota.  She was
born on November 10, 1870.

Children born to this union were: Lydia (Mrs.  Philip Guidice), born January
12, 1890, died May 21, 1975; John J., born May 12, 1892, died November 24,
1980; Gottfried, born November 29, 1893, died May 13, 191 1; Henry, born
September 10, 1895, died August 23, 1957; Edward, born March 2, 1897; Emma
(Mrs.  Fred Tesky), born February 9, 1899; Emmanuel, born March 10, 1902; and
Martha (Mrs. John Stacker), born September 15, 1903, died September 10, 1981.

The mother of these children passed away in 1906.  On November 10, 1909, John
was remarried to Frederricka (Goehring) Fisher.  In 1916 they moved to Hosmer
after residing on a farm near Hillsview, South Dakota.  John Mehlhaff Sr.
passed away January 14, 1936.

NORMAN and EMILIA MEHLHAFF

Norman E. Mehlhaff was born August 6, 1925 to Edward and Christina Thurn
Mehlhaff.  He received an eighth grade education and lived on the family farm
until the time of his marriage to Emilia Schnabel on October 26, 1947.  Emilia
was born on January 6, 1925 to Gottfried and Lydia Maisch Schnabel.  She also
received an eighth grade education.

Norman and Emilia are currently farming 4 1/2 miles northwest of Hosmer, where
they have lived all of their married lives and where Norman grew up.

From this marriage union, five children were born: Mrs. Mavis Ann (Dwight)
Lehr, Roscoe, South Dakota, born July 1, 1951 and graduated from Hosmer High
School in 1969. The Lehrs have two children.  Mrs. Bonnie Lou (Steve)
Bettenhausen, Lisbon, North Dakota, was born December 12, 1954 and graduated
from Hosmer High School in 1973. She has three children.  LeRoy, Hosmer, was
born on December 4, 1958 and graduated from Hosmer High School in 1977. LeRoy
is currently farming with his father.  Mrs. Judith Jean (Troy) Splitt, was born
June 6, 1962 and graduated from Hosmer High School in 1980.  They are currently
residing in Avon, South Dakota.  Norma Elaine was born on December 5, 1966 and
graduated from Hosmer High School in 1985. She is currently on the farm with
her parents.

JOHN J. and LYDIA MEHLHOFF

John J. Mehlhoff, son of John and Margaretha (nee Goehring) was born May 12,
1892, in McPherson County.  At eighteen, he started a carpentry career building
farms.  On November 26, 1914, he married Lydia Bender, whose parents were Jacob
and Christina (Martel) Bender.

In 1917, they bought a school section of 320 acres which included the farm site
northeast of Hillsview.  They had four horses, a plow, and a wagon to start
farming.  They were considered progressive farmers even through the depression
years.  In 1940, they bought the Hillsview Elevator.  In September 1942, they
retired and moved to Hosmer.

They were blessed with four children: Lenora, Ruben, Arlene, and Adeline.

Lenora was born on December 26, 1917.  She graduated from Central High School
in Aberdeen, attended NSC and SDSU at Brookings.  She married Otto Schick.
They reside in Eureka.

Ruben, born August 4, 1921, a World War II veteran, married Jacquelyn Haar.
They lived on the family farm until December 1979, when they moved to Eureka.
They have three children: Jeanne of Minneapolis, Minnesota; John of Salt Lake
City, Utah; and Ross, living in San Francisco, California. They have three
grandchildren.

Arlene was born December 13, 1927, attended NSC and married Arnold Herrboldt,
Hosmer.  They have three children: Paulette of Columbia, Missouri; Sydney of
Tucson, Arizona; and Tamara of Aberdeen.  They have two grand-children.

Adeline, known as Addie, Dorsey received a B.S. degree from NSC, is an
actress/writer and lives in San Francisco, California.  She has one son, John,
of Santa Barbara, California.

Mrs. Mehlhoff passed away in September of 1964; Mr. Mehlhoff passed away in
November 1980.

MR. and MRS.  ADOLPH MOHR

Adolph Mohr was born to Fred and Fredricka (Schmitt) Mohr on December 24, 1913.
He attended the rural township school while growing up on his parents' farm.
He was united in marriage to Gertrude Walth of rural Leola, South Dakota on
October 3, 1937.

Gertrude, the daughter of John and Magdelina Walth of rural Leola, South
Dakota, was born June 20, 1916.  She also attended rural school and received a
high school diploma by taking the G.E.D. test.

Adolph and Gertrude made their home on a farm ten miles northeast of Hosmer and
lived there until the spring of 1965, at which time they sold the farm and
their personal property and moved to Eureka, South Dakota.

Adolph is a custodian's assistant at the Eureka school and Gertrude was
activity director at the Eureka Lutheran Home.  Both are members of the Senior
Citizens and are also active in church and civic affairs.

Four children were born to this union.  Baby Boy Mohr was born on December 31,
1939 and died on January 1, 1940. Sharon Kay (see Willard Hieb history in this
book).  Jerome Gene was born on December 21, 1946.  He attended rural school
and graduated from Hosmer High School in 1964.  He married JoAnne McDonald of
Irene, South Dakota.  They have four children and reside at Florence, Colorado,
where he operates a carpentry, organ tuning and trophy shop.  Imogene Gertrude
was born on September 12, 195 1. She attended Hosmer grade school and graduated
from Eureka High School in 1969.  She married Paul Schmaht of Henry, South
Dakota.  They have two children and are living in Watertown, South Dakota.  She
is employed at Midtex as a shopist.

Mr. and Mrs. Mohr are enjoying retirement and will be married 50 years in the
fall of 1987.

CHRISTIAN and MAGDALENA MOHR

Christian Mohr was born to Gottfried and Fredricka Stoecker Mohr on May 11,
1895.  His parents came to America in the late 1800s and settled in Blaine
Township where Christian was born.

Magdalena Pietz was born to Mr. and Mrs. Theodore Pietz on October 28, 1897.
She was born in South Russia and came to America as an infant.

Christ and Magdalena were married in 1918 and were blessed with three children:
Mrs. Mathilda (Art) Rohrbach, Hosmer (see Art and Mathilda Rohrbach History);
Mrs. Irene (Warren) Schnaible, Roscoe, South Dakota; and Milbert, Hosmer (see
Milbert and Lena Mohr History).

Christ was born, raised and worked on the family farm until he retired to the
city of Hosmer in 1947.  He died October 24, 1970.  Magdalena is a resident at
the Hosmer Senior Citizen's Home.

EDWIN F. and SELMA MOHR

Edwin F. Mohr (Freddie) son of Fred and Fredericka (Schmitt) Mohr was born
January 9, 1924 on his parents' farm 12 miles northeast of Hosmer.  He attended
school in Cleveland Township.  He worked on his father's farm until 1944 when
his parents moved to Hosmer and he then took over the family farm.  He has two
sisters and three living brothers.

"Freddie" was married March 28, 1943, to Selma 1. Heyne, daughter of August and
Alita (Grischkowsky) Heyne. Selma was born January 26, 1924, and attended
school in Sangamon Township.

"Freddie" and Selma have two children: Duane born in 1943; and Sheila (Graff)
born in 1944.  They have six grandchildren: Paula, Terry and Timothy Mohr,
Monica (Graff) Fuhrmann, Ranae and Glenn Graff.  After the children left home
they continued to farm for several more years.  In 1976 they sold the farm to
Bruce Flittie at which time they moved into Hosmer where they are now residing.
Both are employed part time.  They are members of St. John's Lutheran Church.

FLOYD and MARY LOU MOHR

Floyd Mohr, son of Julius and Magdalena (Guthmiller) Mohr, was born November
13, 1939.  He grew up on his parents' farm northeast of Hosmer.

On June 14, 1975, Floyd was united in marriage to Mary Lou Malsom.  She was
born November 13, 1954, to Anton and Catherine (Zacher) Malsom.  Mary Lou
attended Hosmer public school, graduating in 1973.  They reside in Hosmer.

Floyd was the manager of Hosmer Elevator Co. for several years, worked for Jake
Mohr Construction Co. and at present is employed with Edmunds Co. Highway Dept.
Mary works as a clerk for Martha's Store and part time at City Liquor.

FRED MOHR FAMILY

Fred Mohr, the oldest son of Gottfried Mohr, was born in McPherson County in
1889, which qualifies him as a pioneer.  In 1913 he was married to Fredericka
Schmitt, who was born in McPherson County in 1892.

This young married couple made their home on Section 18 close to the old home
and as the father, the son was also successful in his endeavors.  On this farm
they lived until 1944 when they retired to Hosmer to spend their remaining days.

There were eight children in this family.  One boy died in his early youth and
Leonard lost his life in the service of his country.  Adolph is in another
history in this book.  Edwin is in another history in this book.  Walter lives
in Lodi, California. Benjamin lives in Deadwood, South Dakota.  Esther (Mrs.
Ernest Bastian) lives in San Valejo, California.  Ida (Mrs.  Stanley Keen)
lives in San Francisco, California.

Mrs. Mohr died on February 24, 1963 and Fred died on April 23, 1983.  Both are
buried in the Bethany Cemetery at Hosmer.

FRED W. and ANNA K. MOHR

Fredrich Wilhelm Mohr was born December 7, 1894 in Blaine Township, McPherson
County, South Dakota to Wilhelm and Margaretha (Hoff) Mohr.  Anna K. Mohr was
born December 29, 1896 to Gottfried and Friedericka (Stoecker) Mohr also in
Blaine Township, McPherson County, South Dakota.

Fred was one of nine children, Anna was one of thirteen. Both attended school
in Blaine Township.

Fred and Anna both of Hosmer, South Dakota were united in marriage at Butte,
Montana on the 29th day of June, 1920.  They started to farm eight miles
northeast of Hosmer in Cleveland Township, McPherson County where they spent
the rest of their lives.

Fred served a number of years on the school board, Cleveland Township No. 4. He
also served in the U.S. Army during World War I. He was a member of the American
Legion Post 189 of Hosmer.

Four children were born to this union.  Arthur J. was born on April 7, 1921.
He took charge of the farm sometime after World War II, farmed until October
1982.  He then retired and moved to Hosmer.

Ervin D. was born December 11, 1924.  He married Lorine Treftz of Hosmer.  They
are now farming in the Hosmer area.  They have one son Dale.

Eliard C. was born August 30, 1927.  He is living in Aberdeen and is married to
Carol Barnum of Aberdeen.  They have three children: Mike lives in California;
Dorthy and Mark live in Minnesota.  Eliard and Carol have four grandchildren.

Lorania L. was born September 17, 1937 and died June 21, 1938.

Fred W. died January 21, 1968.  Anna K. died November 29, 1979.

GEORGE C. and MARY JO MOHR

George C. Mohr was born to Milbert and Lena Walth Mohr on October 26, 1950.  He
lived on the family farm 12 miles northeast of Hosmer, South Dakota until his
graduation from Hosmer High School in 1968.  One week after his high school
graduation, George began work as assistant bookkeeper at Farmers State Bank in
Hosmer and is presently employed there as assistant cashier/operations manager.
George was a member of Hosmer's city council from 1972 to 1976 and was also a
member of Hosmer's men's softball team for 11 years.  He is currently a member
of the Hosmer Commercial Club, Hosmer's Sportsman's Club, and the Hosmer
Centennial Steering Committee.  George has five living brothers and sisters and
one brother who passed away at the age of four years.

Mary Jo Heier was born on March 26, 1955 to Joe P. and Josephine Hopfinger
Heier.  She lived on two different farms east of Hosmer with her parents and
eight brothers and sisters (see Joe P. and Josephine Heier History) until 1966,
when her parents purchased the Hosmer Meat Market, now known as Heier's Meat
Market.  Mary Jo worked at the meat market throughout her latter grade school
years and all through high school.  She graduated from Hosmer High School in
1973 and went on to study with Art Instruction Schools, Inc., Minneapolis,
Minnesota and also continued to work part-time at the family business.  Mary Jo
now operates a business-MJM Artworks in Hosmer.  She was a member of the Hosmer
women's softball team for eight years, is a member of the Hosmer Centennial
Steering Committee, the Centennial History Book Committee, the Centennial Art
Committee, and the Centennial School Reunion Program Committee.  She is also a
member of the Holy Trinity Catholic Church in Hosmer and served for a time as
secretary of the Christian Mother's Society in the church.  Mary Jo's father,
Joe P. Heier, passed away on October 15, 1976.

George and Mary Jo were united in marriage at the Holy Trinity Catholic Church
in Hosmer on January 5, 1974.  They are currently residing in the city of
Hosmer and were blessed with four children as of this printing: Anthony Shawn,
born and died on March 3, 1974; Justin George, born May 29, 1980; Megan
Therese, born January 20, 1982; and Owen Charles, born September 14, 1985.

GOTTFRIED MOHR HISTORY

Gottfried Mohr Sr. was born at Mathildendorf, Bessarabia, South Russia, in 1866
to Christian and Wilhemina (Wackward) Mohr.  In January of 1886, Gottfried Sr.
and his widowed mother and three brothers, Wilhelm, Christoph and Johann,
arrived at the port of entry in New York.  Upon arriving in McPherson County he
homesteaded in Blaine Township.  The homestead was the NE 1/4 of Section 17 and
the SE 1/4 was the tree claim.  This was one of the first claims made in Blaine
Township.  On September 16, 1888, he married Friedericka Stoecker.  Friedericka
was born on February 22, 1870 to Frederick and Katharina (Bauer) Stoecker in
Hoffnungsthal, South Russia.  They became successful farmers and in time
acquired many quarters of land.  In the early 1900s they built a wood frame
house for the growing family and other buildings were also added.  The family
operated the farm until 1924 at which time Mr. Mohr passed away.  Mrs. Mohr
carried on the farming operation with the aid of the younger members of the
family until 1936 at which time the youngest son Julius and his wife Magdalina
took over the farm.  The older family members were all married and had left
home.  It may be interesting to note that at one time all of the sons and
daughters except Rosina lived on farms within a five mile radius of the
homestead.

Thirteen children were born to this union, one of which died as an infant.
They are: Friedrich (1889-1983), Mrs. (Rosina) Wunner (1891-1976), Christian
(1893-1894), Christian (1895-1970), Mrs. Fred W. (Anna K.) Mohr (1896-1979),
Gottlieb (1899-1974), Mrs. August (Margaretha) Martel (1901-  ), Gottfried
(1903-1981), Jakob (1905-1975), Wilhelm (1907-  ), Theobald (1909-1976),
Julius (1911-  ), Mrs. Alvin (Magdalina) Guthmiller (1915-1960).  Mrs. Mohr
passed away on April 8, 1955.  Both are buried in the Jehovah Lutheran Cemetery.

HELMUTH and RENA MOHR

Helmuth J. Mohr was born June 20, 1927, in McPherson County northeast of Hosmer
to Gottlieb and Bertha (Albrecht) Mohr.  Gottlieb was born January 27, 1899, and
died in 1974.  Bertha Albrecht was born June 2, 1901, and is a resident of the
Senior Citizen's Home in Hosmer.

Rena was born November 29, 1928, in McPherson County to Otto and Adelgunda
(Grischkowsky) Martel.  Otto was born November 18, 1901, and died in 1952.
Adelgunda was born August 29, 1908, and is living in North Bend, Oregon.

Helmuth lived with his parents on a farm northeast of Hosmer until his marriage
to Rena Martel.  They then moved to another farm northeast of Hosmer living
there until 1976 when they moved to Hosmer.  Helmuth managed the Hosmer
Elevator Co. until 1979, when he and Rena became custodians of the Hosmer
School.

Helmuth and Rena were blessed with three boys.  Herbert was born April 5, 1947.
He graduated from Hosmer High School in 1965 and attended Universal Trade
School, Omaha, Nebraska.  Herbert served in the Navy.  He is presently living
in Las Vegas, Nevada, working at a body shop.  He has three children.

Stanley was born June 26, 1950.  He graduated from Hosmer High School in 1968,
attended State School of Science, Wahpeton, North Dakota and served in the Army.
Stanley has his own repair shop in Hosmer.  He has three children.

Allen was born April 17, 1956.  He graduated from Hosmer High School in 1974
and attended Northern State College.  Allen served in the Army.  He is
presently employed at Redfield State School and Hospital as a medic.  He lives
in Redfield, South Dakota, and has two children.

JACOB MOHR FAMILY HISTORY

Jacob Mohr was born in McPherson County northeast of Hosmer in 1905 to
Gottfried and Fredericka Stoecker Mohr.  Jacob lived with his parents on a farm
until he married Pauline Maisch in Hillsview on November 18, 1928.  They
established a farm of their own on which they lived until 1942.  Pauline, born
May 6, 1907, was the daughter of Ludwig and Katherina Hoffman Maisch.

In 1942, Jacob and Pauline moved into Hosmer.  He was custodian of the Hosmer
school for several years.  In 1952 he started Mohr Construction Company.  He
was later joined in the business by sons, Raymond and Werner, and a nephew,
Milton Mohr.  He semi-retired in 1967.  He also served on the Hosmer City
Council.

Two sons, Raymond and Werner, both of Hosmer, and a daughter who died at birth,
were born to Pauline and Jacob.

Pauline passed away on April 25, 1965.  Jacob married Helen Mohr on March 31,
1966.  Jacob passed away October 4, 1975.

JAMES and MARCELLA MOHR

James Mohr was born to Julius and Magdalena (Guthmiller) Mohr on April 8, 1941,
in McPherson County.  He attended rural school in Blaine Township and graduated
from Hosmer High School in 1958.  He served two years in the army and was
stationed in Germany.  He married Marcella Heyne on June 14, 1964, at St.
John's Lutheran Church in Hosmer. They are farming on the farm located 12 miles
northeast of Hosmer, which was homesteaded in 1886 by his grandfather Gottfried
Mohr and then farmed by his father, Julius Mohr. This farm has been in the Mohr
family for 100 years.

Marcella was born November 13, 1944 to Edward and Ida (Preszler) Heyne, who
lived on a farm nine miles southeast of Hosmer.  She attended grade school in
Hosmer and Roscoe and graduated from Hosmer High School in 1963.

James and Marcella are members of St. John's Lutheran Church where James has
served on church council.  He is a member of the Hosmer Legion and the rural
fire board. Marcella is a member of ALCW and the Highlighters Extension Club.

They have two children.  Jeffery was born December 14, 1965.  He attended
Hosmer School and graduated from Hosmer High School in 1984.  He is currently
farming with his parents, making them the third and fourth generation on this
farm.  Carol was born September 16, 1969.  She attended Hosmer school and will
graduate from Hosmer High School in 1987.

JULIUS and MAGDELINA MOHR

Julius Mohr, the youngest son of Gottfried and Fredricka (Stoecker) Mohr, was
born on October 6, 191 1, on the Mohr homestead 12 miles northeast of Hosmer in
McPherson County.  Here he grew to manhood and helped his widowed mother run
the farm.

On September 27, 1936, he was united in marriage with Magdelina Guthmiller the
daughter of Theobald and Katherina (Mayer) Guthmiller of rural Hosmer, at the
Jehovah Lutheran Church northeast of Hosmer.  He took over the family farm and
farmed until the summer of 1964 when he turned the farm over to his youngest
son James and moved to Hosmer.  He worked with the Jacob Mohr Construction Co.
for a number of years and later was custodian at St. John's Lutheran Church.

Two sons were born to this union.  Floyd was born November 13, 1939 (in another
history in this book).  James was born April 8, 1941 (in another history in
this book). Julius and Magdelina are members of St. John's Lutheran Church and
were both active in community and church affairs.  They are currently enjoying
retirement in Hosmer.

LAWRENCE and VIRGINIA MOHR

Lawrence Mohr was born on August 19, 1931 in McPherson County northeast of
Hosmer to Theobald and Bertha (Maisch) Mohr.  He helped farm until he entered
the U.S. Army in 1952.  He served in the Korean War and was discharged in 1954.

He married Virginia Graff on June 21, 1954.  They farmed for three years.  They
moved to Hosmer where they started the Hosmer Meat Market.  And later sold the
meat market to Jake Mohr and Sons.  A year later they moved to Clinton, Iowa
where Lawrence worked as a welder at Central Steel and Tube.  Three years later
they moved back to Hosmer.  They operated Larry's Cafe and L&M Sportsman's Bar
for several years.  For the past 20 years as an auctioneer, Lawrence owns and
operates Mohr's Auction Service.

Virginia was born in McPherson County on May 3, 1933 to Gottlieb and Hulda
(Pfeifle) Graff.  She attended country school and graduated from high school.
She taught country school for four years and worked at the Hosmer school as an
aide and secretary for 13 years.

They have five children.  Dennis Dean lives in Newberg, Oregon.  Mrs. Peter
(Dixie Lee) Janker lives in Colorado Springs, Colorado.  Leon is deceased.
Mrs. Victor (Lori) Brandner lives in Sioux Falls, South Dakota and Becky lives
at home.  They have one grandson, Paul Leon Janker.

MILBERT and ADELINE MOHR

Milbert C. Mohr was born November 18, 1924 to Christian and Magdalena Pietz
Mohr.  He was raised on the family farm about 12 miles northeast of Hosmer with
his two sisters: Mrs. Mathilda (Art) Rohrbach, Hosmer (see Art and Mathilda
Rohrbach History), and Mrs. Irene (Warren) Schnaible, Roscoe.  Milbert went to
a rural school in the area and received an eighth grade education.

Lena (Adeline) Walth was born September 4, 1925 to George and Margaret
Guthihiller Walth.  She was raised on the family farm northeast of Hosmer with
her seven brothers and sisters: Calvin, deceased; Mrs. Miriam Lauer; Otto;
Robert, deceased; Leona Miller; Paul; and Delbert.  Lena attended rural school
and received an eighth grade education. She lived on the family farm until the
time of her marriage to Milbert Mohr on February 4, 1945.

Milbert and Lena were blessed with seven children: LeRoy, born July 13, 1945,
married Pam Barnes and has three children; Kathy Kimmes, born June 17, 1947,
has one child; George, born October 26, 1950 (see George and Mary Jo Mohr
History); Robert, born October 7, 1953, married Karen Schock and has one child;
Janet, born November 21, 1955, has one child; Ivadell, born February 21, 1957;
and Daniel Derek, born March 6, 1967 and died March 17, 1971.

Milbert has been living and farming on the family farm his entire life.  Lena
is currently employed as a cook at the Hosmer Senior Citizen's Home.  They are
members of St. John's Lutheran Church in Hosmer.

MILTON and BETTY MOHR

Milton M. Mohr was born October 31, 1937 to Theobald and Bertha (Maisch) Mohr
in rural Hosmer.  Milton attended a rural school until the eighth grade,
graduated from Hosmer High School, and attended Northern State College.  He
taught in a rural school for one year and in 1959 enlisted in the U.S. Army.
After serving in the army, he joined the Mohr Construction Company.

On June 24, 1962 he married Betty J. Aman.  Betty was born June 12, 1939 to
Herbert and Erna (Weisz) Aman of Hosmer.  After graduating from Hosmer High
School, she attended Northern State College to major in elementary education
and minor in speech and art.  In 1980 she earned her masters in guidance and
counseling.  She has taught in the Bowdle and Hosmer schools.

After renting a house for several years, they built a new home in 1966.

The couple has five children.  Margo Ann was born on March 8, 1963.  After
graduating from Hosmer High School, she attended Mary College where she
received a bachelor's degree in nursing.  Margo married Dennis Gab on August 10,
1985.

Karla Mae, born on May 27, 1964, graduated from Hosmer High School in 1982 and
later attended Bismarck Junior College and majored in medical secretary.

Paul Milton, born on August 22, 1966, graduated from the Hosmer High School in
1984 and enlisted in the Air Force.  After basic training in Texas he was
stationed at Luke Air Force Base in Arizona.

Jayne Marie was born on August 5, 1970 and Sarah Jo was born on May 1, 1976.
Both are attending the Hosmer school.

RAYMOND MOHR FAMILY HISTORY

Raymond was born to Jacob and Pauline Maisch Mohr near Hosmer on December 13,
1930.  He graduated from Hosmer High School in 1949.  He served in the U.S.
Army for two years during the Korean Conflict.  After discharge from the
service, he worked for Holsworth Brothers Construction Company of Mobridge for
several years.  In 1957, Ray joined his father in construction work and they
organized as J and R Construction.  The company dissolved in 1983.  Raymond did
construction work for two years and in 1985 began work for WEB Water
Development in the local area.

Raymond married Bernice Thomas Malsam December 26, 1962, at Hosmer.  He adopted
Richard and Donald Malsam.  Bernice was born March 4, 1936 to Adam and Jennie
Thomas near Bowdle, South Dakota.  Bernice graduated from Bowdle High School
and has a degree in elementary education from NSC, Aberdeen.  Bernice taught in
the rural schools for four years and in the Hosmer school system in 1962-63.

Sons of Raymond and Bernice are: Richard, born August 28, 1958, and married
Peggy Eberhardt May 17, 1986. They are employed in Aberdeen, South Dakota.
Donald was born September 28, 1959, and married Cathy Malsam November 6, 1982.
The couple is employed in Huron, South Dakota.  Thomas was born November 9,
1963 and is employed by South Dakota Wheat Growers, Aberdeen, South Dakota.
Clyde was born June 10, 1966 and is a student at South Dakota School of Mines
and Technology, Rapid City, South Dakota.  John was born July 11, 1973 and is a
student at Hosmer Public School.

STANLEY and KATHLEEN MOHR

Stanley Gene Mohr was born June 26, 1950 to Helmuth and Rena (Martel) Mohr.  He
attended Hosmer school and graduated from Hosmer High School in 1968.  He
attended North Dakota State School of Science in Wahpeton for two years.  He
spent from 1970-72 in the armed forces, serving in Vietnam.

On October 26, 1974 he married Kathleen M. Heier. Kathleen was born on August
3, 1954 to Adam and Margaret (Hopfinger) Heier.  She attended Hosmer school and
graduated from Hosmer High School in 1972.

Three children were born to this union: Matthew G. was born July 26, 1975,
Stacy M. was born May 31, 1978, and Heather Ann was born December 22, 1980.

Stanley and Kathy own and operate Stan's Auto Service of Hosmer and both belong
to the Civic Club.  Stanley is a member of the volunteer fire department and
the American Legion.  Kathleen is a member of the Holy Trinity Christian
Mothers and the Legion Auxiliary.

WALTER and MARCELLA MOHR

Walter Mohr was born on a farm 12 miles northeast of Hosmer to Fred and
Fredricka Schmitt Mohr.  His parents were homesteaders in McPherson County.  He
was the youngest son in a family of seven.  He was educated in a one-room
country school.  He served in the infantry in the South Pacific during World
War II.

After returning to Hosmer, he began working in the Gamble Store and soon
started playing at dances as a drummer with his employer, Gerhardt Bauman.  He
married Marcella Stacker at the Hosmer Reformed Church.

In 1950, he accepted the position of bulk plant manager for the Standard Oil
Co. In 1955, he went into the produce business and in 1960 moved to Lodi,
California where he is presently employed at the Guild Wine Co. and is a drummer
with the Elmer Aippersbach Band.  He is a member of the VFW and the Dakota Club.

Marcella Mohr was born in Hosmer to John and Martha Mehlhaff Stacker, the elder
of two daughters.  She attended Hosmer Public School and was employed at the
Eureka Hospital and at Koerner's Drug Store in Hosmer for several years.
Later, she married Walter Mohr and they have a daughter, Margie.  Today, they
reside in Lodi, California where she is employed at a local hospital and has
been a church organist for many years.

WERNER MOHR HISTORY

Werner was born October 23, 1941 to Jacob and Pauline Maisch Mohr.  Werner
attended grade school and high school in Hosmer.  Following graduation he
became a partner of Jake Mohr and Sons Construction.  Werner served in the U.S.
Army from 1964 to 1966.  He married Berniece Bellack on June 1, 1966.  He
worked in the construction business until 1982.  In 1982, he began working as
an insurance adjustor for Northwest G. F. Mutual Insurance in Eureka.

Berniece was born in Faulkton on September 13, 1944. She grew up in the Seneca
area and attended a country grade school.  She graduated from Faulkton High and
NSC. Berniece taught in Hosmer from 1965 to 1967.  Berniece now does upholstery
work and owns a shop called Berniece's Upholstery and Woodcraft.

Werner and Berniece have three children.  Pauline was born July 11, 1967 and is
attending NSC.  Joleen was born November 24, 1968 and is attending Hosmer High
School. Michael was born August 24, 1973 and is a student at Hosmer elementary.

WILLIAM C. and ANNA G. MOHR

William C. Mohr was born September 20, 1907 to Gottfried and Fredricka Mohr,
northeast of Hosmer in Blaine Township in McPherson County.

On November 16, 1930 he was married to Anna Guthmiller in the Jehovah Lutheran
Church.  Mr. Mohr and his young bride farmed 1 1/2 miles from the home place
until moving into Hosmer.  Willie, as he is known to his friends, served as
church deacon, Sunday school teacher and trustee of the Jehovah church,
chairman and clerk of the school board, township assessor, director of the Coop
in Hosmer.

This union was blessed with 11 children: Arlene (Curtis) Job, Leola; Elton
Richmond, Wisconsin; Deloris (Lloyd) Seiler, Eureka; Elnora (Ben) Jung,
Aberdeen; Eileen "Vera" (Ray) Jung, Roscoe; Eunice Schaible, Aberdeen; Linda
(Lyle) Stabbe, Lyons; Wylie, Missoula, Montana, manager of an apartment house
and refrigeration repair work; Charles, Hosmer, works for the Edmunds County
highway department; Cynthia (Ronald) Kauck, Artas, owns and operates a beauty
shop in Artas; and Wilma, who passed away in infancy.

DONALD and TAMARA NEIFER

Donald L. Neifer was born January 21, 1956, to Ruben and Freida Neifer.  Don
lived on a farm until 1962, when his family moved to Hosmer.  Don attended and
graduated from Hosmer High School in 1974.  He is presently employed at
Schurr's Manufacturing at Roscoe.

Tamara L. Jeschke Neifer was born December 22, 1959, to Oscar and Martha
Jeschke.  She grew up on a farm one-half mile northeast of Roscoe.  Tammy
graduated from Roscoe High School in 1978.  She has been employed as activity
director at the Senior Citizen's Home of Hosmer for the last 5 1/2 years.

Don and Tammy were married on December 1, 1979, in Roscoe.  They have one son,
Corey, born on November 23, 1982.  The Neifers are members of St. John's
Lutheran Church of Hosmer.

JOHN and CINDY NEIFER

John Christ was born to Lenhart and Ella Wolff Neifer at Eureka, South Dakota.
John attended grade school in Moscow Township, graduating from Hosmer High
School in 1967.  He served as a MP from June 1968 to January 1970 in Thailand.
After his discharge, he returned to the farm.

Cindy Jean was born at Fort Riley, Kansas to Floyd and Gladys Zimmerman Beck.
Cindy attended elementary grades at Artas and graduated from Herreid High
School and completed a secretarial course at Mitchell Vo-Tech.  She was
employed as vice president's assistant at NWGF at Eureka.

John and Cindy were married May 26, 1979 and ate living on the family farm John
purchased in 1978.  They are the parents of three children: Jeanne Marie, born
April 13, 1980; Carmen Kay, born May 14, 1981; and Travis John, born November
6, 1984.

John and Cindy are members of the Hosmer Reformed Church.  John is also active
with the Hosmer American Legion and McPherson County Extension.

JOHN and ROSINA NEIFER

John Neifer was born August 10. 1885. in Bon Homme County, Menno, South Dakota
to John and Katherina Wruck Neifer.  He moved to Leola with his parents where
they lost all their possessions in a house fire.  After this, they moved to
Eureka where his father worked as a well-driller until his death in 1890.  John
and his mother moved back to Yankton where they lived with relatives.

In 1901 John returned to the vicinity of Hosmer.  He became employed by John
Ehresmann for seven years and Uncle Jacob Gross for two years.

On October 27, 1908, John was united in marriage to Rosina Hieb.  Rosina was
the daughter of Michael and Rosina Borkirchert Hieb.  They lived on a farm 13
miles east of Hosmer until they retired in 1948 and moved to the city of
Hosmer.  John and Rosina were blessed with nine children.

Jacob was born October 6, 1909.  He was killed in an auto accident at the age
of 19 years on September 16, 1928.

Ruben was born November 20, 191 1. fie married Frieda Wanner of Eureka.  They
lived on the family farm until forced to retire due to ill health.  Ruben died
on November 20, 1978. Their children are Dennis and Donnie of Hosmer.

Lenhart was born September 10, 1913.  He married Ella Wolff of Eureka.  Their
children are: LeRoy, Shirley, John, and Elaine.  A set of twins died in infancy.

Wanitha was born July 15, 1916, and married Gideon Jung of Roscoe.  They have
one son, Allan.

Ella was born April 24, 1918.  She married Ben Gramm of Hosmer.  To this union
three sons were born: Calvin, James, and Gary.  A further history of the Ben
Gramms is also in this book.

Calvin was born October 16, 1921.  He is now a resident of the Senior Citizen's
Home in Hosmer. Leo was born May 2, 1923 and married Violet Gill of Eureka.
They have one daughter, Jeraldine. Leo graduated from Hosmer High School and
Northern State College in Aberdeen.  He also served in the U.S. Army for a few
years. Leo and Violet currently reside at Atlanta, Georgia.

Tillman was born September 11, 1926.  He died at the age of 1 1/2 years on May
17, 1928.

Ruth was born October 4, 1929.  She married Melvin Schumacher and they have one
son, Dean.  A more extensive history of Ruth and Melvin is also in this book.

John and Rosina were residents of the Hosmer Senior Citizen's Home at the times
of their deaths.  Rosina died March 9, 1977 and John died on September 20, 1982.

LENHART and ELLA NEIFER

Lenhart Neifer was born 13 miles northeast of Hosmer, South Dakota to John and
Rosina Hieb Neifer.  Ella Neifer was born in Perkins County, South Dakota on
April 20, 1915 to Mr. and Mrs. Christ J. Wolff.  They were united in marriage
on October 10, 1937 at the Zion Lutheran Church of Eureka.

They farmed 18 miles northeast of Hosmer on what was known as the "old Jakober
farm" until October 1978 when they sold the farm to their son, John.  Since
their retirement in 1979, Mr. and Mrs. Neifer have lived in their new home in
Hosmer.  They are members of the Hosmer Reformed Church.

Six children were born to the Neifers.  Twin sons were born December 5, 1940,
both died in infancy.

Their living children are: LeRoy, married Karen Bender of Ashley, North Dakota.
They have three children and farm northeast of Hosmer.  Shirley is married to
John Linde of Custer, South Dakota.  They live in Wilmington, Delaware and have
two sons.  John is married to Cynthia Beck of Artas and they have three
children.  Elaine is married to Bernie Tomek Jr. They have three children and
live in Aberdeen.

LEROY L. and KAREN NEIFER

LeRoy L. Neifer was born to Lenhart and Ella (Wolff) Neifer April 6, 1939.  On
March 6, 1968 he married Karen Bender at Ashley, North Dakota.  Karen's Grandma
Bender was a daughter of George and Christine Rueb.

LeRoy and Karen farm on the former Peter Hauck farm, 15 miles northeast of
Hosmer.

They have three children: Stacy, born January 30, 1970; Michael, born May 9,
1972; and Russell was born February 5, 1975.  They are members of the Hosmer
Reformed Church.

RUBEN and FRIEDA NEIFER

Ruben was born November 24, 1911 near Hosmer to John and Rosina (Hieb) Neifer.
He lived and farmed with his parents until his marriage to Frieda Warmer of
Eureka in 1951.  She was born March 7, 1914 near Eureka to Carl and Magdalena
(Preszler) Warmer.  Frieda lived at home with her parents and worked in Eureka
until her marriage.

Ruben and Frieda farmed the family homestead 13 miles east of Hosmer, until
1962 when they moved into Hosmer. Ruben was employed at Walth Motor Co.,
Edmunds County Highway Dept., and other part-time jobs until his death November
23, 1975.  Frieda was employed at the Hosmer Senior Citizen's Home from
1966-1984.  She is currently residing in Hosmer.

Two sons were born to this couple.  Dennis was born August 7, 1953 at Ipswich
and graduated from Hosmer High School in 1971.  He is presently the rural mail
carrier at Hosmer.  Donald was born January 21, 1956, at Ipswich and graduated
from Hosmer High School in 1974.  He is married to Tamara Jeschke and they have
a son, Corey.  Don is employed at Schurr's at Roscoe.

PAUL and SOPHIA OBERLANDER

Paul Oberlander was born in Odessa, Russia, on May 27, 1877.  He came to
America in 1900 to the Eureka area.  He married Sophia Kallenberger on December
22, 1902.  They moved on a farm eight miles southwest of Artas, South Dakota.
They lived there until 1917, when they moved to Hosmer, South Dakota, where
Paul did mason work, and was employed with Milwaukee Railroad.  He died on
April 28, 1937.

Sophia was a daughter of Baltzer and Margaret (Doffee) Kallenberger.  She was
born in Odessa, Russia, on October 30, 1880.  After the death of her husband,
she lived in Hosmer until 1952, when she moved in with her daughter, Helen
Bobby. She lived with Helen until she moved into the Senior Citizen's Home at
Hosmer.  She passed away on December 23, 1979, at the age of 99 year, 2 months.

Paul and Sophia had nine children, five boys and four girls.  Eight of the
children preceded the parents in death as infants.  Helen is the remaining
daughter.

Helen was born June 5, 1920, at Hosmer, South Dakota. On June 2, 1946, she
married LaVern Bobby at St. John's Lutheran Church.  LaVern and Helen lived on
farms in the vicinity of Beebe and Ipswich, South Dakota.  In 1955 they moved
to Roscoe, South Dakota, where they owned and operated "Bobby's Place." LaVern
became foreman on the Milwaukee Railroad, and worked the tracks between Roscoe
and Linton before the Milwaukee was abandoned.

Helen and LaVern have six children: Roger, Duane, Albert, William, Dale, and
Shirley.

LaVern passed away December 29, 1982 and is buried in Bethany Cemetery in
Hosmer, South Dakota.

Helen now lives at Country Village in Aberdeen, South Dakota.

SEBASTIAN OCHS FAMILY

Sebastian Ochs was born in Russia in 1875.  At the age of 18 he came to America
with his parents.  When he became of age he homesteaded west of Hosmer.

In 1899 he married Phillipine Reinbold at Hillsview, South Dakota.  Mr. and
Mrs. Ochs had nine children.

In 1933 they retired and moved to Aberdeen, South Dakota.  Mr. Ochs passed away
in 1961 and Mrs. Ochs in 1962.

LEW and CAROL PAULSON

Lew Paulson, son of Henry and Helen (deceased) Paulson was born in 1950 at
Pierpont, South Dakota.  He graduated from Northern State College after which
he began his teaching and coaching career at the Hosmer school system.  He is
still with the Hosmer school system.  In 1984 he was united in marriage to
Carol Job.  Carol is the daughter of Martha Job and the late Sam Job, and was
born in 1955. Carol has been employed in her mother's grocery store (Martha's
Store) for the past 12 years.

Both Lew and Carol are involved with the sports programs in Hosmer.  Lew has
managed the summer program for several years.  Carol is a member of the
softball team.

ARNOLD and VERA PIETZ

Arnold is the son of Daniel and Ottillia (Knittel) Pietz. He was born January
5, 1920, at Hosmer, South Dakota.  He graduated from Hosmer High School in
1938, and spent six months in the Citizens Conservation Corps (CCC) at Camp
Custer, South Dakota.  In the summer of 1939 he worked for Armour and Co. at
West Fargo, North Dakota.

As a member of the South Dakota National Guard ("A" Battery of the 147th Field
Artillery Regiment) stationed at Aberdeen, South Dakota, he was called into
active service on November 6, 1940 when all national guard units were activated
under the National Conscription Act.  His unit was transferred to Ft . Ord,
California, for further training.  While stationed at Fort Ord he continued his
education, taking a course in civil engineering.  Prior to the declaration of
war he transferred to the 75th Field Artillery also stationed at Ft. Ord.
During his military service he served in the North Pacific Theatre (Aleutian
Islands), Africa, and the European Theatre of operations (Italy and Austria).
He was discharged from active military services in October 1945.

Upon his return from military service he joined the firm of G. J. Walth & Co.,
Inc., dealing in Super Valu groceries and general merchandise.  He was active
in this business until 1969 when the business was sold to James Bauer.  In the
interim, in 1948, along with Earl Walth, they established a bowling alley
business known as the Lucky Strike Lanes.  This business was sold to Mrs.
Leonard Mayer in 1956.  In 1969 he established the manufacturing firm under the
name of Lustermar Cultured Marble Mfg.  Co., manufacturing cultured marble
products.  This firm was sold to Chuck Pietz in January 1982.

As an active member in civic and community affairs he served as commander of
the American Legion, president of the Hosmer Civic Club, Hosmer Medical Board,
and the Hosmer Independent School District, mayor of the city of Hosmer,
chairman of the 75th Jubilee Committee, chairman of St. John's Lutheran Church,
member of the American Missions Board of the S.D. District of the ALC, delegate
to the S.D. District of the ALC, Edmunds County Civil Defense director,
chairman of the Edmunds County Republican Central Committee, and served on the
Fargo Division of Super Valu Stores, Inc. as a member of the advisory council.

On May 8, 1942 he married Vera Walth, daughter of Gottlieb and Emma (Herrboldt)
Walth.  Vera was born July 11, 1920.  She graduated from Hosmer High School in
1938 and then attended Northern State Teachers College in Aberdeen, South
Dakota.  She taught school for three years. During World War II she was
employed by Douglas Aircraft as a bookkeeper at Long Beach, California.  In
1945 she joined the firm of G. J. Walth & Co., Inc. as manager of the dry goods
department and bookkeeper.  Vera has been active in community and church
affairs as a Sunday school teacher, church choir, ALCW, and a member of the
civic club.

The Pietzes have three children.  Bonnie was born June 11, 1945.  She graduated
from Hosmer High School and from Northern State College with a B.S. degree
majoring in elementary education, with minors in music and special education.
In 1970 she received her M.S. degree in guidance and counseling.  She has
taught in the Leola and Roscoe schools, and presently is teaching in the Hosmer
school.  In 1966 she married Dale Roesch of Roscoe, South Dakota.  They have
three children: Jessica, Edward and Lindsey.  They are living on a farm
northwest of Roscoe.

Sherida was born February 1, 1949.  She graduated from Hosmer High School and
Northern State College with a B.S. degree in speech correction and English.  In
1971 she received a M.S. degree in special education.  She taught in several
schools in South Dakota and Minnesota.  She married Orville Hieb of Hosmer,
South Dakota, on May 31, 1970.  They have three children: Nathan, Rebecca and
Andrew.  They live in Blaine, Minnesota.

Charlton lives at Mina Lake, Mina, South Dakota.

The Pietzes had two sons who passed away in infancy: Daniel and Timothy.

CHARLTON and CHERYL PIETZ

Charlton (Chuck), the son of Arnold and Vera (Walth) Pietz, was born January 4,
1955.  He graduated from Hosmer High School in 1973.  He attended Northern
State College, graduating in 1978, with a B.S. degree in coaching and physical
education.  He was the head football, basketball and track coach at Roslyn High
School, Roslyn, South Dakota, in 1978-79.  In 1980 he went to work for
Lustermar Cultured Marble Mfg.  Co., and in 1982 he purchased this company and
is presently the chief executive officer of the company.

He was selected to the All State football team in his senior year in high
school.  He lettered in college football for three years, he is a member of the
"N" Club.

He married Cheryl Keller, daughter of Ed and Mary Keller, Aberdeen, South
Dakota, on August 14, 1981.  Cheryl graduated from Roncalli High School,
Aberdeen, South Dakota, in 1974.  After graduating from high school she went to
work for 3M at Aberdeen, South Dakota, continuing her education by taking
computer classes.  She is a certified laboratrician in Quality Control for 3M.

The Pietzes have one daughter, Danielle Lee, born September 18, 1985.

Chuck and Cheryl reside at Mina Lake, Mina, South Dakota.

FIVE GENERATIONS OF THE CHRISTOPH PIETZ FAMILY

Christoph and Augustina Pietz settled in Cleveland Township, McPherson County
in 1896.  They spent sometime in the Grand Forks, North Dakota area as Jacob,
the oldest son and Juliana, the eldest daughter settled in that area.  Four
children came with the family to their new home--Theodore, Daniel, John and
Magdalena.  Christoph purchased a farm in Section 10 of Cleveland Township.
Theodore and John homesteaded land in Section 2, 3, and 11 in Cleveland
Township.  They received patents to their land from the United States
Government in 1907.

In 1909 Christoph, his wife and Daniel moved to Hosmer.  John and Theodore
married sisters, Katherine and Christina Pietz, daughters of Friedrich and
Christine Pietz. Other children of the Friedrich Pietz, were: Caroline (Fred)
Job, Magdalena (Christ) Mohr, Emma (Gottfried) Stoecker, Doretha (Fred)
Dockter, Lydia (Rudolph) Dewalt, Hilda (John) Wlasenko, Mathilda Tucher,
August, married to Sedonia Bashams, and Emil, married to Lydia Gutjahr.

John and Katherine had six children: Lydia Miller Junket; John; Justina
Hoffman; Gust, deceased in 1970; Adeline Bannerman; and Arthur, deceased.  John
and Katherine retired in 1924 and moved to Hosmer.  Gust and John then farmed
their father's land.

Gust married Magdalena Dockter in 1924.  Two sons were born to the young
couple: Alexander and Alfred, now deceased. (More history of this family
elsewhere.)

In 1931 John left the area and Gust purchased John's share of the land which he
farmed until 1946 when they moved into Hosmer.

In 1946, Alex received his discharge from the army and took over the farm.  In
December 1946 Alex was married to Irene Geist.  They have six children.

Linda (Jack) Heilman lives in Sioux Falls.  Linda is employed by Eros and Jack
owns a VCR rental business and owns and operates a convenience store in
Flandreau, South Dakota.  They have one daughter Stephanie.

Jean (Leonard) Heier lives in Rapid City.  Jean teaches and Leonard is employed
by Dakota Typewriter Co. They have one daughter Michelle.

Sandra (Bruce) Flittie and her husband farm in Cleveland Township.  Sandie is a
registered nurse and is employed part time at the Hosmer Senior Citizen's Home.
They have two children: Amanda and Brett.

Marilyn (Mark) Anderson lives in Vermillion where Mark is attending the
University.  They have two children: Jarrod and Sorene.

Glenn married Mary Steward, they live in Columbus, Nebraska where Glenn manages
a trailer court.  They have one son Tyler.

Steven farms with his father, previously he had worked in Phoenix, Arizona.

The Pietz farm consists of 1160 acres, mostly grassland. The Pietzes operate a
dairy, consisting of 100-plus cows in addition to 50 cow-beef herd.
Replacement dairy heifers are also raised.  The cropland is used for forage
production.  Feed grains are purchased.

In the year 1986 it will be 90 years of continuous farming for the Pietz
family.  Steven is the fifth generation to farm the land.

Irene, wife of Alex Pietz, is the daughter of Jacob and Mary (Jundt) Geist.
She was born and raised on her parents' farm northeast of her present home.
Irene's parents have passed away.  Her brother Otto farms the home place; a
brother Marcus lives in Sioux Falls; Leah Knapp lives in Indianapolis, Indiana;
Sam lives in Ashley, North Dakota; and John lives in Rapid City.

DANIEL and OTTILLIA PIETZ

Daniel Pietz was born in South Russia in 1887.  In 1893 his parents immigrated
to the United States and took up a homestead in McPherson County, about 12
miles northeast of Hosmer.  Dan completed his education at Redfield College,
Redfield, South Dakota.  In 1908 he went to work for Dr. Quinn as a druggist.
In 1920 he built the Hosmer Drug Store (presently Stoecker's Jewelry), and
operated a drugstore until 1931.  He took the position of acting postmaster at
Hosmer from 1933 to 1935.

He married Ottillia Knittel, the daughter of William and Jacobina Knittel, in
1914.  Ottillia was born in Freeman, South Dakota, on January 3, 1894.  In
1901, her parents moved to Hosmer where her father went into the general
merchandise and grocery business, operating a store where the city liquor store
now stands.

The Pietzes made their home in Hosmer until 1942, when they moved to Lodi,
California.  Here they made their home until their passing.  Dan died in 1954
at the age of 67.  Ottillia died in 1985 at the age of 91 years.

The couple had four sons: Richard (deceased in 1955); Harold, living in
Oroville, California; Arnold, living in Hosmer, South Dakota; and Farroel
(Fido), living in Fresno, California.

SAM and MATILDA PIETZ

Sam Pietz was born on a farm northeast of Hosmer to Michael and Katherine Pietz
and moved into town when he was a young boy.  He attended school here and
graduated from Hosmer High School in 1932.  Sam with the Melhoff brothers,
Rudolph and Gottfried, started and operated the Liquor Store in Hosmer, which
they owned.  Pietz and the Melhoffs built the dance hall in 1940, now the
present 102 Ball Room.  They owned and managed it for many years as a dance
hall and a theatre.  When the Melhoffs moved away from Hosmer, Sam continued
with the business.  He later built a new theatre on Hosmer's Main Street in
1958.

He was also co-owner of Pietz and Walth Motor Co. for 13 years and drove a
school bus for 25 years.

Sam was a great athlete and was manager of the Hosmer baseball team for many
years.  In 1962 his name was added to the South Dakota Amateur Baseball Hall.
of Fame in Lake Norton, South Dakota.

Sam's health began to fail and he was ailing for a number of years.  He passed
away in February 1980 at the age of 67.

Matilda Pietz (nee Walth) was born to parents Jacob and Margaret (nee Huber)
Walth in Hosmer.  She worked as a telephone operator at Hosmer for 15 years.
She was married to Sam Pietz in 1939.  They have two sons: Clyde and Milo.
After the boys were through school and went to college, Matilda was employed at
the Walth Super Value Store and later was cook at the Hosmer school for 14
years.  She is now retired and lives in Hosmer.

Clyde Pietz was born at Hosmer and attended school there.  After graduation
from Hosmer High, he went to the School of Mines at Rapid City and graduated as
a civil engineer.  He was inducted into the U.S. Navy and served four years.
He was married to La Vaughn Roesch and has two children: Marcia and Mike.  La
Vaughn is a registered nurse, Marcia is attending USD in Vermillion and Michael
is attending NSC in Aberdeen.  Clyde is presently employed as the director of
the Division of Operations in the Department of Transportation in Pierre, South
Dakota.

Milo Pietz was also born at Hosmer, attended school there and graduated from
NSC in Aberdeen.  He taught and coached for several years.  He is now making
his home in Minneapolis where he is employed.

ADAM PRESSLER SR.FAMILY

Adam Pressler Sr. came to America in 1876 at the age of nine with his parents,
Martin Sr. and Elizabeth Melhoff Pressler.  His parents settled at Freeman,
South Dakota.  In 1892 he was united in marriage with Barbara Goehring,
daughter of Gottfried and Margaret Goehring.  In 1903 the family moved to
Eureka, South Dakota and in 1904 moved to Hosmer, South Dakota.  He purchased a
share in the Hosmer Flour Mill where he was a partner and shareholder.

In 1913 he started a hardware store and furniture store in partnership with his
son, Adam Jr. and George Guthmiller. In 1924 the partnership was dissolved when
Adam Jr. purchased a hardware store in Selby, South Dakota.  At this time Mr.
Guthmiller took the furniture store business and Adam Pressler Sr. sold his
share in the flour mill and became sole owner of the hardware store.

Mr. Pressler became ill and his son-in-law, Albert Storm, became a partner in
the business.

Mr. Pressler was an officer of the Reformed Church and was active in community
work until his death in 1930.  Mrs. Pressler lived in Sheboygan, Wisconsin
until her death in 1970 at the age of 96.

The Presslers were blessed with 13 children: Adam Pressler Jr., deceased 1981;
Martha (Mrs.  Jacob Sonnenfeld), Minneapolis, Minnesota; Lydia (Mrs.  Reinhold
Goehring), deceased 1974; Emil, who died at age 25 in 1925; Melita (Mrs. Tony
Reinboldt), Spring Valley, California; Esther (Mrs. Dave Bieber), Hutchinson,
Minnesota; Hulda (Mrs.  Henry Gross), deceased 1979; Lea (Mrs.  Albert Storm),
Sheboygan Falls, Wisconsin; and five children who died in infancy.

To date, survivors of Adam Sr. and Barbara Pressler are 4 daughters: Martha,
Melita, Esther, Lea; 1 son-in-law, Tony Reinboldt; 29 grandchildren; 103
great-grandchildren; 72 great-great-grandchildren; and 1
great-great-great-grandchild.

ADAM JR. and JACOBINE PRESSLER

Adam Pressler Jr. was born in 1893 at Freeman, South Dakota to Adam Sr. and
Barbara Pressler.  In 1903, at the age of 10 years, his family moved to Eureka,
South Dakota and in 1904 they moved to Hosmer, South Dakota.  He was educated
in the Hosmer grade school and attended Redfield College. After college he was
employed with the railroad in Hosmer and in 1913 he became a partner with his
father in the hardware and furniture business.

On November 5, 1914 he married Jacobine Blumhardt, daughter of Carl and Rosina
Blumhardt.  Mr. and Mrs. Pressler built their new home in 1916 in Hosmer.  The
home is directly south across the street from the Reformed Church. Adam Jr. was
a charter member of the Hosmer Commercial Club.  The Presslers had two sons:
Harold (Harry) and Emanuel (Manny), who were born in their new home --Harold in
1917 and Emanuel in 1918.

In 1924 Adam Jr. purchased a hardware store in Selby, South Dakota where they
resided for 46 years.  They then moved to Phoenix, Arizona where Adam passed
away in 1981 and Jacobine in 1982.  The oldest son, Harold, passed away in
Phoenix in 1977.  Manny is retired and resides in Arvada, Colorado.

ALVIN and MILDRED PRESZLER

Alvin Preszler was born June 3, 1933 in Edmunds County to Arthur and Anna
Himmerich Preszler.  He attended grade school in Edmunds County and graduated
from Hosmer High School.  He grew up on the farm with his parents southeast of
Hosmer and married Mildred Schumacher November 21, 1954.  Mildred was born June
28, 1933 to Gottlieb and Emma (Graf) Schumacher.  She attended grade school in
Edmunds County and grew up on her parents' farm southeast of Hosmer.

Alvin and Mildred farmed for a year and then were employed at Aberdeen for a
year before Alvin entered the U.S. Army.  Upon his return in 1958, they bought
a farm four miles east and one mile south of Hosmer where they are presently
farming.  They are members of the United Church of Christ in Hosmer.

Alvin and Mildred were blessed with three children.  Connie was born December
7, 1956 in Ft.  Ord, California and graduated from Hosmer High School and SDSU
in Brookings as an R.N. She married Neal Woehlhaff of Eureka where they reside.
Connie was director of nursing at the Senior Citizen's Home in Hosmer from
1979-1985 and is presently director of nursing at the Lutheran Home in Eureka.
They have two daughters: Heather Ann and Jessica Lynn.

Debra was born February 15, 1959 in Aberdeen.  She graduated from Hosmer High
School and attended Presentation College at Aberdeen for two years and worked
for Dr. Berbos as a dental assistant.  She is presently attending SDSU in
Brookings.

Kim was born in Aberdeen October 17, 1967 and graduated from Hosmer High School
and is presently attending Lake Area Vo-Tech in Watertown.

ARTHUR and ANNA PRESZLER

Arthur Preszler was born February 25, 1908 to John and Louisia (Handel)
Preszler.  He grew up on a farm southeast of Hosmer and married Anna Himmerich,

Arthur and Anna farmed the family homestead until 1970 when they retired and
