MARVIN T. and NORMA PRESZLER

Marvin Theodore Preszler was born in Hosmer, South Dakota on August 8, 1932.
His father was Reinhold Preszler and his mother was Helena (Krein) Preszler.
His parents lived and farmed in the Hosmer area most of their lives, where
Marvin grew up and attended rural school.  Marvin lived on a farm with his
parents until he was inducted into the army on the 9th of September 1953 and
served four months at Ft. Leonard Wood, Missouri.  After that he was sent
overseas to Paris, France, where he served the rest of his two years.  He
returned home on August 23, 1955, and farmed with his parents.

Norma Delores Schaible was born June 26, 1936, at Hosmer, South Dakota.  Her
father was Fred Schaible and her mother was Eva (Gutjahr) Schaible.  Norma
lived with her parents on a farm near Hosmer until October 1956.

Marvin and Norma were married on October 21, 1956, by Rev.  Meidinger in St.
John's Lutheran Church.  They lived with Marvin's parents until November 1957.
Then they moved to and rented a farm northwest of Roscoe.  In March of 1964
they bought a farm two miles east, two miles south, one-half mile east, and
one-fourth mile south of Hosmer. They have been farming there since, raising
cattle and crops of small grain.  They are members of the Hosmer Reformed
Church and Marvin is a member of the American Legion.

Marvin and Norma were blessed with a family of six (four girls and two boys).

Londa Lee was born April 13, 1958, graduated from Hosmer High School in 1976
and from Presentation College as a medical secretary in 1978.  On June 3, 1978,
Londa married Mathew Malsom of Roscoe, South Dakota, who was engaged in farming
10 miles southeast of Roscoe.  They are presently farming on the family farm
and have two boys: Kevin Mathew, born October 18, 1979; and Timothy Lee, born
March 6, 1984.

Darcy Rae was born on July 8, 1961, a graduate of Hosmer High School in 1979
and from Presentation College in 1981 with a business administration degree.
On September 26, 1981, she married Keith Kramlich of Eureka, South Dakota, who
is employed as a mechanic at K & A Implement in Eureka.  They now live in
Eureka and both are employed at K & A Implement.  Keith is the manager and
Darcy is the bookkeeper.  They have two daughters: Kari Lynn, born August 27,
1984; and Kelly Marie, born January 6, 1986.

Lisa Marie was born April 26, 1965, graduating from Hosmer High School in 1983.
She lived with her parents and was enrolled in a home course in veterinarian
assistance.  On October 6, 1984, Lisa married Rick Rau of Java, South Dakota,
who is employed as a mechanic with K & A Implement in Eureka.  Lisa and Rick
live on a farm east of Eureka. Lisa is employed at the Eureka Equity Exchange
and Rick is employed at K & A Implement.  Lisa received her diploma in
veterinarian assistance in October of 1985.

Stacy Lynn was born on August 28, 1968.  She is a senior at Hosmer High School

RAY and GLADYS PRESZLER

Raymond (Ray) Preszler was born March 30, 1932 north of Hosmer to Arthur and
Anna (Himmerich) Preszler.  He received his elementary education in a country
school in Sangamon Township and his freshman year at Roscoe.  He farmed with
his father until 1952, served two years in the U.S. Army, and then continued
his farming occupation.

Ray and Gladys Vetch of Roscoe were united in marriage June 1957 at the Hosmer
Reformed Church.  Gladys was born March 7, 1938 to John and Elizabeth Vetch and
received her elementary education and high school in the Roscoe Public School.
Gladys and Ray farmed 11 miles northeast of Hosmer and later purchased the Fred
Hieb farm.  In 1962 they moved to Hosmer and purchased a home from Jacob
Schaible.  Ray worked for Schlaht and Job as a mechanic and later worked for
the Hosmer Co-op.  In 1964 they moved to Redfield for a short time and then to
Aberdeen where the family is currently residing and are members of the Plymouth
Congregational Church.  Ray is employed at Safeguard Power Tech Products as a
mechanist since 1967 and was self employed doing furnace repair for 12 years.
He has served on the board of directors of Hub-Co Federal Credit Union for the
past nine years. Gladys is a homemaker and does babysitting in their home. She
enjoys cake decorating, crocheting, knitting and calligraphy writing as hobbies.

They have two children: Raylene, born March 7, 1958 and works at Control Data
in Aberdeen; and Karen, born November 25, 1962, is employed at Aberdeen Grain
Inspection and attends NSC.

REINHOLD and HELENA PRESZLER

Reinhold Preszler was born December 18, 1900, to Christof and Magdalina
(Schnabel) Preszler.  Christof was born in South Russia in 1873 and died at
Hosmer in 1946. Magdalina Schnabel was born in Menno, South Dakota in 1879 and
died at Hosmer in 1957.  They both were members of the Hosmer Reformed Church.
Reinhold lived with his parents on a farm in Menno until 1902, when they moved
to a farm five miles southeast of Hosmer.  A sod house was built and lived in
until 1906 when they built a new house.  Reinhold had three sisters and two
brothers.

On December 2, 1926, he married Helena Krein of Eureka, South Dakota.  Helena
was born on February 26, 1908.  She was the daughter of Friedrich and Margareta
(Mehlhaff) Krien who also came from South Russia.  They homesteaded in 1885
eleven miles southeast of Eureka. Friederich Krien died in 1936 and Margareta
died in 1942. Reinhold and Helena lived on a newly built farm two miles east
and three miles south of Hosmer where they grain farmed and raised cattle all
their lives.  They had seven children, two girls and five boys (three boys died
in infancy).

Irene was born February 1, 1929.  She married Lawrence Benson on December 26,
1954, and lives in Aberdeen.  They have four children: Kim married to Judy
Becker, Sonia married to Mike Arnoldy, Susan married to Robert Margheim, and
Sally.  They have seven grandchildren.

Marvin was born on August 8, 1932 and married Norma Schaible on October 21,
1956.  They live on Marvin's grandfather's farm home southeast of Hosmer.  They
have six children: Londa married to Mathew Malsom, Darcy married Keith
Kramlich, Lisa married Rick Rau, Stacy, Chad, and Jason.  They have four
grandchildren.

Luella was born December 28, 1935 and married Marvin Schumacher on December 12,
1954 and lives in Rapid City. They have three children: Cheryl, Greg, and Lori,
married to Kurt Weber.

Harry was born December 30, 1941, graduated from Hosmer High School in 1959 and
married Sylvia Forkel on February 25, 1968.  They live on the family farm and
have four children: Travis, Dale, Michelle and Jodi Marie.

Helena Preszler died on June 25, 1969 at the age of 61. Reinhold lived on the
farm with Harry and Sylvia until May 26, 1980 when he died at the age of 79.
They were both members of the Hosmer Reformed Church.

WILLIAM and ESTHER ROHRBACH

William (Bill) Rohrbach was born December 5, 1913, to Fred and Lydia (Hauck)

moved on Esther's parents' farm, which is now the Arnold Schmidt farm.

Bill passed away on August 5, 1959.  Esther moved into Hosmer in the fall of
1959, into the house in which she is presently living, which was her
grandparents' home.  Esther started working in the Senior Citizen's Home when

a cook's helper until November of 1985, at which time she retired.

and has been employed at Radio Shack, Lang's and Pitney-Bowes.

FRIEDRICH and EVA SCHAIBLE

Friedrich Schaible was born in South Russia on December 19, 1892.  He was the
son of Mr. and Mrs. Christian Schaible.  He came to America with his parents in
1907 at the age of 15, and they settled on a farm 6 1/2 miles southeast of
Hosmer.  In June 1918, he was inducted into the army and served 7 1/2 months in
Camp Funston, Kansas, being discharged in February 1919.  He was a member of
the Legion Post at Hosmer.

On November 18, 1919, he married Eva Gutjahr, daughter of Mr. and Mrs.,Jacob
Gutjahr.  She was born August 11, 1899.  Eva did all the sewing for her family
and has made approximately 100 quilts which she has given to her children,
grandchildren, and great-grandchildren.

Friedrich and Eva took over his parents' farm 6 1/2 miles southeast of Hosmer.
They became members of a country church, Hope Lutheran.  He was active in the
church--he served as a deacon for 20 years, treasurer, Sunday school teacher,
organist, and sang in the choir.  In January 1955, they became members of St.
John's Lutheran Church in Hosmer. They moved into Hosmer in December 1956.

Friedrich passed away October 25, 1959, and Eva resides at the Hosmer Senior
Citizen's Home.

They were blessed with six children: Adina (Mrs.  Alvin Kirschenmann), Leone
(Mrs.  Boas Spitzer), Edna (Mrs. Herbert Guthmiller), Hildegarde (Mrs.  Raymond
Lehr), Herbert (married Evelyn Anderson), and Norma (Mrs. Marvin Preszler).

six miles east of Hosmer.  He was in the Army from 1953-1955.  He married
Evelyn Anderson on November 11, 1956 at St. James Lutheran Church at Leola.
Her parents were Josephine and Carl Anderson of Forbes, North Dakota.  Evelyn

Donna is a junior at Hosmer High School.  She is also one of the organists at
St. John's Lutheran Church in Hosmer.

JACOB C. SCHAIBLE FAMILY

One of the various limbs of the Schaible tree which grew up in Hosmer was that
of Jacob Christian, who arrived in this country from Odessa, Russia in 1907,
having just graduated from the "gymnasium," a German secondary school.

Because he was the eldest son, and because the custom at the time was for the
eldest son to become a minister, Jacob was promptly enrolled at the Lutheran
College in Eureka, South Dakota.  The depression of 1911 changed that plan, and
he, like several of his friends, went to Montana to "homestead."

In 1917, he was married to Magdalena Morlock and soon after became a signalman
in the United States Army.  After being discharged he returned to Hosmer where
he was in business until his retirement in 1955.

While J. C. minded his business, Maggie minded hers.  It took both, especially
during the depression years when their children were growing up, to provide
opportunities for them, they themselves never had.  Like most parents of that
era, a good education for their children was most important.  J. C. worked hard
to get a high school built in Hosmer.  He believed that once you have a high
school you would have a band, a basketball team, a good library, and more and
better teachers.  This is where Maggie and other mothers like her became
involved.  Maggie didn't play the piano herself but she recognized that for
children to learn to play the piano it would not only make for a bigger and
better band but allow each child to enjoy music as adults.  So, her house
became the "music" school every Saturday where for years young people came to
take their lessons from an "imported" teacher.  Her own children all played in
the band and played the piano for their own enjoyment.

Jacob was very active in the community serving for years as secretary and/or
treasurer of the school board, he was the first commander of the Adolph Forkel
American Legion Post, he was active both as a Sunday school teacher and deacon
of St. John's Lutheran Church.

The Schaibles had four children: Clifford and his wife Caroline live in Dayton,
Ohio, they have two children; Aurora (Maynard) Williams lives in Bismarck, North
Dakota, they have three children; Arlovina (Rev.  Donald) Hesterman lives in
Cleveland, Ohio, they have four children; and Ayeliffe (Bob) Lenihan lives in
Silver Spring, Maryland, they have three children.

After Jacob's death in 1966 Mrs. Schaible spent some time with each of her
children.  On her 80th birthday she announced to her children that it was time
for her to go "home." She presently is residing in Hosmer.

JOHN and FRIEDERICKA SCHAIBLE

John Schaible was born May 1, 1895, in Glueckstal, Russia, to Christ and
Friedericka Schaible.  He came to the United States when he was 13 years old.
He married Friedericka Bieber in September 1919.  She was born November 24,
1894, in Russia, to Philip and Friedericka Bieber.  She came to the United
States at age 13 years.

They farmed seven miles southeast of Hosmer, South Dakota.  In May 1943 they
retired in Hosmer.  John died June 18, 1943 at the age of 48 years.
Friedericka died February 3, 1974 at the age of 79 years.  They were members of
St. John's Lutheran Church, and both are buried at Bethany Cemetery at Hosmer.

They had two daughters.  Alma was born October 13, 1920.  On March 7, 1943, she
married Ernest Guthmiller. Ernest was born January 11, 1920, to Philip and
Rosina Guthmiller.  They farmed on the John Schaible farm until November 1949,
when they moved to Aberdeen, South Dakota.  Ernest worked for the city of
Aberdeen, Sherman Hotel, and for DeVries Dairy, now Lakeside Terrace Park Dairy
for 32 years.  They have two children: Dean, who lives in Aberdeen, South
Dakota; and Carol Upton, who lives in Denver, Colorado.  They have one
grandchild, Jennifer Upton, who was born in 1972 in Hawaii.

Eleonora was married to Edmund Aman, son of Jacob and Louisa (Barth) Aman.
Eleonora and Edmund were living in Seattle, Washington, where she passed away
on February 10, 1974, at the age of 51 years.  Eleonora was just coming home
from her mother's funeral when she had a heart attack, and passed away.

ARTHUR and RUBY SCHILLING

Arthur was born at Ashley, North Dakota, his parents were Reinhold and Margaret
(Rall) Schilling, both deceased. Art was one of six children.  The family moved
from Ashley to a farm northeast of Leola.  Arthur served in the Korean
Conflict. He married Ruby Mayer at Hosmer Congregational Church in September
1953.

Ruby was born on the farm 18 miles northeast of Hosmer to Otto (deceased) and
Emma (Gugel) Mayer, residing at Mobridge, South Dakota.  Ruby was one of five
children.  The Schillings moved to Colorado in 1976 from Marshfield, Wisconsin.
The Schillings had previously lived on the farm five miles north of Hosmer for
several years.  They were members of St. John's Lutheran Church.  Ruby was
active with the Highlighters Extension Club.  They also had lived in the Leola
and Wetonka area.  Ruby served on the school board in Wetonka and worked at the
director of equalization office for several years in Leola.

Ruby and Art are now active with Valley Lutheran Church in Thornton, Colorado,
serving various functions and community affairs, i.e. Legion and DAV.  Ruby has

Hosmer High School in 1973.  Dale attended Brookings College and served in the
Air Force.  Dale is at home with his parents and has been working with the fire
sprinkler life saving service for several years.

JOHANN and ROSINA SCHILLING

Johann Schilling was born December 8, 1872, in Glueckstal, South Russia.  He
married Rosina Kessler November 27, 1895.  She was born June 28, 1873, in
Glueckstal, South Russia, to Jacob and Johanna Kessler.  They both were
educated in South Russia.

In April 1898, the young couple left Russia for the United States, arriving in
Eureka, South Dakota, and spent the first summer with Johann's sister and
husband, the Joe Gabs.  In the fall of 1898 they homesteaded southwest of
Wishek, North Dakota.  They moved to a farm ten miles west and one-half mile
north of Hosmer in 1903 and seven years later to a farm three-quarters of a
mile southwest of where they lived.  They lived on this farm until March 17,
1927, when they retired and moved to Hosmer.

Johann and Rosina were blessed with eight children, two died in infancy.

Jacob married Lydia Sandmeier, they have three children and live in Bowdle.

Christina married Edward Kaseman, they have eight children, and live in Wishek,
North Dakota.

Magdalina married Gotthold Sulzle, they have three daughters, and live in
Akaska, South Dakota.

Rose married Edwin Walth, they have six children, and live in Aberdeen, South
Dakota.

William lives in Columbus, New Mexico.

George died December 12, 1953, at Hosmer.

JOHN and MARTHA SCHICK

John Schick, son of William and Rosina (Oster) Schick was born August 14, 1909
at Venturia, North Dakota.

John's grandparents, Mr. and Mrs. Wilhelm Schick, came to America in 1888 from
Neudorf, South Russia and at this time John's dad was five years old.

William was  united in marriage to Rosina Oster.  They settled on a farm five
miles north of Bowdle and later they took up farming in the Venturia, North
Dakota area.  John was one year old when they moved to South Dakota and settled
on a farm nine miles northeast of Bowdle.  On this farm John grew to manhood,
attended rural school in Willow Township and was confirmed in the
Congregational Church in Hosmer.  The family did much of their trading in the
town of Hosmer.

On September 24, 1929 John was united in marriage to Martha Oster, daughter of
John and Christina (Severt) Oster. This was a difficult time for a young couple
to get started since it was the year of the stock market crash and drought
years followed.

John was engaged in farming for a number of years close to his father's farm
and later moved into Bowdle and was employed as a mechanic at the Bill
Schilling Auto Service Station.  In the late 30's they went back to farming and
farmed in various locations in the Leola and Hosmer area.  In 1955 they moved
to Washington and John was employed at the Puget Sound Electric Co. for 15
years.  Upon retirement they moved back to South Dakota.

John is presently living in Aberdeen and Martha passed away in 1975.

Eight children were born to this couple: Lorraine, who died in infancy; Irene
(DeBord); Ardella (Pietz); Virginia (Hieb); Phyllis (Adair); Kenneth; James;
and Raymond.

JOHANN and BARBARA (EISENBEISZ) SCHLAHT

An early farmer in the Hosmer area was Johann Schlaht. He was born in 1853.  He
married Barbara Eisenbeisz.  They farmed at Glueckstal, South Russia until they
immigrated to the United States in 1893.  They farmed 1 1/2 miles south of
Hosmer for several years then moved about six miles south of Hosmer.

They had nine children with seven growing to adulthood. In 1909 Johann and
Barbara retired to Hosmer.  Daughter Barbara (born 1875) married Adolph Ehrmann
from Eureka. Son John (born 1878) married Lena Oberlander and started a
blacksmith shop with his brother Christ.  Son Jacob (born 1880) married
Christina Hertel, he was one of the first cream buyers in Hosmer.  Later he
moved to the McLaughlin area to farm.  Daughter Rosina (born 1890) married
Richard Huber, they farmed south of Hosmer all their lives.  Son Joseph (born
1896) married Katherine Stahlacker, he had a theater at Bowdle before moving to
California.  Son Fredrich (born 1893) married Rosina Walth.

JOHN JR. and LENA SCHLAHT

John Schlaht Jr. was born March 20, 1878, to John Sr. and Barbara (Eisenbeisz)
Schlaht in South Russia.  He came to America with his parents in 1894.  They
homesteaded on the edge of Hosmer--finding work in town when possible.  In 1908
he was united in marriage to Lena Oberlander, who was born October 19, 1885 to
Jacob and Bertha (Rositkska) Oberlander.  John Jr. also worked for the railroad
for a time.  He and his brother Christ opened a blacksmith shop working side by
side for a time.  Christ later moved to Bowdle leaving John to operate the
business on his own; being joined by a son and son-in-law and then selling his
share to them.

John and Lena were blessed with six children: Johnny, deceased; Albert, Renton,
Washington; Joe (died in infancy); Bertha (Mrs.  Emanuel Hauck), Sun City West,
Arizona; Martha (Job), Hosmer; and Emma (Cox), deceased.

ARNOLD and EVA SCHMIDT

Arnold Schmidt was born at Tolstoy, South Dakota, January 20, 1933, to Edward
and Marie (Heintz) Schmidt.

Eva Geier Schmidt was born to Anton and Mary Malsam Geier at Bowdle, South
Dakota, October 11, 1934.

Arnold's family moved to a farm eight miles west of Roscoe in 1947.  He
graduated from Roscoe High School in 1950.  In 1953, his family moved to
Washington.  Arnold joined the Army and was stationed in France.

Eva grew up on a farm four miles north of Bowdle with her parents and three
brothers and three sisters.  She attended country school for eight years.  Eva
graduated from Bowdle High School in 1952, attended Northern State College in
Aberdeen, South Dakota, and taught four years of rural school in the Bowdle and
Roscoe area.

On August 20, 1956, Arnold and Eva were married at St. Augustine's Catholic
Church in Bowdle, South Dakota.  They packed their belongings in their car and
traveled west.  They lived in Seattle, Washington, where Arnold worked for
Longview Fiber Co. and Eva worked for Kenworth Truck Co. Later they returned to
South Dakota and farmed two miles south of Hillsview for two years.  On
November 4, 1959, they moved to the farm they own and operate, located 16 miles
north and two miles east of Roscoe, South Dakota.

They are members of St. Thomas Catholic Church of Roscoe where Arnold is an
usher and serves on the church council.  Eva is a member of the Altar Society,
a catechism teacher and is a choir member.  Eva is also a member of the
Highlighters Extension Club.  Arnold is a member of the American Legion Post of
Hosmer, South Dakota.

Their home was blessed with five children.  Douglas was born June 15, 1957, and
is a graduate of Hosmer High School, and a graduate of LAVTI of Watertown, South
Dakota, in agribusiness and T.V. electronics.  He is parts manager at Gourley
Distributing Co. at Sioux Falls, South Dakota.

Anthony was born February 20, 1959, is a graduate of Hosmer High School and a
graduate of LAVTI of Watertown, South Dakota in building trades.  He is married
to Teresa Imberi.  They own Schmidt's Construction in Aberdeen, South Dakota.
Teresa is sales representative for Philip-Morris.  They have one son, Tanner.

Mariann was born October 22, 1960, is a graduate of Hosmer High School, and a
graduate of Northern State College in Aberdeen with a degree in accounting.
She is married to Lonnie Brokke and they reside in Watertown, South Dakota,
where Lonnie is recreation superintendent for the city.  They have two
children, Nicholas Lea and Cassie Marie.

Theresa was born September 26, 1967, is a graduate of Hosmer High School, and
will graduate from Presentation College in May 1987 as a medical records
technician.

Lisa Marie was born January 10, 1970 and is attending Hosmer High School, and
will graduate in 1988.

GOTTFRIED and LYDIA SCHNABEL

Gottfried Schnabel was the third eldest child born to Johann and Katherine
Kraemer Schnabel, born on October 19, 1898.  He married Lydia Maisch, daughter
of Mr. and Mrs. Ludwig Maisch.  Together they began farming 12 miles east of
Eureka, South Dakota.

Twelve children were born to this union: Allen and Martin, both of Fort
Collins, Colorado; Johnnie, Marvin, and Mrs. Irene (Herbert) Herman, all of
Eureka, South Dakota; Jimmie, Frazee, Minnesota; Mrs. Freida (Emil) Fieock,
Lodi, California; Mrs. Emilia (Norman) Mehlhaff, Hosmer (see Norman and Emilia
Mehlhaff History); Mrs. Lorine (Rheinie) Guthmiller, Excelsior, Minnesota; Mrs.
Elenor (Elmer) Nies and Mrs. Darlene (Al Nathan) Nies, both of St. Paul,
Minnesota; and Milbert, who died in infancy.

The Schnabels farmed for about 40 years and retired on their homestead.
Gottfried died on August 23, 1961 at the age of 63 years.  Lydia passed away on
June 2, 1984 at the age of 83 years in Eureka, South Dakota.

JOHANN and KATHERINE SCHNABEL

Johann Schnabel was born at Kassel, South Russia on November 5, 1858.  At the
age of 22 years he married Katherine Kraemer.  In 1883, Johann and Katherine
immigrated to America and settled in the Menno, South Dakota area.  In 1884
they moved to the Eureka, South Dakota area. Eleven children were born to this
union, one of whom was Gottfried.

KARL and IDA SCHOCK

Ida was born on September 3, 1906 to John and Christina (Kurle) Kirschenmann.
She attended the country school near their farm.  On December 16, 1948 she was
married to Jacob H. Rueb.  Jacob was born October 29, 1902.  The couple resided
in Hosmer where Jacob worked for the Milwaukee Railroad.  On July 3, 1969 Jacob
passed away.

Ida married Karl K. Schock on May 2, 1973.  Karl was born January 12, 1896.
After their marriage they moved to Long Lake, South Dakota, where Karl passed
away on March 29, 1984.  Ida moved back to Hosmer in 1984.  She is a member of
the U.C.C. church, the Women's Fellowship, and the American Legion Auxiliary.

JOHN SCHOCH SR.

Pioneer Experiences Written By John M. Schoch in 1949

I was born in Russia somewhere close to Odessa on March 29, 1889 to Joseph and
Katherine Benfit Schoch and arrived in Hosmer, Edmunds County, South Dakota the
first part of April - 1893 with my parents, one sister and two brothers.  As
for the voyage I cannot say much, being only four years old.  In those days all
the country around here was open for settlers.  A man or woman 21 years or over
could get 160 acres free from the government if they would build a shanty on it
and break up five acres of land.  My father had no money so he got a job on the
railroad as a section hand, 10 hours a day at $ 1.10 per day.  He built a sod
shanty next to the section house and there we lived until July 4, 1896, then he
moved to 160 acres of land right outside of town which he got from the
government free.  There is where we had our first fire crackers and did we have
fun.  Father built a sod shanty on the land, also a sod barn, it was all under
one roof.  In the sod shanty we had a stove made out of mud and brick that my
folks fired with straw and cow chips.

I had eight brothers and four sisters.  For beds we children had straw on the
floor with covers on it.  But the folks had a bed.  Our floors were bare
ground.  In that place we lived until 1898.  The winter 1896-1897 was a bad
one, all you could see of our house and barn was the chimney of the house, snow
was 10 to 12 feet deep in most places.  The winter started and stayed that way
until sometime in April.  People living a few miles from town did not get to
town all winter. We only had six blocks so we could go to town when we needed
something.  In those days there were no section lines, if we wanted to go to
Eureka, we followed the railroad or going to Bowdle we went across country.  In
the first years we used to have some bad winters, we had to get food for the
stock from the hay stack as the barns in those days had no room for it.

I stayed with my folks until I was 23 years of age.  During that time I had
some bad times and some good times.  When I was 20 years old, my folks allowed
me to go to dances and did have good times there.  I would take our team and
the buggy, take some of the town girls and boys along.  If the dance was in the
country in some barn upstairs, the horses were all in the barn below so that
helped keep us warm upstairs.  But most of the dances were in the town depot,
that was a better place to dance.  Those were good times and a lot of fun, good
old time dances.  When I was 22 I left home for awhile and worked as a railroad
brakeman, but quit that and went back home.

I was 23 when I married Katherine Kreitel (Katie as she was known) on May 20,
1912 at St. Thomas Catholic Church in Roscoe.  Katie was born May 14, 1892 in
the Mina area and grew up there.  We moved into Hosmer where I started a dray
line, had that four years, then started a farmers cream station, held that two
years.  By that time I had saved enough money to have a nice home in Hosmer and
had 320 acres of land, so I started farming, but started at the wrong time.
Prices dropped, crops were poor.  I sold wheat for 20 cents a bushel in 1932,
barley 8 cents, oats 3 cents, cream 8 cents a pound of butter fat, sheep wool 8
cents a pound.  I lost my land but held on to my house in town, which I later
sold to my sister, Carolina Gisi (now known as the Frank Gisi residence). In
1933 I was called in as a relief investigator, I held that job 16 months then
back home again.  The family was on the farm all that time from 1933-36.  We
had no crops at all, but from then on we started getting crops.  Prices went up
on everything so we farmers started to make money.  In 1943 in July I bought
the old home where I was raised, but in September that same year my wife died
so that set me back.  I farmed two more years then rented to one of my boys.

In 1946 I sold my belongings and bought a house in Aberdeen.  In our married
life we became parents to ten children.  Joseph was born June 15, 1913 and died
January 17, 1932.  Edward, born May 23, 1915, married Clara Malsom, has two
children, lives in Lodi, California.  John, born June 28, 1917, married Helen
Zimmerman, has three girls, lives in Aberdeen.  Helen, born May 18, 1919,
married Sylvester Schwan, is widowed, has five daughters and lives in Aberdeen.
Eva, born June 20, 1921, married John Roesler, has one son and lives in Lodi,
California.  Marion, born May 14, 1923, married Mathew Malsom, has eight
children and lives in Aberdeen.  Frances, born January 13, 1926, married
Milbert Guthmiller, has one son and lives in Minneapolis, Minnesota.  Gladys,
born January 14, 1928, married Marcus Streifel, has two children and lives in
Eureka.  George, born January 9, 1930, is single and lives in Bakersfield,
California. Harvey, born July 27, 1937, married Anna Bader, has two sons and
lives in Sacramento, California.

Katherine Schoch passed away on September 25, 1943 and John Schoch passed away
on February 5, 1950.  Both are buried in the Catholic cemetery in Hosmer, South
Dakota.

JOHN SCHOCH JR.

John Schoch Jr. was born in Hosmer on June 28, 1917 to John Schoch Sr. and
Katie (Krietel) Schoch.  Helen Zimmerman Schoch was born at Hillsview on
October 25, 1920. John, as a young man, was in the C.C.C. camps for two years
from 1937-1939.  John and Helen got married at Hillsview, South Dakota on June
26, 1939.  They had three children, all girls.

John and Helen farmed around the Hosmer area from 1941-1950 when he sold out
and moved to Aberdeen.  He worked for the highway department for 28 years until
retiring in 1979.  He enjoys his retirement.

Their children are: Lorretta, who married Robert Kazen and lives in Everett,
Washington.  She has five children. Judeen married Larry Colins and lives in
Blaine, Minnesota. They have two children.  Mary Jane married Bernard Reis and
lives in Watertown.  They have one child.

JOSEPH and KATHERINE SCHOCH

Excerpts from an account written by Joseph Schoch

Joseph Schoch, son of Felix and Barbara Schoch was born on July 20, 1860 and
Katherine Schoch, daughter of John and Frances Benfit, was born October 20,
1864.  They were married in South Russia on October 20, 1883.  All that
Joseph's father gave him when he went to work for himself was a prayer book,
but Katherine received two cows and her bed and bed clothing and some
kitchenware from her parents.

"We lived the first year of our marriage with my father. We were able to find
some land and went out on our own. The next two years were poor and then it
rained so much that what we did have planted rotted.  The following year we
moved in with my father again and bought two horses and an old wagon for 35
rubles all on credit.

Now that we had horses and a wagon, we began to farm for ourselves; but we made
slow progress as my father had only 25 desadin (similar to acres) of land and
there were my father, my brother, and myself to farm it.  We had to rent most
of our farming land.  Later my father quit farming and turned his land over to
my brother and me; however, there were some debts on the land that had to be
paid.

After we had farmed in this manner for seven years, a friend approached me with
the idea of going to America.  The crops had been poor so we had very little
money.  We sold our land for 300 rubles, but we could not get any money at that
time.  The purchaser of the land was to send us the money after we arrived in
America.  When the time came for us to leave, we received word that we couldn't
leave because of the cholera disease raging in some parts of Russia.  We had to
spend the winter in Russia, but what were we to live on?  We had previously
sold all our belongings.  It took the few rubles we had to live on that winter
of 1892.

In the spring of 1893 word came that we could go to America, so we went to
Odessa, purchased our traveling tickets for 193 rubles, and returned home to
pack for America with only 4 rubles in our pockets.  It was very hard to leave
our old home, especially our loved ones.  It was heartbreaking to see our poor
old folks crying so bitterly for their children whom they knew they would never
see again.  On the way to the depot from which we were to leave, one of our
children was born.  Our new son was just two days old when we left South Russia
for America.  We had three other small children. Three days and nights later we
arrived in Bremen, Germany, where we had to wait six days for the ship which
was to take us to America.  At this time my wife was so ill that I thought she
would die, and where did I want to go with four small children?  However, a few
days after we had boarded the ship with comfortable beds and good food, she
began to recover; and when we docked in New York, she was well and happy.

After we had been examined at the port of entry, we heard a rumor that anyone
who did not have at least $100 would have to be returned.  Of course, I had no
money.  When they asked me, I told them the truth.  However, when I told them I
was here to work, they slapped me on the back and said "good." Finally we were
off for Chicago and still later, for Aberdeen.  There we had to change trains
and we were off once more for Hillsview.  When we arrived in Hillsview, a
friend took us to his farm where we spent the night.  In the morning I went to
Eureka looking for work, but there was no work to be found.  The next day I
found three hours work loading wheat into box cars for 25 cents an hour.  The
following day I went to Hosmer and found a job working on the railroad.  I
worked three days for $ 1.10 a day plus 50 cents for board.  At this time I was
lonely for my family and told the foreman I would work all summer, but I would
need a little time off to bring my family to Hosmer.  This granted, I went to
Hillsview, packed our belongings, and moved into an empty dwelling one mile
southeast of Hosmer.  This house was half full of snow from the winter.  After
I had shoveled the snow out, I found a little cook stove and an old bed that had
to be fixed up.  When we made a fire in the stove and the house began to get
warm, the stove began making a gas so strong that my children got sick.  We
didn't know what was causing the illness but when lizards began to crawl up
between the cracks in the floor, we moved outside until I had the holes and
cracks closed.  By this time the children were recovering and the house was
drying out so we moved back into the house.

Many times during these trying days we cried and wished we had stayed in
Russia.  But what could we do?  We didn't have any money to go back home.
Also, we had to wait two months before I could get my pay for the first month's
work on the railroad.  Soon after this I became very ill and had to be taken to
Eureka to see a doctor.  I lay on my back for over a week and had a doctor bill
of $45.  This was a large bill considering that all I earned was $ 1.10 a day.

During one of the early winters in Dakota, we nearly froze to death because we
couldn't afford to buy any coal with which to heat our home.  We used wood and
chips which burned very fast, especially at night after we had gone to bed. We
had little to eat during the first and second winters in Hosmer as I was
repaying the money loaned to me by my many friends while enroute to America and
was getting established in the Hosmer community.  Our meals usually consisted
of bread, syrup, coffee, and milk.  In the spring of 1899 my children became
ill with diphtheria and one child died.

Times gradually became better and we prospered more and more.  It was at this
time that we began to thank God that we had been too poor to return to Russia."

The family farm was located one-half mile west of Hosmer.  Over the years,
Joseph became a prosperous farmer, at the time of his death he owned six
quarters of land.  He was also a leader in the local Catholic Church.  He was
one of the people instrumental in having the first church built in Hosmer and
also donated the land for the present Holy Trinity Catholic Cemetery.  He was
the organist in the church from the time it was built until his death, a
tradition that has been carried on in the Schoch family for four generations as
daughters Carolina Gisi and Frances Imbery, granddaughters Caroline (Gisi) Rath
and Rose (Gisi) Hanaburgh, and greatgranddaughter, Janet Gisi, have also
served as organists in the Holy Trinity Catholic Church.

Joseph and Katherine had nine sons: Adam and Jacob, who both died in infancy
and are buried in Russia; Edward, who died in infancy, and Joseph, who died at
eight years of age of diphtheria, both are buried in the Catholic Cemetery
eight miles west and three and one-half miles north of Hosmer; and Martin, who
died during the flu epidemic at the age of eighteen years; as well as John,
Ludwig, Felix, and Carl, who all married and established farms in the Hosmer
area.

They also had four daughters: Marion (Baer), Theresa (Lacher), Carolina (Gisi),
and Frances (Imbery).  Of all their children, Carolina and Theresa still
survive.

Joseph Schoch died on July 1, 1923 and Katherine on May 24, 1940.  Both are
buried in the Holy Trinity Cemetery. In 1983 when a reunion was held for the
descendants of Joseph and Katherine Schoch, 270 people were present of which
approximately 180 were direct descendants of the Joseph Schochs.

DEAN and BONNIE SCHUMACHER

Dean W. Schumacher was born December 14, 1950, in Edmunds County to Melvin and
Ruth Neifer Schumacher. Dean attended rural school and Hosmer Public Schools
graduating from Hosmer High School in 1969.  He then attended NSC receiving a
B.S. degree in secondary education. He lived with his parents on a farm east of
Hosmer while working part-time as a bartender at the Hosmer Liquor Store. He
became the manager of the liquor store in the fall of 1976.

Dean married Bonnie Harty on April 23, 1977.  Bonnie was born April 21, 1957,
to Joseph and Elizabeth Hartman Harty.  She attended Hosmer Public Schools
graduating from high school in 1975.  Bonnie worked at the Senior Citizen's
Home until the fall of 1979 when she started working at the Farmers State Bank
of Hosmer as a computer operator and teller.

Dean is presently employed as a sales representative for Sodak Distributing of
Sioux Falls.  Dean is a member of the Hosmer Volunteer Fire Department,
Sportsman Club and is Centennial Chairman.

Dean and Bonnie have one son, Darren, born April 20, 1982.

FRED and CLARA SCHUMACHER

Fred Schumacher was born on December 31, 1902 to Jacob and Magdalena (Meier)
Schumacher.  He received an education equivalent to fifth grade while growing
up on the family farm, homesteaded by his parents in 1900, eight miles
southeast of Hosmer in Sangamon Township.  On December 7, 1930 he married Clara
Guthmiller.  Clara, the daughter. of Theobald and Katherine (Mayer) Guthmiller,
was born March 5, 1911. Fred and Clara farmed the family homestead until 1966
when they retired and moved to Ipswich.  They were members of the Hope Lutheran
Church southeast of Hosmer of which he was the treasurer until it was
disbanded.  They then became members of St. John's at Hosmer.  He also served
as a township supervisor for many years.  Three children were born to this
marriage.  Verdella was born June 15, 1932.  She received her elementary
education in the SE Sangamon Township School and graduated from Hosmer High
School in 1950.  She married Marvin Gross on June 23, 1952.  She and Marvin are
residing in Volga, South Dakota where they own and operate the Gross
Construction Co. They have five children.

Inez was born July 10, 1936.  She received her elementary education in the SE
Sangamon Township School.  She married James Kramer on April 28, 1957.  They
have a home in Rapid City, South Dakota.  They are associated with Samon's
Trucking out of Missoula, Montana, hauling cargo anywhere from Chicago,
Illinois to the West Coast.

Peggy (see LeRoy Jung History).

Fred and Clara are currently living in Ipswich and enjoying retirement.

GOTTLIEB and EMMA SCHUMACHER

Gottlieb Schumacher was born in 1895, in Russia, to Jacob and Maggie (Meier)
Schumacher.  When he was four years old he came with his parents to the United
States.  His parents homesteaded on land about eight miles southeast of Hosmer,
South Dakota, where Gottlieb's son, Ivan, now lives.  He attended rural school,
and worked on his parents' farm.

On June 14, 1926, he was united in marriage to Emma Graf, at the Reformed
pastor's home at Streeter, North Dakota.

Emma was born in 1905, at Streeter, North Dakota, to John and Margaret
(Oberlander) Graf.  She attended rural school and grew up on her parents' farm
at Streeter.

After their marriage they lived with Gottlieb's parents for about three months,
and then moved on their own farm, eight miles southeast of Hosmer, South
Dakota.  This is the same farm on which Emma still resides.

In the summer of 1937, a tornado hit their farm destroying the barn and
granaries.  With the help of the neighbors and friends everything was rebuilt.
In the summer of 1949 a tornado again hit their farm destroying a big barn,
garages, granaries, and the windows in the house.  Again their neighbors and
friends helped them to rebuild.

Gottlieb passed away on November 10, 1970.

Gottlieb and Emma were the parents of 11 children.  One daughter died in
infancy.  They had two sets of twins: Marvin and Ivan, and Delbert and Darlene.

Viola is married to Marvin Winkler, and lives on a farm five and one-half miles
west of Roscoe, South Dakota.

Mildred is married to Alvin Preszler, and they live on a farm five miles
southeast of Hosmer.

Darlene is married to Kenneth Kramlich and they reside in Lodi, California.

Herbert is married to Alice Dabbert, and they farm three and one-fourth miles
east of Hosmer.

Ivan married Arvella Preszler, and they live seven and one-half miles southeast
of Hosmer.

Leo is married to Ardella Forkel, and they live on a farm in the Long Lake,
South Dakota area.

Lawrence is married to Elaine Kranzler and resides in Eureka, South Dakota.

Marvin married Luella Preszler and they live in Rapid City, South Dakota.

Robert and Delbert live on the home farm with their mother, Emma.  She does the
cooking and washing for them for pastime.

MR. and MRS.  HERBERT SCHUMACHER

Herbert Schumacher was born May 7, 1927 to Gottlieb and Emma (Graf) Schumacher
southeast of Hosmer in Sangamon Township.  He is one of 11 children.  He
attended the southeast Sangamon School.  At age 18 he entered the army on
November 9, 1945 and was discharged February 20, 1947.

On June 8, 1952 he married Alice Dabbert at Roscoe, who was born north of
Roscoe April 15, 1932 to Christ R. and Lydia (Kranzler) Dabbert.  They lived on
the John Schaible farm for a few months, then they lived on the Philip Gutjahr
farm until October of 1956 when they moved on the Emil Walth farm where they
are still living.

This union was blessed with four children.  Keith was born December 30, 1954.
He married Marie Malsom June 11, 1977.  They have two daughters Leanne and
Denae and live in Aberdeen.  Twila was born June 15, 1956.  She married Kenny
Pfaff August 6, 1977.  They have two children Mellisa and Devin, and live in
Ipswich.  Shirley was born July 4, 1958.  She married Edwin Farris June 16,
1979.  They have three sons Eddie Lee, Christopher and Daniel.  They live in
South Sioux City, Nebraska.  Stanley was born April 4, 1960.  He lives in
Aberdeen.

IVAN and ARVELLA SCHUMACHER

Ivan Schumacher was born on October 26, 1930 at Roscoe to Gottlieb and Emma
(Graf) Schumacher.  He grew up on his parents' farm, attended the southeast
Sangamon Township school, and also worked as a hired hand for neighboring
farmers.  He was drafted into the U.S. Marines in 1952 and served for two years
during the Korean War.  On September 21, 1954, he married Arvella Preszler in
the Reformed Church in Hosmer.  Arvella was born to Arthur and Anna (Himmerich)
Preszler on June 17, 1935.

They have farmed in the Hosmer area since then and are at present time living
on the Schumacher homestead, which they purchased from Ivan's uncle, Fred, who
is now retired in Ipswich.  They have four children and six grandchildren.
Sandra was born August 7, 1955.  She is married to Donald Hettich of Roscoe.
They have two sons, Christian and Joshua, and live in Roscoe.  Susan was born
on March 10, 1959.  She married Pius P. Malsom of Roscoe.  They have two sons
and one daughter: Jamie, Casey, and Craig.  They live on a farm near Roscoe.
Bruce was born on November 7, 1963.  He is married to Michele Markovetz and has
one son Bruce Jr. They live in Roscoe.  Bob was born on August 9, 1969.  He is
living at home and attending high school in Hosmer.

Ivan has served on the church council and is also township supervisor and
Arvella is a member of the ALCW.  They are members of St. John's Lutheran
Church in Hosmer.

JACOB SCHUMACHER FAMILY

Jacob Schumacher and wife Magdalena (Meier) Schumacher were born and raised in
Glueckstal, South Russia.  In the year 1900 they and their young family
immigrated to the United States and settled on a homestead eight miles
southeast of Hosmer in Sangamon Township.

A short nine years later Mrs. Schumacher passed away, leaving behind a family
of ten children.  The children were: Jacob Jr., Christian, Mrs. Michael
(Fredaricka) Hauck, Mrs. Mary Martinsen, Gottlieb, Mrs. Rosa Grant, Mrs. Jacob
(Margaret) Rohrbach, Fred, Mrs. Daniel (Magdalena) Junkert and John.

JOHN P. and KATHERINA SCHUMACHER

John P. Schumacher was born November 13, 1895 in Glueckstahl, South Russia to
Peter and Christina Geist Schumacher.  He came to America in 1911 with his
parents. His first stop was New York.  There, they boarded a train which took
them to Chicago, Minneapolis, Aberdeen, and finally to Roscoe, where they
decided to stay.

John and his parents settled on a farm 6 1/2 miles east of Hosmer, where Melvin
and Ruth Schumacher farm today. John lived with his parents until his marriage
to Katherina Eckmann on March 28, 1920.  Katherina was the daughter of
Frederick and Magdalena Gramm Eckmann.  John and Katherina farmed in this area
until 1968, when he retired.

Two children were born to this union: Leontina was born January 6, 1921.  She
attended rural school for eight years and graduated from Hosmer High School in
1940.  Leontina taught rural schools in Edmunds and McPherson counties for
eight years.  She remained on the farm home where she took care of her parents
until the time of their deaths.

Melvin was born June 15, 1922.  He married Ruth Neifer on September 4, 1949.
Their entire family history is also in this book.

John died on August 11, 1984 and Katherina died April 1, 1979.

MELVIN and RUTH SCHUMACHER

Melvin Schumacher was born on June 15, 1922 to John and Katherina Eckmann
Schumacher.  He attended grade school in Edmunds County and received his eighth
grade diploma.  He attended Hosmer High School from where he graduated in 1940.
He farmed with his father on his present day farm.  Melvin also taught rural
school for three years.

On September 4, 1949 Melvin was united in marriage to Ruth Neifer.  She is the
daughter of John and Rosina Hieb Neifer.  Ruth attended grade school in
McPherson County, graduated from Hosmer High School in 1946 and received a
Bachelor of Science degree in elementary education in 1972 from Northern State
College in Aberdeen, South Dakota. Following graduation she taught rural
schools in Edmunds and McPherson counties and in the Hosmer Elementary School
since 1964.  Ruth is presently teaching fifth and sixth grades at Hosmer Public
School.

Melvin and Ruth live on Melvin's family farm located 6 1/2 miles east of
Hosmer.  They are members of St. John's Lutheran Church in Hosmer and Melvin is
a member of Hosmer's Sportsman's Club.

Melvin and Ruth have one son, Dean Wade, who was born on December 14, 1950.  He
is married to the former Bonnie Harty and they have one son, Darren Dean.  Dean
and Bonnie's history is also in this book.

DALE and DELENE SCHUTZ

Dale, born June 8, 1983.

FRANCIS and AGNES SCHWINGLER

Francis Schwingler was born March 23, 1939 in Edmunds County, to Leo and Agnes
(Schumacher) Schwingler. Francis lived with his parents until his marriage to
Agnes Meier on January 2, 1961.  Agnes was born on June 18, 1941 in Strasburg,
North Dakota.  Francis and Agnes farmed near Roscoe until 1977 when they
purchased his father's farm west of Hosmer, where they presently reside.

Francis and Agnes were blessed with six children.  Dennis was born April 18,
1962 and graduated from Hosmer High School.  He then attended Bismarck Jr.
College and completed a one-year course in auto mechanics.  He married Joyce
Miller from Hague, North Dakota and they now live in Bismarck.  Dennis is
employed at Winger's Cabinet Shop and Joyce is working at First Federal Savings
and Loan.

Richard was born August 19, 1963 and graduated from Hosmer High School.  He
attended Bismarck Jr. College and completed a one-year course in heating and
air conditioning. He married Claudia Miller of Hague, North Dakota and they now
live in Bismarck.  Richard is employed at the Ramada Inn and is taking a home
course in electronics.  Claudia is an R.N. at Medcenter One.

Vivian was born September 15, 1964 and graduated from Hosmer High School.  She
married Wallace Berger of Glen Ullin, North Dakota.  They live on a dairy farm
near Glen Ullin.

Kerry was born August 22, 1967 and graduated from Hosmer High School.  He then
went to Bismarck where he is employed as a bellman at the Ramada Inn.

Kimberly was born April 20, 1971, and Johnny was born February 8, 1974, both
are students in the Hosmer school.

MICHAEL and SHARON SCHWINGLER

Michael Schwingler was born March 13, 1955 in Eureka to Leo and Agnes
Schwingler.  He grew up on a farm 12 miles west of Hosmer and attended Hosmer
Public School.  After his graduation from Hosmer High School, he moved to

and was employed at High Grade Oil.  Mike married Sharon Schneider, daughter of
