ALBERT and HILDEGARD STABBE

Albert Stabbe and Hildegard Walth were both born and raised in Hosmer, South
Dakota.  They attended school here and both graduated from Hosmer High School
in 1931.

Albert was an auto mechanic and Hildegard was employed at the Hosmer Telephone
Company.  After their marriage on August 27, 1937, they made their home in
Sheboygan, Wisconsin for two years then returned to Hosmer.

Albert was a mechanic for 42 years, being employed at the Pontiac and Ford
Garage and later at the Walth Motor Company for 25 years.  The Walth Motor was
destroyed by fire May 2, 1973, after that Albert did carpenter work and
continued with mechanic jobs.  Hildegard was employed at the Hosmer Senior
Citizens Home for 15 years.

Mr. Stabbe passed away July 3, 1981.

The Stabbes have two sons: Ronald, a guidance counselor in Imperial, Nebraska;
and Lyle, a math teacher at Tri-Valley High School in Lyons, South Dakota and
two granddaughters, Lisa and Leila.  Lisa is attending Augustana College in
Sioux Falls and Leila is attending Tri-Valley High in Lyons.  Lyle is married
to Linda Mohr, presently employed at the Lyons Bank.

The Albert Stabbe family had the distinction and honor of being the first
family where all four members were graduates of Hosmer High School.

FERDINAND and REGINA (RIEDE) STABBE

Ferdinand and Regina (Riede) Stabbe came to the United States in 1910 from
Russia, with five children, also Grandmother Riede; Grandfather Riede had died
in Russia.  They came to Lowry, South Dakota where two of Mrs. Stabbe's
brothers had settled, and they stayed with them for only a short time.  They
then moved to Bowdle, South Dakota.  Mr. Stabbe was a carpenter and wood worker
by trade.  Finding no work at Bowdle, they moved to Hosmer, South Dakota in
1911, where he found work, helping build the Catholic church which later was
moved to Eureka, South Dakota in the 1960s, when a new Catholic church was
built in Hosmer.  Mr. Stabbe also helped build a Lutheran church about nine
miles northeast of Hosmer.  He also built the present Reformed church in
Hosmer, plus many houses and business places, also in surrounding area, and
other towns.

Mr. Stabbe was fluent in reading, writing and speaking the Russian and German
languages.  He wrote letters in the Russian language for other people to be
sent to their relatives in Russia.

During the years the family increased by six more children.

Their forefathers had migrated to South Russia from Germany, when Czarina
Catherine the second had her ministers solicit German people to come and settle
in Russia, their village was Zgrichthal in the Crimea section of Russia, close
to the city of Odessa, on the Black Sea.

Russia had good fertile soil and the settlers made progress, but finally
because of the envy of the Russians, so many emigrated to the United States and
Canada, another reason was the compulsory military training.  Mr. Stabbe had
served in the Russian Army.

Mr. Stabbe was born in 1877 and died in 1931 at the age of 54.

Mrs. Stabbe was born in 1877 and died in 1965 at the age of 87.

Mrs. Stabbe usually had a large garden, plus raising chickens, having a milk
cow, which most pioneer families had.  If the rain came, the food, etc., raised
helped fill the winter pantry needs.

After Mr. Stabbe's death, Mrs. Stabbe made her home with her children until her
death.

These pioneer families lived through hard times, also the dirty thirties, when
there wasn't any rain, only blowing dust most every day.  There were no
government handouts, people had to make their own way.

They had to do without many things, but their faith and trust in God carried
them through all the time, and they were content with what they had.  Most
pioneer families were large and cared for each other; their families and their
church were their support.  Their sustaining strength came from the Bible,
Psalm 121, King James version: "I will lift my eyes unto the hills, from whence
cometh my help.  My help cometh from the Lord, which made Heaven and Earth."

At this writing, the following Stabbe children are still among the living, plus
grandchildren and great-grandchildren: Mrs. Ernestine Kunde, Senior Citizen's
Home, Hosmer, South Dakota; Mrs. Elsie Wondergem, Sheboygan, Wisconsin; Mrs.
Emma (Rudy) Heier, Akaska, South Dakota; Mr. Edward Stabbe, Mobridge, South
Dakota; Mrs. Martha Kranzler, Roscoe, South Dakota.

Those who died are as follows: William Stabbe, born 1898, died 1939; John
Stabbe, born 1903, died 1961; Lydia Stabbe Sattler, born 1906, died 1983,
Albert Stabbe, born 1913, died 1981; Eugene Stabbe, born 1920, died 1937; and
Daniel Stabbe died 1986.

FLOYD W. STABBE

Floyd W. Stabbe was born in Hosmer and was the son of William and Ida Stabbe.
He worked at the Hosmer Red and White Store while in his junior and senior year
in high school. After he graduated in 1947 he started to work for Farmers Union
Co-op Oil Co. as a bookkeeper and worked there until 1963.  In 1949 he also
started to work part time at Farmers State Bank as a bookkeeper and teller.  In
1963 he worked part time as bookkeeper for Farmers Elevator Co. of Hosmer, Inc.
until 1967.  In 1964 he became an employee of The Haerter-Heckenlaible
Corporation, and in 1980 became the manager of the corporation.

Mr. Stabbe became the city finance officer in 1964, and also became part-time
clerk at the post office in 1970.  He is still working at all three places.

ROYAL O. STABBE

Royal O. Stabbe was born in Hosmer and was the son of William and Ida Stabbe.
After graduation from high school in 1945 he became employed at Farmers State
Bank as a bookkeeper and teller, and later became assistant cashier and also a
director.

In 1968 he started working part time for John Heckenlaible, Inc. as bookkeeper,
and is still working there.  He worked for the Hosmer Theatre from 1943 to
1969, and he also was treasurer for the Hosmer school district from 1960 to
1976.

After having worked 40 1/2 years at the bank he decided to retire in 1986.

WILLIAM and IDA STABBE

William Stabbe was born at Krim, South Russia in 1898, and was the son of
Ferdinand and Regina Stabbe.  He came with his parents to America in 1910 and
settled in Hosmer, where he assisted his father with carpenter work for several
years.  In 1913 he became employed as a mechanic in the local Ford Garage where
he worked until his death.

In 1921 he married Ida Dockter who was the daughter of John and Katherina
Dockter and grew up on a farm in McPherson County northeast of Hosmer.  They
had made their home in Hosmer, and had three children: Norma, who was married
to Andy Schauer, she died in 1973; Royal and Floyd living in Hosmer.

Mr. Stabbe died of a heart attack while at work in 1939 at the age of 41 years.
 Mrs. Stabbe continued to live in Hosmer until her death in 1970 at the age of
69.

JOHN and MARTHA STACKER

John Stacker was born October 30, 1893 in Blaine Township, McPherson County to
Frederick and Katherine Martell Stacker.  His parents were born of German
descent in Hoffnungstahl, South Russia and had homesteaded in the Eureka area
in 1890.  He was the fourth child in a family of nine; six sons and three
daughters.  He served in World War I, worked for the railroad in Colorado, and
was a chimney builder in North Dakota before returning to South Dakota to work
as a field man for a cream company out of Mitchell, South Dakota.  He started a
produce business in Hosmer, buying and selling cream, wool and eggs about a
year before his marriage to Martha Mehlhaff on October 22, 1925 at the Reformed
Church in Hosmer.  In later years he switched to shoe repair.

Martha Mehlhaff was born September 15, 1903 to John and Margaret Goehring
Mehlhaff on a farm in the Hillsview area, the youngest in a family of eight
children.  She later moved to Hosmer with her family at the age of thirteen and
they lived three houses down the street from John's parents on the southwest
end of Hosmer.  She attended school in Hosmer.  As a young woman, she managed a
dry goods store and worked as a hotel clerk.

John and Martha were blessed with three daughters, the youngest died in
infancy.  Marcella graduated from Hosmer High School.  She is married to Walter
Mohr.  Their history is in this book also.

Virginia was born April 16, 1932.  She graduated from Hosmer High School and is
married to Don Anderson from Minneapolis, Minnesota where they currently
reside.  She is employed at Betty Crocker in Minneapolis.  They have two
children; Anita (Mrs.  Steve Manders) who lives in Eagan, Minnesota.  Tom lives
in Hollywood, California.

Martha Stacker passed away September 10, 1981 and John Stacker resides at the
Hosmer Senior Citizen's Home.

ALBERT STOECKER

Albert (Stack) Stoecker was born in Hosmer in 1913 to Gottlieb and Katherine
Stoecker.  He was the third son in a family of four boys and three girls.

Albert attended school in Hosmer graduating from Hosmer High School in 1931.
This was the first four year class to graduate from Hosmer High School.  He
then attended business college for one year.

Albert was employed for several years in a service station, and managed his
father's elevator for a year.  In 1935 he and Edwin Eisenbeisz took over the
general merchandise store located where Lustermar Cultured Marble Mfg.  Co. is
today.

He married Lydia Roesler in 1936.  They were the parents of a son, Weston, and
a son that died in infancy.

After Albert and Edwin Eisenbeisz sold their store to Gottlieb Walth in 1940,
the family moved to Billings, Montana. Here Albert managed meat departments
until 1948 when Safeway Stores transferred him to Bozeman, Montana. While in
Bozeman he became a salesman for Crescent Mfg. Co. of Seattle, manufacturers
and distributors of flavors and spices.  He retired from Crescent in 1978 and
returned to Hosmer in 1980 to enjoy his retirement years.

Lydia died in October 1972, after a five month illness.

His son, Weston, married Janean Kessler in Bozeman, Montana, in 1962 and they
are the parents of three children: Craig, Brian, and Wendy.  Weston is a
partner in an electrical supply business in Anchorage, Alaska where he and his
family reside.

CARL and HELEN STOECKER

By Grandson Stephen C. Bakken

Carl and Helen Stoecker's 52-year love-affair with each other is something
their children and grandchildren continue to remember about the Hosmer, South
Dakota couple, even though they died within eight months of each other in 1979.

Carl Stoecker was born about 15 miles northeast of, Hosmer on May 13, 1896, one
of five sons and three daughters raised by Fred and Kathrina Stoecker, emigrants
from South Russia.  His formal education consisted of attending rural school
for 10 years until 1912, studies at the Redfield, South Dakota Academy from
1912 to 1916, followed by one year at the Bismarck, North Dakota Business
College.

From September 1917 to July 1919, Carl served as U.S. Army Mess Sergeant at
Camp Funston, Kansas, and later, Fort Worth, Texas.  After receiving his
honorable discharge, Carl returned to Edmunds County, South Dakota, where he
would spend the rest of his life.

It was during Carl's return to the Dakotas that the strapping young World War I
veteran noticed and started dating a pretty, fresh-faced girl whose parents
also came from South Russia.  Helen Emilie Haerter was born at Hosmer May 16,
1899, to John and Christina Heckenlaible Haerter, the only daughter in a family
of five children.

Helen furthered her education in South Dakota by attending cooking and sewing
school in nearby Aberdeen. When her father chartered Hosmer's first savings
institution on October 18, 1919, the Farmers State Bank, Helen and her brother,
Arthur, were named among its first stockholders.

During a sunny day in March 1927, Helen Haerter married Carl Stoecker in
Bowdle, South Dakota.  The couple eventually made their home in Hosmer in a
large two-story house built by Helen's father.  The Stoeckers had two children:
Alice Christina Bakken was born July 3, 1928; and Robert Carl was born January
29, 1934.

While Helen's next half-century was consumed with domestic tranquility, Carl's
business career revolved around cars, corn, and candidates.  From May 20, 1920,
to February 8, 1929, Carl owned and managed the Hosmer Auto Company, even
rebuilding it in 1925 after it burned to the ground on August 24, 1924.  On May
1, 1930, Carl joined Martin Preszler in buying the Thompson Lumber Yard.  Ten
years later, his partner bought Carl out.  Carl then purchased the Interior
Lumber Company, which he also sold, in 1940, to Heckenlaible and Meier.

In 1940, Carl took over ownership of the Gottlieb Stoecker Grain Company, which
he sold July 1, 1944, to Ed Heyd of Long Lake.  That November 15, Carl acquired
the Kranzler Grain Elevator and the next March, the Walth Grain Elevator.
Jacob Goehring was his partner until July 1, 1950, when they amiably dissolved
their partnership.  Carl continued operating the re-named Hosmer Grain Company
until July 1, 1958, when he sold out to Leo Goehring, and was supposed to
retire.  Well, he didn't. (The only thing Carl would never retire from was
gardening.)

Four years earlier, in 1954, the life-long Democrat had been elected Edmunds
County commissioner, an office he would hold for 20 years, until 1974, when he
decided to retire (again).  In his honor, Carl was always allowed the license
number '1896,' his birth year.

Hosmer's 1969 Outstanding Citizen of the Year was also on the town council,
town board, Northeast Mental Health Center, Civic Club, park commission,
American Legion, Sportsman's Club, and was a volunteer fire fighter and city
assessor.

By this time, daughter, Alice, had married Cliff Bakken and they gave the
Stoeckers three grandchildren: Steve, Tom and Christine.  Since the couple's
birthdays were only three days apart, the family often celebrated birthdays
together.

The couple were life-long members of St. John's Lutheran Church in Hosmer and
its many circles, committees and organizations.

Helen's family and friends recall her as a person with great inner strength.
Helen reflected the culmination of years of experience in knowing just how to
keep calm; knowing God would work all things out.  An incredibly petite person,
she withstood 79 years of both good and ill health before dying of a stroke in
Aberdeen, South Dakota on January II, 1979.  Four days later her funeral was
held at St. John's Lutheran Church on the coldest day of the year.

Carl never fully recovered from the loss of his wife.  Once a hardy man with
bottomless energy, he now became easily exhausted, often tired.  Within eight
months he, too, suffered a stroke.  Carl died at the age of 83 on the evening
of August 26, 1979, also in Aberdeen, and was buried five days later with full
military honors from the American Legion alongside his wife at Bethany Cemetery
in Hosmer.

More importantly than the memorials at St. John's, Carl and Helen are
remembered by their family and friends as people who taught others how to love,
how to hope; how to laugh, how to weep; how to live, and finally, how to die.

JOHN G. and LUANE THURN

John G. Thurn was born February 22, 1947, at Eureka, South Dakota, to Rudolph
G. and Alma (Schick) Thurn.  He was raised on a farm seven miles southeast of
Eureka.  He attended Eureka High School, Northern State College and graduated
from the University of South Dakota with a degree in accounting.  John is
presently engaged in a tax service practice in Hosmer.  Luane J. (Oster) Thurn
was born September 27, 1954, to Gerhart and Dorothy (Schmidtgall) Oster.  She
was raised on a farm 12 miles northeast of Java.  Her family moved to Bowdle in
1969.  She graduated from Bowdle High School and graduated from Stewart's
School of Hairstyling in Aberdeen.  Luane worked for beauty shops in Mobridge
and Bowdle and is still licensed in South and North Dakota.

John and Luane were married May 25, 1984.  They presently reside in Hosmer
where they own and operate a tavern and restaurant known as the Good Friends
Tavern.

They have one daughter, Amber Lemke, born November 1, 1979.

ALICE ULMER

Alice Ulmer was born on January 21, 1903 to Christ and Katharine Ulmer, on a
farm southeast of Menno, South Dakota.  In the spring of 1917, when Alice was
14 years old, Christ with his family moved from Menno to Hosmer, South Dakota
where Alice finished her schooling and was confirmed in the Lutheran faith.
They established their place of residence on the farm at the west end of Hosmer
which is now owned by her nephew, Curtis Aman.  At their new location the
Christ Ulmers were engaged in active farming for many years, with Alice taking
an active part in most farm activities.

During her lifetime Alice managed to keep busy.  She was employed at the Boldt
Store for a number of years, and later she was an assistant cook for the Hosmer
School Lunch Program for several years.

Alice's hobby, which later turned into quite a full-time occupation, was making
quilts.  Alice has some of her homemade quilts in many states of the United
States.  When Alice died she left behind many unfinished quilts which were
later finished by friends and relatives.

Alice was a member of the St. John's Lutheran Church of Hosmer and also an
active member of the Ladies Aid.

Alice never married and lived on the Ulmer homestead here in Hosmer until she
died on January 11, 1968.  She was 65 years old when she died.

DARWIN and KAY ULMER

Darwin Ulmer was born April 6, 1941 to Rupert and Orma (Gross) Ulmer in
Sangamon Township, Roscoe, South Dakota.  He was the only boy in a family of
five girls.

On April 3, 1961 he married Kay Krumm, daughter of Val and Clara (Dewald)
Krumm.  There were five children born to this marriage.  Tera, born June 3,
1962, married Marco Weisser and lives on a farm four miles south and on mile
east of Roscoe.

Kristan was born June 1, 1963 and married Steve Scherr. They live on a farm
seven miles west and three-fourths of a mile south of Roscoe.

Bradley was born September 12, 1964 and married Paulette Vetch.  They are
living in Watertown.

Russell was born August 16, 1968 and died July 20, 1972.

Tricia was born September 7, 1973 and lives at home with her parents.

Darwin and Kay have six grandchildren: Derrick and Brenton Weisser, Bryan and
Lori Scherr, Amanda and Mark Ulmer.  They reside on the family farm located six
miles east and three-fourths of a mile south of Hosmer and raise registered
Holsteins and Arabian horses.

EDMUND and ERNA ULMER

Edmund C. Ulmer was born on August 21, 1892, on a farm southeast of Menno, the
third of 11 children of Christ and Katharine Ulmer.  Christ and Katharine had
come as youngsters from Russia, when their parents immigrated to the United
States to avoid serving in the Russian military.  Their two families came to
America on the same boat.

Edmund grew up on the family farm.  As a young man he spent some time in St.
Paul, attending college at the University of Minnesota.  He worked for awhile
as a schoolteacher in Menno, and ran a grain elevator in Lesterville, South
Dakota.

Christ Ulmer moved his family to Hosmer in 1917.  In 1918, as the First World
War was nearing its end, Edmund was drafted into the army.  While he was
undergoing basic training he contracted spinal meningitis, and very nearly died.
Instead, he lost his hearing and his sense of balance, making it impossible for
him to finish his education, and to become a lawyer, as he had hoped.  He found
himself, at the age of 26, forced to look for alternatives.  For many years he
traveled the region around Hosmer, where he bought cattle from farmers and
shipped them to stockyards in the east.  During this time he practiced some
veterinary medicine.

In 1939 Edmund married Erna Boldt, and they lived in a small house which he
built on the 80-acre farm he owned just east of the Hosmer city limits.  They
eventually moved into town, to a white frame house which Erna's father, Frank
Boldt, had built.  The couple had two children, Victor, born in 1940, and
Milton, born in 1942.

He served as the town treasurer for many years.  He helped many people prepare
their tax forms.  In between he played a lot of pinochle, euchre, and gin rummy
at the "hangout," as he called it, or at the Legion Hall.  After Erna's death
in 1960, Edmund lived alone, usually sharing the house with a dog (Rusty, and
later Myrtle, whom he referred to, for simplicity's sake, as "Fido").  He
adored young children and dogs.

Despite his deafness, Edmund never felt that life had been unfair to him.  He
had a wonderful sense of humor, and an openness to people of all ages and
backgrounds.  Since he was unable to hear, Edmund carried on a voluminous
correspondence; it seemed that a lot of people wanted to remain in contact with
him.  His house was always full of papers.  As he himself admitted, "I have the
magpie instinct too strongly developed." He would charm waitresses (who often
were unaware of his deafness) by telling them, "That's the kind of service you
read about, but so seldom get."

Edmund remained young at heart throughout his life.  He died on January 30,
1980, at the age of 87.

EDWARD and ANNA ULMER

Edward J. Ulmer was born to Christ and Katharine Ulmer at Menno, Hutchinson
County, South Dakota, on November 29, 1887.  He attended school and grew to
manhood on a farm about eight miles southeast of Menno.  In 1910 he married
Anna, also a born Ulmer, at Menno.  Anna was born to Andrew and Carolyn Ulmer
on January 10, 1887, on a farm southeast of Menno, where she attended school and
lived until she was married.

Anna was quite a talented person.  Besides being a good wife and mother she was
quite artistic, being able to draw most anything she set her mind to.  She also
was musically inclined in that she could play a number of instruments, namely
piano, violin, mouth organ and accordion.  Due to the hard pioneer days and
lack of time and money, she was unable to pursue these talents to any degree.

After Edward and Anna were married they moved to a farm also in Hutchinson
County, South Dakota, about two miles southeast of Edward's home place where
they farmed until Edward's folks, the Christ Ulmers, decided to sell all of
their holdings in the Menno area and move to Hosmer, South Dakota.

The Edward Ulmer family along with the entire Christ Ulmer family arrived in
Hosmer on the 17th of March, 1917. After everything was organized and settled,
Edward and his family moved to a farm two miles southeast of Hosmer where they
farmed till about 1943 at which time Edward retired from farming and moved into
Hosmer.

Edward and Anna were blessed with six children: Gertrude (Ulmer) Preszler, now
farming five miles southwest of Roscoe, South Dakota; Flora (Ulmer) Jung,
retired and living in Hosmer; Rupert, retired and also living in Hosmer; Allan,
a building contractor, and living in Circle Pines, Minnesota; Gloria (Ulmer)
Geier, along with her daughter have a doll boutique in a part of her husband's
barber shop at Aberdeen, South Dakota; Virginia (Ulmer) Meier; lives in
Rocklin, California, now retired after selling their car dealership which they
had for many years.

In November of 1937, Anna died of cancer at the age of 50 years, after which
Edward no longer had the desire to farm, and moved to Hosmer where he worked at
various jobs, such as elevator construction, K. O. Lee in Aberdeen and farm
work.  In August of 1956, after having heart trouble for some time, he died.
At the time of this writing all of Edward's and Anna's children are still
living.  Edward and Anna were members of St. John's Lutheran Church of Hosmer
for as long as they lived in Hosmer.

FLOYD and LORNA ULMER

Floyd Ulmer was born to Julius (1905-1984) and Eva Gisi Ulmer (born June 28,
1908) on March 15, 1930.  Lorna was born to Robert Ernst and Ida Schick Ernst
on March 8, 1929. Floyd and Lorna were married on September 30, 1948 and this
union was blessed with three children.

Mrs. Sandra Anderson was born January 17, 1950 and graduated from Hosmer High
School in 1968.  She married Rick Anderson and they have two children: Marlin
and Melissa.

Terry was born August 27, 1952. (See Terry and Carol Ulmer History.)

Mrs. Shelly Warmer, born August 10, 1957, graduated from Hosmer High School in
1975.  She married Perry Wanner and they have one daughter, Kristie.

Floyd and Lorna have been farming three miles southeast of Hosmer since their
marriage.

GUSTAVE and ELENORA ULMER

Gustave Ulmer was born to Christian and Katharine Ulmer at Menno, South Dakota,
January 13, 1890.  He lived with his parents on a farm in Menno until March
1917 when they moved to Hosmer.  He was united in marriage to Elenora Walter
April 2, 1916 in Menno.  Elenora was born in Derebchin, Russia May 28, 1896 to
Johan and Julianna (Koch) Walter.  She came to the United States with her
parents at the age of four and they settled in the Menno area.

Several moves were made before Gust and Elenora settled on a farm 13 miles west
of Hosmer where they resided until 1953 when they retired and moved into Hosmer.

This union was blessed with five children.  A son, Willard, was born to them
while living in Menno, January 10, 1917.  Willard married Alice Gross and they
have two children and reside on a farm southwest of Leola.  Erna was born at
Hosmer June 18, 1918 and married Edmund Sonnenfeld. They live in Seattle,
Washington and have five children and one son died as the result of an
automobile accident.  Naomi was born January 2, 1920.  She married Arnold Hieb
and they have five children.  They live on a farm north of Hosmer. Viola was
born November 3, 1921 and resides in Hosmer.  She married Reinhold Weishaar,
who passed away June 8, 1984 and they have six children.  Kermit was born
January 19, 1924 and is married to Myra Gentz and is residing in Oakes, North
Dakota.  He has two daughters.

Gust passed away December 5, 1962 at the Ellendale Nursing Home.  Elenora was a
resident at the Hosmer Senior Citizens Home from 1975 until her death, January
17, 1986.

JULIUS and EVA ULMER

Julius (Joe) Ulmer was born in Menno, South Dakota June 1, 1905, to Christian
and Katharine Ulmer.  He lived with his parents in Menno until March 1917 when
they moved to Hosmer.  He attended school here and worked on the farm with his
parents and operated the silent movie theater for a period of time.

He married Eva Gisi at Hosmer in 1929.  Eva was born in Leola January 28, 1908,
to Joseph and Katherine Gisi.  She was one year old when she moved to Hosmer
with her parents. After attending school, she worked in the Hosmer area doing
housework.

Joe and Eva built up a farm 1 1/2 miles east of Hosmer where they farmed until
1977 when they moved into Hosmer.

Joe spent many years as a clerk of Sangamon Township, served on the
Agricultural Adjustment Act Committee, the Agricultural Stabilization
Conservation, the Soil Conservation Service Committee and a Federal Land Bank
director. Eva raised her family and also had a bout with tuberculosis and spent
a number of years in the sanitarium in Custer, South Dakota.  When her family
was grown, she was employed at the Senior Citizen's Home in Hosmer for many
years.

Joe and Eva have five children.  Floyd was born March 15, 1930 and attended
school in Hosmer and married Lorna Ernst.  They are farming three miles
southeast of Hosmer and have three children.  Leo was born in 1932 and died in
infancy. Harley was born September 21, 1938 and attended school in Hosmer.  He
married Gloria Martell and they farmed with his parents until his death in
January 1973.  Harley and Gloria had three daughters and triplet sons born to
them September 1964.  One triplet son died in infancy.  Jeanette was born
December 31, 1940 and attended school in Hosmer.  She is married to John
Dimensky and lives in Ear Claire, Wisconsin.  She is employed as an accountant
and has three children.  Elaine was born August 31, 1942.  She attended school
in Hosmer and Business College in Rapid City.  She moved to Minneapolis and
married John Jahraus.  They have two children.

Joe died June 9, 1984 and Eva is retired and living in Hosmer.

RUPERT and ORMA ULMER

Rupert Ulmer was born June 3, 1915, at Menno, South Dakota, to Edward and Anna
Ulmer.  In the spring of 1917 they moved from their farm home southeast of
Menno to a farm two miles southeast of Hosmer where they resided until Rupert
had grown to manhood.  Rupert attended grade school in Sangamon Township, high
school in Hosmer, and one year of college at Northern State College, Aberdeen,
South Dakota.  He taught school in Sangamon Township for five years.

Rupert married Orma Gross on April 11, 1937, in Hosmer.  Orma was born on April
9, 1917, at Leola, South Dakota, to Christ and Katy (Hieb) Gross.  When Orma was
about a year old Christ and Katy moved with their family to a farm 12 miles
east and one-half mile north of Hosmer. During the 30's Orma worked for
friends, neighbors, and relatives before going to Aberdeen where she worked as a
nurse's aide at the Good Samaritan Hospital.

In 1940 Rupert and Orma bought a farm six miles east and three-fourths of a
mile south of Hosmer.  They moved to their new home in Hosmer in December 1983.

Rupert and Orma have six children.  Lora married Clarence DeWald and they live
in Aberdeen, South Dakota. Lora works with Medical Records in the hospital and
Clarence is with the SD Highway Patrol.  Evelyn married Don Twite.  She is a
part-time teacher and Don is superintendent of the DeSmet school system.
Darwin married Kay Krumm and they operate a dairy on the home farm.  Ileen
married Doug Lindner and they manage a Fairway Store in Dickinson, North
Dakota.  Ileen works in a Dickinson bank.  Patty married John Beyers and they
operate a large grain farm.  John works in the Roscoe Bank.  Carolyn married
Donley Lang. They own and operate Lang's TV & Appliance in Aberdeen, South
Dakota.  At the present time Rupert and Orma have 24 grandchildren and 9
great-grandchildren.  Included in the nine great-grandchildren is one set of
triplets: Erin, Megan, and Lory Fry (grandchildren of Lora and Clarence DeWald).

Rupert and Orma during their lifetime have been involved in various community
activities such as school board, church board, Ladies Aid, ALCW, Farmers Union,
etc.  They have been members of St. John's Lutheran Church of Hosmer all their
married life.

SOLOMON and JOHANNA ULMER

Solomon Ulmer, son of Christ and Katharine Ulmer, was born and raised at Menno,
South Dakota, where he married Johanna Steiger.  They moved to the Hosmer area
in 1917, and resided on a farm three miles east of Hosmer until 1949 when they
retired and moved into Hosmer.

Solomon served as county commissioner from 1942 to 1954, as a member of the
County Extension Service, and on St. John's Lutheran Church Council.

Solomon and Johanna were the parents of two children: Irene Roesch of Roscoe,
South Dakota; and Edgar of McIntosh, South Dakota.

TERRY and CAROL ULMER

Terry Ulmer was born to Floyd and Lorna Ernst Ulmer on August 27, 1952.  He
grew up on the family farm east of Hosmer with his two sisters; Sandra and
Shelly. (See Floyd and Lorna Ulmer History.) He graduated from Hosmer High
School in 1970 and attended the University of South Dakota, Vermillion.  Terry
worked for a time as a Brown County sheriff's deputy in the Aberdeen, South
Dakota area, then moved back to the Hosmer area in the spring of 1976 to take
over his grandfather's farm 1 1/4 miles east of Hosmer.

Carol Schumacher was born on September 3, 1953 to August and Ida Schumacher.
She grew up in Aberdeen, South Dakota and graduated from Central High School,
Aberdeen, in 1971.  She also attended the University of South Dakota at
Vermillion receiving a degree in social work.

Terry and Carol were married on August 26, 1972, and this union was blessed
with one son, Shannon, who was born on November 10, 1982.

Terry is currently farming and Carol is a Geriatric Social Worker.  Terry is an
avid hunter and fisherman and is a member of the Hosmer Sportsman's Club.  The
family belongs to St. John's Lutheran Church in Hosmer.

PAUL P. VETCH FAMILY

Paul P. Vetch was born August 16, 1924 at Roscoe, South Dakota, son of John and
Elizabeth (Biernbaum) Vetch.  He attended school at Roscoe.  He spent two years
in the service (1945-46) and served in the Southwest Pacific. After returning
home, he was engaged in farming.

He married Katherine Malsom in Hosmer on May 17, 1948.  Katherine, daughter of
Anton and Magdalena (Fisher) Malsom, was born June 6, 1925 at Hosmer.  She
attended a rural school in the Hosmer area.  After their marriage they farmed
in the Bowdle-Hosmer area until poor health forced Paul to quit farming in
1973.  They moved into Hosmer where Kate was employed at the Senior Citizen's
Home.  Paul was a township supervisor and A.S.C.S. committeeman for a number of
years.

They have 14 children: Janis married Ralph Borge, they live in rural Aberdeen;
Judy married Don Bremer, they live in Aberdeen where they own and operate
Bremer Photography; Joyce married Bruce Mettler, they live in Selby; Jeanette
married Chuck Nietert, they live in Aberdeen where she works in the Brown
County Auditor's office; Kathleen married Mike Beck, they live in Rapid City
where she works in a day care center; James married Patty Savage, they live in
Aberdeen; Wilbert married Patricia Bauer, they live in Bloomington, Minnesota;
John married Ronda Jacobs, they live in Mitchell; Paul Jr. married Diane
Kjenstad, Paul is assistant manager at Ken's Fairway in Ipswich; Mary married
Rodney Job, they live in Aberdeen; Richard lives in Aberdeen and works at
Dutch's Place; Paulette married Bradley Ulmer and lives in Watertown where Brad
is attending Vocational School; Thomas died in infancy; and Michelle is
attending Roncalli School in Aberdeen.

Paul is a member of the Hosmer American Legion and the D.A.V. of Aberdeen.

KEN and DORIS VILHAUER

Ken Vilhauer's great-grandfather, Friedrick Vilhauer, purchased his current
farmstead from the United States Government, through the Aberdeen Land Office,
in 1890.

In 1926, Friedrick Vilhauer sold the farm to his son, Christian (Christ)
Vilhauer, who is Ken's grandfather.

In 1946, Alvin Vilhauer, Ken's father, inherited an undivided one-fifth
interest in the farm.

In 1948, Alvin and Ella Vilhauer, Ken's parents, became sole owners of the
farmstead.

In 1975, Ken rented the farm from his parents.  On November 6, 1976,,Ken
married Doris Schwartz from Aberdeen, South Dakota.  After the wedding, they
continued to rent the farm from Ken's parents until they purchased the farm in
1977.

Ken and Doris have four children at home: Jeff, born April 2, 1969; Kelly, born
November 29, 1971; Kathy, born August 3, 1973; and Jason, born February 13,
1982.

The Ken Vilhauer farm has been in his family for four generations--ninety-six
years.

CHRISTIAN and ROSA WALTH FAMILY

Christian Walth, son of Johann and Maria (Jutzy) Walth was born January 31,
1897.  He was joined in marriage to Rosa Hieb November 28, 1915 at Leola.  She
was born May 18, 1895 to Mr. and Mrs. Jacob Hieb.

Christian and Rosa farmed his father's farm northeast of Hosmer from 1915 until
1945 when they moved to Hosmer, and son, Milbert took over the farming
operation.

This union was blessed with two sons: Milbert was born February 22, 1924 and
Walter was born August 28, 1933 and died at the age of 16, December 24, 1949.

Christian died at the age of 56, May 23, 1953.

Rosa married Emanuel Aman June 8, 1962 and he died November 11, 1983.  Rosa is
a resident at the Ipswich Colonial Manor.

Milbert Walth was born to Christian and Rosa (Hieb) Walth February 22, 1924.
He attended rural school and farmed with his parents 10 miles northeast of
Hosmer until his marriage to Melitha Guthmiller March 15, 1943.  Melitha was
born October 2, 1923 to Theobald and Katherina (Mayer) Guthmiller.

Milbert and Melitha farmed with his parents until they retired and moved to
Hosmer.  Milbert and Melitha continued to farm the family homestead until
December 1979 when they moved to Aberdeen.

They were blessed with four daughters.  Shirley was born October 16, 1943,
married DeWayne Jeschke of Roscoe June 10, 1962 and they have three children.
They reside in Roscoe where Shirley is employed as the main cook at the Roscoe
Public School.

Marlene was born November 11, 1944 and married Jim Holzer of Aberdeen January
20, 1968.  They live in Minneapolis where Marlene is employed with Honeywell Co.

Mary was born August 21, 1946 and married Paul Heier of Hosmer June 3, 1966.
They farm near Hosmer and have four children.

Myra was born October 2, 1955 and married Don Oswald of Eureka November 10,
1972.  They have two children and reside in Aberdeen where Myra is employed as a
Tupperware manager.

Milbert and Melitha worked for the Elks Lodge for four years and are presently
employed as custodians at the Moose Lodge in Aberdeen.

EARL WALTH

Earl Walth was born December 23, 1921, at Hosmer, South Dakota.  He graduated
from Hosmer High School and attended Chillicothe Business College in
Chillicothe, Missouri.  He worked at the Eureka State Bank from 1940 to 1942.
He enlisted in the marines in 1942, and spent 31 months in the South Pacific.
He was discharged April 4, 1946.

Earl married Peggy Rasmussen June 1, 1946, in Seattle, Washington.  In 1946
they moved to Hosmer and went into business with his father in the G. J. Walth
& Co. In 1948, along with Arnold Pietz, they established the Lucky Strike
Bowling Alley in Hosmer.

Earl and Peggy had four children: Michael, Janet, Stephen, and Mary.  In 1951
they moved to Colorado Springs, Colorado.  There Earl worked at several jobs.

On Friday, January 13, 1961, he was the victim of a head-on collision which
left him badly crippled.  He was laid up for almost one year.  He then became
manager of a lumber company.

In 1964 he was project manager in a construction company and worked with
construction.

He moved to Dallas, Texas and continued in construction. In 1980 he married
Jean Withrow from Texas.  He suffered a massive heart attack on September 21,
1985, and pass ed away on October 6, 1985.

Earl was the son of Gottlieb and Emma (Herrboldt) Walth.

EDWIN and ALMA WALTH

Edwin John Walth was born to John and Christina (Frey) Walth on June 1, 1906.
He grew up on the family farm three miles east and one-half mile north of
Hosmer.  He attended the rural township school and then attended one term at
the State Agriculture School (now known as South Dakota State University) in
Brookings, South Dakota.  On November 13, 1927 he married Alma Guthmiller at
the Jehovah Lutheran Church northeast of Hosmer.  Alma is the daughter of
Theobald and Katherina (Mayer) Guthmiller.  She was born on June 18, 1908 on a
farm five miles east and one mile north of Hosmer.  She received an elementary
education at the NE Sangamon Township School while growing up on the family
farm.  After their marriage they moved to a farm three and one-half miles east
of Hosmer and farmed there until October 1956.  They then moved to Hosmer
leaving son Vernon in charge of the farm.  Edwin did some side-walk farming and
carpenter work until he retired.  Alea was employed at the meat market for some
time and later at the Senior Citizen's Home.  Two sons were born to this
marriage, Vernon (in another history) and Milton.

Milton was born January 23, 1938.  He grew up on the farm and attended the NE
Sangamon Township grade school and graduated from Hosmer High School in May
1956.  In June 1956 he enlisted in the US Navy and eventually made it into a 20
year career.  He retired from the Navy in September 1975.  He married Margaret
Patterson of Bellevue, Idaho on December 2, 1961.  They have two children:
Richard and Pamela.  They are now residing in Bellevue, Idaho where Margaret
teaches school and Milton raises cutter bees.

Edwin and Alma are members of the Senior Citizen's and are enjoying retirement.
Edwin served as Sangamon Township school board clerk and also as a township
supervisor. He served as a director of the Farmers Union Oil Co. and was a city
alderman.  He served as a deacon and treasurer of St. John's Lutheran Church of
which both are members. Alma is a member of the ALCW Honorary Circle.

GOTTLIEB and EMMA WALTH

Gottlieb J. Walth, son of Johann and Maria (Jutzy) Walth, was born October 7,
1893, on the family homestead in McPherson County, Blaine Township, near
Hosmer, South Dakota.  It was here that he grew to manhood living in a sod
house joined with the barn.

Emma Herrboldt, daughter of Adam and Fredericka (Ulmer) Herrboldt, was born
near Menno, South Dakota on August 4, 1897.  The family moved to Hosmer, South
Dakota, in 1906 where she attended school and grew up.  Gottlieb and Emma were
married April 1, 1918 and lived in Hosmer all of their lives.

Gottlieb worked for John Haerter & Co., a grain and implement business from
1915 to January 1917.  He spent the early months of 1917 working in a factory
in Detroit, Michigan.  In April 1917, in association with John Haerter, Jacob
G. Walth, and Edwin Herrboldt, they formed the partnership business known as
Midwest Motor Co., an automobile dealership selling Buick, Maxwell, and Ford
cars.  Due to World War I he sold his stock in this company and joined the
armed forces in May 1917, and served in the U.S. Army in France and was
discharged in June 1919.  Upon his return home he joined a new firm along with
Adam Herrboldt, Edwin Herrboldt, and Eugene Huber, known as A. Herrboldt & Co.,
an implement dealership for International farm machinery.  Due to the severe
business depression and economic decline that started in the early 1920s he
sold his stock to Edwin Herrboldt.  In 1929 he accepted the appointment of
postmaster at Hosmer, a position he held until 1933 when he resigned and
purchased the Jacob Gottlieb grocery store.  In October 1940, he bought the IGA
grocery store from Edwin Eisenbeisz and Albert Stoecker and took on a Super
Valu franchise operating a grocery and general merchandise store.  In 1948 this
business was incorporated under the name of G. J. Walth & Co., with
stockholders being Gottlieb Walth, Emma Walth, Arnold Pietz, Vera Pietz, Earl
Walth, Nora Danduran, and Ellen Hoffman.  Gottlieb retired from active business
in 1959, but remained active in civic and community affairs.  He was an active
member of the American Legion and Hosmer Civic Club.  He held various offices
in the St. John's Lutheran Church and was a member of the building committee
when the new Lutheran Church was built in 1948.  He was a past mayor of the
city of Hosmer, and served as secretary of the Bethany Cemetery Association for
21 years.  As a tribute to his long and devoted service to the Cemetery
Association the archway to the cemetery was built as a memorial in his honor.

Mr. Walth died November 10, 1967 at the age of 74.  Mrs. Walth died July 2,
1977, at the age of 79.

Gottlieb and Emma had four children: Vera, Mrs. Arnold Pietz, Hosmer; Earl,
Fort Worth, Texas; Nora, Mrs. Bob Danduran, Irvine, California; and Ellen, Mrs.
Marvin Hoffman, Redfield, South Dakota.

Nora lived in Hosmer until graduation from high school. She graduated from
Sioux Falls College, Sioux Falls, South Dakota and Long Beach College, Long
Beach, California, with an associate of arts degree in general office.  In 1950
she married Bob Danduran.  Bob is a graduate of Woodbury College, Los Angeles,
with a degree in business administration. They lived in Long Beach 26 years and
moved in Irvine in 1976, both in California.  They have one son, Edward, who
lives in Irvine also, and is married to Priscilla.  They have three girls:
Lisa, Erin, and Kara.  Edward is a graduate of University of Southern
California School of Business and is presently vice president of Finance with
Water and Wood in Gardena, California.  Nora and Bob are both retired
accountants.  They prefer to be semi-retired and do temporary office  work, and
are active in the Homeowner's Association and members of People for Irvine
Community Hospital.

HERBERT J. and RUTH WALTH

Herbert J. Walth was born in Hosmer on March 27, 1916.  He graduated from
Hosmer High School and worked for the Milwaukee Railroad for 43 years as an
agent/operator in Hillsview, South Dakota; Zeeland, North Dakota; and in
Warner, Britton and Milbank, South Dakota.  He was a control tower operator in
the Army Air Corps for 4 1/2 years during World War II and spent three years in
Guadalcanal.  He married Ruth Ibis of Freeman, South Dakota, in Ephrata,
Washington on July 19, 1943.  Herb retired from the railroad in 1980.

Ruth was an elementary schoolteacher for 11 years, three of which were spent in
or near Hosmer.  She was the secretary in the Milbank elementary school for 17
years.  Herb and Ruth have lived at 514 S. 6th St. in Milbank since 1964 and
are the parents of two children: Sheila and Gene.

Sheila was born on March 21, 1953 while they lived at Warner.  She graduated
from Milbank High School and the University of South Dakota.  She is living in
Evergreen, Colorado and is a briefing consultant for AT&T in Denver.

Gene was born on March 22, 1957 in Britton.  He graduated from Milbank High
School and the Lake Area Vocational Technical Institute of Watertown.  Gene
lives in Mitchell, South Dakota and is a draftsman for Dakota Manufacturing.

JACOB and KATHARINA WALTH

Jacob Walth Sr., the son of Jacob and Karolina (Reuter) Walth, immigrated from
Glueckstahl, South Russia in the fall of 1889 with his wife Katharina and three
children.  They filed a claim on the NE 1/4 of Section 31 in Blaine Township,
McPherson County northeast of Hosmer.  There they farmed until they retired, at
which time their son George took over the farm.

Nine children were born to this union, three in Russia and six in this country.
Their children are: Jacob F., John, George, Gottfried, Mrs. Rudolph (Maria)
Hieb, Mrs. Philip (Katherine) Blumhardt, Mrs. Frank (Caroline) Blumhardt, Mrs.
John (Mathilda) Stabbe, and Mrs. Frederrich (Rosina) Schlaht.  They moved to
Hosmer to live their remaining years. Katharina died in 1925 and Jacob died in
1934.

JACOB F. and ROSINA WALTH

Jacob F. Walth was born to Jacob Sr. and Katharina Walth on May 17, 1883 in
Glueckstahl, South Russia.  He came to this country with his parents in the
fall of 1889.  He received only a limited education while he grew up on the
family homestead.  Jacob married Rosina Schumacher on November 24 1907.

Rosina was born to Solomon and Rosina (Bieber) Schumacher on June 28, 1889 also
in Glueckstahl, South Russia.  She came to this country with her parents in
1898 who homesteaded on the SW 1/4 of Section 2 of Sangamon Township. In 1905
they moved about one mile east to the farm last occupied by the Ervin Kranzler
family.  After their marriage, Jacob and Rosina homesteaded a farm 11 miles
east of Hosmer, located on the NE 1/2 of Section 10 of north Bryant Township.
Here they farmed until their deaths.

Eight children were born to this family, four of which died during the 1918 flu
epidemic.  Survivors were: Edwin, Melitha, Herman, and Melvin.  Edwin married
Rosina Schilling of Hosmer and they have a family of six children.

Melitha married August Guthmiller of Hosmer and they have a family of two
children.

Herman (deceased) never married.

Melvin, refer to the Melvin Walth history.

JACOB G. and MARGARETHA WALTH

Jacob G. Walth was born in August 1881 in Glueckstahl, South Russia to Johann
and Maria (Jutzy) Walth.  At the age of eight, he came to this country with his
parents settling in McPherson County near Eureka.

Margaretha Huber was also born in Glueckstahl, South Russia in May 1886.  As a
young girl, she and her parents, John and Katherina (Dockter) Huber, settled
near Hillsview, South Dakota.

In January 1906, Jacob and Margaretha were married and the young couple made
their home in Hosmer.

Jacob bought a partnership in the grain and implement business with John
Haerter Sr.  In 1919 a new firm, Midwest Motor Company was founded by Haerter
and Walth.  Soon after, Haerter sold his grain and implement share to Jacob
Lammle and Haerter took over Walth's share in the Midwest Motor Company.  This
resulted in the forming of a new partnership of Walth and Lammle in the grain
and implement business.  In 1930, the partnership was dissolved with Walth
keeping the grain elevator, which he operated until 1945 when he sold the
elevator to Jacob Goehring.  Jacob then opened a small business of feed, flour
and paint just to be in contact with the people.

Jacob Walth served on the city board from 1912 to 1914 when he became city
treasurer until 1926.  In 1928, he was elected the first mayor of Hosmer.  Mr.
Walth was very involved in community affairs.  He played baritone in the Hosmer
Brass Band, sang in the church choir and men's chorus and was a charter member
of the Hosmer Commercial Club which now is known as the Civic Club, he also was
treasurer of the Hosmer Volunteer Fire Department.

He passed away at his home in Hosmer in 1961 at the age of 79 years.  Mrs.
Walth passed away in 1976 at the age of 90 years.

The Walths had five children: Lydia, who passed away in 1933; Matilda (Mrs.
Sam Pietz); Hildegard (Mrs.  Albert Stabbe); Reinhold (Chip) all of Hosmer; and
Herbert of Milbank, South Dakota; eight grandchildren and eight great-
grandchildren.

JOHANN WALTH FAMILY

Johann Walth was born on July 30, 1854 to Jacob and Karolina (Reuter) Walth in
Glueckstahl, South Russia.  Here he grew to manhood and was joined in marriage
to Maria Jutzy on November 1, 1877.  Maria's parents were Johann and Rosina
(Stotz) Jutzy.  Following the marriage he served a term in the Russian army and
then farmed until September of 1889.  He then sold his belongings and
immigrated to America with his wife and a family of five children, ranging from
six months to eleven years of age.  They arrived in McPherson County on October
11, 1889.

To this family of five Russian children, namely John (1878-1969), Jacob
(1881-1961), Mrs. Jacob (Karolina) Lammle (1883-1947), Mrs. George (Rosina)
Keszler (1887-1969), and Mrs. Philip (Mary) Huber (1889-1983), four Americans
were born, making the total nine.  The Americans were: George (1891-1957),
Gottlieb (1893-1967), Martin (1894-1966), and Christian (1897-1953).

In the fall of 1915, Johann and Maria returned to village living as they had
been accustomed to in Russia and retired to Hosmer.  A cow and a large garden
provided pastime and sustenance.  Johann died on March 3, 1932 and Maria died on
April 4, 1936.  Both are buried in the Bethany Cemetery in Hosmer.

JOHN J. WALTH FAMILY

John J. Walth, the son of Maria and Johann Walth, was born on August 27, 1878
in Glueckstal, South Russia.  In the fall of 1889 when he was 11 years old he
was brought to this country by his parents.  John grew up during the trying
pioneer years when most of the farm work was done with oxen.  He and his
brother, Jacob, broke most of the land on their father's farm when they were
only II and 8 years old. This work was done with a team of oxen.  The land had
to be cleared of rocks before and after plowing, even their mother helped.

John Walth was married to Christina Frey on November 18, 1902.  Christina, the
daughter of Jacob and Barbara (Becker) Frey, was born on January 5, 1884 near
Tripp, South Dakota.  As a small girl she and her parents moved from Tripp to a
homestead north of Hosmer where she lived until her marriage.  After their
marriage they homesteaded on the SW 1/4 of Section 3 in Sangamon Township.  The
NW 1/4 of the same section was given to John by his father to insure a better
start.  Their income was small for the first two years. They had to clear the
land, break the sod, and seed flax.  They had three horses, a wagon, a hand
plow, a small drag, four cows and a heifer calf, and one dozen chickens.
However, health, fortitude, courage, and high hopes won out and thirty years
later, at the time of their retirement they owned 800 acres.  The price they
paid for some of the last land they bought would have bought half a township
when they started out.  In the last few years of farming the Walths used a
tractor. John also owned a steam-powered threshing machine along with his
father and brother Jacob.  Later on they used a small thresher driven by a
tractor.

Three sons were born to this union.  Rev.  Arthur was born September 11, 1903.
He attended the rural township school and went on to continue his education at
the Lutheran Academy at Eureka, South Dakota.  He graduated from the Wartbury
Seminary at Dubuque, Iowa in 1932 and was ordained into the ministry the same
year.  On June 19, 1948 he married Emily Boelter at Jamestown, North Dakota.  He
served various parishes in North Dakota and is now retired in Minot, North
Dakota.  Two children, Sandra Kay and Gary Kent, were born to this union.

Edwin (in another history in this book).

Emil was born on November 18, 1908.  He attended the rural township school
while growing up on the family farm. He married Albina Guthmiller on February
7, 1932.  Albina was born on March 8, 1913 to Theobald and Katherina (Mayer)
Guthmiller.  She grew up on the family farm five miles east and one mile north
of Hosmer.  She attended the rural township school.  They made their home on
the family farm and farmed there until March 1954 at which time they moved to
Lead, South Dakota where he was employed at the Bald Mt.  Mining Co. Some years
later they moved to Deadwood.  He was employed at the city water works until he
retired.  They are still residing in Deadwood, South Dakota.

In the fall of 1932, John and Christina retired and moved to Hosmer where they
lived their remaining years.  For many years they had a cow, some chickens and
a large garden making them self-sufficient.  In August 1966, they became one of
the first residents of the Senior Citizen's Home.  John died on July 4, 1969
and Christina died on June 17, 1982.  Both are buried in the Bethany Cemetery
at Hosmer.

THE MARTIN WALTH FAMILY

Martin Walth was born December 14, 1894 to Johann and Maria (Jutzy) Walth on a
homestead on Section 19, Blaine Township, McPherson County, South Dakota.  Along
with his older brothers and sisters he helped on the farm with herding cattle,
carrying water, and chores of all sorts.  He attended rural grade school which
was built on a high hill.  Thus he could later on tell his children that he
went to the high school.  As he grew to manhood he met Josephine Hieb, a
neighbor girl who was born to Michael and Rosina (Borkirchert) Hieb on May 6,
1896.  Martin and Josephine were married November 18, 1915 after which they
established a farm three miles east and one mile north of Hosmer in McPherson
County.  The farm after many years of hard labor and good management became a
successful one.  On this farm a new family of one daughter and three sons was
raised.  Edna was born August 20, 1916 and married Arthur C. Buechler of rural
Bowdle in 1935.  Their family of two sons and one daughter are Norman and Alan,
currently living in Arizona and Mardella (Mrs.  Dale Bieber), now residing on a
farm at rural Bowdle, South Dakota.

Ervin, born June 5, 1918, married Josephine Farone of St. Paul, Minnesota in
November 1949.  Ervin served in the infantry in World War II in the New Guinea
area.  After he returned, he went into the implement business with Milbert
Heckenliable in New Prague, Minnesota.  Currently, he is residing in Rochester,
Minnesota, now in the real estate business.  They have one son, Daniel.

Harold, born May 24, 1920, an air force lieutenant, died in 1943 during a
bombing mission over China.

Elton, born February 17, 1926, married Violet Graff of Aberdeen, South Dakota.
Three children were born to this union; two girls, Susan and Janet, and one
son, Mark.  Elton is an ALC pastor currently serving a Lutheran congregation in
Geneva, Nebraska.

In the fall of 1945 Martin and Josephine sold their property and moved to
Aberdeen.  He was a custodian for a number of years.  Martin died of cerebral
hemorrhage May 30, 1966.  Josephine died of Parkinson's disease November 26,
1970.

MELVIN and DELORES WALTH

Melvin A. Walth was born to Jacob F. and Rosina (Schumacher) Walth on February
17, 1927.  He attended rural township school and grew to manhood on his
father's farm. In 1950 he was drafted into the U.S. Army and served for two
years.  On May 27, 1954 he married Delores Rohrbach. Delores was born on
February 27, 1933 to Fred and Lydia (Voegele) Rohrbach of Hosmer.  They made
their home on the family homestead.  They have two children: Lynell and Monica.

Lynell was born July 5, 1964.  He attended the Hosmer grade and high school and
graduated from high school in 1983.  He then attended Lake Area Vo-Tech in
Watertown and studied electronics.  He graduated from Vo-Tech in June 1985 and
is employed in San Diego, California.

Monica was born on March 7, 1967.  She attended the Hosmer schools and
graduated from Hosmer High School in 1985.  She is enrolled at Stewart School
of Hairstyling in Aberdeen.

Melvin has been township treasurer for some 20 years, a previous assessor and
served as a church council member.  He sells Funk's seeds and they are Shaklee
distributors.  They are members of St. John's Lutheran Church of Hosmer.

REINHOLD P. (CHIP) and AMELIA WALTH

Reinhold Paul Walth was born October 6, 1919 in Hosmer to Jacob G. and
Margaretha (Huber) Walth.  He attended school in Hosmer and graduated in 1938.
In July of 1938, he entered the CCC's in the Black Hills area for a year. Upon
return, he helped his father in the grain elevator.  He was drafted into the
army in 1942 and was discharged in 1945. He worked as a postal clerk in the
Hosmer Post Office from 1945-1947.

On July 21, 1946 he married Amelia Goetz at Java, South Dakota.  Amelia is a
graduate of Java High School and NSC.  She taught school in Hosmer for 27
years, retiring in 1981.

On August 1, 1947, Chip and Sam Pietz purchased J. Heckenlaible and Son
Chevrolet Agency, Repair Shop and Hardware.  They operated the business and
were the first to own and operate school buses for the Hosmer School District.
Pietz and Walth Motor Company was dissolved July 1, 1961 when the firm's name
was changed to Walth Motor Company. The firm was destroyed by fire May 2, 1973.
In December 1973, Chip was employed by the State Motor Vehicle Division as a
dealer inspector until June 1984 when he was transferred to the Highway Patrol
Auto Theft Unit.  Chip retired June 1, 1985 and is now living in Hosmer.

Chip and Amelia have two daughters.  Jacqueline Marie was born October 2, 1949.
She graduated from Hosmer High and attended SDSU and NSC.  She taught at
Edgemont and Philip, South Dakota.  On May 25, 1980, she married Glen Radway at
Faith, South Dakota, and they now live on a ranch near Milesville, South
Dakota.  They have two children: Leah Marie, born March 16, 1983; and Carey
Jacob, born July 22, 1985.

Nancy Jane was born November 16, 1952.  She graduated from Hosmer High in 1971
and attended Presentation College and NSC in Aberdeen.  Upon graduation she
began teaching in Zeeland, North Dakota Public School where she is presently
employed.  She married Craig Rutschke on December 27, 1980.  Craig and Nancy
own Rocky's Bar in Zeeland.  They have two daughters: Rochelle Rae, born
November 2, 1982; and Jaime Lee, born July 11, 1985.

VERNON and VERNA WALTH

Vernon Walth was born on the family farm three and a half miles east of Hosmer
to Edwin and Alma (Guthmiller) Walth on March 9, 1932.  He attended the NE
Sangamon Township School for eight years and graduated from Hosmer High School
in May of 1950.  He farmed with his father after graduation.  On November 1,

education in the NW Sangamon Township School and graduated from Hosmer High
School in May 1952.  After their marriage, they moved to a neighboring farm and

farm upon his parents' retirement.  Six children were born to this union.
Robert was born on May 10, 1955 in Bowdle, South Dakota.  He attended the
Hosmer school and graduated in May 1973.  He then continued his education at
Northern State College in Aberdeen, majoring in medical technology.  He
graduated in 1977.  He interned at Sioux Valley Hospital in Sioux Falls, South
Dakota for one year and has been employed at the same hospital since.  He
married Glenda Johnson of Watertown on June 22, 1985.  They have one daughter,
Robyn Lyndee.  Michael was born December 14, 1956 at Leola, South Dakota.
He attended the Hosmer school and graduated in May 1975.  He then attended the
North Dakota State School of Science in Wahpeton, North Dakota for two years

employed at the Rapp Chevrolet garage in Marion, South Dakota for a number of

Wakefield, Nebraska as a route truck driver.  He married Patricia (Talbert)
Sweeny of Marion on May 1, 1981.  He has three stepchildren.

Valerie was born on September 17, 1961 at Leola, South Dakota.  She attended

she was the music instructor in the Viborg school system for one year, after

music.

Patricia was born on April 17, 1966 at Leola, South Dakota.  She attended the

major and graduated in 1986.  On June 28, 1986 she married Raymond Frydenlund
of Hopkins, Minnesota.

Susanne was born January 27, 1969 at Aberdeen, South Dakota.  She attended
Hosmer grade school and will graduate from Hosmer High School in the centennial
year of 1987.

Karen was born October 12, 1971 at Aberdeen, South Dakota.  She attended Hosmer
grade school and will graduate from Hosmer High School in 1989.  Both Susanne
and Karen are members of the Bells of Celebration (handbell choir).

Both Vernon and Verna are active in church and community affairs.  Verna is
active in ALCW, was Sunday school teacher and superintendent and a choir
member.  Vernon served on the Cenex board of directors, elevator board and the
community fire department, now reorganized to Hosmer Fire District.  He was
also a Sunday school teacher, choir and council member.  They are members of
St. John's Lutheran Church at Hosmer.

GARY and JUDY WEISHAAR

Gary Weishaar was born April 28, 1948 at Ipswich to Reinhold and Viola (Ulmer)

JACOB and MARTHA WEISHAAR

Jacob Weishaar was born to John and Magdalena (Eman) Weishaar May 11, 1905 in
McPherson County.  His father was born in South Russia March 4, 1876 and died
December 16, 1959.  Magdalena was born in South Russia November 8, 1880 and
died January 18, 1960.

Jacob lived with his parents on a farm northeast of Hosmer until his marriage
to Martha Guthmiller November 23, 1930.  Martha was born November 28, 1909 in
Edmunds County to Theobald and Katherina (Mayer) Guthmiller. Theobald was born
in South Russia October 21, 1879 and died in February 1951, and Katherina was
born October 20, 1884 and died in March 1961.

Jacob and Martha farmed in the Hosmer, Roscoe and Bowdle areas until they moved
to Aberdeen in 1961.  Jacob was employed with the city department for several
years and numerous other jobs and Martha did babysitting in her home. They are
presently residing in Aberdeen.

Jacob and Martha were blessed with four children.  Erma was born October 26,
1931 and married Roy Meier and resides in Roscoe.  They have five children:
Annette, Sonia, Mark, Jeffery (deceased) and Jennifer.

Rosella, born  May 24, 1937, married Warren Reimer, and living in Mankato,
Minnesota.  Their family consists of four children: Greg, Dori, Dana, and Nikki.

Lyle was born July 25, 1942 and married Margie Daugherty.  They are residing in
Carbondale, Colorado and have two children: Derek and Rachel.

Linda was born February 18, 1946 and is living in Racine, Wisconsin.

Jacob and Martha have 11 grandchildren and 7 greatgrandchildren.

JOHN SR. and MAGDALENA WEISHAAR

John and Magdalena Weishaar came to the United States with their six month old
son, Fred, in March 1902.  They were on a ship a month before they arrived here
and homesteaded six miles northeast of Hosmer, where they farmed for forty
years.

John, son of Gottfried and Christina (Bollinger) Weishaar was born March 4,
1876 in Hoffnungstal, South Russia. He was united in marriage to Magdalena Eman
June 26, 1900 at Hoffnungstal, South Russia.  She was born November 8, 1880 in
Russia to Frederick and Dorothea (Krein) Eman. Fifteen children were born to
this union.  Two sons, Edwin and Reinhold died in infancy and one daughter,
Helen, died at the age of four.  Fred was born September 4, 1901 in South
Russia and passed away February 21, 1968 at Aberdeen. He never married.  John
was born February 6, 1903 and married Ann Neuharth.  Jacob was born May 11,
1905 and married Martha Guthmiller.  Arthur was born August 17, 1906 and died
October 13, 1982 at Leola.  He married Ramona Reede.  Emma was born August 13,
1909 and married Christ Maier, who died February 16, 1970.  Lydia was born
February 19, 1911 and married Milbert Adam, who died in 1963.  Edwin was born
October 10, 1912 and married Marie Kirschenmann.  William was born October 2,
1914 and married Alma Zelmer.  She died January 21, 1978.  Later he married
Althea Buechler.  Martha was born March 9, 1916 and married Eugene Opp.
Pauline was born December 2, 1918 and died August 23, 1978 at Selby.  She
married Harold Horst.  Reinhold was born August 26, 1920 and died June 8, 1984,
at Hosmer.  He married Viola Ulmer, and for 30 years they farmed the family
homestead.  Esther was born December 12, 1922 and married Ervin Preszler.

John and Magdalena farmed until April 4, 1942 when they retired and moved to
Leola.  On October 12, 1958 they entered the Eureka Lutheran Nursing Home.
John died December 16, 1959 and Magdalena passed away January 18, 1960.  They
are buried at the Green Mound Cemetery, Leola.

ARNOLD and MARGARET WIEDERRICH

Arnold Wiederrich was born to Edwin and Paulina (Stoecker) Wiederrich on
February 19, 1945.  He grew up on the farm and attended rural township school
and Hosmer grade school.  In 1963 he graduated from the Hosmer High School.  He
enlisted in the U.S. Navy where he spent some time at the U.S. Naval Base in
Cuba, and two years on the USS Cadmus.  Following his father's death in 1966 he
took over the family farm.  On October 27, 1968 he married Margaret Imberi.

Margaret, the daughter of Frank M. and Cecelia (Heilman) Imberi, was born on
March 26, 1949.  Margaret attended the Hosmer schools and graduated from Hosmer
High School in 1967.  She attended the Humbold Institute airline training
school and was an airline reservationist at St. Louis, Their family consists of
four children: Stuart, born August 24, 1969; Laura, born October 25, 1970;
Erica, born April 19, 1973; and Todd, born March 25, 1974.  All are attending
Hosmer grade and high school at the present time.

Arnold is active in community affairs, having served as secretary of the
elevator board of directors, as Legion post commander, and as vice president
and president of Civic Club.  Margaret is a member of Legion Auxiliary, Holy
Trinity Christian Mothers and a catechism instructor.  Arnold is the third
generation on this farm, homesteaded by his grandfather in 1893.

EDWIN WIEDERRICH FAMILY

Edwin Wiederrich was born on February 19, 1903 to Jacob and Philipina (Will)
Wiederrich on a farm in Sangamon Township.  He received a few years of
schooling at the rural township school and grew to manhood working on his
father's farm.  On November 15, 1925 he married Emma Bollinger of Merricourt,
North Dakota.  The marriage, however, was short-lived when Emma became ill and
passed away on April 16, 1929.  She was buried in a cemetery west of Monango,
North Dakota.

On June 22, 1929 he married Pauline Stoecker.  She was born on a farm northeast
of Hosmer to Jacob and Carolina (Gienger) Stoecker on February 20, 1906.  Six
children were born to this union.  Irma was born March 28, 1930.  She attended
the rural township school and graduated from Hosmer High School in May 1949.
On October 24, 1949 she was married to John Grischkowsky Jr., the son of Mr.
and Mrs. John Grischkowsky Sr. of Hosmer.  After the marriage they made their
home at the Black Hills Ordinance Depot at Igloo, South Dakota.  Here their
three children, Nancy, Charles and Gene, were born.  When the depot was closed
down they moved to a similar installation at Pueblo, Colorado.  They are now
residing in Aurora, Colorado.

Arlene was born on November 27, 1931.  She attended rural school and on October
7, 1951 she married Elmer Grosz, son of Mr. and Mrs. Martin Grosz of rural
Eureka, South Dakota.  At this time Martin and his wife retired to Eureka
leaving the farm to Elmer and Arlene on which they are still residing.  They
have four children: Lavonne, Esther, Nora, and David.

Verna (see Vernon Walth history).

Vivian was born May 16, 1937.  She attended the rural township school and
graduated from Hosmer High School in May 1955.  On May 28, 1957 she married
Harry Martell, son of Jacob and Magdelina (Mohr) Martell of rural Hosmer. After
their marriage they farmed in McPherson County for a number of years and then
moved to Hosmer where Harry was employed as city police and Vivian was employed
at the Senior Citizen's Home.  After a few years they moved to Redfield, South
Dakota where Harry is employed with the State Highway Department and Vivian is
employed at the State Hospital.  Five children were born to this union: Doreen,
Harlan, Danny, Monte, and Janeen.

Wilmer was born on March 2, 1941.  He attended the township school and
graduated from Hosmer High School in May 1958.  He then continued his education
at Northern State College.  On April 22, 1962 he married Elizabeth
Heckenlaible, daughter of Wilmer and Amy (Jakober) Heckenlaible. After their
marriage they lived on the family farm and farmed with his father until 1966
when they left the farm and moved to Aberdeen, where he is employed with
Safeguard Industries. They have two children: Steven and Mark.

Arnold (see Arnold Wiederrich history).

Edwin and Pauline retired in the fall of 1963 and moved to Hosmer.  Edwin
served as a school board treasurer in Sangamon Township and also as a Sunday
school teacher and church treasurer of St. Paul's Congregational Church of
which they both were members.  Edwin passed away on December 3, 1966 and
Pauline passed away April 21, 1974.  Both are buried in the Hosmer Cemetery.

THE JACOB WIEDERRICH FAMILY

Jacob Wiederrich was born to George and Julianna (Bentz) Wiederrich on February
2, 1862 in Neudorf, South Russia.  Here he grew to manhood and on February 18,
1886 he was united in marriage to Philapina Will.  Philapina was born on
January 1, 1865 also in Neudorf, South Russia.

In 1893 they left Russia with three small children and arrived in this country
by way of Canada.  After a brief stay in Canada they resumed their journey and
homesteaded on the SE 1/4 of Section 6 in Sangamon Township.

Some time later the farm was relocated to the SW 1/4 of the same section.  Here
they farmed until 1925 at which time they retired from the farm and moved into
Hosmer, leaving son Edwin in charge of the farm.

Nine children were born to this union.  Mrs. Fred (Christina) Hoffer was born
in 1887 and died in 1955.  Mrs. Gottlieb (Julianna) Heyne was born in 1889 and
died in 1964. Jacob was born in 1891 and died in 1908.  John was born on board
the ship while crossing the Atlantic Ocean in 1893. Edwin was born in 1895.
Martha was born in 1897.  Early in 1898 John, Edwin and Martha became ill with
scarlet fever.  On February 6, 1898 Edwin and Martha died.  John survived the
illness but permanently lost his hearing.  He never married and died in 1960.
Mrs. John (Martha Marie) Roesch was born in 1899 and died in 1940.  Mrs. Philip
(Magdalena) Gutjahr was born in 1901 and is still living in Aberdeen.  Edwin
was born in 1903 and died in 1966.  Philapina Wiederrich died on December 14,
1934 and Jacob died on August 25, 1943.  Both are buried in the cemetery in
Hosmer.

ARTHUR and EDNA (ZASKE) WILL

Arthur Will was born May 14, 1896 to Johann and Katharina (Krein) Will.  He
attended rural school in Hillsview District 11.  On November 19, 1917 he
enlisted in the navy and served until August 9, 1919.  After his discharge he
attended Bismarck Business College at Bismarck, North Dakota and The Mission
House Academy at Plymouth, Wisconsin.

On November 27, 1926 he married Edna Zaske, daughter of Frederick and Martha
(Schmidt) Zaske of Olivia, Minnesota. Edna taught in several of McPherson
County's rural schools.  They made their home on the Will homestead.  Arthur
Will passed away on March 2, 1977.  After that, the farm was sold to the
neighbors Stanley and Kenneth Hieb.  Edna moved to Underwood, Minnesota where
she made her home until her death on November 28, 1981.  Both are buried in the
city cemetery, Eureka, South Dakota.

They had one child, Marcella June Will, born August 29, 1927.  Marcella also
attended rural school in Hillsview District 11. She was confirmed in St. John's
Lutheran Church, Hosmer.  She graduated from Hosmer High School and went on to
college at NSC in Aberdeen, where she obtained her B.S. degree in elementary
education.

On May 9, 1953 she married Marcus Job, son of William and Bertha (Opp) Job, of
rural Leola, South Dakota.  Marcus earned his M.S. degree in secondary
education at NSC, Aberdeen, South Dakota.  Marcus and Marcella taught school in
Minnesota and South Dakota before moving to their farm near Underwood,
Minnesota.

They have one daughter, Cindy June, born June 15, 1959.  She married George
Hopkins on June 9, 1979.  They have a daughter, Emily Megan, age three, and a
son, Marcus Joseph, born May 25, 1984.  Cindy is an RN and George is an
electrical engineer.  They live in Chippewa Falls, Wisconsin.

JOHANN and KATHARINA (KREIN) WILL

Johann and Katharina's ancestors went to Russia in the early 1800s.

Johann Will was born April 19, 1850, in Neudorf, South Russia to Philipp and
Margaretha (Krein) Will.  On October 21, 1873 he married Katharina Krein.  She
was born January 19, 1854 in Neudorf.  Her parents were Karl Krein and his
wife, Christina (Heinz) Krein.  Johann and Katharina lived in Russia for 12
years before coming to the United States.  On April 19, 1885 they and their
five children left for America. They arrived here on May 24, 1885, after
undergoing a storm at sea in which the ship lost its course.  Coming directly to
Dakota Territory they homesteaded in Hillsview Township approximately four
miles north of Hosmer.  They built a combination sod house and barn.  After the
family was settled Mr. Will walked to Columbia, a three day journey, to obtain
work.  In 1886 he planted his first crop.  Also during 1886 he helped build the
railroad between Hosmer and Eureka.  During his years of farming he built up a
farm which was later owned by his youngest son, Arthur.

Johann and Katharina had 12 children.  Only eight of them survived: Katharina
Schmitt Reule, Karolina Jung, Jacob Will, Christina Will, Margaretha Maier, Eva
Kirk, Lydia Keim, and Arthur Johann Will.

Katharina (Krein) Will passed away February 6, 1915. She is buried on the
homestead along with three of their children.  One child was buried in Russia.
Johann Will passed away February 17, 1942.  He is buried in the Hosmer City
Cemetery.

CHARLES and SANDY WOLF

Charles Wolf was born June 2, 1950 to Leonard and Maggie (Gehring) Wolf.  He
grew up on his parents' farm 20 miles northeast of Hosmer, and graduated from
Hosmer High School in 1968.  Later he farmed with his parents and married Sandy
Schock of Forbes, North Dakota, October 11, 1975.  Sandy was born September 23,
1953 to Ernest and Olivia (Sandmeier) Schock.  She graduated from Forbes High
School, attended Stewart School of Hairstyling, and was employed at the Classic
in Aberdeen until her marriage.

Charles and Sandy farmed on the family farm until 1977 when they purchased a
3.2 bar and restaurant from Terry Cox, in Hosmer.  They owned and operated C.
W.'s until 1979 when they bought a farm 4 1/2 miles west of Hosmer where they
are presently residing.

The couple was blessed with three children: Jason Charles was born April 29,
1979; Lisa Marie was born April 27, 1982; and Jeremy Charles was born May 3,
1986.

They are members of St. John's Lutheran Church in Hosmer.

LARRY and JOYCE WOLF

Larry Wolf was born to Leonard and Maggie (Gehring) Wolf on June 30, 1945 in
Aberdeen.  As a youngster he attended Aberdeen and rural grade school and two
years of high school at Hosmer.  He worked at several jobs before his marriage.

They are members of St. John's Lutheran Church in Hosmer, where Larry has
